Assume a physically realized marked Golay interface code, and construct a Leech representative either by Theorem R.4.9's explicit marked Construction-B coordinates or by Lemma R.4.5's separate $(\sqrt2E_8)^3$ gluing/minimum certificate. Also fix the lattice-to-physical-carrier map, defect configuration space, local operator algebra, dynamics, and superselection criterion. On this branch:

* a **defect (syndrome excitation)** is a localized violation of a declared subset of the interface constraints;
* two defects lie in the same **superselection sector** only when the registered local redundancy operations identify them and the admissible local observables cannot distinguish them;
* interactions require a separately defined fusion or composition law. Appendix G.8.4i supplies a conditional finite predictive defect-fusion category, and Theorems 14.5.7b and 14.5.7n compute the fusion and braiding data on the toric branch and on the coupled Golay–Leech source.

**Conditional flavor comparison branch.** Appendix T supplies a hierarchy model only after additional assumptions independent of Golay/Leech rigidity. The Bures variance $\sigma_B^2=1/24$ requires a centered isotropic tangent distribution with unit mean-squared radius. The coefficient $\alpha=3/2$ additionally uses the convention $\alpha=1/(16\sigma_B^2)$. The charged-lepton distance triple $(2,6,4)$ is an explicitly realizable $E_8$ root triad, not a unique code consequence, and the logarithmic hierarchy relation
$$
\ln\!\left(\frac{m_j}{m_i}\right)=\alpha d_{E_8}^2(r_i,r_j)
$$
is an anchored common-coefficient suppression-law hypothesis. Under that complete flavor branch, the displayed mass ratios are comparison values. The leading absolute value $m_\tau^{(0)}\approx0.94\,\mathrm{GeV}$ retains the recorded factor-of-two normalization gap.

This viewpoint also records the threefold base-lattice decomposition $(\sqrt2E_8)^3$ used by Lemma R.4.5. Proposition R.4.7 proves only its dimensional compatibility with $24=3d_0$ on the $d_0=8$ branch. A triplication of defect-family or generation sectors requires an independent anomaly, topology, or superselection index.

<a id="theorem-1457a-finite-stabilizer-code-vacuum-and-gap-construction"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7a (Finite Stabilizer-Code Vacuum and Gap Construction).** Let $1\le r\le n$, and let $S_1,\ldots,S_r$ be independent commuting Hermitian Pauli operators on $n$ qubits, with $-I$ absent from the generated stabilizer group. The Hamiltonian
$$
H_S=\sum_{j=1}^r\frac{I-S_j}{2}
\tag{14.5.7a.1}
$$
is a commuting-projector Hamiltonian whose ground space is exactly the stabilizer code, has dimension $2^{n-r}$, and is separated from the rest of the spectrum by a gap of one. Choose independent commuting logical operators $\overline Z_1,\ldots,\overline Z_{n-r}$ extending the stabilizers to a maximal commuting Pauli family and signs $\zeta_a\in\{\pm1\}$. Then
$$
H_{S,\zeta}
=H_S+\sum_{a=1}^{n-r}\frac{I-\zeta_a\overline Z_a}{2}
\tag{14.5.7a.2}
$$
has a unique marked ground state and gap one. For every self-adjoint perturbation $V$ with $\lVert V\rVert<1/2$, the perturbed Hamiltonian $H_{S,\zeta}+V$ retains a unique ground state and has spectral gap at least
$$
1-2\lVert V\rVert>0.
\tag{14.5.7a.3}
$$

*Proof.* Every term in (14.5.7a.1) is the projector onto the $-1$ eigenspace of $S_j$, and all terms commute. Their common zero eigenspace is the simultaneous $+1$ stabilizer eigenspace, of dimension $2^{n-r}$ by independence. Every orthogonal joint eigenvector violates at least one projector, so its energy is at least one, and a single violated independent eigenvalue gives energy one. The logical operators split the remaining $2^{n-r}$-dimensional joint eigenspace into one-dimensional simultaneous eigenspaces; the chosen signs select exactly one, with the same unit gap. Weyl's eigenvalue bound moves the ground energy upward by at most $\lVert V\rVert$ and the first excited energy downward by at most $\lVert V\rVert$, giving (14.5.7a.3). ∎

This theorem supplies an exact finite Hamiltonian, vacuum degeneracy/marking classification, gap, and global operator-norm stability bound for every stabilizer code. Theorem 14.5.7m extends it to a marked Golay-to-Leech carrier with local marking terms, extensive-perturbation and tunneling bounds, a response-faithful realization and an all-time bound for the held mark under baths that carry it. Theorem 14.5.7m.11 gives a finite decoded lifetime for an unknown word on a local thermal source.

<a id="theorem-1457b-typed-mathbb-z_2-defect-ribbon-and-response-branch"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7b (Typed $\mathbb Z_2$ Defect, Ribbon, and Response Branch).** Let $\Lambda$ be a finite square cellulation of an oriented torus, put one qubit on each edge, and define
$$
A_v:=\prod_{e\ni v}X_e,
\qquad
B_p:=\prod_{e\subset\partial p}Z_e,
\qquad
H_\Lambda:=\frac{\Delta}{2}\sum_v(I-A_v)+\frac{\Delta}{2}\sum_p(I-B_p),
\tag{14.5.7b.1}
$$
with $\Delta>0$ in energy units. Fix eigenvalues $\omega_x,\omega_y\in\{\pm1\}$ of two commuting noncontractible logical $Z$ loops $\overline Z_x,\overline Z_y$, and use the carrier
$$
\mathcal H_\omega:=Q_\omega(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes E},
\qquad
Q_\omega:=\frac{I+\omega_x\overline Z_x}{2}
\frac{I+\omega_y\overline Z_y}{2}.
\tag{14.5.7b.2}
$$
Choose disjoint contractible probe and reference disks $D,D_{\mathrm{ref}}$, each containing a vertex and a plaquette. Require vacuum syndrome outside their interiors and put in $D_{\mathrm{ref}}$ the compensating parity charge forced by $\prod_vA_v=\prod_pB_p=I$. Two probe configurations are locally equivalent when an interior-$D$ Pauli followed by stabilizers relates them without changing either annular boundary-loop response. The isolated probe sectors are exactly
$$
\mathcal S=\{1,e,m,\epsilon\}\cong\mathbb Z_2\times\mathbb Z_2.
\tag{14.5.7b.3}
$$
Writing $e=(1,0)$, $m=(0,1)$, and $\epsilon=(1,1)$, fusion and full monodromy are
$$
(a,b)\otimes(a',b')=(a+a',b+b')\pmod2,
\qquad
M((a,b),(a',b'))=(-1)^{ab'+ba'},
\tag{14.5.7b.4}
$$
and a framed exchange-ribbon calculation gives the quadratic refinement
$$
q(a,b)=\theta_{(a,b)}=(-1)^{ab},
\qquad
q(x+y)=q(x)q(y)M(x,y).
\tag{14.5.7b.5}
$$
Thus $e$ and $m$ are bosonic, $\epsilon$ is fermionic, and the mutual $e$--$m$ monodromy is $-1$.

The configuration response is the quantum instrument on $\mathcal H_\omega$
$$
\Pi^\omega_{\mathbf s,\mathbf t}
:=Q_\omega
\prod_v\frac{I+s_vA_v}{2}
\prod_p\frac{I+t_pB_p}{2},
\qquad
\mathcal I_{\mathbf s,\mathbf t}(\rho)
:=\Pi^\omega_{\mathbf s,\mathbf t}\rho\Pi^\omega_{\mathbf s,\mathbf t},
\tag{14.5.7b.6}
$$
for parity-admissible sign strings $(\mathbf s,\mathbf t)$. Its maps are completely positive and trace nonincreasing, and their sum is trace preserving on $\mathcal H_\omega$. Register only physical ribbon operations that preserve $\mathcal H_\omega$; for a Hermitian ribbon operator $R$, impose $[R,Q_\omega]=0$ and restrict $Q_\omega RQ_\omega$ to this sector. Choose a finite family $\mathcal O_\omega$ of Hermitian operators on $\mathcal H_\omega$ containing a Hermitian basis, the two annular charge-detection loops, and the selected Hermitian sector-preserving ribbon observables. The family may be linearly dependent. Rescale its elements so $\|O\|\le1$, and register the binary POVM
$$
E^O_\pm=\frac{Q_\omega\pm O}{2},
\qquad
p(\pm\mid O,\rho)=\operatorname{Tr}(\rho E^O_\pm).
\tag{14.5.7b.7}
$$
A ribbon that changes a frozen logical eigenvalue is not an operation within this carrier. The typed finite protocol response is
$$
\mathfrak R_{\Lambda,\omega}(\rho)
:=
\left(
\{\mathcal I_{\mathbf s,\mathbf t}(\rho)\}_{\mathbf s,\mathbf t},
\{p(\pm\mid O,\rho)\}_{O\in\mathcal O_\omega}
\right).
\tag{14.5.7b.8}
$$
It records the configuration syndrome and is tomographically complete on the frozen operator algebra.

*Proof.* Every $A_v$ and $B_p$ is a Hermitian involution. A star and plaquette overlap on zero or two edges, so the Hamiltonian terms commute. A primal $Z$ ribbon anticommutes with precisely its endpoint stars, and a dual $X$ ribbon anticommutes with precisely its endpoint plaquettes. The reference disk supplies the second endpoint required by each global parity identity. Ribbon multiplication moves and fuses endpoints, so the two probe parities add modulo two and give the four labels in (14.5.7b.3).

Configurations with the same two parities differ inside the contractible probe disk by star/plaquette boundaries and contractible paired endpoints, so stabilizers and interior Pauli moves relate them. The two annular loops commute with every interior move and have eigenvalues $(-1)^a$ and $(-1)^b$, so different labels cannot be locally equivalent. This proves exhaustion and separation of the isolated sectors.

For oriented primal and dual ribbons $\gamma,\gamma^*$, Pauli reordering gives
$$
X(\gamma^*)Z(\gamma)
=(-1)^{I_2(\gamma^*,\gamma)}
Z(\gamma)X(\gamma^*),
\tag{14.5.7b.9}
$$
where $I_2$ is the mod-two crossing number. A full braid of $(a,b)$ around $(a',b')$ has crossing parity $ab'+ba'$, proving (14.5.7b.4). To compute exchange independently, compare the framed half-exchange ribbon word with the unexchanged reference word having the same endpoints. Two $e$ ribbons contain only $Z$ factors and give sign $+1$; two $m$ ribbons contain only $X$ factors and also give $+1$. For two composite $\epsilon$ ribbons, the $X$ part of one crosses the $Z$ part of the other once relative to the reference word, and (14.5.7b.9) gives $-1$. Thus $q(e)=q(m)=1$ and $q(\epsilon)=-1$; expanding $(-1)^{(a+a')(b+b')}$ proves (14.5.7b.5).

Finally, the commuting projectors in (14.5.7b.6) resolve the identity on $\mathcal H_\omega$, proving the instrument claim. Since $O=O^*$ and $\|O\|\le1$, both effects in (14.5.7b.7) are positive and sum to $Q_\omega$. A Hermitian operator basis separates density operators, so the POVM table is tomographically complete. ∎

This supplies one fully typed finite $\mathbb Z_2$ stabilizer branch. Theorem 14.5.7b-a and Theorem 14.5.7n below supply typed Golay/Leech-to-toric intertwiners; identifying its sectors with Standard-Model sectors requires a further typed intertwiner.

<a id="proposition-1457b-l-leech-inversion-source-and-the-full-response-boundary"></a>**Proposition 14.5.7b-L (Leech Inversion Source and the Full-Response Boundary).** Nominate the chiral source
$$
\mathcal B=\mathcal A_{V_\Lambda^+}=\mathcal A_{V_\Lambda}^{\langle\theta\rangle},
$$
where $\Lambda$ is the marked Leech lattice and $\theta$ is inversion. This nomination is a physical-carrier choice made in addition to the code/lattice identification.

1. This is a strongly local conformal source with central charge $24$. Its irreducible DHR sectors are exactly $1,e,m,\epsilon$, with statistical dimensions one, fusion $\mathbb Z_2^2$, lowest conformal weights $0,1,2,3/2$ and spins $1,1,1,-1$. Here $e=V_\Lambda^-$, $m=V_\Lambda^{T,+}$ and $\epsilon=V_\Lambda^{T,-}$; the twisted ground space has the minus sign in this convention. Its unitary braided category is the toric metric category, with $q(a,b)=(-1)^{ab}$. This exhausts the DHR sectors of this source.

2. For a unitary braided equivalence $F$ of these categories and any finite direct sum $X$, the restriction $\Phi_X:\operatorname{End}(X)\to\operatorname{End}(FX)$ is a unital $*$-isomorphism. Here finite-Kraus instruments may have several Kraus operators $K_{y,\alpha}$ per outcome $y$, with $\sum_{y,\alpha}K_{y,\alpha}^*K_{y,\alpha}=1$; equivalently refine the record to $(y,\alpha)$ and then sum over $\alpha$. Hence it transports every such normalized instrument in that algebra, all finite response histories, and $e^{\mathrm{itK}}$ for self-adjoint $K$ and finite $t$. These are the bounded protocols of the intertwiner algebras. Finite-angle transport is an algebraic identity; finite-energy, control-cost and cutoff statements for it require the energy-regularity or moment-domain hypotheses of Proposition P.13.30b.

3. The actual conformal inclusions $\mathcal B\subset\mathcal A_{V_\Lambda}$ and $\mathcal B\subset\mathcal A_{V^\natural}$ preserve the stress tensor, native rotation, and all bounded local instruments of the common subnet. They retain the physical energy $\hbar\omega_0(L_0-1)$ at the common frequency $\omega_0>0$. This frequency is a declared shared physical-unit calibration. The corresponding field parent $V_\Lambda\oplus V_\Lambda^T$ retains every descendant; it is an abelian intertwining algebra, whose charged fields are not local observables.

4. There is no unital $*$-homomorphism from any nonzero local algebra $\mathcal B(I)$ into the entire finite toric matrix algebra of Theorem 14.5.7b. There is also no isometric all-energy intertwiner from the source into that finite carrier with nonzero affine energy conversion. Finite ancillas do not remove either obstruction.

*Proof.* Strong locality and fixed-point identification follow from Carpi et al. (2023, Theorem 7.19) and Carpi et al. (2015, Theorem 7.1 and Proposition 7.6). Carpi et al. (2015, Theorem 8.15) supplies strong locality of Moonshine and its identification with the Moonshine net. The identification with the lattice net and its twisted representations is supplied by the paragraph preceding Theorem 7.19 of Carpi et al. (2023) and by Dong and Xu (2004, Proposition 4.25 and Theorem 4.30). The concrete sector result is Kawahigashi–Longo, Lemmas 3.1 and 3.5; the lowest-weight convention is Lam–Yamauchi, concluding proof of Theorem 1.1. The squared dimensions sum to the fixed-point $\mu$-index four, proving completeness. The ribbon identity gives mutual monodromy $(-1)^{ab'+ba'}$; pointed braided-category classification identifies this quadratic form with the toric category.

For item 2, first refine $y$ to include the Kraus index and write $\phi'=\phi\circ\Phi_X^{-1}$, $K'_y=\Phi_X(K_y)$. Then
$$
\phi'(K_y'^*\Phi_X(E)K'_y)=\phi(K_y^*EK_y),\qquad
\sum_yK_y'^*K'_y=1.
$$
Summing over the refined Kraus index proves the original outcome instrument; iteration proves histories and normalized posteriors whenever the conditioning probability is nonzero; functional calculus proves finite-angle transport. Localized intertwiners belong to the containing local algebra by Haag duality.

For item 3, a conformal inclusion $j$ preserves every product and adjoint, while $\omega\circ j$ is the restricted normal state. Substituting $j(K_y)$ into any instrument history proves equality of its full response on the common subnet. The common conformal vector intertwines rotation. Huang's Theorems 3.7–3.8 supply the field parent; its charged formal fields are not thereby bounded Kraus operators.

For item 4, the type-III factor $\mathcal B(I)$ contains orthogonal isometries $v_1,v_2$. A unital $*$-homomorphism into square matrices would send each to a unitary, contradicting their orthogonal ranges. Independently, the vacuum descendants obey
$$
\|L_{-n}\Omega\|^2=\frac{24}{12}(n^3-n)>0,\qquad L_0L_{-n}\Omega=nL_{-n}\Omega\quad(n\ge2).
$$
They have unbounded energies, whereas the finite toric Hamiltonian has a finite bounded spectrum. ∎

The central charge is $24$; its residue modulo eight agrees with the toric Gauss sum, and the thermal and stress responses of the source are additional data. A sector-record coarse-graining forgets, for example, the energy difference between $\Omega$ and a normalized $L_{-n}\Omega$, although both have vacuum charge, so a response-faithful map must retain the descendants. The disk/interval geometry map, reference charge preparation, torus-loop labels, coupling Hamiltonian and predictive-interface realization of this chiral source are not fixed by the categorical equivalence or by the common chiral subnet. Theorem 14.5.7n supplies these records on a distinct finite coupled Golay–Leech rotor source; by item 4 the chiral net is not equivalent to that finite source.

References for the proof: Carpi et al. (2023); Carpi et al. (2015); Kawahigashi and Longo (2004); Lam and Yamauchi (2006); Huang (1994); Etingof et al. (2015, Theorem 8.4.9).

Lattice-net identification and exhaustive twisted representations: Dong and Xu (2004).

<a id="theorem-1457b-a-exact-parity-restricted-golay-csstoric-intertwiner"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7b-a (Exact Parity-Restricted Golay-CSS/Toric Intertwiner).** Select the following additional finite-model data: a qubit quantization of the marked extended binary Golay code $C\subset\mathbb F_2^{24}$, an ordered basis $c_1,\ldots,c_{12}$ of $C$, and the Hamiltonian defined below. These are choices of a concrete source model.

On $\mathcal V_G=(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes24}$ put
$$
S_i=X(c_i),\qquad S_{12+i}=Z(c_i)\quad(1\le i\le12),
\qquad H_G=\frac{\Delta_G}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{24}(I-S_j),\quad\Delta_G>0.
\tag{14.5.7b-a.1}
$$
The ordered generating basis is part of the Hamiltonian data: a change of stabilizer generators need not preserve this particular sum. Define the source parity constraints and carrier by
$$
P_X=\prod_{i=1}^{12}S_i,\quad P_Z=\prod_{i=1}^{12}S_{12+i},
\quad P=\frac{(I+P_X)(I+P_Z)}4,
\quad\mathcal K_G=P\mathcal V_G.
\tag{14.5.7b-a.2}
$$
For the target, take the square $3\times4$ periodic torus, with $24$ edges, $12$ star checks $A_i$, and $12$ face checks $B_i$. Let $L_x,L_y$ be independent commuting noncontractible logical $Z$ loops, let $\omega_x,\omega_y\in\{\pm1\}$, and put
$$
Q_\omega=\frac{(I+\omega_xL_x)(I+\omega_yL_y)}4,
\quad\mathcal K_T=Q_\omega(\mathbb C^2)^{\otimes24},
\quad H_T=\frac{\Delta_T}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{12}(2I-A_i-B_i),
\quad\Delta_T>0.
\tag{14.5.7b-a.3}
$$
Then the following statements hold.

1. There is a signed Clifford unitary $U$ on the full $24$-qubit spaces such that $UPU^*=Q_\omega$. Its restriction $V:\mathcal K_G\to\mathcal K_T$ is a unitary equivalence, with full image and zero kernel, between spaces of dimension $2^{22}$.
2. On these carriers, with $\alpha=\Delta_G/\Delta_T$,
$$
V(H_G|_{\mathcal K_G})V^*=\alpha H_T|_{\mathcal K_T}.
\tag{14.5.7b-a.4}
$$
All retained excitation energies and multiplicities are preserved in the indicated units. With a common $\hbar$, the time map is $t_T=\alpha t_G$ and the inverse-temperature map is $\beta_T=\alpha\beta_G$.
3. The full source check-syndrome instrument on $\mathcal K_G$ is carried to (14.5.7b.6), including unnormalized outcome states, probabilities, and normalized conditional states whenever the outcome probability is nonzero. Conjugation by $V$ also intertwines every retained operator, effect, preparation, and channel when the source protocol family is declared to be the conjugate of the target family. It preserves a complete separating observable basis.
4. On the unrestricted spaces, the exact identity is instead
$$
UH_GU^*=\alpha H_T+
\frac{\Delta_G}{2}A_{12}(I-\omega_xL_x)+
\frac{\Delta_G}{2}B_{12}(I-\omega_yL_y).
\tag{14.5.7b-a.5}
$$
Thus the source parity sectors omitted in (14.5.7b-a.2) carry explicit energy-bearing loop-controlled correction terms, which vanish only on $\mathcal K_T$.
5. Transporting the complete region algebras, probe/reference restrictions, local moves, annular observables, and admissible framed ribbons of Theorem 14.5.7b through $V$ gives an exact source realization of those same defect relations. The source region structure here is the transported one; $U$ is a global Clifford unitary and does not preserve locality.

*Proof.* Self-duality of $C$ implies $c_i\cdot c_j=0\pmod2$, so all $S_j$ commute. Their binary Pauli vectors have rank $24$: the $12$ independent $X$ vectors and $12$ independent $Z$ vectors occupy separate halves of the binary Pauli space. They generate no $-I$. The unique common $+1$ vector is
$$
|C\rangle=2^{-6}\sum_{c\in C}|c\rangle.
\tag{14.5.7b-a.6}
$$
The two parity checks in (14.5.7b-a.2) are independent, giving $\dim\mathcal K_G=2^{22}$.

Order the target checks so that deleting $A_{12}$ and $B_{12}$ leaves an independent family. The only relations among all $24$ target checks are $\prod_iA_i=\prod_iB_i=I$. Define
$$
\begin{aligned}
T_i&=A_i &&(1\le i\le11),& T_{12}&=\omega_xL_xA_{12},\\
T_{12+i}&=B_i &&(1\le i\le11),& T_{24}&=\omega_yL_yB_{12}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{14.5.7b-a.7}
$$
These are commuting Hermitian involutions. The first $22$ independent checks, together with the two independent logical loops, show that this whole family has binary rank $24$ and no nontrivial scalar product. Moreover,
$$
\prod_{i=1}^{12}T_i=\omega_xL_x,
\qquad \prod_{i=13}^{24}T_i=\omega_yL_y.
\tag{14.5.7b-a.8}
$$

Here is a constructive signed Clifford lift. In $\mathbb F_2^{48}$ write the Pauli form as
$$
b((x,z),(x',z'))=x\cdot z'+z\cdot x'.
$$
Complete the ordered maximal isotropic family of source vectors $s_i$ to a symplectic basis $(s_i,d_i)$ with $b(s_i,d_j)=\delta_{ij}$ and $b(d_i,d_j)=0$. To do this, first solve the linear equations $b(s_i,d_j)=\delta_{ij}$ and, successively for $i<j$, replace $d_j$ by $d_j+b(d_i,d_j)s_i$. This removes its pairing with earlier duals without changing any prescribed pairing with the $s_i$. The same algorithm constructs $(t_i,e_i)$ for the target. The linear map
$$
F(a)=\sum_{i=1}^{24}\bigl[b(a,d_i)t_i+b(s_i,a)e_i\bigr]
\tag{14.5.7b-a.9}
$$
is symplectic and maps every $s_i$ to $t_i$.

The signs are fixed as follows. Choose commuting Hermitian Pauli representatives $D_i,E_i$ of the two dual families, and let $|T+\rangle$ be the common $+1$ eigenstate of the **signed** operators in (14.5.7b-a.7). The families
$$
D_1^{a_1}\cdots D_{24}^{a_{24}}|C\rangle,
\qquad
E_1^{a_1}\cdots E_{24}^{a_{24}}|T+\rangle,
\qquad a\in\mathbb F_2^{24},
$$
are orthonormal bases with the same indexed stabilizer signs. Map the first basis to the second. The resulting unitary $U$ satisfies $US_iU^*=T_i$ and $UD_iU^*=E_i$. It is a Clifford unitary because these operators generate the full Pauli group. Equations (14.5.7b-a.8) now give $UPU^*=Q_\omega$, proving statement 1.

On $\mathcal K_T$, $\omega_xL_x=\omega_yL_y=I$, so the last modified check in each group reduces to the ordinary omitted star or face. Substitution in the Hamiltonian proves (14.5.7b-a.4). Before imposing those loop values, the same substitution gives precisely (14.5.7b-a.5), proving statements 2 and 4. In particular,
$$
Ve^{-it_GH_G/\hbar}|_{\mathcal K_G}V^*
=e^{-i\alpha t_GH_T/\hbar}|_{\mathcal K_T}.
$$
The spectral generating polynomial, in units $\Delta_G$, is
$$
\sum_E\operatorname{mult}(E)x^{E/\Delta_G}
=\left[\frac{(1+x)^{12}+(1-x)^{12}}2\right]^2.
\tag{14.5.7b-a.10}
$$
Each parity-admissible syndrome has one state, so the coefficient sum is $2^{22}$, the ground multiplicity is $1$, and the first excitation has energy $2\Delta_G$ and multiplicity $2\binom{12}{2}=132$.

For $\sigma\in\{\pm1\}^{24}$ with $\prod_{i=1}^{12}\sigma_i=\prod_{i=13}^{24}\sigma_i=1$, define
$$
\Pi^G_\sigma=P\prod_{j=1}^{24}\frac{I+\sigma_jS_j}{2}.
\tag{14.5.7b-a.11}
$$
These rank-one orthogonal projections resolve $P$, and
$$
V\Pi^G_\sigma V^*
=Q_\omega\prod_{i=1}^{12}\frac{I+\sigma_iA_i}{2}
\prod_{i=1}^{12}\frac{I+\sigma_{12+i}B_i}{2}.
\tag{14.5.7b-a.12}
$$
Thus for $\rho$ supported on $\mathcal K_G$, conjugation carries $\Pi^G_\sigma\rho\Pi^G_\sigma$ to its target counterpart. Traces and conditional normalizations agree. The sum of these maps is trace preserving on the declared source carrier. For an arbitrary target observable $O_T$, put $O_G=V^*O_TV$; the effects $(P\pm O_G)/2$ transport to $(Q_\omega\pm O_T)/2$ whenever $O_T$ is Hermitian of norm at most one. For a target channel $\mathcal E_T$ define
$$
\mathcal E_G(\rho)=V^*\mathcal E_T(V\rho V^*)V.
$$
This is a completely positive trace-preserving channel on the source carrier, and intertwines by construction. These identities include coherent off-diagonal syndrome matrix entries and prove statement 3.

Finally conjugation preserves products, adjoints, spectral projectors and all operator relations. Pull back the complete target local-move and framed-ribbon protocols. Then local equivalence is preserved and reflected, annular separating observables retain their responses, the probe/reference compensation conditions retain their parity constraints, and the frozen-loop restrictions remain in force. Every braid or exchange word has the identical image and hence the same scalar phase. A ribbon changing the frozen loop sector is not introduced as an operation on either restricted carrier. This proves statement 5 with its specified transported region structure. ∎

**Finite data for Theorem 14.5.7b-a.** For the cyclic presentation of the extended Golay code, which is $C$ up to a permutation of coordinates (codewords $x^jg(x)$, $0\le j\le11$, of $g(x)=1+x+x^5+x^6+x^7+x^9+x^{11}$, each extended by an overall parity bit), and with the $3\times4$ torus of (14.5.7b-a.3): the weight enumerator of $C$ is $1+759y^8+2576y^{12}+759y^{16}+y^{24}$; the families $\{S_j\}$ and $\{T_j\}$ commute and have binary rank $24$; the $24$ toric checks have rank $22$, and rank $24$ together with $L_x,L_y$; the map (14.5.7b-a.9) is symplectic and invertible on $\mathbb F_2^{48}$, sends $s_i$ to $t_i$, and sends the binary vectors of $P_X$ and $P_Z$ to those of $L_x$ and $L_y$; and the coefficients of (14.5.7b-a.10) sum to $2^{22}$, with ground multiplicity $1$ and $132$ states at energy $2\Delta_G$. These finite statements are exact binary linear algebra and are invariant under coordinate permutations of the source code; the loop signs are fixed by the signed lift in the proof.

**Scope of Theorem 14.5.7b-a.** The theorem gives an exact finite state/operator/instrument/energy bridge for the selected CSS model on the parity-restricted carrier $\mathcal K_G$. Restricting a full-source state to $\mathcal K_G$, $\rho\mapsto P\rho P/\operatorname{Tr}(P\rho)$, is postselection, with success probability $1/4$ on the maximally mixed full-source state; equation (14.5.7b-a.5) retains every full-source parity sector with its energy correction. The construction applies to every ordered maximal stabilizer source with this grouping.

For a target region $D$, the transported algebra is $\mathcal A_G(D)=V^*\mathcal A_T(D)V$. A globally entangling Clifford can turn a short ribbon into a high-weight Pauli, so the transported regions carry no source metric or causal range; Theorem 14.5.7n supplies a locality-preserving on-site intertwiner on a different source.

The coordinate construction following Lemma R.4.5 gives $C\mapsto\Lambda_C$ and its lattice-norm certificate; the CSS model of Theorem 14.5.7b-a uses the code and not the lattice. Theorem 14.5.7n joins code, lattice and toric carrier on one locally coupled Golay–Leech source with an on-site regional map and a complete defect census.

**Clarification on the Discrete-Continuous Correspondence.**
At MPU resolution, a finite candidate constraint ledger can be represented by stabilizer-like generators and automorphisms (Appendix G, Remark G.8.4g.1a), implementing an abstract operational equivalence relation. Calling these generators physical vacuum constraints requires the separate vacuum configuration, dynamics, ground-state, encoder, and response-realization record. This is consistent with Definition X.9.1 (MPU-equivalence).

On a branch supplying an accepted response-preserving coarse-graining/gauge-subspace map, the image of the finite operational equivalence can be represented as local gauge redundancy on coarse-grained fields. Appendix X assumes this map, and Theorem X.3 fixes its unit-branch normalization after acceptance. The conditional correspondence operates as follows:

1. **Operational level:** Two field configurations $\varphi_1, \varphi_2$ are operationally equivalent if $p(\cdot \mid O, c; \varphi_1) = p(\cdot \mid O, c; \varphi_2)$ for all MPU-admissible $(O, c)$.

2. **Effective action level:** On the accepted gauge-subspace bridge, gauge variations $\varphi\mapsto\varphi+\delta_\xi\varphi$ act along directions that the bridge maps to vanishing operational distinguishability. The Predictive Ward Identity of Theorem X.3 then fixes the unit normalization of the already-supplied gauge kernel.

3. **Structural correspondence:** In the capacity-saturating regime of Theorem G.8.4b, the *dimension* of the gauge algebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{SM}}$ (12 generators) matches the Lagrangian capacity bound from the symplectic structure on the interface (Theorem G.8.2e). This is a structural/dimensional correspondence, not a literal isomorphism between a finite stabilizer group and a continuous gauge group.

This status is made precise in Remark G.8.4g.2: the classical Golay code provides a *structural analogy* for the gauge organization, with the predictive-recovery MacWilliams partition $k=n-k=12$ matching the same 12+12 organizational split that reappears in the capacity-saturating regime, while the 12-generator gauge budget itself is determined by Theorem G.8.2e.

<a id="theorem-1457c-finite-to-continuum-gauge-orbit-no-go-and-exact-defect-ledger"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7c (Finite-to-Continuum Gauge-Orbit No-Go and Exact Defect Ledger).** Let $\mathcal G_f$ be a finite groupoid and $F:\mathcal G_f\to\mathcal H$ a functor. Write $\pi_0(\mathcal C)$ for the set of isomorphism classes of objects of a groupoid $\mathcal C$. Then:

1. $|\operatorname{im}\pi_0(F)|\le |\pi_0(\mathcal G_f)|<\infty$;
2. $F$ is faithful exactly when every map
   $$
   F_{x,y}:\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal G_f}(x,y)
   \longrightarrow
   \operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal H}(Fx,Fy)
   $$
   is injective; its morphism kernel is the family of equalizer relations $g\equiv_Fh\iff Fg=Fh$;
3. its orbit cokernel is
   $$
   \operatorname{coker}_{\pi_0}F
   :=\pi_0(\mathcal H)\setminus\operatorname{im}\pi_0(F).
   \tag{14.5.7c.1}
   $$

Consequently, if $\mathcal H$ has infinitely many gauge orbits, no functor from a finite groupoid is essentially surjective, whether or not it is faithful. In particular, for the groupoid of $U(1)$ connections on $S^1$ modulo gauge transformations, holonomy gives
$$
\pi_0(\mathcal H)\cong U(1),
$$
so the orbit cokernel of every $F:\mathcal G_f\to\mathcal H$ is uncountable.

*Proof.* A functor sends isomorphic objects to isomorphic objects and hence induces a function $\pi_0(F)$. A function from a finite set has finite image, proving item 1 and the essential-surjectivity obstruction. Item 2 is the definition of faithfulness, and equality after applying $F$ is a congruence on every hom-set because functors preserve composition. Item 3 is the exact set of omitted target orbits.

For the last statement, write a smooth connection on the trivial $U(1)$ bundle as $A=a(\theta)d\theta$. A gauge transformation changes $A$ by a closed real one-form whose period lies in $2\pi\mathbb Z$; this form is locally exact but need not be globally exact. Thus
$$
\operatorname{Hol}(A)=\exp\!\left(i\int_{S^1}A\right)
$$
is gauge invariant. Put $\alpha=\int_0^{2\pi}a(\theta)\,d\theta$. The function $f(\theta)=\int_0^\theta(a(u)-\alpha/(2\pi))\,du$ is periodic, so $A-\alpha d\theta/(2\pi)=df$ is removable by a single-valued gauge transformation. Every connection is therefore gauge equivalent to $A_\alpha=\alpha d\theta/(2\pi)$. The transformations $e^{in\theta}$ shift $\alpha$ by $2\pi n$, up to the chosen sign convention, so two constant connections are gauge equivalent exactly when their holonomies agree. The orbit set is consequently $U(1)$, and removing the finite image of $\pi_0(F)$ leaves an uncountable cokernel. ∎

Therefore a literal finite equivalence groupoid cannot be both a faithful finite description and orbit-surjective onto this continuum gauge comparison class. A positive bridge must instead restrict the continuum target to finitely many retained orbits or pass through an infinite limit groupoid and separately prove its response, topology, kernel, and surjectivity properties.

<a id="theorem-1457d-refinement-profinite-and-retained-quotient-classification-for-the-u1-holonomy-bridge"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7d (Refinement, Profinite and Retained-Quotient Classification for the $U(1)$ Holonomy Bridge).** Let $\mathcal H$ be the groupoid of smooth $U(1)$ connections on the trivial bundle over $S^1$ modulo gauge transformations, with $\pi_0(\mathcal H)\cong U(1)$ by holonomy as in Theorem 14.5.7c. For $n\ge1$, let $\mathcal G_n$ be the discrete groupoid on $\mathbb Z/n$ and let $F_n:\mathcal G_n\to\mathcal H$ send $k\in\{0,\ldots,n-1\}$ to the constant connection $A_{2\pi k/n}=k\,d\theta/n$ in the notation of the proof of Theorem 14.5.7c, whose holonomy is $e^{2\pi ik/n}$. Then:

1. **Countable refinements.** For every groupoid $\mathcal G$ with countably many isomorphism classes, including the union of an increasing chain of finite groupoids, every functor $F:\mathcal G\to\mathcal H$ has countable image in $\pi_0(\mathcal H)$, so $F$ is not essentially surjective and its orbit cokernel (14.5.7c.1) is uncountable. For the chain $F_{2^j}$, $j\ge0$, the union of the images is the dense countable group of dyadic roots of unity.

2. **Profinite sources.** Every continuous homomorphism from a profinite group into $U(1)$ has finite image. Hence no profinite group maps continuously and homomorphically onto the holonomy group $U(1)$, whose law is $\operatorname{Hol}(A+A')=\operatorname{Hol}(A)\operatorname{Hol}(A')$. The Cantor space $\{0,1\}^{\mathbb N}$ maps continuously onto $U(1)$ by
$$
b\longmapsto\exp\!\Big(2\pi i\sum_{k\ge1}b_k2^{-k}\Big),
\tag{14.5.7d.1}
$$
and by the first statement no profinite group structure on $\{0,1\}^{\mathbb N}$ makes this surjection a homomorphism.

3. **Finite retained quotients.** Let $q:U(1)\to R$ be a retained response quantizer onto a finite label set $R$, and suppose every fiber $q^{-1}(r)$ contains an open arc of length at least $\ell>0$. Then for every integer $n>2\pi/\ell$, the composite of $\pi_0(F_n)$ with $q$ is surjective. Thus $F_n$ followed by the quotient of $\mathcal H$ onto the discrete retained groupoid $R$ is essentially surjective, two labels $k,k'$ become isomorphic exactly when $q(e^{2\pi ik/n})=q(e^{2\pi ik'/n})$, and every retained response $q(\operatorname{Hol}(A))$ is attained on the image of $F_n$.

*Proof.* For item 1, a functor induces a function $\pi_0(F):\pi_0(\mathcal G)\to\pi_0(\mathcal H)$, and a function from a countable set has countable image, while $U(1)$ is uncountable. A union of an increasing chain of finite groupoids has countably many objects. The images of $F_{2^j}$ are the $2^j$-th roots of unity, whose union contains $e^{2\pi ia}$ for every dyadic rational $a$ and is therefore dense.

For item 2, let $\phi:\Gamma\to U(1)$ be a continuous homomorphism from a profinite group. The neighborhood $W=\{e^{i\vartheta}:|\vartheta|<2\pi/3\}$ contains no nontrivial subgroup: if $z=e^{i\vartheta}$ with $0<|\vartheta|<2\pi/3$, the least $k\ge1$ with $|k\vartheta|\ge2\pi/3$ satisfies $2\pi/3\le|k\vartheta|<4\pi/3$, so $z^k\notin W$. Open normal subgroups form a neighborhood basis of the identity in a profinite group, so some open normal $N$ lies in $\phi^{-1}(W)$. Then $\phi(N)$ is a subgroup contained in $W$, hence trivial, and $\phi$ factors through the finite group $\Gamma/N$, which is finite because an open subgroup of a compact group has finite index. The holonomy of a connection is $\exp(i\int_{S^1}A)$, which is multiplicative under addition of connections. The map (14.5.7d.1) is the composite of the continuous binary-expansion surjection $\{0,1\}^{\mathbb N}\to[0,1]$ with $t\mapsto e^{2\pi it}$, which maps $[0,1]$ onto $U(1)$.

For item 3, consecutive $n$-th roots of unity are separated by arc length $2\pi/n<\ell$, so every open arc of length at least $\ell$ contains one of them. Each fiber of $q$ therefore meets $\{e^{2\pi ik/n}\}$, which is surjectivity. The objects $k,k'$ have isomorphic images in the discrete quotient exactly when their labels agree. ∎

Theorem 14.5.7d settles the three bridge types for this comparison class. Countable refinements can approximate every orbit arbitrarily closely, as the dyadic chain does, and still miss uncountably many orbits. Profinite sources reach the continuum orbit set only through maps that do not intertwine the holonomy group law. Finite retained quotients whose fibers contain open arcs are reached at the explicit finite stage $n>2\pi/\ell$.

**Compact-Group Transport Convention.**

Let $M$ be a connected, finite-dimensional, Hausdorff second-countable smooth manifold, possibly with boundary; smoothness at a boundary means smooth extendibility in boundary charts. Let $G$ be a compact Lie group. Fix a faithful finite-dimensional unitary representation $\rho:G\hookrightarrow U(N)$. Such a representation exists for a compact Lie group. No particular central quotient or physical charge normalization is selected by this choice.

The primary category $\mathsf{Conn}_G(M)$ has objects $(P,A)$, where $P\to M$ is a smooth principal right-$G$ bundle and $A$ a smooth principal connection, and arrows all smooth connection-preserving principal bundle isomorphisms covering $\operatorname{id}_M$. Thus bundle topology is included, rather than silently assuming a trivial bundle. Restricting to one fixed $P$ gives the usual action groupoid of its smooth gauge group. All components of that gauge group are included unless explicitly restricted below. There are no gauge-field equations, flatness constraints, energy bounds, quotient by diffeomorphisms, or quantum measure in this category.

Paths are finite concatenations of smooth paths with sitting instants, taken modulo smooth thin homotopy relative endpoints; a thin homotopy has differential rank at most one. All path families used in smoothness statements are jointly smooth finite-dimensional families, with sitting instants locally uniform in the family parameter. The thin path groupoid is $\mathcal P_1(M)$. Connection transport factors through it. Concatenation $\gamma_2\circ\gamma_1$ means first $\gamma_1$, then $\gamma_2$. In a local frame we use
$$
\dot U(t)=-A_{\gamma(t)}(\dot\gamma(t))U(t),\qquad U(0)=I.
$$
All comparison observables are dimensionless. A physical length scale, time cost, energy, coupling, or detector precision requires its own registered bridge.

<a id="theorem-1457g-complete-compact-group-tensor-response-and-smooth-gauge-orbit-reconstruction"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7g (Complete Compact-Group Tensor Response and Smooth Gauge-Orbit Reconstruction).**

Fix $x_0\in M$. For every finite ordered tuple $\boldsymbol\ell=(\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_k)$ of loops based at $x_0$, select a frame $p_0\in P_{x_0}$ and write
$$
\operatorname{PT}_A(\ell_i)(p_0)=p_0h_i.
$$
For every monomial $m$ in matrix entries of $\rho(h_1),\ldots,\rho(h_k)$ and their complex conjugates, including the constant monomial, define its simultaneous-conjugation average
$$
f_m(h_1,\ldots,h_k)
=\int_G m(ch_1c^{-1},\ldots,ch_kc^{-1})\,d\mu_G(c),
\qquad |f_m|\le1,
\tag{14.5.7g.1}
$$
where Haar measure is normalized. The real and imaginary parts of these averages are finite invariant tensor contractions. For $s\in\{\operatorname{Re},\operatorname{Im}\}$, register the binary experiment
$$
p_A(+\mid\boldsymbol\ell,m,s)
=\frac12+\frac14 s f_m(h_1,\ldots,h_k),
\qquad p_A(-\mid\boldsymbol\ell,m,s)=1-p_A(+\mid\boldsymbol\ell,m,s).
\tag{14.5.7g.2}
$$
The outcomes are already aligned; their retained outcome quantizer is the identity on $\{+,-\}$. Each probability lies in $[1/4,3/4]$. The declared finite protocols consist of finitely many such experiments on a fixed connection, with experiment choice depending on earlier outcomes, and any additional finite classical randomization and postprocessing independent of the unknown connection. The conditional trial law is (14.5.7g.2). No connection-changing intervention is added implicitly. Here finite protocol means finitely many trials and finite outcome branching. The mathematical family places no uniform bound on path encoding, loop size, monomial degree, accuracy, or trial cost; those admission and resource bounds belong to the actual MPU protocol record.

For two objects $(P,A),(Q,B)$, the following are equivalent:

1. every basic probability (14.5.7g.2) agrees;
2. every declared finite adaptive protocol has the same entire outcome law;
3. there is a smooth connection-preserving $G$-bundle isomorphism $\Phi:P\to Q$ over $M$.

For fixed frames $p_0\in P_{x_0}$ and $q_0\in Q_{x_0}$, all such isomorphisms are explicitly parametrized by
$$
C(A,B)=\{c\in G:h_B(\ell)c=c\,h_A(\ell)\ \text{for every based loop }\ell\}.
\tag{14.5.7g.3}
$$
For $c\in C(A,B)$ the associated map is
$$
\Phi_c\bigl(\operatorname{PT}_A(\gamma)(p_0)g\bigr)
=\operatorname{PT}_B(\gamma)(q_0)c g,
\qquad \gamma:x_0\to x.
\tag{14.5.7g.4}
$$
In particular, the automorphism group of $(P,A)$ is isomorphic to the centralizer of its based holonomy group. Complete scalar response equality determines isomorphism classes; equation (14.5.7g.3) separately retains the automorphism data.

*Proof.*

First consider $G^k$. Let $\mathscr P_k$ be the complex algebra of finite linear combinations of the matrix-entry monomials in (14.5.7g.1). It contains constants, is closed under complex conjugation, and separates points: if two tuples differ, faithfulness of $\rho$ gives one matrix entry that differs. The complex Stone–Weierstrass theorem therefore gives uniform density of $\mathscr P_k$ in $C(G^k)$.

Let $\mathsf A$ denote Haar averaging under simultaneous conjugation. It is a norm-one projection onto $C(G^k)^G$. For an invariant continuous function $F$ and $p\in\mathscr P_k$,
$$
\|\mathsf A p-F\|_\infty
=\|\mathsf A(p-F)\|_\infty\le\|p-F\|_\infty.
$$
Thus the linear span of the $f_m$ is uniformly dense in $C(G^k)^G$. Averaging a monomial contracts its finite tensor indices with the Haar projection onto the invariant vectors of the associated finite-dimensional tensor representation. This proves the invariant-tensor assertion directly, with the full tensor contractions used in spin networks (Baez 1996, Lemma 3). Individual Wilson traces are not assumed to generate the invariant algebra for every compact group.

Continuous invariants separate simultaneous-conjugation orbits. For an explicit separator, use
$$
d(g,h)=\|\rho(g)-\rho(h)\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
$$
and the maximum product metric on $G^k$. This metric is left and right invariant and is invariant under conjugation. Each orbit is compact. If $\mathbf h,\mathbf k$ are in different orbits, the distance from $\mathbf k$ to the orbit of $\mathbf h$ is strictly positive. The function assigning distance to that orbit is continuous and invariant, vanishes on $\mathbf h$, and is positive on $\mathbf k$. Uniform density then implies that some $f_m$ differs on the two tuples. Consequently, equality of every (14.5.7g.2) for a fixed tuple of loops is equivalent to simultaneous conjugacy of its two holonomy tuples.

Assume item 1. For each finite set $L$ of based loops let
$$
C_L=\{c\in G:h_B(\ell)c=c h_A(\ell)\text{ for all }\ell\in L\}.
$$
The preceding paragraph makes $C_L$ nonempty. It is closed. Moreover $C_{L_1}\cap\cdots\cap C_{L_r}=C_{L_1\cup\cdots\cup L_r}$, so this family has the finite intersection property. Compactness of $G$ supplies one $c\in\bigcap_L C_L$. This step is what turns a separately chosen conjugator for every finite family into one conjugator for all loops, including an uncountable path family.

Define $\Phi_c$ by (14.5.7g.4). Every fiber point has the indicated form. To verify independence of the path, take $\gamma,\delta:x_0\to x$ and let $\ell=\delta^{-1}\circ\gamma$. Then
$$
\operatorname{PT}_A(\gamma)(p_0)
=\operatorname{PT}_A(\delta)(p_0)h_A(\ell).
$$
If $\operatorname{PT}_A(\gamma)(p_0)g=\operatorname{PT}_A(\delta)(p_0)k$, then $k=h_A(\ell)g$. The corresponding right sides of (14.5.7g.4) agree because
$$
\operatorname{PT}_B(\gamma)(q_0)c g
=\operatorname{PT}_B(\delta)(q_0)h_B(\ell)c g
=\operatorname{PT}_B(\delta)(q_0)c h_A(\ell)g.
$$
The map is fiberwise equivariant and invertible, with inverse obtained using $c^{-1}$ and interchanging $A,B$.

For smoothness, choose a coordinate ball $U$ centered at $z$, a fixed path from $x_0$ to $z$, and a smooth family of radial paths from $z$ to $x\in U$, using a fixed sitting-instant reparametrization. Their concatenation yields smooth local sections $p_A(x)$ and $q_B(x)$ by the smooth parameter dependence of the parallel-transport ODE. In these sections the map is $(x,g)\mapsto(x,cg)$, hence smooth. The same argument works in boundary half-balls. Path concatenation shows that $\Phi_c$ intertwines transport on every path. Differentiating along paths shows that it sends the $A$-horizontal distribution onto the $B$-horizontal distribution, so it preserves the connections. This proves 1 implies 3.

Conversely, if $\Phi$ is connection preserving and $\Phi(p_0)=q_0c$, equivariance and transport preservation give $h_B(\ell)c=c h_A(\ell)$ for every loop. Hence all invariant tensors and probabilities agree. The same transport identity forces $\Phi=\Phi_c$, proving the morphism parametrization and, for $A=B,p_0=q_0$, the centralizer assertion.

A finite adaptive protocol has joint probability given by the product of its chosen conditional probabilities, followed by a finite sum over any classical randomization or coarse graining. Equality of basic probabilities therefore gives equality of every finite protocol law. Conversely each basic experiment is an admitted one-step protocol. This proves 1 equivalent to 2 and completes the proof. ∎

**Boundary Frames and Retained Gauge Components.**

There are two exact framed variants.

* For a specified frame at $x_0$, admit only bundle isomorphisms preserving it. Replace the averaged monomials by all real and imaginary matrix entries of $\rho(h_A(\ell))$. They separate holonomies as group elements. The preceding proof applies with $c=e$ and yields the unique frame-preserving isomorphism.
* Let $\varnothing\ne B\subseteq M$ be a boundary subset with specified smooth bundle frames, and choose $x_0\in B$. Admit only isomorphisms preserving those frames on all of $B$. In addition to the unaveraged based-loop coefficients, retain the matrix coefficients of transport along every path $\gamma:x_0\to b$, $b\in B$, expressed in the two given endpoint frames. Equation (14.5.7g.4) first gives the unique base-frame-preserving isomorphism. Equality of these open-path matrices is then exactly the assertion that this isomorphism preserves the frame at every $b$. The converse follows by transport preservation.

A global frame on $B$ is part of the hypothesis; when no such frame exists, the corresponding statement uses specified local boundary torsor identifications and their overlap data. Omitting open boundary transport changes the allowed gauge quotient. These frame-sensitive experiments are a different retained algebra from the unframed invariant one.

<a id="theorem-1457h-constructive-smooth-continuum-transport-realization"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7h (Constructive Smooth Continuum Transport Realization).**

Let $\mathsf{Trans}^\infty_G(M)$ be the category of $G$-torsor-valued transport functors on $\mathcal P_1(M)$ with smooth local trivialization and smooth descent, and smooth locally represented natural isomorphisms. Concretely, choose a good open cover $\{U_i\}$ and local torsor frames. In these frames:

1. a path in $U_i$ has a transport matrix $U_i(\gamma)\in G$, satisfying identity and composition, invariant under thin homotopy;
2. for every jointly smooth finite-dimensional family of paths $c_u$ in $U_i$, the map $u\mapsto U_i(c_u)$ is smooth;
3. local fiber coordinates are related by smooth transition functions $g_{ji}:U_i\cap U_j\to G$, with $v_j=g_{ji}v_i$, $g_{ki}=g_{kj}g_{ji}$, and
$$
U_j(\gamma)=g_{ji}(y)U_i(\gamma)g_{ji}(x)^{-1},\qquad \gamma:x\to y.
\tag{14.5.7h.1}
$$
For boundary charts, require these local functors and their smooth descent to extend across the boundary to open ambient charts; smooth connection data satisfy this local extendibility. The local data are the local representations of the given functor, so transport across charts is obtained by its functorial composition. Refinement and changes of local trivialization describe the same object by their smooth natural identifications.

Parallel transport defines an equivalence
$$
\mathsf{Hol}:\mathsf{Conn}_G(M)\simeq\mathsf{Trans}^\infty_G(M).
\tag{14.5.7h.2}
$$
The inverse reconstructs actual local connection forms and a smooth bundle. For any curve $c$ with $c(0)=x$, $c'(0)=v$, let $c_{0,t}$ denote its reparametrized short segment. Then
$$
(A_i)_x(v)=-\left.\frac{d}{dt}U_i(c_{0,t})\right|_{t=0}\in\mathfrak g,
\tag{14.5.7h.3}
$$
and these smooth one-forms satisfy
$$
A_j=g_{ji}A_i g_{ji}^{-1}-dg_{ji}\,g_{ji}^{-1}.
\tag{14.5.7h.4}
$$
The reconstruction preserves every experiment and finite protocol in Theorem 14.5.7g exactly. Restricting to a fixed bundle requires that the reconstructed transition cocycle define that bundle's isomorphism class. The boundary-framed variants require the corresponding boundary transition and frame restrictions.

*Proof.*

The local differential step is the smooth-path reconstruction theorem of Schreiber and Waldorf [2009, Proposition 4.7 and Appendix B.4, equations (B.15)–(B.18)]. Its exact statement used here is: a smooth functor $\mathcal P_1(U)\to BG$ uniquely determines a smooth Lie-algebra-valued one-form by (14.5.7h.3), independent of the curve having the specified tangent; this assignment is linear in $v$; and the original functor is precisely the solution of the displayed negative-sign transport ODE. The required smoothness is evaluation on every jointly smooth finite-dimensional path family, as in that paper's Definition 3.1. Lemma B.2 proves independence of the representing curve, the following argument proves linearity, and Lemma B.3 proves smoothness. Thus the result imports a proved local reconstruction theorem, not the assumption that an arbitrary compatible table already arises from a connection.

Apply that result on each $U_i$. Differentiating (14.5.7h.1) at a short segment starting at $x$ gives
$$
-A_j(v)=dg_{ji}(v)g_{ji}^{-1}-g_{ji}A_i(v)g_{ji}^{-1},
$$
which is (14.5.7h.4). The cocycle glues $U_i\times G$ by the right-equivariant coordinate identifications $(x,v_i)\sim(x,g_{ji}(x)v_i)$. The cocycle identities make this equivalence relation consistent and produce a smooth principal bundle. On $U_i\times G$ the principal connection form is $\omega_i=\operatorname{Ad}_{v_i^{-1}}A_i+v_i^{-1}dv_i$. Substitution of $v_j=g_{ji}v_i$ and (14.5.7h.4) gives $\omega_j=\omega_i$, so these forms glue. Equivalently, (14.5.7h.4) glues the local horizontal distributions of the ODE. It therefore gives a global smooth connection.

Its transport on each chart is the original local functor by uniqueness for the ODE. Subdivision of a compact path into finitely many chart pieces and functoriality then give equality on every path. The resulting identification of torsor fibers is natural, proving essential surjectivity.

A smooth natural isomorphism is locally a smooth map $k_i:U_i\to G$. Naturality says that it intertwines the local path transports. Differentiating again gives the connection transformation law. Compatibility on overlaps glues the $k_i$ to a smooth connection-preserving bundle map. Conversely any such bundle map gives a natural isomorphism. Local matrix equality is equality of bundle maps, so this arrow correspondence is both full and faithful. This proves (14.5.7h.2), also the content of Schreiber–Waldorf Theorem 5.4.

Theorem 14.5.7g's response functions use finitely many holonomies. Since reconstruction preserves each path transport, it preserves every basic response, and the product/sum formula preserves every finite protocol. Bundle and boundary restrictions are respected exactly when the gluing data obey the restrictions stated in the theorem. ∎

**Transport Topology and Operational Admission.**

Connection objects and gauge maps here are $C^\infty$, with the usual compact-open $C^\infty$ topology in local bundle charts. Each fixed path-transport map, invariant tensor response, and finite protocol law is continuous in that topology. This follows from continuous dependence of the linear transport ODE on the connection coefficients along each compact path, followed by composition and finite products/sums. The transport reconstruction uses first derivatives in path-family parameters. Pointwise convergence on each fixed path does not control those derivatives. The theorem therefore asserts a smooth categorical reconstruction under the displayed regularity, without identifying the weak pointwise-holonomy topology with the $C^\infty$ connection topology.

The source of this functor is a smooth infinite transport category with complete local descent data. A separately given finite operational groupoid must still be mapped into it with preserved responses, transitions, resource ledgers and admitted context/intervention laws. The scalar response quotient by itself contains no nontrivial automorphism arrows; faithfulness of the categorical transport equivalence is furnished by its explicit natural transformations.

<a id="theorem-1457i-compatible-finite-holonomies-without-smooth-continuum-realization"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7i (Compatible Finite Holonomies Without Smooth Continuum Realization).**

For every $d\ge2$, there exist smooth $SU(2)$ connections $A_N$ on the trivial bundle over $\mathbb R^d$, a sequence of disjoint smooth contractible loops $C_n$ shrinking to the origin, and a pointwise-convergent subnet of their complete path transports whose limit $H$ satisfies all path composition, inverse and thin-homotopy relations, but has no smooth-connection representative, even after arbitrary fiberwise conjugations of loop transports.

Every finite collection of prescribed loop values $H(C_1),\ldots,H(C_n)$ is realized by a smooth $A_N$ for all $N\ge n$. The limit obeys every finite path relation inherited from the smooth transports. Nevertheless it lies outside $\mathsf{Trans}^\infty_{SU(2)}(\mathbb R^d)$.

*Proof.*

Choose pairwise disjoint balls $B_n$ centered at $(2^{-n},0,\ldots,0)$ with radii $2^{-n-3}$. In a two-dimensional plane inside each ball choose a round oriented circle $C_n$, with circumference tending to zero. A tubular neighborhood of $C_n$ admits a smooth one-form $\alpha_n$, compactly supported in $B_n$, with
$$
\int_{C_n}\alpha_n=1.
$$
For example pull back $d\theta/(2\pi)$ from the circle along the tubular projection, multiply by a bump that is one on the circle and zero near the tube boundary, and extend by zero. Set
$$
T=\begin{pmatrix} i&0\\0&-i\end{pmatrix},\qquad
A_N=T\sum_{n=1}^N\alpha_n,\qquad h=e^{-T}\ne I.
\tag{14.5.7i.1}
$$
Each $A_N$ is a smooth compactly supported connection one-form. All coefficients are in one abelian subalgebra of $\mathfrak{su}(2)$, so path ordering is immaterial and $\operatorname{Hol}_{A_N}(C_n)=h$ whenever $N\ge n$.

In the global trivial frame each $A_N$ defines an assignment in
$$
\prod_{\gamma\in\mathcal P_1(\mathbb R^d)}SU(2),
$$
where one representative of each arrow is sufficient. This is a compact space by Tychonoff's theorem. A subnet converges pointwise to some assignment $H$. Each identity, inverse or composition condition is an equality involving finitely many coordinates and continuous group operations, hence defines a closed subset. The limit satisfies every such condition. Thin invariance is already built into the index groupoid. Cofinality of the subnet and the eventual exact value above give $H(C_n)=h$ for every $n$.

If $H$ were the transport of a smooth connection $B$ in some smooth local frame near zero, its matrix coefficients would be bounded on a compact neighborhood of zero. For a unitary transport ODE,
$$
\|\operatorname{Hol}_B(C_n)-I\|\le\int_{C_n}\|B\|\,ds
\le K\operatorname{length}(C_n)\longrightarrow0.
\tag{14.5.7i.2}
$$
A different, even point-dependent, fiber frame conjugates each loop holonomy. The operator norm distance to $I$ and its trace are invariant under this conjugation. The prescribed $h$ has a fixed strictly positive distance to $I$ and normalized trace $\cos 1\ne1$, contradicting (14.5.7i.2). Hence no smooth connection can represent $H$ in any such gauge.

The finite-loop realization statement follows from (14.5.7i.1). The categorical reconstruction theorem would produce a smooth connection if $H$ admitted smooth local descent, so $H$ fails that regularity requirement. ∎

This obstruction identifies the missing continuum input concretely: refinement identities and finite realizability do not control short-path derivatives or curvature. The regularity and local descent in Theorem 14.5.7h provide that input. It is additional to the finite/countable orbit-cardinality obstruction of Theorems 14.5.7c–d.

<a id="theorem-1457j-nonlinear-gauge-separation-and-first-order-null-kernel-obstruction"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7j (Nonlinear Gauge Separation and First-Order Null-Kernel Obstruction).**

Let $G$ be a compact connected semisimple Lie group of positive dimension and let $P=M\times G$ be trivial. Let $A_0=0$, and let $\mathscr R$ be any family of continuously differentiable real scalar response functions with continuous linear first differentials on a $C^\infty$ neighborhood of $0$ in $\Omega^1(M,\mathfrak g)$ that are invariant under all smooth gauge transformations whenever both arguments lie in the neighborhood. In particular they are invariant under all constant gauge transformations. Then
$$
dR_0(a)=0\qquad
\text{for every }R\in\mathscr R,\ a\in\Omega^1(M,\mathfrak g).
\tag{14.5.7j.1}
$$
For $\dim M\ge2$, the common differential kernel is strictly larger than the tangent space to the gauge orbit:
$$
\bigcap_{R\in\mathscr R}\ker dR_0
=\Omega^1(M,\mathfrak g)
\supsetneq d\Omega^0(M,\mathfrak g).
\tag{14.5.7j.2}
$$
This applies to all the invariant tensor responses of Theorem 14.5.7g and every smooth finite protocol probability constructed from them. At $A_0$, the classical Fisher matrix of every such finite protocol with its retained positive-probability outcomes is zero.

For $\dim M\ge2$ and $G=SU(2)$ there are explicit non-gauge directions that these responses distinguish at second order. For this example choose the standing faithful representation $\rho$ to be the fundamental representation on $\mathbb C^2$. Take $T$ as in (14.5.7i.1), choose a smooth real one-form $\alpha$ with $d\alpha\ne0$, and a loop $C$ based at $x_0$ with $c=\int_C\alpha\ne0$. Along $A_t=tT\alpha$ use
$$
f(A_t)=\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{Hol}_{A_t}(C)=\cos(ct),\qquad
p_t(+)=\tfrac12+\tfrac14\cos(ct).
\tag{14.5.7j.3}
$$
This experiment is the equal classical mixture of the two real diagonal-coefficient probes in the fundamental representation, so it belongs to the declared protocol class. Then
$$
p_t(+)-p_0(+)=-\frac{c^2t^2}{8}+O(t^4),\qquad
I(t)=\frac{(p'_t(+))^2}{p_t(+)(1-p_t(+))}
=\frac{c^4t^2}{3}+O(t^4).
\tag{14.5.7j.4}
$$
In particular $I(0)=0$, while $F_{A_t}=tT\,d\alpha\ne0$ for $t\ne0$, so $A_t$ is not gauge equivalent to $0$.

*Proof.*

For a constant gauge transformation $g\in G$, the action on a connection at the zero connection is $a\mapsto\operatorname{Ad}_{g^{-1}}a$. Gauge invariance and differentiation at $0$ give
$$
dR_0(a)=dR_0(\operatorname{Ad}_g a)\qquad(g\in G).
$$
The continuous linear functional $dR_0$ commutes with the compact Haar average. The image of the averaging projection
$$
a\longmapsto\int_G\operatorname{Ad}_g a\,d\mu_G(g)
$$
is the center of $\mathfrak g$ pointwise. For a semisimple Lie algebra that center is zero. Therefore
$$
dR_0(a)=dR_0\left(\int_G\operatorname{Ad}_g a\,d\mu_G(g)\right)=dR_0(0)=0.
$$
The integration is harmless in the $C^\infty$ topology: in a basis of the finite-dimensional Lie algebra it is a finite linear operation on smooth coefficient one-forms.

Differentiating $A^u=u^{-1}Au+u^{-1}du$ at $A=0,u=\exp(s\xi)$ gives the gauge tangent $d\xi$. In a coordinate ball choose a compactly supported one-form $\alpha$ with $d\alpha\ne0$, and choose $0\ne T\in\mathfrak g$. Then $a=T\alpha$ has $da=T\,d\alpha\ne0$, whereas $d(d\xi)=0$. Thus $a$ is outside the gauge tangent. This proves strictness of (14.5.7j.2), without an assumption about irreducibility or gauge fixing.

Each invariant holonomy tensor is smooth along finite-dimensional smooth connection families by smooth ODE dependence. Any fixed finite adaptive protocol probability is a finite sum of products of such smooth invariant functions, hence is itself smooth and invariant. Its derivative vanishes by (14.5.7j.1). For a finite outcome law $p_y(A)$ with $p_y(0)>0$, the Fisher form is
$$
\mathcal I_0(a,b)=\sum_y\frac{dp_y|_0(a)\,dp_y|_0(b)}{p_y(0)}=0.
$$
Outcomes excluded by a connection-independent deterministic randomization or postprocessing can be removed from the sample space; the remaining supported outcomes have positive probability because every basic branch probability is at least $1/4$.

For the explicit example, Stokes' theorem supplies a sufficiently small loop bounding a disk on which $\int_C\alpha\ne0$ whenever $d\alpha$ is nonzero at a point. Joining that loop to $x_0$ by a path and its reverse makes $C$ a based loop; the added line integrals cancel, and its holonomy trace is unchanged. Since the one-form is everywhere proportional to $T$, its wedge-square vanishes and $F_{A_t}=tT\,d\alpha$. The commuting transport ODE gives $\operatorname{Hol}_{A_t}(C)=\exp(-ctT)$ and normalized trace $\cos(ct)$. Taylor expansion gives the first expression of (14.5.7j.4); using $p_0=3/4$, $p'_t=-(c/4)\sin(ct)$ gives the second. Curvature covariance makes a connection with nonzero curvature inequivalent to the flat zero connection. ∎

**Higher-Order Detectability and Finite-Resource Interpretation.**

The full nonlinear response family separates gauge orbits by Theorem 14.5.7g, even though its differential fails to separate them at this reducible stratum. The vanishing in Theorem 14.5.7j is a statement about first derivatives of gauge-invariant probabilities and their first-order Fisher metric; it is not exact response equality along the non-gauge curve.

For $n$ independent repetitions of the explicit binary experiment, additivity of relative entropy and Taylor expansion give
$$
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_0^{\otimes n}\|P_t^{\otimes n})
=n\left(\frac{c^4t^4}{24}+O(t^6)\right).
\tag{14.5.7j.5}
$$
Pinsker's inequality implies $\|P_0^{\otimes n}-P_t^{\otimes n}\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\le\sqrt{nD_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_0\|P_t)/2}$. Fix $\eta\in(0,1)$. Equal-prior Bayes error at most $(1-\eta)/2$ requires total variation at least $\eta$, so the Pinsker bound implies $n\ge2\eta^2/D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_0\|P_t)=\Omega(|t|^{-4})$. Conversely, for any fixed per-hypothesis error target $\varepsilon\in(0,1/2)$, an empirical-frequency threshold halfway between the two probabilities gives each error at most $\exp(-n|p_t-p_0|^2/2)$ by Hoeffding's inequality. Hence $n\ge2\log(1/\varepsilon)/|p_t-p_0|^2=O(|t|^{-4})$ suffices. These bounds concern this named experiment and independent-repeat protocol, with fixed $c\ne0$ and the uniformly positive probabilities $p_t,1-p_t\in[1/4,3/4]$. Support-changing laws and other physical protocols require their own testing analysis.

For a compact group with nonzero Lie-algebra center, the same proof says every first derivative at zero factors through the central part of the connection variation. The semisimple directions are still killed. At a general reducible connection, averaging over its stabilizer kills the nontrivial stabilizer-representation components; the complete-vanishing statement above uses the full constant-$G$ stabilizer of $A_0=0$.

Theorem X.3 assumes a positive invertible retained kernel on its regular unit Predictive-Ward branch. Equations (14.5.7j.1)–(14.5.7j.2) exhibit a class where first-order gauge-invariant response data cannot supply that branch merely by quotienting $d\Omega^0$. An accepted construction must choose and justify an appropriate regular response stratum, frame-sensitive context, nonlinear quotient coordinate, or other response structure before applying that inversion.

<a id="proposition-1457k-finite-law-quantizers-and-large-gauge-response-quotients"></a>**Proposition 14.5.7k (Finite Law Quantizers and Large-Gauge Response Quotients).**

**A. Outcome Binning and Law Binning.**

The binary outcome quantizer in (14.5.7g.2) retains a continuum of probability laws. A different operation is to quantize a finite list of probabilities themselves. Let $J$ be a finite set of basic experiments and let $q:[1/4,3/4]^J\to R$ have finite range. The retained equivalence is precisely
$$
A\sim_{J,q}B
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
q((p_j(A))_{j\in J})=q((p_j(B))_{j\in J}).
\tag{14.5.7k.1}
$$
It contains gauge equivalence. On the nonflat $SU(2)$ family of Theorem 14.5.7j, with $\dim M\ge2$ and the trivial bundle, if $q$ is constant on a neighborhood of the probability vector at $A=0$, it also identifies $0$ with smooth nonflat connections $tT\alpha$ for all sufficiently small nonzero $t$, hence strictly exceeds gauge equivalence.

Proof: every $p_j$ is continuous. The inverse image of that neighborhood contains a $C^\infty$ neighborhood of zero, and $tT\alpha\to0$ in $C^\infty$ while its curvature remains nonzero for $t\ne0$. This proves the claim. A concrete quantizer satisfying the hypothesis is obtained by placing each finite collection of bin thresholds away from the finitely many reference probabilities $p_j(0)$. This quantizer is an example supplied for the theorem, not a proposed physical detector choice.

Conversely, taking all finite $J$ and all dyadic precision levels of coordinatewise law binning recovers equality of each exact probability, because distinct real numbers eventually occupy distinct bins. Thus an inverse system of finite law records can encode the response quotient of Theorem 14.5.7g; its smooth realization still requires Theorem 14.5.7h and is obstructed for irregular compatible transport data by Theorem 14.5.7i. Neither finite output alphabets nor a formal inverse limit supply the actual MPU quantizer.

**B. Small and Full Gauge Groups.**

On $M=S^3$ with $G=SU(2)$ and a trivial bundle, let $g:S^3\to SU(2)\cong S^3$ be a smooth map of degree one, and set $A=g^{-1}dg$. Then every loop holonomy is $I$, so $A$ and $0$ give the same complete response table of Theorem 14.5.7g. They are equivalent under the full smooth gauge group. They are inequivalent under its identity component $\mathcal G_0$.

Proof: the pure-gauge transport formula gives $U_A(\gamma)=g(y)^{-1}g(x)$ and therefore the identity on loops. If $A=h^{-1}dh$ for some degree-zero $h$, then $d(gh^{-1})=0$, so connectedness makes $gh^{-1}$ constant. Hence $g$ and $h$ differ by constant multiplication. Constant multiplication in the connected group $SU(2)$ is homotopic to the identity, forcing $\deg g=\deg h$, a contradiction. Any map in $\mathcal G_0$ has degree zero. Thus full-gauge-invariant loop responses cannot distinguish these small-gauge sectors. One can choose $g(x_0)=I$ by constant multiplication, so fixing a single base frame does not remove this example. ∎

When a physical theory retains large-gauge sectors, its observable/context algebra must retain the associated sector information, such as a suitably defined lifted topological quantity or boundary/charged reference. Which such information is admitted is part of the physical protocol specification. Full-gauge-invariant tensor responses automatically quotient all components and cannot silently implement a small-gauge quotient.

- global group $SU(2)$, in its specified fundamental representation and its faithful coherently controlled representation;
- the trivial principal bundle on the same four-torus as the regularity source of Theorems C.6c.3–C.6c.4a and C.6c.6, no boundary, a specified base frame, and the full based smooth gauge group, including all components;
- the gauge-saturation of a bounded twelve-parameter family of constant connections, including noncommuting, nonflat connections;
- actual finite coherent-transport/readout macros on new owned probe factors at the qualified vertices, paid link routing, a positive completion clock and a complete incremental resource ledger;
- the identity outcome quantizer on the binary probe results, a declared finite adaptive protocol family and its exact finite source law;
- a countable finite-description source, an explicitly defined infinite completion, an object/arrow functor to smooth transport, and quantitative approximation of every retained smooth loop experiment;
- a twelve-dimensional regular response quotient with an explicit positive Fisher bound. On the gauge-saturated family its differential nulls are exactly the full based gauge directions.

This is a framed physical witness. It does not identify its frame-sensitive Fisher form with an unframed Ward consumer, prove the separate unit Ward/PCE hypotheses of X.3, choose the Standard Model global group, or realize every smooth connection physically. The preceding universal comparison results keep their universal domain; this physical realization has the exact declared domain above. ## 3. Why an additional coherent context is necessary

For a two-dimensional payload, conjugation alone satisfies

$$
\mathrm{Ad}(U)=\mathrm{Ad}(-U),\quad\text{for } U\in\mathrm{SU}(2).
$$

Equality holds on all density matrices and after every spectator extension. Any finite adaptive circuit using only these identical channel calls and the same other instruments has identical complete outcome laws, by induction on its unnormalized branches. Yet normalized fundamental traces of I and −I are 1 and −1. The binary response 1/2+tr(U)/8 is respectively 3/4 and 1/4. Consequently no classical decoding of the conjugation-only response table can intertwine both of these center-sensitive responses; any one common proposed binary law has maximum total-variation error at least 1/4 against them.

The same ambiguity has a smooth global witness on $T^4$. Put $T=i\sigma_3$ and

$$
A_\omega=\pi T \sum_i \omega_i\, dx_i,\qquad \omega\in\{0,1\}^4.
$$

These smooth flat connections on the trivial $SU(2)$ bundle have coordinate-cycle holonomies $(-1)^{\omega_i}I$. Distinct $\omega$ give distinct full-gauge orbits, since the corresponding traces differ. Their flat adjoint transport has the same trivial holonomy. In local parallel frames the only fundamental transition functions are central signs; conjugation removes every one. On a periodic grid the equivalent central edge signs are

$$
z_\omega(x,s)=(-1)^{\sum_i \omega_i \lfloor (x_i+s_i)/n\rfloor}.
$$

Their products around contractible lifted loops are one; a loop of winding $k$ has product $(-1)^{\omega\cdot k}$. All conjugation channels of these signs are the identity. This illustrates loss of the $SU(2)$ lift in an adjoint/channel quotient even when smoothness is perfect.

This is a **factorization obstruction**, not a prohibition of a new coherent experiment and not a uniqueness condition in the original target. A channel-only statement also does not imply equality of every conceivable physical pulse descriptor or cost record. The source/resource coordinates must be compared separately.

The coherent repair is the explicitly specified unitary

$$
C(U)=|0\rangle\langle 0|_C \otimes I_B + |1\rangle\langle 1|_C \otimes U_B.
$$

It is a unitary on the whole $C,B$ support, $C(UV)=C(U)C(V)$, and $C$ is faithful on $SU(2)$. In particular $C(-I)=Z_C\otimes I_{B}$, a real relative phase observable on $C$. No black-box procedure taking the channel $\operatorname{Ad}(U)$ as input is being claimed. The physical matrix/generator for $C(U)$ is independently supplied and paid below.

## 4. The nominated smooth gauge sector

Use dimensionless coordinates $x_{i}$ of period one on $M=T^4$, with the same physical metric $\ell_*\,d_{0}$ and normalized volume as the regularity source of Theorems C.6c.3–C.6c.4a and C.6c.6. The physical coordinates are $y=\ell_*x$. Set $r_{0}=1/4$ and

$$
\Theta=\{\theta=(a_1,\dots,a_4) \in (\mathbb{R}^3)^4: |a_i|\le r_0\}.
$$

The regular stratum is its interior; the formulas extend to the open neighborhood $|a_i|<\pi/2$, so limits on its boundary introduce no rank singularity. For the usual Hermitian Pauli matrices $\sigma_1,\sigma_2,\sigma_3$ set

$$
A_\theta=\sum_i i(a_i \cdot \sigma)\, dx_i.
\tag{1}
$$

This is a genuine anti-Hermitian $\mathfrak{su}(2)$-valued connection on the trivial principal $SU(2)$ bundle. In physical coordinates its coefficient is $A_y=A_x/\ell_*$. No diffusion-time or heat-generator identification is imposed on it.

Let $x_{0}=0$, with its specified fundamental basis and coherent phase reference. Let

$$
G_*=\{g \in C^\infty(T^4,\mathrm{SU}(2)): g(x_0)=I\},
$$

with **all** homotopy components included. Define $A^g=g^{-1}Ag+g^{-1}dg$. The physical target category $C$ has objects $A_\theta^g$, $\theta\in\Theta$, $g\in G_*$, on the specified trivial bundle, and all smooth connection-preserving based bundle maps between these objects. There is no small-gauge sector label. Thus the large-gauge counterexample in 14.5.7k is correctly quotiented, not silently lost. There are no boundary conditions because $M$ has no boundary.

A concrete nonflat point is $a_1=(1/8,0,0)$, $a_2=(0,1/8,0)$, $a_3=a_4=0$. Its curvature contains

$$
[i\sigma_1/8,i\sigma_2/8]\,dx_1\wedge dx_2=-i\sigma_3\,dx_1\wedge dx_2/32,
$$

which is nonzero. The branch is nonabelian in its actual transport and is four-dimensional; it is not just a $U(1)$ holonomy example renamed $SU(2)$.

### 4.1 Exact orbit slice and arrows

Let $\gamma_{i}$ be the positive coordinate loop at $x_{0}$. Its holonomy for (1), with the negative-sign ODE convention of 14.5.7, is

$$
U_i(\theta)=\exp(-i\, a_i \cdot \sigma).
\tag{2}
$$

A based gauge transformation leaves these matrices unchanged. The exponential map is injective on $|a|\le 1/4$: explicitly

$$
\begin{aligned}
U_i&=\cos(r_i)I-i\,v_i\cdot\sigma,\\
v_i&=(\sin r_i/r_i)a_i,\quad r_i=|a_i|,
\end{aligned}
$$

and $a_i=[\arcsin|v_i|/|v_i|]\,v_i$, continuously interpreted at zero. The chosen range has $\cos r_{i}>0$. Therefore a based connection isomorphism between $A_{\theta}$ and $A_{\theta}'$ forces $\theta=\theta'$. This uses actual coordinate-loop matrices, not only a claim that constants are a gauge slice.

A based connection automorphism is the identity. Indeed, it intertwines every path transport and equals $I$ at $x_{0}$; transporting that equality to any point on the connected torus determines it there. Consequently a given object $A_\theta^g$ has a unique $g$ relative to its canonical representative. Between $(\theta,g)$ and $(\theta,h)$ the unique gauge arrow is

$$
k=g^{-1}h,\qquad (A_\theta^g)^k=A_\theta^h.
\tag{3}
$$

Between different $\theta$ there are no arrows. Composition gives $(g^{-1}h)(h^{-1}j)=g^{-1}j$. These facts prove all arrow claims, including stabilizers; no scalar response quotient is mistaken for unrecorded automorphism data.

## 5. Actual finite source and coherent probe macro

At level $n=3^{2j}$, $j\ge 2$, use exactly the graph, $m=3^j$, $S_m$, paid lanes, units and tariff of Theorems C.6c.3–C.6c.4a and C.6c.6. Its $N=n^4$ vertices remain the actual persistent P17 occurrences. First complete the existing qualification-containing finite heat experiment, with its original fixed core durations and endpoints. Append the following separately scheduled gauge-probe phase. Earlier heat records and core/null laws are unchanged; there is no heat endpoint in this added phase to which an old clock is falsely assigned.

Every probe uses newly owned auxiliary factors at these same vertices. It never reads or modifies the old arbitrary passive heat payload, any core bit, ROM, cursor or archive. The new communication is carried by the same actual paid graph links. An additional local appliance is not called a complexity-one cell. Finite field inputs $\theta$ have rational components satisfying the displayed norm bounds, with an exact rational certificate $\sum_a a_{i,a}^{2}\le 1/16$. Their number is countable. For a directed edge from x to x+s modulo n, choose its descriptor-defined straight lifted segment. The field's actual local service is

$$
U_{\theta,e}=\exp\Bigl[-i \sum_i (s_i/n)(a_i \cdot \sigma)\Bigr].
\tag{4}
$$

Its entries are finite expressions using rational arithmetic, a positive square root, sine and cosine (equivalently a finite matrix exponential). The identity $\exp(-i\,b\cdot\sigma)=\cos|b|\,I-i(\sin|b|/|b|)\,b\cdot\sigma$ supplies its exact unitarity and determinant-one certificate. These are finitely described ideal matrices of the already admitted local-pulse class. At $b=0$ use $I$, avoiding division by zero.

A graph path is an ordered finite edge list. Reserve two probe-port qubits at every vertex for each trial, initially zero except for the prepared packet at $x_{0}$. They carry reference C and target B. For each edge:

1. use one paid unconditional SWAP to move $C$ to the next vertex;
2. use a second paid SWAP to move $B$ to the next vertex;
3. apply the local, fully specified $C(U_{\theta,e})$ at that destination.

The predecessor ports receive ready zeros by the SWAPs; revisiting a vertex does not erase a record. A separate fresh packet bank per trial avoids any uncounted inter-trial reset. The full path operation on the transported packet is exactly $C(U_{\mathrm{path}})$, with $U_{\mathrm{path}}$ the ordered product of (4), including every external spectator. This follows by unitary composition, not by a classical simulation of a predicted probability.

### 5.1 Preparation, terminal read and quantizer

At $x_{0}$ prepare four fresh factors C,D,B,R in the pure state

$$
\bigl[\sqrt{3}/2\, |+\rangle_C|0\rangle_D + 1/2\, |-\rangle_C|1\rangle_D \bigr] \otimes |\Phi^+\rangle_{BR}.
\tag{5}
$$

A fixed finite unitary maps four ready zeros to this normalized vector; a Householder construction, or its finite state-preparation circuit, gives its full matrix. $D$ and $R$ stay at $x_{0}$ and are not fed back. The reduced states are $\rho_C=(I+X/2)/2$ and $\rho_B=I/2$. All four factors and their preparation are charged.

After a based loop, apply $C(i\sigma_a)$ at $x_{0}$ for $a=1,2,3$; for $a=0$ reserve the corresponding identity slot. Apply $H$ to $C$ and make the normalized computational-basis record, labeling 0 by $+$ and 1 by $-$. This is a destructive terminal read of the **new** probe. It is not an informative pre-read of the protected old heat payload.

Define

$$
f_0(U)=\operatorname{Re} \operatorname{tr}(U)/2,\qquad f_a(U)=\operatorname{Re} \operatorname{tr}(i \sigma_a U)/2\ \ (a=1,2,3).
$$

Direct multiplication of the control density matrix gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
p_\theta(+ \mid \gamma,a)&=1/2+f_a(U_\theta(\gamma))/4, \\
p_\theta(- \mid \gamma,a)&=1-p_\theta(+ \mid \gamma,a).
\end{aligned}
\tag{6}
$$

The probabilities lie in $[1/4,3/4]$. For $SU(2)$ these four real coefficients specify $U$ completely. Damping in (5) is essential to the factor $1/4$. The full physical outcome quantizer is the identity on the aligned binary labels; it is **not** finite binning of the numerical probability itself. Auxiliary purifying factors remain part of the complete source state and ledger; the declared gauge response is precisely this terminal output, experiment label and clock. Old heat/audit outputs have their separately declared restriction maps.

For $U=I$ versus $U=-I$ and $a=0$, the actual macro gives $3/4$ versus $1/4$. Thus the field service really fixes the global $SU(2)$ lift. A center-sensitive calibration may use the finitely described local control $C(-I)$; it requires no enlargement of the stated primitive alphabet. This calibration demonstrates the representation's global faithfulness and is not asserted to be a field configuration inside the small regular parameter ball.

### 5.2 Contexts, interventions and complete finite law

The admitted gauge contexts are finite based graph loops, a in {0,1,2,3}, the fixed preparation (5), the registered base frame/phase, and finite adaptive repetitions with fresh banks.
