times any declared independent classical branch weights. The CP maps are the specified unitary packet maps followed by projectors summing to identity. ## 6. Finite resource ledger and actual clock

The new macro has a finite emitted event list for every finite word. This is a direct positive source extension. It does not invoke a zero-increment substitution or hide the new coherent reference inside the old passive payload.

For a nonadaptive list of $Q$ trials let $L$ be the total number of edges and $S=\sum_{\mathrm{edges}}|s_e|$, with occurrences counted. with the latter disjoint fields counted from the actual finite serialization/event list. No separately listed bit appears twice. Each edge has two link SWAPs and one local controlled transport pulse. A completion flag has its own record/clock support. Hence the data-path counters are

$$
G_{\mathrm{local}}=L+3Q,\quad J_{\mathrm{read}}=Q,\quad N_{\mathrm{link}}=2L,\quad C_{\mathrm{link}}=2S.
\tag{9}
$$

The final clock flag and controller drive events are appended explicitly to these data-path counts, not declared cost-free. If a clock model emits extra reversible clock gates or ROM-delivery events, its emitted list contributes them and their supported generators to G, J, K and B. The estimates below use $G_{\mathrm{total}}$ and the actual full serialized schedule after those additions.

One completely specified timing convention assigns every local/control/record event one service unit $\eta$ and a directed link event $\eta|s_e|$. Emit the controller's finite delivery/clock events with the same local-unit convention. Let $D_{\mathrm{word}}$ be the sum of these positive service-unit lengths plus one final completion slot. Choose

$$
\eta=T_{\mathrm{probe}}/D_{\mathrm{word}},\quad T_{\mathrm{probe}}=\tau_*>0.
\tag{10}
$$

Execute a unitary event of any type during its first $\eta/2$; a link has $|s_e|\ge 1$, so its pulse fits. All other reserved time is held or idle. The source period $\tau_*$ is a declared gauge-macro completion period, not a diffusion time or a speed-limit assertion. Every duration is positive at finite $n$ and finite protocol size.

For an event matrix $W$ use $H_W=i\hbar\operatorname{Log}(W)/(\eta/2)$, the principal-log source rule. Therefore

$$
\begin{aligned}
\max_t \lVert H_{\mathrm{probe}}(t)\rVert &\le 2\pi \hbar/\eta, \\
\int \lVert H_{\mathrm{probe}}(t)\rVert\,dt &\le \pi \hbar\, N_{\mathrm{unitary\,events}}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{11}
$$

These are finite bounds; the exact norm/action is computed from the actual finite event matrices. Adaptive alternatives are placed in the fixed finite tree allocation and selected using retained classical control; unused branches have the padded declared slot and held resources. Use worst-branch duration/padding, or serially reserve all alternatives. There is no branch-dependent unscheduled communication.

The joint tariff is exactly the C.6c.4a tariff, with its same fixed coefficients, applied to the joint word:

$$
\begin{aligned}
V/u_*&=\alpha_E E+\alpha_0 I_0+\alpha_B B+\alpha_I I+\alpha_G G+\alpha_J J \\
&\quad +\alpha_C C_{\mathrm{link}}+\alpha_K K+\alpha_\Omega(\tau_*/\hbar)\max_t\lVert H_{\mathrm{joint}}(t)\rVert.
\end{aligned}
$$

Existing lanes are reused, so $\Delta E=0$. For the simple sequential phase all old bits retained until its end add $B_{\mathrm{old}}T_{\mathrm{probe}}/\tau_*$ to $I$; every new allocated factor adds its actual holding interval. The extra lane idle time is its total allocated lane-time minus the new occupied link intervals, in the same units; none of these intervals is also charged as nonlane bit-time. New state preparation, descriptor production, compiler input/output and execution trace, coherent controls, all measurement/clock records and final collection have explicit owners. If final records are collected from another node, that collection uses paid links; the loop probes already finish at $x_{0}$. The exact peak term is max of the old phase and new phase peaks because the phases are disjoint, not a fictitious zero increment or an assumed sum.

This algorithmically complete finite ledger is the same kind of finite-word ownership used by the regularity source of Theorems C.6c.3–C.6c.4a and C.6c.6. It does not assert that an exponentially large physical device was manufactured or that all parameter precisions are free. Description lengths and preparation/compilation effort grow and remain charged. No bandwidth, strength or total-cost bound uniform in refinement is claimed.

The following finite algorithm resolves the controller entries in (8)–(11); it is not a new primitive oracle. ## 7. Exact refinement and smooth-path estimates

### 7.1 Same graph geometry, an additional typed path map

The physical graph and metric interpolation are those of Theorems C.6c.3–C.6c.4a and C.6c.6. The gauge path comparison uses the vertex embedding $x\to x/n$ on that same torus. It is a path map, not a claim that coarse/fine heat generators commute.

Consecutive levels have $n'=9n$ and $m'=3m$. Embed a coarse vertex $x$ as $9x$. Replace a coarse directed edge of descriptor $s$ by three fine edges of descriptor $3s$. They are admitted because $|3s|\le m'$. Each displacement is $s/(3n)$, and

$$
\Bigl[ \exp\Bigl(-i \sum_i (s_i/(3n))(a_i \cdot \sigma)\Bigr) \Bigr]^3=U_{\theta,e}.
\tag{12}
$$

Thus path composition/refinement preserves the full controlled matrix and every finite probe law exactly for fixed rational $\theta$. The physical lengths agree: three lengths $\ell_*|3s|/(9n)$ sum to $\ell_*|s|/n$. Raw service debits and descriptions do grow and are retained. The exact path-refinement identity is not claimed for unrelated staircase replacements or changed $\theta$.

For a coordinate cycle one may use $m$ successive edges of descriptor $m\,e_{i}$, since $n=m^2$. Its product is exactly (2) at every level. Consequently the twelve coordinate responses used below are exact finite physical responses, not just continuum extrapolations.

### 7.2 Approximation of every retained smooth loop

Let $\gamma:[0,1]\to T^4$ be a smooth based loop, with a smooth lift to $\mathbb{R}^4$ starting at zero and ending at its winding vector. Put $V=\lVert\gamma'\rVert_\infty$, $W=\lVert\gamma''\rVert_\infty$, length $L_\gamma=\int|\gamma'|$, $\delta=1/n$ and $h=m/n$. Take

$$
k=\lceil 2 \max(1,V)/h\rceil.
$$

At times $j/k$ round the lift to the nearest lattice point in $n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}^4$; its error is at most $\delta$. Keep the initial and terminal lattice points exactly, which is possible at zero and an integer winding vector. Join consecutive rounded points linearly; omit repeated vertices. Each increment has norm at most $V/k+2\delta\le h$ for $m\ge 4$, so its nonzero descriptor lies in $S_{m}$. Denote this paid graph loop by $\gamma_{n}$.

On each time interval the chord derivative differs from $\gamma'$ by at most W/k, and rounding changes its derivative by at most 2k $\delta$. Therefore

$$
\int |\gamma_n'-\gamma'| \le W/k+2k \delta =: b_n(\gamma) \to 0.
\tag{13}
$$

For constant connections, $\lVert A_\theta(v)\rVert\le\lVert\theta\rVert_F|v|\le 2r_0|v|$. Unitarity and Duhamel's identity give, for $\theta_{n}$ in $\Theta$ with $e_n=\lVert\theta_n-\theta\rVert_F$,

$$
\lVert U_{\theta_n}(\gamma_n)-U_\theta(\gamma)\rVert\le e_nL_\gamma+2r_0b_n(\gamma)=:E_n(\gamma).
\tag{14}
$$

Indeed first compare $\theta_{n}$ and $\theta$ on the fixed smooth path, then compare $\gamma_{n}$ and $\gamma$ at $\theta_{n}$. No exponentially growing ODE factor is needed because all evolutions are unitary. Each graph segment's exact transport is (4), so the actual physical product is the one in (14).

For every $a=0,1,2,3$, $|f_a(U)-f_a(V)|\le\lVert U-V\rVert$. Hence the binary-law total-variation error is at most $E_n(\gamma)/4$. In a fixed finite adaptive protocol, induction/telescoping on its unnormalized branches bounds the complete-record TV error by the largest branch sum of those trial errors. Equivalently a coupling matching the choices until the first differing outcome gives the same bound. It tends to zero for each fixed finite tree, without dividing by branch probabilities or claiming uniformity over arbitrary growing trees.

For paths split into finitely many smooth pieces use the sum of the bounds. The target transports are invariant under sitting-instant reparameterization and thin homotopy because they solve the connection ODE. Finite graph approximants are not asserted to be thin-homotopic to $\gamma$; their limit has the required relations by (14) and the exact target construction.

## 8. Finite descriptions, completion and smooth local descent

### 8.1 The parameter completion is explicit and response-controlled

Let $\Theta_{Q}$ be the rational points of $\Theta$. No finite apparatus is assigned an arbitrary real input. A completed source parameter is an equivalence class of Cauchy sequences $\theta_{j}$ in $\Theta_{Q}$ in Euclidean norm, equivalent when their difference tends to zero. This completion is exactly $\Theta$.

It is also the completion in the twelve **actual** coordinate-response laws. Write $R(\theta)$ for $p(+\mid\gamma_i,a)$, $i=1,\dots,4$, $a=1,2,3$. The derivative of $v(a)=(\sin|a|/|a|)a$ is symmetric with radial eigenvalue $\cos|a|$ and two tangential eigenvalues $\sin|a|/|a|$. On the ball it satisfies

$$
\cos(r_0)I \le Dv \le I.
$$

Integrating along a line segment in the convex ball and using the scalar product with its displacement proves

$$
\cos(r_0)|a-b| \le |v(a)-v(b)| \le |a-b|.
$$

Consequently

$$
\begin{aligned}
\cos(r_0)\lVert\theta-\theta'\rVert_F/4 \\
&\le \lVert R(\theta)-R(\theta')\rVert_2 \le \lVert\theta-\theta'\rVert_F/4.
\end{aligned}
\tag{15}
$$

Thus Cauchy equivalence and separation are not an arbitrarily imposed hidden gauge topology; they are quantitatively determined by retained source response laws on this nominated family. The completion genuinely supplies uncountably many limiting objects, whereas every finite stage and the countable union still have only countably many finitely described source inputs. No finite/countable essentially-surjective claim contradicts 14.5.7c–d.

Different Cauchy names for the same $\theta$ can have different finite-stage controller and cost records. Equation (15) identifies their limiting **binary gauge response**, not these finite implementation records. Each requested finite approximation retains its actual supplied rational coefficient and its own finite audit. Gauge-presentation arrows compare the identical canonical physical word at that same finite approximation; they do not replace one approximating word by another at equal cost. The continuum response is independent of names by (14)–(15), while costs need not converge and are never assigned an unproved name-independent finite limit. One may fix a deterministic rational rounding context for each parameter/precision when a common finite representative is wanted; such a context is additional source input, not an oracle that computes an arbitrary real parameter.

The canonical connection map $\theta\to A_\theta$ is linear into $C^\infty$: all spatial derivatives of its coefficients of positive order vanish and every $C^k$ norm difference is bounded by a fixed multiple of $\lVert\theta-\theta'\rVert$. Therefore the completed canonical source has a smooth connection limit, including control of short-path derivatives. This is a proved regularity statement about this family, not an inference from scalar Mosco convergence.

### 8.2 Complete based frame presentations, including large components

To retain explicit gauge-arrow data one may use finite frame descriptions as coordinate representations of the same physical canonical word. A dense finite-description class is obtained from four real rational trigonometric polynomials $q=(q_0,q_1,q_2,q_3)$ with a supplied certified lower bound $|q(x)|\ge c>0$. Normalize $q/|q|$ as a unit quaternion/$SU(2)$ matrix and then right-multiply by its value at $x_{0}$ inverse to obtain $g(x_0)=I$. Products and inverses of these descriptions are permitted finite expressions.

A positive lower bound can be certified by a finite rational grid, lower bounds on its finitely described sample norms and an explicit derivative/Lipschitz bound on the polynomial; the grid fill distance times that bound is subtracted. The certificate is supplied and checked. A constant polynomial gives a nonempty class.

Every smooth based $SU(2)$ map $g$ has such approximants in $C^\infty$, including maps in nonidentity components. Regard $g$ as four smooth real periodic quaternion components. Fourier approximation, followed by rational coefficient approximation, gives $q_j\to g$ in every $C^k$ by a diagonal choice. Eventually $|q_j|\ge 1/2$; normalization and base normalization preserve $C^k$ convergence because division is smooth on that set. Close unit-quaternion maps are homotopic through normalized straight interpolation; hence approximation does not omit a large component. $C^\infty$-Cauchy sequences converge in $C^\infty$ on the compact torus, and the closed $SU(2)$ and basepoint constraints persist. Thus the completed frame class is exactly $G_*$.

These frames are **passive coordinate descriptions**, not added physical g-dependent drive sequences. The canonical physical packet word, preparation/readout, clock and resource ledger stay fixed; only its coordinate representation changes. If a user of the branch actually executes frame-changing pulses instead, those are new physical calls with their own debit and are not declared cost-null. Extra copies of a descriptive frame certificate, if physically stored, likewise carry their actual storage/compilation costs. None of those optional costs is erased by a response-equivalence assertion.

### 8.3 Smooth transport and explicit descent

For a completed object $(\theta,g)$, define on every smooth path $\gamma:x\to y$

$$
U_{\theta,g}(\gamma)=g(y)^{-1} U_\theta(\gamma) g(x),
\tag{16}
$$

where $U_{\theta}$ solves $\dot{U}=-A_\theta(\gamma')U$, $U(0)=I$. This is exactly the transport of $A_\theta^g$. Identity, inverse and composition follow directly. Smooth dependence on every jointly smooth finite-dimensional path family follows from the finite matrix ODE and its parameter-derivative equations; repeated differentiation gives continuous smooth derivatives on compact parameter sets because $A_\theta^g$ is smooth. For based loops, $g(x_0)=I$ removes both endpoint factors, so every retained law is unchanged.

The bundle has a global trivialization. On any finite good cover use the restrictions of $A_\theta^g$ and transition functions identically $I$. They are smooth, obey the cocycle and overlap laws, and extend on local ambient charts (there is no boundary). Alternatively different smooth local frames give $g_{ji}$ with the exact transformation laws of 14.5.7h. Differentiating short paths reconstructs the actual coefficients $A_\theta^g$. Thus all hypotheses of the accepted smooth reconstruction are explicitly supplied; one need not invoke it to manufacture smoothness from a weak limit.

### 8.4 The inherited smooth topology, not only forward continuity

The parameterization $(\theta,g)\to B=A_\theta^g$ is a homeomorphism onto this declared family in its inherited compact-open $C^\infty$ connection topology, and is smooth along every smooth finite-dimensional family. Forward continuity follows from multiplication, inversion and differentiation of smooth matrices. For the inverse, coordinate-loop holonomies of $B$ equal (2), because its frame is based. Smooth ODE dependence and the local principal logarithm recover $\theta$ continuously, smoothly along parameter families.

To recover $g$ near a point $z$, fix a path from $x_{0}$ to $z$ and concatenate it with a smooth radial-path family from $z$ to $x$ in a coordinate ball. The transport relation at its endpoint is $U_B(\gamma_x)=g(x)^{-1}U_\theta(\gamma_x)$, hence

$$
g(x)=U_\theta(\gamma_x) U_B(\gamma_x)^{-1}.
$$

Smooth ODE dependence, including every derivative in $x$ and in finite-dimensional family parameters, proves $C^\infty$ continuity of this recovery on that ball. A finite cover suffices by compactness. The local recoveries agree on overlaps because each $B$ belongs to the specified gauge-saturated family and has the unique based $g$ already proved in Section 4. This gives the inverse topology assertion. The same formulas establish continuous/smooth arrow maps through $k=g^{-1}h$.

The twelve scalar response laws control only the $\theta$ quotient by (15). They do not control an arbitrary unregularized frame sequence $g_{n}$. Frame/presentation/arrow convergence is the separately specified $C^\infty$ convergence of Section 8.2; alternatively one first reconstructs the canonical $A_{\theta}$ from the response completion and then takes its full smooth gauge saturation. Both descriptions give the same family and topology. No inference of $C^\infty$ frame convergence from scalar heat or gauge responses is made.

For a compact chart and any fixed completed presentation, $\lVert U(C)-I\rVert\le\sup\lVert A_\theta^g\rVert\,\mathrm{length}(C)$. Consequently shrinking loops have holonomy tending to $I$. This excludes the 14.5.7i irregular witness on this family. On canonical presentations the bound is uniform in $\theta$, with $\sup\lVert A_\theta\rVert\le 2r_0$; on any $C^\infty$-convergent frame sequence the transformed coefficient bound is also uniform.

## 9. The response-faithful object/arrow functor

Let $S_{\mathrm{fin}}$ be the groupoid of finite canonical source parameters $\theta$ in $\Theta_{Q}$ with the permitted finite based frame presentations, identifying two duplicate finite frame expressions when they represent the same smooth frame. Its morphisms between $(\theta,g)$ and $(\theta,h)$ are their explicit passive based frame change $k=g^{-1}h$; there are no morphisms between different $\theta$. This is the gauge-description groupoid of the actual registered source. It does not identify a physical parameter change with a redundancy or pretend arbitrary physical measurement branches are invertible.

The physical implementation of (theta,g) is the canonical word of Section 5 written in the g frames. At finite graph vertices its represented edge matrices are

$$
g(y)^{-1}U_{\theta,e}g(x).
$$

These formulas do not request additional black-box access: they re-express the already specified matrix on transported fiber bases. Products telescope. **The frame action on the coherent packet is $C(g)$, not $I_C\otimes g$.** The four-dimensional packet representation is the direct sum of a trivial two-dimensional reference arm and the fundamental two-dimensional active arm. Thus the associated controlled-representation bundle is $\mathbb{C}^2_{\mathrm{trivial}}\oplus E_{\mathrm{fundamental}}$, with fiber-coordinate change $\operatorname{diag}(I_2,g(x))=C(g(x))$. The whole completed edge has represented matrix

$$
C(g(y))^{-1} C(U_e) C(g(x))=C(g(y)^{-1}U_e g(x)).
$$

An ordinary target-only basis change $I_{C}\otimes g$ would instead change the reference-arm block to $g(y)^{-1}g(x)$, which differs from the routed frame action used here. For the actual two-SWAP routing, conjugate the representation of every intermediate physical event by the corresponding complete packet-bank coordinate transformations. Individual represented SWAP matrices need not retain their old literal two-qubit entries. Their completed canonical packet-transfer map is identity between the two joint packet fibers, so its new representation is $C(g(y))^{-1}C(g(x))$; composing with the represented destination field pulse gives the displayed edge formula. This is coordinate conjugation of the identical finite physical word, not the execution of extra controlled-$g$ operations. The other empty packet ports have $C=0$ and hence lie in the fixed trivial arm. Initial preparation and terminal measurement are unchanged because $g(x_0)=I$; $D,R$ and the old source remain outside the frame action. Static unitary coordinate changes preserve all pulse matrix norms, the same laboratory event, its vertex ports and its physical ownership. Because $C(g)$ can entangle $C$ and $B$ at one vertex, a represented individual SWAP can occupy more coordinate tensor factors at its same two vertices than its literal two-qubit laboratory matrix. Coordinate tensor-factor support is not asserted invariant. This representation enlargement introduces no new laboratory gate, factor or pulse charge.

The coherently controlled path word at its specified base frame therefore has exactly (6), independently of this passive description. All resource labels retain their original physical owners. In particular source-description strings are not themselves included as a gauge observable that would destroy every coordinate redundancy.

Complete $\theta$ and frames as in Section 8, obtaining S. Define

$$
\begin{aligned}
&F:S \to \mathrm{Trans}_{\mathrm{SU}(2)}^\infty(T^4),\quad (\theta,g) \to U_{\theta,g}, \\
&F(k)_x=k(x)^{-1} \ \text{on the displayed fiber coordinates}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{17}
$$

Naturality is the exact identity

$$
U_{\theta,h}(\gamma)=k(y)^{-1}U_{\theta,g}(\gamma)k(x).
\tag{18}
$$

Composition and identities were checked in (3). Through 14.5.7h, this is the connection functor $(\theta,g)\to A_\theta^g$. It is full and faithful onto $C$: the slice argument in Section 4 shows that all target arrows are exactly the unique ones in (3). It is onto the declared gauge-saturated object family, hence essentially surjective there. The target is not the entire space of smooth connections.

The complete retained response functor Q evaluates (6) on all based smooth loops and the finite protocol products (7), keeping their actual binary outcome quantizer. At finite graph level the physical decoder is the terminal record extraction in Section 5, so Q composed with F agrees exactly with the finite source/graph word. Equations (12)–(14) give the refinement and continuum commuting squares. At the completed level every basic and finite adaptive response is exactly QF, by continuity and the finite-tree estimate.

Response faithfulness on objects is explicit:

all retained laws agree
 iff the twelve coordinate laws agree
 iff $\theta=\theta'$
 iff there is a unique based smooth connection isomorphism.           (19)

The forward implication uses the twelve admitted trials, the middle one (15), and the last the slice proof. The converse holds for every retained loop and protocol by (16)–(18). Arrows are retained by their natural transformations, not inferred from scalar laws alone. Finite classical protocol updates map to the identical outcome/policy tree over this fixed field object as specified in Section 5.2. This supplies both the actual gauge-description arrow map and the actual admitted update/response map without sending a noninvertible measurement into a fictitious connection isomorphism.

## 10. Explicit retained rank and gauge kernel

For the coordinate loops and $a=1,2,3$, equation (6) is

$$
\begin{aligned}
p_{i,a}(+)&=1/2+v_{i,a}/4, \\
v_i&=(\sin|a_i|/|a_i|)a_i.
\end{aligned}
\tag{20}
$$

Take one independent fresh trial in each of these twelve contexts, retaining all twelve binary outcomes. Since $p(1-p)$ is in $[3/16,1/4]$, its actual classical Fisher form is

$$
I_\theta(u,u)=\sum_{i,a} [ (Dv_i u_i)_a/4 ]^2 / [p_{i,a}(1-p_{i,a})].
$$

The derivative eigenvalues stated above yield the explicit uniform bounds

$$
\cos^2(1/4)\lVert u\rVert_F^2/4 \le I_\theta(u,u) \le \lVert u\rVert_F^2/3.
\tag{21}
$$

Thus the retained binary statistical quotient rank is twelve, with a bounded inverse on that quotient. This is the conditional experiment of Section 5.3, not an enlarged law that appends deterministic theta-valued audit coordinates. All probabilities are positive and the finite product law is differentiable; no support-changing Fisher limit is used. Independent repeat blocks multiply this form by the number of blocks, with the corresponding resource multiplier.

On the gauge-saturated family parameterized by $(\theta,g)$, responses depend only on $\theta$. A differentiable change $g\to g\exp(t\xi)$, $\xi(x_0)=0$, gives the gauge tangent $d_{A_\theta^g}\xi$. The response differential kills it. Conversely a tangent in this **declared family** consists of a coefficient variation plus such a gauge variation. By (21), a null response tangent has coefficient variation zero and is precisely a based gauge tangent. There is no nonzero based infinitesimal stabilizer: $d_A\xi=0$ and $\xi(x_0)=0$ imply $\xi=0$ by parallel transport. This proves the exact kernel/rank statement on the whole declared tangent family.

It is not a kernel theorem on all $\Omega^1(T^4,\mathfrak{su}(2))$, whose transverse directions are not this physical parameter branch. It is also not the unframed zero-connection assertion excluded by 14.5.7j. Our base frame and the $i\sigma_{a}$ terminal contexts make constant conjugations with nonidentity base value physically distinguishable. The retained gauge group has been changed explicitly to the based one, exactly as permitted by 14.5.7g's framed variant. Even at $\theta=0$ the twelve framed first derivatives are nonzero; the theorem requiring invariance under all constant gauge transformations does not apply.

The source-to-field gauge-subspace map is $(\theta,g)\to A_\theta^g$ with the just-computed differential. It supplies a nondegenerate finite response kernel on its quotient, so it is legitimate to form its inverse there. Identifying this Fisher form with the separate unframed/effective-action/QFI or unit Predictive-Ward kernel would still require that consumer's own common-sector and normalization map. No such cross-owner equality is smuggled into (21).

<a id="proposition-1457e-exact-gibbs-ledger-of-stabilizer-and-marked-vacua"></a>**Proposition 14.5.7e (Exact Gibbs Ledger of Stabilizer and Marked Vacua).** Let $H_S$ and $H_{S,\zeta}$ be the Hamiltonians (14.5.7a.1) and (14.5.7a.2) of Theorem 14.5.7a, let $\beta>0$ be the inverse temperature in units of their unit gap, and put
$$
p_\beta=\frac{e^{-\beta}}{1+e^{-\beta}}.
\tag{14.5.7e.1}
$$
For $\mathbf s\in\{0,1\}^r$, let $\Pi_{\mathbf s}=\prod_j\bigl(I+(-1)^{s_j}S_j\bigr)/2$ be the syndrome projector. Then:

1. $\operatorname{tr}e^{-\beta H_S}=2^{n-r}(1+e^{-\beta})^r$, and the Gibbs state is
$$
\rho_\beta=\sum_{\mathbf s\in\{0,1\}^r}\prod_{j=1}^rp_\beta^{s_j}(1-p_\beta)^{1-s_j}\,\frac{\Pi_{\mathbf s}}{2^{n-r}}.
\tag{14.5.7e.2}
$$
Its syndrome bits are independent with $P(s_j=1)=p_\beta$, it is maximally mixed on every syndrome sector, and therefore every Hermitian Pauli logical operator $L$ commuting with all $S_j$ and outside $\pm$ the stabilizer group has $\operatorname{tr}(\rho_\beta L)=0$. The code-space weight is $(1+e^{-\beta})^{-r}\le e^{-rp_\beta}$.

2. For $H_{S,\zeta}$, the $n$ commuting Pauli operators $S_1,\ldots,S_r,\zeta_1\overline Z_1,\ldots,\zeta_{n-r}\overline Z_{n-r}$ are independent, $\operatorname{tr}e^{-\beta H_{S,\zeta}}=(1+e^{-\beta})^n$, and under the Gibbs state $\rho_\beta^{\zeta}$ their $\pm1$ values are independent, each equal to $-1$ with probability $p_\beta$. The marked ground state has Gibbs weight $(1+e^{-\beta})^{-n}$, and every marked logical operator has thermal polarization
$$
\operatorname{tr}\bigl(\rho_\beta^{\zeta}\,\zeta_a\overline Z_a\bigr)=\tanh(\beta/2),
\qquad
a=1,\ldots,n-r,
\tag{14.5.7e.3}
$$
independently of $n$ and $r$.

3. For $0<\varepsilon<1$ and $L_\varepsilon=-\ln(1-\varepsilon)$, the code-space weight is at least $1-\varepsilon$ exactly when $\beta\ge\beta_r(\varepsilon):=-\ln\bigl(e^{L_\varepsilon/r}-1\bigr)$, and
$$
\ln\frac r{L_\varepsilon}-\frac{L_\varepsilon}r
\le\beta_r(\varepsilon)
\le\ln\frac r{L_\varepsilon}.
\tag{14.5.7e.4}
$$
Along any family with $r\to\infty$ at fixed $\beta$, the vacuum occupation of $H_S$ and the marked ground-state occupation of $H_{S,\zeta}$ tend to zero.

*Proof.* The terms of $H_S$ commute, so $e^{-\beta H_S}=\prod_j\bigl[(I+S_j)/2+e^{-\beta}(I-S_j)/2\bigr]=\sum_{\mathbf s}e^{-\beta|\mathbf s|}\Pi_{\mathbf s}$. Because the generators are independent and $-I$ is absent, nondegeneracy of the symplectic commutation form supplies, for each $\mathbf s$, a Pauli operator anticommuting exactly with the $S_j$ having $s_j=1$; conjugation by it maps $\Pi_{\mathbf 0}$ onto $\Pi_{\mathbf s}$, so every $\Pi_{\mathbf s}$ has the rank $2^{n-r}$ of the code space in Theorem 14.5.7a. Taking the trace gives $2^{n-r}\sum_{\mathbf s}e^{-\beta|\mathbf s|}=2^{n-r}(1+e^{-\beta})^r$, and dividing gives (14.5.7e.2), a product of Bernoulli weights with parameter $p_\beta$. A Pauli logical operator $L$ outside $\pm$ the stabilizer group anticommutes with some Pauli operator $M$ commuting with every $S_j$, because the symplectic double complement of the span of the stabilizer generators is that span; hence $M\Pi_{\mathbf s}M^*=\Pi_{\mathbf s}$ and $\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_{\mathbf s}L)=-\operatorname{tr}(\Pi_{\mathbf s}L)=0$. The inequality $\ln(1+x)\ge x/(1+x)$ gives $(1+e^{-\beta})^{-r}\le e^{-rp_\beta}$. Item 2 applies the same computation to the $n$ operators $S_1,\ldots,S_r,\zeta_1\overline Z_1,\ldots,\zeta_{n-r}\overline Z_{n-r}$, which Theorem 14.5.7a supplies as an independent commuting Pauli family; sign changes of generators preserve independence and the absence of $-I$, and the sectors have rank one; the factor for $\zeta_a\overline Z_a$ gives $(1-p_\beta)-p_\beta=\tanh(\beta/2)$. For item 3, $(1+e^{-\beta})^{-r}\ge1-\varepsilon$ is equivalent to $\ln(1+e^{-\beta})\le L_\varepsilon/r$, hence to $e^{-\beta}\le e^{L_\varepsilon/r}-1$. The bounds $y\le e^y-1\le ye^y$ at $y=L_\varepsilon/r$ give (14.5.7e.4), and item 1's exponential bound gives the limit. ∎

Proposition 14.5.7e settles the equilibrium ledger of every stabilizer vacuum of Theorem 14.5.7a. The literal vacuum carries Gibbs weight at least $1-\varepsilon$ exactly for $\beta\ge\beta_r(\varepsilon)$, which grows like $\ln r$ by (14.5.7e.4); the unmarked Gibbs state carries no logical marking, and a marking term held in the Hamiltonian retains the $n$-independent polarization (14.5.7e.3). Theorem 14.5.7m supplies local marking terms, stability under extensive perturbations and an all-time bound for the held mark under local collisions with baths prepared in its Gibbs state. Theorem 14.5.7m.11 proves a finite-time storage bound under collisions with baths independent of the stored word.

<a id="theorem-1457m"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457m-local-marked-golay-leech-vacuum-thermal-dynamics-and-extensive-stability"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7m (Local Marked Golay-Leech Vacuum, Thermal Dynamics and Extensive Stability).** Fix the marked extended binary Golay code $C\subset\mathbb F_2^{24}$, the marked Construction-B Leech lattice $\Lambda_C$ of Theorem R.4.9 with the rotor of Theorem R.4.5b, a marked information word $w\in\mathbb F_2^{12}$, marking energies $e_1,\ldots,e_{24}>0$ and an inverse temperature $\beta>0$. For $N\ge1$ laboratory cells of common diameter $a_0$, each holding 24 two-level registers and one Leech rotor, let $H_w$ be the cell Hamiltonian of Section 14.5.7m.3, with $Z_0(\beta)=\operatorname{Tr}e^{-\beta H_w}$, $p_0=1-1/Z_0(\beta)$ and gap $g=\min(\min_ae_a,\,8\pi^2\hbar^2/I_{\mathrm{rot}})$. Let each cell $x$ carry a static bounded self-adjoint perturbation $V_x$ with $\lVert V_x\rVert\le\epsilon<g/2$, put $d=\epsilon/(g-\epsilon)$, and let $\sigma>0$ be the collision duration of Section 14.5.7m.6, with $b=\epsilon\sigma/\hbar$. Then:

1. $H_w$ is positive and self-adjoint with compact resolvent; its unique ground ray is $\Psi_w=|c_w\rangle\otimes e_{p(c_w)}$, its gap is $g$, and its code checks and marking parities are products of register $Z$ operators supported in one cell. Coherent code input enters the joint register/rotor carrier through the isometry $V_C$ of Section 14.5.7m.3, which a finite-action pulse prepares exactly, and the laboratory momentum of $\Psi_w$ is $2\pi\hbar\,p(c_w)$.
2. Each $H_w+V_x$ has a unique ground projection $P_x$, gap at least $g-2\epsilon$ and $\lVert P_x-P_w\rVert\le d$, and $\lVert(I-P_w)e^{-i(H_w+V_x)t/\hbar}P_w\rVert^2\le4d^2$ for every real $t$. The $N$-cell Hamiltonian $\sum_x(H_w+V_x)$ has gap at least $g-2\epsilon$ although $\lVert\sum_xV_x\rVert$ can equal $N\epsilon$, and the perturbation of Section 14.5.7m.4 that dresses $\lambda X_a$, $0<|\lambda|\le\epsilon$, has a nonzero tunneling probability.
3. The Gibbs state $\tau_x$ of $H_w+V_x$ satisfies $1-\operatorname{Tr}(P_w\tau_x)\le e^{2\beta\epsilon}\bigl(Z_0(\beta)-1\bigr)+d^2$.
4. Under the fixed-bath collision dynamics of Sections 14.5.7m.6–14.5.7m.7, every cell prepared in $P_w$ has, at every physical time, marked-ray failure probability $1-\operatorname{Tr}(P_w\rho_x(t))\le q_{\mathrm{all}}:=2(p_0+4d^2+b)$.
5. If $q_{\mathrm{all}}<\tfrac12$, the majority decoder of Section 14.5.7m.8 returns $w$ at any chosen observation time with probability at least $1-\exp[-2N(\tfrac12-q_{\mathrm{all}})^2]$. This regime is nonempty: $\epsilon\le g/10$, $p_0\le1/100$ and $b\le1/100$ give $q_{\mathrm{all}}<0.139$.
6. Energy-graded finite implementations reproduce items 1–5 with the explicit truncation errors of Section 14.5.7m.10.

*Proof.* Sections 14.5.7m.1–14.5.7m.10 construct the source and prove items 1–6.

##### 14.5.7m.1 Source

This construction extends the finite stabilizer vacuum and equilibrium results of Theorem 14.5.7a and Proposition 14.5.7e to a specified spatially local, supplied-resource physical-model family. The code is the actual marked extended binary Golay code $C$ in $\mathbb{F}_2^{24}$; the lattice is exactly the marked Construction-B Leech lattice $\Lambda_{C}$ of Theorem R.4.9. The rotor, its physical inertia, full Fourier carrier and marked covering are those of Theorem R.4.5b. Theorem R.4.5b also supplies 24 scalar-potential probes. The source is enlarged by bounded local commissioning swaps, full-cell collision swaps, finite spectral-projector pointer couplings, independent flags and Gibbs-cell preparation services. Their Hamiltonians, domains, costs and finite approximants are specified below as additional constitutive source data.

All cells have the same fixed bounded physical diameter $a_{0}$, independent of their number. Place their centers on a fixed cubic grid of spacing $4a_0$ in three-dimensional laboratory space. A cell contains 24 two-level registers, the 24 internal configuration coordinates of one Leech rotor, and its local interaction terminals. Put each register and terminal at a nominated point within distance $a_0/4$ of its center. This metric and these positions are fixed before the Hamiltonian, encoder or change of representation. A term supported on one cell consequently has diameter at most $a_{0}$, even if it acts on all 24 registers and all internal rotor coordinates. The 24-dimensional configuration torus is internal to the cell.

The family excludes interactions between different memory cells. It includes arbitrary bounded noncommuting perturbations of each complete memory cell with a fixed norm bound, arbitrary positive temperature low enough to satisfy the displayed error inequality, actual local thermal collisions with independently fixed unperturbed Gibbs bath cells, and every number of cells. Thus the total perturbation norm can grow linearly with the number of cells.

##### 14.5.7m.2 The marked Golay code and a literal local marking Hamiltonian

Choose an ordered generator basis $c_1,\dots,c_{12}$ of the already marked code $C$. Extend those 12 columns to an invertible binary matrix $B$ of size 24 by adjoining 12 specified binary columns. Fix this extension once. The unitary $U_{B}$ on 24 qubits is the classical reversible encoder

$$
U_B |z\rangle = |Bz\rangle,\qquad z \in \mathbb{F}_2^{24}.
$$

Gaussian elimination gives a finite CNOT implementation and its exact inverse. For a marked information word $w\in\mathbb{F}_2^{12}$, let $z_w=(w,0,\dots,0)$ and $c_w=Bz_w\in C$. For $a=1,\dots,24$ put $s_a=(-1)^{(z_w)_a}$ and choose independently calibrated positive energies $e_{a}$. Define

$$
\begin{aligned}
h_w &= \sum_a e_a (I-s_a Z_a)/2, \\
H_{G,w} &= U_B h_w U_B^{*}.
\end{aligned}
$$

Its unique ground is $|c_w\rangle$, its gap is $g_s=\min_a e_a$, and its complete eigenbasis is $U_B|z\rangle$. Its partition function is the exact product

$$
Z_s(\beta)=\prod_a (1+\exp(-\beta e_a)).
$$

Here $\beta$ has units inverse energy. The last 12 operators $U_BZ_aU_B^*$ are the actual code parity checks because their simultaneous $+1$ eigenspace is $\operatorname{span}\{|c\rangle:c\in C\}$. The first 12 are independent logical marking parities. Every one is an explicit product of physical $Z$ registers obtained from a row of $B^{-1}$. They act only inside the predeclared cell and therefore have the fixed physical range bound $a_{0}$. Their body count is at most 24.

For zero marking energies the ground space is the 4096-dimensional classical code span. Positive marking energies select the stated codeword by explicit parity fields. These energies and the chosen w are constitutive source inputs.

A complete read of the physical computational registers reports $b\in\mathbb{F}_2^{24}$. A syndrome measurement reports the last 12 bits of $B^{-1}b$. A codeword's information word is the first 12 bits when that syndrome is zero. The classical encoder, syndrome, inverse and minimum-distance properties are the actual selected Golay maps. All inputs and all noncode error words remain in the 24-qubit carrier.

##### 14.5.7m.3 The actual code-to-Leech carrier map

Use the fixed lattice of the original Construction-B theorem:

$$
\begin{aligned}
K_C&=\{x \in \mathbb{Z}^{24} : x \bmod 2 \in C,\ \sum_i x_i =0 \bmod 4\}, \\
L_B&=K_C/\sqrt{2}, \\
\nu&=(-3,1,\ldots,1)/\sqrt{8}, \\
\Lambda_C&=L_B \cup (\nu+L_B).
\end{aligned}
$$

It is the accepted even unimodular rank-24 lattice with minimum squared norm four. For every codeword c, regarded as its binary integer vector,

$$
p(c)=c/\sqrt{2} \text{ belongs to } L_B \subset \Lambda_C.
$$

Indeed $c\bmod 2=c$ and its weight is divisible by four. This map is injective, and

$$
|p(c)-p(c')|^{2}=\operatorname{wt}(c+c')/2 \ge 4
$$

for distinct codewords; subtraction of their integer representatives has squared norm exactly their Hamming distance. Thus p is a marked code-coordinate embedding into $L_{B}$.

Extend $p$ to an injective table on all $b\in\mathbb{F}_2^{24}$ with values in $\Lambda_{C}$. A total explicit rule is: use $p(c)$ on $C$; enumerate noncode words in binary order, fix a nonzero nominated lattice vector $l$, and assign successively the smallest positive multiples $kl$ not already used and not equal to a codeword image. Each step terminates, and there are only $2^{24}$ entries. This finite table is retained and charged.

Let $T=\mathbb{R}^{24}/\Lambda_C$, with normalized Haar measure, and $H_R=L^2(T)$. Physical action $\hbar>0$ and generalized inertia $I_{\mathrm{rot}}>0$ are independently calibrated as in R.4.5b. Set

$$
\begin{aligned}
P_j&=-i \hbar\, \partial_j, \\
H_{R_0}&=P^{2}/(2 I_{\mathrm{rot}}), \\
A&=2 \pi^{2} \hbar^{2}/I_{\mathrm{rot}}.
\end{aligned}
$$

For $r\in\Lambda_C^*=\Lambda_C$, the normalized character $e_r(q)=\exp(2\pi i\,r\cdot q)$ has energy $A|r|^{2}$. The rotor has unique ground $e_{0}=1$, gap $g_r=4A$ and first-shell multiplicity 196560. Its full spectrum is retained.

Multiplication $M_{p}$ by $e_p(q)$ is unitary. Define on the whole spin/rotor carrier

$$
\begin{aligned}
U_p&=\sum_b |b\rangle\langle b| \otimes M_{p(b)}, \\
T_B&=U_p(U_B \otimes I).
\end{aligned}
$$

The memory Hamiltonian is the closed positive operator

$$
H_w=T_B(h_w \otimes I + I \otimes H_{R_0})T_B^{*}.
$$

Because $H_{G,w}$ is computationally diagonal, this has the literal physical form

$$
H_w=H_{G,w} + [P-2 \pi \hbar\, p(b)]^{2}/(2 I_{\mathrm{rot}}),
$$

where $p(b)$ is the diagonal finite spin-controlled vector operator. It includes the complete square, its scalar contact term and the finite, possibly large, control coefficients. The domain is $T_B(\mathbb{C}^{2^{24}}\otimes H^2(T))$; the quadratic-form domain is its $H^1$ counterpart. Since the spin table is finite, these are also the usual direct-sum Sobolev domains. The unitary equivalence establishes self-adjointness, positivity, compact resolvent, all spectral multiplicities and the exact product partition function.

The unique physical marked ground is

$$
\begin{aligned}
\Psi_w&=|c_w\rangle \otimes e_{p(c_w)}, \\
P_w&=|\Psi_w\rangle\langle\Psi_w|, \\
g&=\min(g_s,g_r)>0.
\end{aligned}
$$

The map from coherent code input is the isometry

$$
V_C: \sum_{c \in C} \alpha_c |c\rangle \to \sum_c \alpha_c |c\rangle \otimes e_{p(c)}.
$$

This isometry has an actual finite-energy preparation. First encode the 12 information registers and 12 supplied zero syndrome registers with $U_{B}$. Supply the rotor $e_{0}$. For each of the finitely many spin words $b$, apply a controlled swap between $e_{0}$ and $e_{p(b)}$, acting as the identity on their orthogonal complement; when $p(b)=0$ use the identity. The direct sum of these swaps is a bounded self-adjoint involution. A positive pulse proportional to $I$ minus that involution realizes it exactly, with finite action and with reached rotor momenta contained in the finite set $\{0\}\cup\{p(b)\}$. The maximal reached kinetic energy is finite and explicitly bounded by $A\max_b|p(b)|^{2}$; all table, control and preparation costs are retained. This gate agrees with $U_{p}$ on the prepared $e_{0}$ input, which is exactly what $V_{C}$ requires. On the full carrier, $T_{B}$ is the exact mathematical identification of the specified Hamiltonians and responses; its physical implementation on prepared inputs is the finite pulse above.

The exact preparation is a commissioning stage before the static $V_{x}$ exposure used in the stability experiment, carried out with $H_{w}$ switched on. Write $S_{p}$ for the preceding controlled swap, choose a positive duration $\tau_{p}$ and $k(t)\ge 0$ with $\int k=\pi/2$, and use the interaction-picture drive

$$
K_p(t)=\exp(-i H_w t/\hbar)\, [\hbar k(t)(I-S_p)]\, \exp(i H_w t/\hbar).
$$

It is positive, bounded, has the same finite action and physical cell support, and its nontrivial matrix elements remain inside the finite reached Fourier span. The exact total propagator generated by $H_w+K_p(t)$ is $\exp(-iH_{w}t/\hbar)$ times the propagator of $\hbar k(t)(I-S_p)$. At $\tau_{p}$ it therefore equals $\exp(-iH_w\tau_p/\hbar)S_p$. On $|b\rangle\otimes e_{0}$ the output is $\exp[-iE_G(b)\tau_p/\hbar]|b\rangle\otimes e_{p(b)}$, where $E_G(b)$ is the actual diagonal eigenvalue of $H_{G,w}$. These are known phases.

For a further positive duration $\tau_{f}$, choose integers $n_{b}$ large enough that

$$
L_b=[2 \pi n_b \hbar-E_G(b)(\tau_p+\tau_f)]/\tau_f \ge 0,
$$

and apply the positive diagonal spin control $\sum_b L_b|b\rangle\langle b|$ alongside $H_{w}$. It commutes with $H_{w}$. At the end the total $b$-dependent phase is exactly $\exp(-2\pi i\,n_b)=1$. The finite spin encoder $U_{B}$ can likewise be realized in its finite reached Fourier subspace with an explicit interaction-picture endpoint drive and known phase correction. All these controller matrices, timing, switching work, peak energies, ready-state purity and record resources are part of commissioning. If unknown $V_{x}$ is instead present during a commissioning interval $T_{\mathrm{prep}}$, the same bounded-perturbation Duhamel argument gives half trace-norm preparation error at most $\epsilon T_{\mathrm{prep}}/\hbar$; it must be added to the stated readout error budget. Exact preparation holds on the commissioning-before-exposure branch.

This retains arbitrary coherent superpositions and arbitrary untouched references in the joint spin/rotor carrier. Tracing out the rotor generally dephases the spin code, so the response-faithful map is $V_{C}$ on the joint carrier.

The lattice is visible to an independently fixed laboratory momentum measurement. Fix P as the laboratory canonical momentum before $U_{p}$. Then

$$
U_p^{*} P U_p=P+2 \pi \hbar\, p(b).
$$

The selected codeword produces actual momentum $2\pi\hbar\,p(c_w)$ under this instrument, while its covariant kinetic momentum and kinetic energy are zero. These are distinct observables; the laboratory momentum instrument detects the shift, which a co-transported covariant momentum would hide.

The marked covering $W$ of R.4.5b, from $T$ to the base torus $\mathbb{R}^{24}/L_0^T$, remains the same deck-invariant pullback. Every character $e_{p}$ here has $p\in\Lambda_{C}\subset(L_0^T)^*$, hence is deck invariant. Therefore $WM_p=M_{p,\mathrm{lift}}W$, $WH_{R0}=H_{R0,\mathrm{base}}W$ and $(I\otimes W)U_p=U_{p,\mathrm{lift}}(I\otimes W)$. The full spin/rotor carrier, momentum spectral projectors, bounded potential probes, encoder, and all declared operations intertwine on that invariant sector. All other base-torus deck sectors lie outside this geometrically fixed source.

##### 14.5.7m.4 Local extensive perturbations and actual tunneling

In cell x choose a specified bounded self-adjoint operator $V_{x}$ on its full spin/rotor carrier, with

$$
\lVert V_x\rVert\le\epsilon<g/2.
$$

It can mix codewords, syndromes, rotor modes and their coherences. It need not commute with $H_{w}$ or $P_{w}$, need not be diagonal, and need not be a function of the code syndrome. Its support is the actual fixed cell. Put $H_x=H_w+V_x$. Add a declared positive scalar reserve if desired for nonnegative total physical energy; this reserve changes none of the following gaps, states or transition probabilities and is charged rather than subtracted.

Min-max implies a unique perturbed ground projection $P_{x}$, lowest energy $E_{x}$ in [-epsilon,epsilon], and gap at least

$$
g'=g-2\epsilon>0.
$$

For its normalized ground vector $\psi_{x}$, project the eigenvalue equation onto $Q_w=I-P_w$. The restriction of $H_{w}$ to $Q_{w}$ is at least $g$, and $|E_x|\le\epsilon$. Hence

$$
\begin{aligned}
\lVert Q_w \psi_x\rVert &\le d := \epsilon/(g-\epsilon), \\
\lVert P_x-P_w\rVert &\le d.
\end{aligned}
$$

For two rank-one projections the norm difference equals the sine of the angle, which gives the second statement exactly. Under $U_x(t)=\exp(-iH_xt/\hbar)$, $P_{x}$ commutes with $U_{x}$. Consequently

$$
\begin{aligned}
\lVert Q_w U_x(t)P_w\rVert \\
&\le \lVert Q_w P_x U_x(t)P_w\rVert \\
&\quad+\lVert Q_w(I-P_x)U_x(t)P_w\rVert \\
&\le d+d=2d,
\end{aligned}
$$

and the probability of leaving the original marked ray under this actual perturbed dynamics is at most $4d^{2}$, uniformly for all real physical times. This is a dynamical tunneling bound.

A nonzero explicit tunneling member is obtained in normal coordinates by

$$
V_x=T_B[\lambda X_a \otimes I]T_B^{*},\quad 0<|\lambda|\le\epsilon,
$$

for one selected register a, all other modes unchanged. Starting its marked Z eigenstate, the exact transition probability of that register is

$$
\begin{aligned}
&[4 \lambda^{2}/(e_a^{2}+4 \lambda^{2})] \\
&\quad\sin^{2}(\sqrt{e_a^{2}+4 \lambda^{2}}\, t/(2 \hbar)).
\end{aligned}
$$

The dressing turns the spin flip into the actual finite spin transition and its rotor character kick. It has bounded cell support. More generally every bounded $V_{x}$ in the class has the proved uniform upper bound, whether or not a two-level formula is available.

For N cells, let

$$
\begin{aligned}
H_N&=\sum_{x=1}^N H_x, \\
V_N&=\sum_x V_x.
\end{aligned}
$$

The terms act on different factors. Thus the ground is the product of the perturbed ground vectors, the excitation gap is at least $g'$, and $\lVert V_N\rVert\le N\epsilon$. For aligned positive perturbations equality can be of order $N$. The extensive result follows from the tensor decomposition and the per-cell bounds; the global criterion $\lVert V_N\rVert<g/2$ of Theorem 14.5.7a is not used.

##### 14.5.7m.5 Full positive-temperature Gibbs states and rotor tails

For $\beta>0$ define the complete cell Gibbs state

$$
\tau_x=\exp(-\beta H_x)/Z_x(\beta).
$$

It is trace class. To see this and obtain a computable thermal error bound, first write

$$
\begin{aligned}
Z_0(\beta)&=Z_s(\beta)\, Z_R(\beta), \\
Z_R(\beta)&=\sum_{r \in \Lambda_C} \exp(-\beta A |r|^{2}).
\end{aligned}
$$

The lattice is discrete and has minimum separation two. Balls of radius one around its points have disjoint interiors. The number of points with $|r|\le R$ is therefore at most $(R+1)^{24}$, by comparing their balls with the radius-$(R+1)$ ball in $\mathbb{R}^{24}$. In particular an explicit convergent upper bound is

$$
\begin{aligned}
Z_R(\beta)-1 \\
&\le \sum_{n=2}^\infty (n+2)^{24} \exp(-\beta A n^{2}).
\end{aligned}
$$

The $n=2$ shell includes the minimal vectors. Tighter numerical certificates enumerate a finite marked lattice ball exactly and bound the remaining scalar Gaussian series. The displayed bound tends to zero as $\beta$ tends to infinity.

Enumerate the unperturbed eigenvalues, with multiplicity, as $0=E_0^0<E_1^0\le\dots$ and those of $H_x$ as $E_0^x<E_1^x\le\dots$. Bounded perturbation min-max gives $E_j^x\ge E_j^0-\epsilon$ and $E_0^x\le\epsilon$. Hence the thermal excited weight relative to $P_{x}$ satisfies

$$
\begin{aligned}
p_x:=1-\operatorname{Tr}(P_x \tau_x) \\
&\le \sum_{j\ge 1} \exp[-\beta(E_j^x-E_0^x)] \\
&\le \exp(2 \beta \epsilon)\, [Z_0(\beta)-1] =: p_{*}.
\end{aligned}
$$

The first bound deliberately omits the positive partition-function denominator. It remains a valid upper bound; use $\min(1,p_*)$ if needed. Since $\epsilon<g/2$, the upper bound tends to zero for sufficiently low but nonzero temperature. This follows either from the exact spectrum or from the explicit product and Gaussian-series bound; polynomial lattice growth cannot overwhelm $\exp[-\beta(g-2\epsilon)]$.

The marked-ray thermal error obeys

$$
1-\operatorname{Tr}(P_w \tau_x) \le p_x+d^{2} \le p_{*}+d^{2}.
$$

Indeed $\operatorname{Tr}(P_wP_x)\ge 1-d^{2}$, so the contribution of the Gibbs ground weight alone is at least $(1-p_x)(1-d^{2})$. Excited contributions are nonnegative. No commutation of $P_{w}$ with $H_{x}$ is assumed.

A definite nonempty parameter regime is any $\epsilon$ with $d<1/\sqrt{8}$, followed by any finite $\beta$ satisfying $p_*+d^{2}<1/2$. The error constant

$$
q=\max(4d^{2},p_{*}+d^{2})
$$

then satisfies $q<1/2$. For example $\epsilon\le g/10$ gives $d\le 1/9$, $4d^{2}\le 4/81$; choose finite $\beta$ so that $p_*\le 1/10$. Then $q\le 1/10+1/81<0.113$. These are sufficient thresholds derived from the stated source energies.

##### 14.5.7m.6 A literal fixed-bath local thermal collision

The main source uses a fixed unperturbed bath, independent of $V_{x}$. The bounded perturbation may be unknown to the controller; it is static during the experiment and acts only on the memory cell. Every fresh bath cell has Hamiltonian $H_{w}$ and the independently prepared Gibbs state

$$
\begin{aligned}
\tau_0&=\exp(-\beta H_w)/Z_0(\beta), \\
p_0&=1-\operatorname{Tr}(P_w \tau_0)=1-1/Z_0(\beta).
\end{aligned}
$$

The mark $w$ and source energies are known registered inputs, whereas $V_{x}$ is not used to choose the bath, its pulse or its preparation. Every bath cell has the complete spin/rotor spectrum. Its preparation is a constitutive thermal resource at temperature $(k_B\beta)^{-1}$, with complete energy, entropy, source preparation and transport invoice. It is delivered to the fixed local terminal of its memory cell and, after one use, moved into retained archive stock without reset or reuse.

Let S exchange the complete memory and bath carriers. It is a bounded self-adjoint involution, and

$$
[S,H_w \otimes I+I \otimes H_w]=0.
$$

The reference swap conserves their total unperturbed internal energy. It exchanges arbitrary states and reference correlations. For an arbitrary memory/reference state $\rho_{\mathrm{MR}}$,

$$
\operatorname{Tr}_B[S(\rho_{MR} \otimes \tau_0)S]=\tau_0 \otimes \operatorname{Tr}_M \rho_{MR}.
$$

The previous memory/reference correlations remain in the outgoing bath/reference state. The complete output is the unitary state before tracing; only the specified memory observable uses the reduced formula.

Use a fresh independent classical flag $F$ with $\Pr(F=1)=\eta$, $0<\eta<1$. Keep its outcome and its positively charged storage. At the docked collision, on $F=1$ apply for positive physical duration $\sigma$ the actual Hamiltonian

$$
\begin{aligned}
H_{\mathrm{actual}}(t)&=(H_w+V_x)_M+(H_w)_B+\hbar k(t)(I-S), \\
&\int_0^\sigma k(t)\,dt=\pi/2,\quad k(t)\ge 0.
\end{aligned}
$$

The collision term is positive, bounded, local to the memory/bath pair and has finite norm action $\pi\hbar$. On $F=0$ the same source time elapses with no collision pulse; memory evolves under $H_w+V_x$ and bath under $H_{w}$. The flag is degenerate in internal energy but its preparation, positive storage, switch and record costs are retained. In the reference $V_{x}=0$ branch, the endpoint is the full swap times the free joint evolution, up to a scalar phase; since $\tau_{0}$ commutes with $H_{w}$, the reduced output is exactly $\tau_{0}$ for every memory input. This is a finite-duration thermal collision.

The full actual pulse need not conserve the sum of unperturbed energies when $V_{x}$ is nonzero. Bounded-perturbation Duhamel gives the uniform propagator estimate

$$
\lVert U_{\mathrm{actual}}-U_{\mathrm{reference}}\rVert \le b:=\epsilon \sigma/\hbar.
$$

It is valid on the full unbounded rotor carrier because the difference of the self-adjoint Hamiltonians is the bounded operator $V_{x}$ tensor I. Consequently the half trace-norm distance of the flag-one reduced output from the reference reset is at most b for every input and every retained reference. The previous memory/reference correlations and all records are still kept in the outgoing complete dilation.

All memory/bath pulse supports have physical diameter at most $2a_0$. A finite horizon uses finite stock, staged on separately declared routes. Those routes, controllers and clocks are not couplings between different memory cells. Their time, energy and storage are charged.

##### 14.5.7m.7 Exact physical-time comparison, thermal noise and memory bound

One cycle consists of a free memory interval $\Delta_{t}>0$ and the complete collision pulse of duration $\sigma>0$. The actual physical duration is $T_c=\Delta_t+\sigma$; $H_w+V_x$ remains active throughout both. Define $U=\exp[-i(H_w+V_x)T_c/\hbar]$. The ideal comparison channel is

$$
C_x(\rho)=(1-\eta)\, U \rho U^{*} + \eta\, \tau_0 \operatorname{Tr}(\rho).
$$

Let $E_{x}$ be the actual finite-cycle channel just constructed. The flag-zero branch agrees exactly with $C_{x}$, and the flag-one comparison is bounded by Duhamel, regardless of the preceding free interval. Therefore, in half diamond norm,

$$
\lVert E_x-C_x\rVert_\diamond/2 \le \eta b.
$$

For equal-trace Hermitian inputs, the half trace-norm contraction coefficient of $C_{x}$ is at most $1-\eta$. The error $d_{k}$ between $E_x^k(P_w)$ and $C_x^k(P_w)$ consequently satisfies

$$
\begin{aligned}
d_{k+1}&\le\eta b+(1-\eta)d_k, \\
d_k&\le b[1-(1-\eta)^k]\le b.
\end{aligned}
$$

This bound is uniform in the number of cycles.

Put $q_0=p_0+4d^{2}$, with $d=\epsilon/(g-\epsilon)$. Since $\tau_0=(1-p_0)P_w+p_0\tau_{\mathrm{exc}}$ for a positive state $\tau_{\mathrm{exc}}$, the uniform tunneling estimate of Section 14.5.7m.4 gives

$$
1-\operatorname{Tr}[P_w U_x(t) \tau_0 U_x(t)^{*}] \le q_0
$$

for every physical $t$. The same estimate for $U_x(t)P_w$ is at most $4d^{2}$. Iteration of $C_{x}$ gives a positive renewal mixture of $U^kP_wU^{-k}$ and $U^j\tau_0U^{-j}$, $j\ge 0$. The argument uses no stationarity of $\tau_{0}$ for $H_w+V_x$. Every mixture term has failure at most $q_{0}$, so the actual cycle-end failure obeys

$$
1-\operatorname{Tr}[P_w E_x^k(P_w)] \le q_{\mathrm{end}}:=q_0+b.
$$

Free intervals after an endpoint retain the bound: all ideal renewal terms are further evolved by $H_w+V_x$, and trace distance does not increase.

There is also a bound during the nonzero-duration collision. For the reference partial-SWAP, purify the incoming memory/archive state and the independent bath. Apart from free $H_{w}$ factors, which preserve $P_{w}$, the unitary is $\cos(\theta)I+i\sin(\theta)S$ times a scalar phase. If $q_{\mathrm{in}}$ is the incoming marked-ray failure and $p_{0}$ the fresh bath failure, the triangle inequality gives

$$
\begin{aligned}
\lVert(I-P_w)_M [\cos(\theta) I+i \sin(\theta) S]|\Psi\rangle\rVert \\
&\le |\cos(\theta)| \sqrt{q_{\mathrm{in}}}+|\sin(\theta)| \sqrt{p_0},
\end{aligned}
$$

hence failure at most $q_{\mathrm{in}}+p_0$. The incoming comparison error is at most $b$, and the partial actual pulse differs from the corresponding reference pulse by at most $\epsilon\sigma/\hbar=b$. Combining these estimates with $q_{\mathrm{in}}\le q_0+b$ gives the conservative all-physical-time bound

$$
1-\operatorname{Tr}[P_w \rho_x(t)] \le q_{\mathrm{all}}:=2(q_0+b).
$$

This applies also to flag-zero pulses, which are free intervals. It is a direct bound on the finite pulse.

Choose the independently specified source parameters to satisfy $q_{\mathrm{all}}<1/2$. This is a nonempty open sufficient regime. For example $\epsilon\le g/10$ gives $d\le 1/9$; choose finite $\beta$ with $p_{0}\le 1/100$, and choose the actual positive pulse duration $\sigma$ so that $b\le 1/100$. Then

$$
q_{\mathrm{all}} \le 2(1/100+4/81+1/100)<0.139.
$$

All high pulse norm, switching, timing and calibration costs of this finite $\sigma$ are retained. The controls belong to the declared bounded local endpoint-control class, with finite-dimensional approximants of each displayed generator. The construction works for any positive finite $\sigma$ meeting the inequality and any $\eta$ in (0,1); an externally imposed minimum latency enters the inequality through $\sigma$.

The resulting lifetime bound is dynamical at nonzero temperature and includes nonzero coherent tunneling and nonzero thermal replacement. Each chosen finite lifetime uses a finite number of bath cells and records. The fixed-bath asymptotic state may differ from $\tau_{0}$ because of $V_{x}$; its marked-output error remains bounded without a Gibbs assumption for the perturbed memory.

##### 14.5.7m.8 The actual decoder, redundancy and error observable

The physical readout measures the 24 computational registers and a finite laboratory momentum spectral instrument. Let $S_p=\{p(b):b\in\mathbb{F}_2^{24}\}$, a finite set fixed by the retained table. The momentum instrument has rank-one projectors onto $e_{r}$ for $r\in S_{p}$ and the complementary overflow projector. It records all outcomes $(b,r)$ for $r\in S_{p}$, and $(b,\text{overflow})$ otherwise. It outputs an information word $u$ only when

$$
b \in C,\quad r=p(b),\quad \text{and } u=(B^{-1}b)_{1,\ldots,12};
$$

otherwise it outputs an explicit erasure/error symbol with the full raw outcome retained. These physical momentum projectors are fixed independently of $w$. A finite pointer initially blank is shifted to the declared distinct outcome word under each orthogonal projector; the controlled-pointer unitary is bounded, acts inside the cell and pointer neighborhood, and has a positive finite-action endpoint pulse. Its Hamiltonian and complete pointer stock are retained. The projectors commute with the unperturbed physical cell Hamiltonian; arbitrary $V_{x}$ need not commute, and its pulse/readout error or work is retained rather than presumed absent. The ideal endpoint instrument is a constitutive finite instrument of this enlarged source; a finite-duration implementation with the perturbation continuously active has uniform Duhamel error bounded by $\epsilon$ times its duration divided by $\hbar$. This error is included below. This instrument is normalized on the full carrier. The correct output event for $w$ contains exactly the ray $P_{w}$ among these rank-one joint outcomes, so its success probability is at least $\operatorname{Tr}(P_w\rho)$ (in this fine readout it equals that value). The output is obtained from physical registers and momentum. The fixed measurement basis and finite overflow outcome are the same for all 4096 choices of $w$.

This is a declared terminal projective instrument, with all errors, large momentum outcomes and records charged. Coarser finite instruments and their certified approximation errors are treated in Section 14.5.7m.10.

Prepare N cells in $P_{w}$ and use independent bath states, independent flags and no intercell interactions. The $V_{x}$ may differ arbitrarily within the per-cell bound. Conditional on these fixed Hamiltonians, their terminal records are independent. Declare the decoded information word to be the unique word occurring in more than N/2 cell outputs; otherwise report failure. The decoder uses only the cell outputs. A strict majority of correct cell outputs guarantees success. Since each bad-cell indicator has expectation at most $q_{\mathrm{all}}<1/2$, Hoeffding's elementary bounded-independent-variable estimate gives

$$
\Pr(\text{decoded word is not } w) \le \exp[-2N(1/2-q_{\mathrm{all}})^{2}]
$$

at every chosen physical lifetime. The bound includes wrong codewords and erasures. It does not assume identical distributions. For a demanded tolerance $\epsilon_{\mathrm{mem}}$, it suffices that

$$
N \ge \log(1/\epsilon_{\mathrm{mem}})/[2(1/2-q_{\mathrm{all}})^{2}].
$$

This is a pointwise-in-observation-time error probability; for a finite schedule of observations the union bound below applies.

This lower bound sizes a replicated source. The 24-bit code and its physical lattice map remain inside every cell, and the across-cell majority is explicit additional redundancy.

A single terminal read may be made at any declared physical time; the all-time bound includes collisions. For a finite-duration readout, add its independently certified error as below. If $K$ separate lifetime tests are required without assuming nondisturbance, allocate $K$ independent cohorts, each with its own $N$ cells, and read each cohort once at its nominated time. The union bound is $K\exp[-2N(1/2-q_{\mathrm{all}})^{2}]$. The preparation and retention of all $KN$ cells is charged.

For comparison, in the unperturbed case at positive temperature the joint Gibbs pure-vacuum probability is $Z_0(\beta)^{-N}$, which tends to zero as $N$ grows (Proposition 14.5.7e); the decoded classical mark is reliable while the global pure-state fidelity vanishes.

##### 14.5.7m.9 Source response, locality and complete instruments

The normal-coordinate representation $T_{B}$ is used to calculate spectra; physical spatial support is that of the located cell. Every factor of $T_{B}$ acts inside the already located cell. The physical Hamiltonian, $V_{x}$, pulses, bath states, momentum instrument and code-register instrument are all explicitly specified on that cell and its docked bath. Their pullbacks through $T_{B}$ give exact equality of every unnormalized history state and every outcome probability, including arbitrary retained references. The marked covering W intertwines the corresponding rotor operations and momenta as in Section 14.5.7m.3.

The observation menu includes the original 24 bounded scalar-potential probes of R.4.5b, the independently fixed canonical momentum instrument, the computational spin instrument, and finite source controls made from the stated operators. Its original rank-24 probe response is retained after the exact unitary join when the probes and preparations are appropriately identified. The codeword readout adds a separating response for the momentum shift; the enlarged source described here extends the response of the undressed rotor.

Locality is a statement about laboratory cells. The finite momentum spectral instrument and full-cell SWAP act on internal variables of a bounded cell. Their body count and internal control complexity may be very large, but do not increase with N at fixed approximation. Finite compiled controls may use local ancillas and switches throughout the cell; their support remains bounded and their stock is explicitly retained. The cell interactions are many-body within the cell.

The input mark and the code/lattice are specified inputs. The Gibbs supplier prepares $\tau_{0}$ for $H_{w}$ and therefore carries w-dependent thermodynamic data; that preparation/marking hardware belongs to the source and its invoice. The protected response is the continued physical manifestation of this held mark.

##### 14.5.7m.10 Finite resources, tails and approximations

The ideal cell has the same infinite-dimensional rotor Hilbert space as Theorem R.4.5b. A finite horizon has finitely many cells, bath copies, flags, controls and records, but the exact rotor and Gibbs states are ideal supplied resources. A finite implementation uses the following controlled truncation.

Let $H_0=H_w$ and use its energy projection $\Pi_{L}$ onto eigenvalues at most $L$. It has finite rank because of compact resolvent. Assume that every input has mean $H_{0}$ energy at most $E$, and that the named $V_{x}$ has a constructive bounded-operator description with certified norm bound and matrix-element approximants, for example a finite spin matrix with finite Fourier character potentials, or a specified norm-convergent sequence of such bounded operators. Arbitrary bounded operators are covered by the stability inequality; the finite compiler requires the constructive description.

For a state of energy $E$, the omitted probability is at most $E/L$, and a normalized retained projection is within trace distance at most $2\sqrt{E/L}+E/L$ under the unhalved trace-norm convention. This follows from the gentle projection bound and normalization. If $L>E$, the normalized projected input has mean energy at most $E/(1-E/L)$, not necessarily $E$. Every subsequent estimate applied to that normalized input uses this larger energy bound. It is an initial-state comparison.

For actual free dynamics $H=H_0+V$, $\lVert V\rVert\le\epsilon$, the $H_{0}$ energy of an evolved pure input with initial $H_{0}$ expectation $E$ is at most $E+2\epsilon$ because the $H$ energy is conserved. The same holds for mixed inputs by averaging. For an initially low-energy vector in $\Pi_{L}$, compare the full evolution with the Galerkin Hamiltonian $H_L=\Pi_LH\Pi_{L}$ on $\Pi_{L}$. The projected equation differs by the forcing $\Pi_LV(I-\Pi_L)\psi(t)$. Duhamel therefore bounds the projected vector error over $|t|\le T$ by

$$
(\epsilon T/\hbar) \sqrt{(E+2\epsilon)/L}.
$$

The full vector comparison additionally includes the omitted evolved component, bounded by $\sqrt{(E+2\epsilon)/L}$. For general energy-bounded inputs first project them, use the previous gentle bound, and apply the estimate to a purification/convex decomposition with the corresponding energy expectation; Cauchy–Schwarz gives the same energy-tail form. This proves a finite-time strong approximation, including arbitrary untouched references, with error tending to zero as $L$ increases.

The optional perturbed Gibbs states of Section 14.5.7m.5 have finite $H_{0}$ energy; the actual bath uses the simpler fixed $\tau_{0}$, whose exact energy is $-\partial_\beta\log Z_0(\beta)$. A completely explicit bound is obtained from convexity of $f(\beta)=\log Z_x(\beta)$:

$$
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Tr}(H_x \tau_x)&=-f'(\beta) \\
&\le (2/\beta)[f(\beta/2)-f(\beta)], \\
\operatorname{Tr}(H_0 \tau_x)&\le\operatorname{Tr}(H_x \tau_x)+\epsilon.
\end{aligned}
$$

Min-max gives $e^{-\beta\epsilon}Z_0(\beta)\le Z_x(\beta)\le e^{\beta\epsilon}Z_0(\beta)$. Substitution yields

$$
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Tr}(H_0 \tau_x) \\
&\le 4\epsilon +(2/\beta) \log[Z_0(\beta/2)/Z_0(\beta)] =: E_\beta.
\end{aligned}
$$

This finite bound suffices for that comparison. The fixed bath preparation has explicit unperturbed product eigenvectors and weights, with the lattice-series bound supplying a direct finite-cutoff preparation and certified error; it does not use $V_{x}$.

The bath is $\tau_{0}$. Its finite preparation uses the explicit product spin eigenbasis and lattice Fourier weights of $H_{w}$, followed by the same finite controlled preparation and source-coordinate dictionary. Enumerate a finite lattice ball, compute its Boltzmann weights with certified scalar arithmetic, retain an explicit tail/error certificate from the positive Gaussian series, and prepare the corresponding finite diagonal mixture.

The joint momentum readout is replaced by a finite list of spectral bins and an explicit overflow outcome. Its projectors on the retained finite input are computed exactly from the declared Fourier basis and the finite spin shifts; the largest $p(b)$ is a retained finite constant. The operator inequality $|P|^{2}\le 2|P-2\pi\hbar\,p(b)|^{2}+2(2\pi\hbar)^{2}\max_b|p(b)|^{2}$ bounds its overflow probability from the same kinetic-energy record. The actual decoder already uses a finite fixed list and an overflow event; any further cutoff overflow is likewise a retained failure outcome.

On each finite cell approximation, SWAP and controlled-SWAP are exact finite unitaries, preparation and readout have finite matrices, and every clocked control has a complete finite endpoint implementation in the newly declared control class. The explicit generator and its supplier/control invoice are part of the construction. If a collision implementation is within diamond error $\delta_{c}$ on the admitted cutoff carrier and a bath input within trace error $\delta_{b}$, $k$ collisions differ by at most $k(\delta_c+\delta_b)$ by the telescoping contractivity of channels. Adding input, free-propagation and final-readout errors gives a declared per-cell terminal error $\delta_{\mathrm{total}}$. Replace $q_{\mathrm{all}}$ by $q_{\mathrm{all}}+\delta_{\mathrm{total}}$ in the majority estimate, keeping $q_{\mathrm{all}}+\delta_{\mathrm{total}}<1/2$. The finite-duration terminal measurement Duhamel error is included in $\delta_{\mathrm{total}}$, even when no rotor truncation is used. Independent finite implementations retain independence; a shared controller or correlated fault source lies outside the declared class. All tolerances can be chosen finite for any finite $N,k,K$ and requested margin.

For a concrete collision-energy envelope choose a constant pulse $k=\pi/(2\sigma)$, so its positive bounded interaction has norm $K_{\max}=\pi\hbar/\sigma$. During that pulse the total Hamiltonian is the unbounded reference $H_{w,M}+H_{w,B}$ plus the bounded $V_{x,M}$ and pulse interaction. Conservation of this constant full Hamiltonian bounds the reference-energy change by $2(\epsilon+K_{\max})$; the on/off switching work and allocated interaction energy are separately recorded. A fresh bath contributes its finite mean $E_{\mathrm{bath}}=-\partial_\beta\log Z_{0}$. Thus after $k$ complete collisions a conservative memory reference-energy bound is $E_{\mathrm{initial}}+k[E_{\mathrm{bath}}+2(\epsilon+K_{\max})]$, with any separately admitted preparation/readout work added. The pair-cutoff $\Pi_{L}\otimes\Pi_{L}$ commutes with the reference free evolution and exact SWAP, so the same bounded-perturbation Duhamel proof applies to the actual collision using this finite-horizon energy envelope. Pointer controls have finite bounded generators; their work is similarly included. This supplies the energy premise needed for the full finite-history cutoff estimate.

The exact normal source and the effective finite approximants have the same stated response limit; their physical invoices remain distinct and positive. ∎

<a id="theorem-1457m11"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457m11-storage-of-an-unknown-golay-word-under-local-thermal-collisions"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7m.11 (Storage of an Unknown Golay Word under Local Thermal Collisions).** Use the Golay code $C$, its encoder and the physical spin–rotor dictionary of Theorem 14.5.7m on $N$ independent cells. For each binary register value $b$, let $d_C(b)=\min_{c\in C}d_H(b,c)$. Replace the word-selective energy by the single cell Hamiltonian
$$
H=\Delta d_C(\widehat b)+\frac{(P-2\pi\hbar p(\widehat b))^2}{2I_{\mathrm{rot}}},
\qquad \Delta>0 .
$$
All codeword ground states $\Psi_w=|c_w\rangle\otimes e_{p(c_w)}$ have the same energy. Encode the unknown classical input into $\Psi_w^{\otimes N}$ with a fixed encoder. At each cell, apply the same cyclic schedule of thermal collisions for the 24 physical bit directions, with one fresh bath pair per collision. Each bath pair has a qubit of gap $\Delta$ at inverse temperature $\beta>0$ and a degenerate qubit in its Gibbs state $I_2/2$. Every collision takes a fixed physical time $\sigma>0$. Let $p_\beta=(1+e^{\beta\Delta})^{-1}$, $\gamma_\beta=\binom{23}{3}e^{-3\beta\Delta}p_\beta$, and $q_m=\min\{1,\binom m4p_\beta^4,m\gamma_\beta\}$, with the binomial coefficient zero for $m<4$. At the end of any $m$ collisions a single cell has Golay decoded-word error at most $q_m$ for every $w$. For $q_m<1/2$, a majority of the $N$ independent cell decoders has error at most
$$
\exp[-2N(1/2-q_m)^2].
\tag{14.5.7m.11.1}
$$
The same bounds hold at physical storage time $t$ with $m=\lceil t/\sigma\rceil$. In particular $q_m\le1/24$ throughout $m\le\max\{\lfloor p_\beta^{-1}\rfloor,\lfloor (24\gamma_\beta)^{-1}\rfloor\}$. The second term gives a certified storage window of order $\sigma e^{4\beta\Delta}$ at low temperature, with all bath copies charged. The couplings have strictly positive probabilities for thermal excitation and de-excitation on the scheduled edges of unequal energy.

*Proof.* In the spin–rotor dictionary of Theorem 14.5.7m, write $|b;k\rangle=|b\rangle\otimes e_{k+p(b)}$. The energy in this basis is $\Delta d_C(b)+2\pi^2\hbar^2\|k\|^2/I_{\mathrm{rot}}$. The whole Hamiltonian is a unitary image of a bounded spin energy plus the free rotor. This gives a self-adjoint positive operator on the transported free domain and leaves the original momentum and deck-cover maps available. The commissioning recipe of Theorem 14.5.7m prepares the $k=0$ code inputs using the free evolution and phase corrections of $H$. This program is common to every word, and the prepared code inputs have zero free energy. To supply $N$ replicas, copy the classical input into $N$ information registers with controlled bit additions before applying that encoder. The input word enters only through the input state.

For bit direction $i$, the unordered pairs $\{b,b\oplus e_i\}$ are disjoint. All codewords have even weight, so $d_C(b)$ has the parity of $b$. A one-bit flip changes this parity. The 1-Lipschitz property therefore makes the energy difference have magnitude exactly $\Delta$. At fixed $k$ and on a pair of unequal energies $b_\ell,b_h$, swap $|b_\ell;k\rangle|1\rangle$ with $|b_h;k\rangle|0\rangle$ on the gap-$\Delta$ bath qubit. Keep the degenerate qubit as an independently prepared spectator and act as the identity on unused states. These pairwise swaps give a bounded self-adjoint involution $S_i$ on the complete cell and bath. It commutes with their free sum Hamiltonian because the swapped vectors have the same total energy. Each physical bit flip is accompanied by the rotor shift $p(b\oplus e_i)-p(b)$, a multiplication by an allowed Leech character. The interaction preserves $k$ and intertwines the same deck-invariant covering as the encoder. The bounded-cell geometry of Section 14.5.7m.1 gives the interaction range, including the bit-value controls and the docked bath.

During the collision add the positive constant pulse $\pi\hbar(I-S_i)/(2\sigma)$ to the free sum Hamiltonian. The endpoint propagator is $S_i$ times the free evolution. Its interaction norm action is at most $\pi\hbar$. With a fresh diagonal Gibbs pair the reduced spin law at the endpoint is a stochastic bit update, with uphill transition probability $p_\beta$, downhill probability $1-p_\beta$, and flat-edge probability $1/2$. Thus the actual collision dynamics exchanges energy with thermal stocks and acts on every bit direction. The schedule, spectra, pulse and initial bath states are independent of the input word. Retaining the outgoing baths gives the same unitary dilation for arbitrary references. The memory error assertion concerns classical words. The storage horizon uses no intermediate correction or postselection.

Fix $c=c_w$. Whenever $d_H(b,c)\le3$, it is the unique nearest codeword by minimum distance eight. At distance four from $c$, the triangle inequality forces every other codeword to have distance at least four, so $d_C(b)=4$. Therefore the first exit from the radius-three Hamming ball requires at least four uphill flips, each occurring while the process is inside that ball. For each fixed set of four indices, successive conditional expectations bound their joint probability by $p_\beta^4$. Summing over index sets gives the first nontrivial term in $q_m$.

Every scheduled bit kernel preserves the strictly positive Gibbs law $\pi(b)\propto e^{-\beta\Delta d_C(b)}$ by the detailed-balance identity $p_\beta=e^{-\beta\Delta}(1-p_\beta)$. If $K$ is any product of the scheduled kernels, stationarity gives $\pi(c)K(c,b)\le\pi(b)$. Thus the unconditional probability at a given distance-three string from $c$ is at most $e^{-3\beta\Delta}$ at each endpoint. For a specified next direction, exactly $\binom{23}{3}$ of those strings can exit the ball along that direction, since the flipped bit must agree with $c$ before the flip. Each such exit has conditional probability $p_\beta$. The sum over collision endpoints bounds the probability of any first exit by $m\gamma_\beta$.

At an endpoint in the ball, the radius-three decoder returns $c$. The decoder reports a failure flag when no radius-three codeword exists. In different cells all bath inputs and dynamics are independent conditional on the encoded word, so the decoded-error indicators, including failure flags, are independent with means at most $q_m$. The majority bound follows from Hoeffding's inequality, as in Theorem 14.5.7m. Finally $\binom m4p_\beta^4\le(mp_\beta)^4/24$, and $\gamma_\beta e^{4\beta\Delta}\to\binom{23}{3}$ as $\beta\Delta\to\infty$.

Within a collision, at elapsed time $0\le\tau\le\sigma$, the two-state rotation gives a transition probability $\sin^2(\pi\tau/(2\sigma))$ times the full-collision probability on each matched bath edge. For a diagonal spin law the spin kernel is therefore $(1-s)I+sK_i$, where $s=\sin^2(\pi\tau/(2\sigma))$ and $K_i$ is the completed collision kernel. The initial and flipped components have orthogonal bath labels on the exchanged pair. The partial kernel preserves $\pi$ and gives uphill probability at most $p_\beta$, so the same first-exit arguments apply to the last partial step with $m=\lceil t/\sigma\rceil$.

Translation by any codeword preserves $d_C$, commutes with every spin kernel, and translates every successful decoded label by that codeword. This gives the same decoded-error probability for every input. In the spin–rotor basis the dressed translation is $|b;k\rangle\mapsto|b\oplus c_0;k\rangle$ for $c_0\in C$. It commutes with every pulse and the free Hamiltonian, since the translation preserves the ordered low and high ends of each edge. Thus storage states for distinct classical input words are related by a unitary acting only on the memory cells. Tracing out those cells gives exactly the same joint marginal of all spent and docked baths for every word, including at partial pulses. For the first-exit time $T_{\rm exit}$ in collision counts, a complete sweep that starts in the ball has conditional exit probability at least $p_\beta^4$. To see this, choose four directions whose starting bits agree with $c$ and have yet to be visited in the sweep. Their updates leave those bits wrong with probability $p_\beta$ each while the path remains in the ball.
