At each selected update either a previous exit has already succeeded, or the next required uphill flip has probability $p_\beta$. If all four flips occur, they persist to the end of the sweep and the path must exit. Thus $\Pr(T_{\rm exit}>24k)\le(1-p_\beta^4)^k$ and $\mathbb E T_{\rm exit}\le24p_\beta^{-4}$. The lower bound on survival from $m\gamma_\beta$ gives the matching low-temperature exponent for the mean first-exit time. Decoding after reentry is a separate observable.

Each full 24-collision sweep has a strictly positive transition probability between any two spin strings, because each scheduled bit can stay fixed or flip with positive probability at finite $\beta\Delta$. The sweep has the unique stationary law $\pi$, and the spin law eventually becomes independent of the encoded word.

For a finite horizon the reachable memory space is exactly contained in the finite set of spin–rotor basis vectors with $k=0$ throughout storage, including during the pulse. All actual canonical momenta then belong to the finite fixed list $2\pi\hbar p(b)$. The occupied physical energy, basis momenta, and bath energies are explicitly bounded by the finite code table and bath gap. Commissioning uses the finite Fourier space and fixed controls of Theorem 14.5.7m. The terminal spin measurement and radius-three decoder use finite reversible circuits and fresh pointers. Their clock and energy costs, the encoder, the $Nm$ Gibbs pairs, the outgoing archive and the collection of the cell records are retained. For an operational report with finite readout latency, the cell can be isolated after the last collision and measured in the spin basis with a commuting controlled pointer, so the endpoint populations are unchanged during the readout. An aggregate half trace-norm implementation error $\varepsilon$ on the final retained states adds at most $\varepsilon$ to (14.5.7m.11.1). Each stated rate, gap and storage time depends on the declared cell parameters and the bath temperature. ∎

<a id="theorem-1457n"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457n-local-coupled-golay-leech-defect-and-full-response-realization"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7n (Local Coupled Golay-Leech Defect and Full-Response Realization).** Let $m,n\ge4$. Place the vertices of a periodic $m\times n$ square cellulation on a material torus in laboratory space and put at each vertex $x$ a marked Golay–Leech macrocell: 24 two-level registers and one rotor on $\mathbb R^{24}/\Lambda_C$, with the on-site encoding $T=\bigotimes_xT_x$ of Section 14.5.7n.3, under which the first two encoded Golay coordinates of $x$ are the edges from $x$ to $x+(1,0)$ and to $x+(0,1)$. Let $H_{\mathrm{src}}=TH_0T^*$, where $H_0=H_T+H_{\mathrm{aux}}+\sum_xH_{R0,x}$ is the toric, spectator and rotor Hamiltonian of Section 14.5.7n.4 with positive gaps $\Delta_e,\Delta_m,e_{x,a}$ and rotor constants $A_x=2\pi^2\hbar^2/I_x$. Then:

1. $H_{\mathrm{src}}$ is positive and self-adjoint with compact resolvent; its toric and spectator terms are commuting bounded projector interactions supported on neighboring macrocells; its ground space has dimension $4$, and its gap is at least $\min(2\Delta_e,2\Delta_m,\min_{x,a}e_{x,a},\min_x4A_x)$.
2. $V=T^*$ maps the complete source unitarily onto the toric, spectator and rotor carrier, with $VH_{\mathrm{src}}V^*=H_0$ and $Ve^{-itH_{\mathrm{src}}/\hbar}V^*=e^{-itH_0/\hbar}$; it intertwines all states, effects, instruments and channels, and for every set $S$ of macrocells it maps the bounded operators supported on $S$ onto the bounded operators supported on $S$. The laboratory rotor momentum is carried to $P_{x,j}+2\pi\hbar\,U_B^*p_j(b_x)U_B$.
3. For every registration of a contractible probe disk and a disjoint reference disk with clean collars and two compatible global loops (Section 14.5.7n.7), the regional charge classes of the isolated probe are exactly $1,e,m,\epsilon$, labeled by $(a,b)\in\mathbb F_2^2$, with fusion by addition, full monodromy $(-1)^{ab'+ba'}$ and exchange phase $(-1)^{ab}$, realized by dressed ribbon operators on the same physical paths as the toric ribbons. The toric stabilizer basis tensored with all spectator strings and all rotor momenta is a complete orthonormal configuration basis of the source.
4. The check-syndrome instrument, the spectator and rotor readouts and the finite-rank binary effects with overflow outcomes separate all normal states of the complete carrier, and every finite protocol on inputs of source energy at most $E$ is implemented at energy grade $K$ with output trace-norm error at most $4\sqrt{E/(A_{\min}K)}+\sum_j\delta_j$.

On the $64\times64$ torus with probe and reference disks around $(16,16)$ and $(48,48)$ all four compensating charge assignments occur.

*Proof.* Sections 14.5.7n.1–14.5.7n.12 construct the source and prove items 1–4.

##### 14.5.7n.1 Source class

Fix the marked extended binary Golay code C, the marked Construction-B Leech lattice $\Lambda_{C}$, its physical rotor and covering from Theorems R.4.5b and R.4.9, and the encoder and controlled momentum table of Theorem 14.5.7m. The source below replaces the independent-cell marked spin Hamiltonian by coupled star/plaquette interactions on a material torus; the thermal results of Theorem 14.5.7m are not transported to it.

The family is specified as follows. Its objects are all finite periodic square cellulations with at least four cells in each direction, all positive independently calibrated toric gaps, all positive local spectator gaps and rotor inertias, all declared marked code bases and physical phase frames, and all normal density operators of finite mean source energy on the complete carrier specified below. Its protocols are arbitrary finite compositions, classical mixtures and history-dependent choices of the explicitly admitted local preparations, bounded finite-grade controls, effects and instruments below, with the complete finite ancillary, controller, phase, work and outcome records retained. Every finite collection of probe/reference disks, ribbons and global loop choices is included when it satisfies the declared geometric restrictions.

The defect equivalence is the finite toric regional equivalence: excitation configurations in a contractible probe disk are compared by interior moves while the compensating-charge reference region, annular observations and specified global loop sector are retained. All within-sector energies, rotor states, positions, coherences and instrument responses remain as physical fibers. A full joint state may have coherences between regional charge sectors; these are retained by the full state/operator map, so the four relative annular charges are classes of the regional relation and not superselection blocks of the global matrix algebra.

##### 14.5.7n.2 Independent geometry and the logical edge assignment

Choose a periodic $m$-by-$n$ square combinatorial cellulation, $m,n\ge 4$. Place its vertices on an independently specified smooth material torus in three-dimensional laboratory space. One explicit placement is

$$
\begin{aligned}
x_{i,j}&=((R+r \cos(2\pi j/n))\cos(2\pi i/m), \\
&\qquad(R+r \cos(2\pi j/n))\sin(2\pi i/m), \\
&\qquad r \sin(2\pi j/n)), \\
&0\le i<m,\quad 0\le j<n,
\end{aligned}
$$

with $R=3r$ and $r$ chosen so that the smallest cell separation exceeds four times the physical memory-cell diameter $a_{0}$. For a uniformly bounded aspect-ratio family, nearest-neighbor separations and star/plaquette diameters are bounded by a constant times $a_{0}$ independent of $m,n$. For arbitrary finite $m,n$, retain their finite maximum separation and charge the corresponding wires/ports and delays. The torus, distances, cell positions and interaction terminals are fixed before choosing or applying any encoder. Its two-dimensional material metric and ribbon paths are distinct from the 24 internal configuration coordinates of a Leech rotor.

At each vertex $x$ put one complete marked Golay–Leech macrocell. It has 24 two-level registers and one internal Leech rotor. The registers and rotor terminals lie in its ball of diameter $a_{0}$. Fix the binary invertible encoder matrix $B$ whose first 12 columns are the marked Golay generator basis. In the encoded coordinates $z=B^{-1}b$, use $z_{1}$ as the horizontal edge from $x$ to $x+(1,0)$ and $z_{2}$ as the vertical edge from $x$ to $x+(0,1)$. This gives each edge exactly one physical owner. The remaining 22 coordinates $z_3,\dots,z_{24}$ are spectator registers. The last 12 are exactly the Golay syndrome coordinates, and $z_3,\dots,z_{12}$ are the other ten logical coordinates.

Choose their held signs to be $+1$. Then the subspace with those 22 spectators in their designated ground states contains exactly four physical codewords per cell, $B(z_1,z_2,0,\dots,0)\in C$. Thus each pair of toric edge qubits is physically encoded in an actual two-dimensional logical coordinate plane of the marked 12-bit Golay code. All other logical words, syndrome errors and noncode spin states remain in the complete carrier.

##### 14.5.7n.3 Full local rotor and on-site encoding

Use the accepted explicit lattice

$$
\begin{aligned}
K_C&=\{x \in \mathbb{Z}^{24}: x \bmod 2 \in C,\ \sum_i x_i=0 \bmod 4\}, \\
L_B&=K_C/\sqrt{2}, \\
\nu&=(-3,1,\ldots,1)/\sqrt{8}, \\
\Lambda_C&=L_B \cup (\nu+L_B).
\end{aligned}
$$

For $c\in C$, $p(c)=c/\sqrt{2}$ belongs to $\Lambda_{C}$, and for $c\ne c'$, $|p(c)-p(c')|^{2}=\operatorname{wt}(c+c')/2\ge 4$. Extend $p$ injectively to every 24-bit word by the fixed finite rule from the marked-cell construction: enumerate noncode words and assign unused positive integer multiples of a nominated nonzero lattice vector, avoiding all codeword images. The whole finite table is retained and charged.

Each cell rotor has

$$
\begin{aligned}
H_{R,x}&=L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{24}/\Lambda_C), \\
P_{x,j}&=-i \hbar\, \partial_{q_j}, \\
H_{R_0,x}&=P_x^{2}/(2 I_x), \\
A_x&=2\pi^{2} \hbar^{2}/I_x>0.
\end{aligned}
$$

The character $e_r(q)=\exp(2\pi i\,r\cdot q)$, $r\in\Lambda_{C}$, has energy $A_x|r|^{2}$. The entire spectrum, including every Fourier mode and multiplicity, is retained. The rotor ground is $e_{0}$, its first gap is $4A_x$, and its first-shell multiplicity is 196560.

Let $M_{p}$ denote multiplication by $e_{p}$ and put

$$
\begin{aligned}
U_{p,x}&=\sum_b |b\rangle\langle b| \otimes M_{p(b)}, \\
T_x&=U_{p,x}(U_B \otimes I_R), \\
T&= \bigotimes_x T_x.
\end{aligned}
$$

These are exact on-site unitaries. Finite implementations are supplied in Sections 14.5.7n.10–14.5.7n.11.

The full source Hilbert space is $\bigotimes_x(\mathbb{C}^{2^{24}}\otimes H_{R,x})$. In the undressed coordinates it is exactly

$$
H_{\mathrm{edges}} \otimes H_{\mathrm{spectators}} \otimes H_{\mathrm{rotors}},
$$

with 2mn edge qubits, 22mn spectator qubits and mn complete rotors. This is a factorization. The original marked covering is applied independently at every cell. Character multiplication, rotor kinetic terms and momentum spectral projections intertwine with that covering on its specified deck-invariant source sector.

##### 14.5.7n.4 Actual coupled Hamiltonian

Let $A_{v}$ be the product of X on the four edges incident on v and $B_{f}$ the product of Z around the four edges of f. Define on the undressed carrier

$$
\begin{aligned}
H_T&=\frac{\Delta_e}{2} \sum_v(I-A_v)+\frac{\Delta_m}{2} \sum_f(I-B_f), \\
H_{\mathrm{aux}}&=\sum_x \sum_{a=3}^{24} e_{x,a}(I-Z_{x,a})/2, \\
H_0&=H_T+H_{\mathrm{aux}}+\sum_x H_{R_0,x},
\end{aligned}
$$

where every coefficient is positive and independently calibrated. The physical source Hamiltonian is

$$
H_{\mathrm{src}} = T H_0 T^{*}.
$$

This is an explicit constitutive Hamiltonian. Its domain is T of the tensor-sum rotor H2 domain; its closed form domain is the corresponding H1 domain. The bounded toric and spectator terms are a finite positive perturbation of the rotor sum. Thus $H_{\mathrm{src}}$ is positive and self-adjoint with compact resolvent, and every spectral multiplicity is inherited exactly from $H_{0}$.

The physical edge Pauli operators are

$$
\begin{aligned}
\bar{X}_{x,a}&=T_x(X_{x,a} \otimes I)T_x^{*}, \\
\bar{Z}_{x,a}&=T_x(Z_{x,a} \otimes I)T_x^{*},\qquad a=1,2.
\end{aligned}
$$

An $\bar{X}$ has the literal finite spin-controlled character-shift form. In the physical computational basis, a nonzero matrix element from $b$ to $b'$ contains the bounded rotor multiplier $M_{p(b')-p(b)}$. $\bar{Z}$ is the corresponding encoded parity. The source star and plaquette terms are the products of these operators on the stated neighboring macrocells. They are bounded positive-projector interactions. The full square of each covariant rotor term is retained:

$$
T_x H_{R_0,x} T_x^{*} = [P_x-2\pi \hbar\, p(b_x)]^{2}/(2 I_x).
$$

In particular, the scalar contact term and the finite, possibly very large, p-table coefficients are retained.

All terms commute, because they are simultaneous on-site conjugates of the commuting terms in $H_{0}$. Each dressed rotor term commutes with every dressed edge operator, including edge X. Consequently the rotor adds no spin-dependent energy to the toric syndrome dynamics. All interactions have support in a fixed collection of independently neighboring physical cells. Their range is bounded on the bounded-aspect-ratio family.

The ground space has dimension 4: toric ground space tensor the unique 22mn-bit spectator ground and the rotor vacuum. The excitation gap is

$$
g \ge \min(2\Delta_e,2\Delta_m,\min_{x,a}e_{x,a},\min_x 4A_x)>0.
$$

The first two factors are doubled because on the closed torus star and face violations occur with even total parity. The bound is a finite-source spectral fact.

##### 14.5.7n.5 Exact state, energy, clock and instrument intertwiner

The source-to-target map is $V=T^*$. It maps the complete physical carrier onto the toric, spectator and rotor tensor carrier. No parity restriction is needed:

$$
\begin{aligned}
V H_{\mathrm{src}} V^{*}&=H_0, \\
V \exp(-it H_{\mathrm{src}}/\hbar)V^{*}&=\exp(-it H_0/\hbar).
\end{aligned}
$$

The time variable, $\hbar$, rotor inertias and toric gaps are the same independently calibrated physical quantities on both sides. Every Gibbs state, source energy distribution, finite-time response and correlation with an arbitrary untouched reference is intertwined. For any full effect E, instrument map I or unitary U, the corresponding target object is VEV*, V I(V*·V)V*, or VUV*. Complete positivity, trace preservation, outcome probabilities and unnormalized conditional states are preserved.

Conversely, the physical local preparations/effects/instruments described below are actually installed implementations of the source conjugates of their explicit target operators. This gives both preservation and reflection.

For every collection S of macrocells,

$$
V B(H_{\mathrm{src}},S) V^{*} = B(H_{\mathrm{target}},S),
$$

with identity on its complement. This is an equality of regional algebras for the independently fixed macrocell regions, because V is a product of on-site unitaries. For edge-shaped regions, assign an edge to its owner macrocell; disk and collar regions are expanded by at most this fixed ownership radius. Keep that geometric collar explicit in every annulus/reference preparation. This local support map is uniform on the bounded-aspect-ratio family.

##### 14.5.7n.6 Rotor and marker responses

For a rotor character multiplication probe,

$$
V(I \otimes M_l)V^{*}=I \otimes M_l.
$$

For the fixed laboratory canonical momentum,

$$
V P_{x,j} V^{*} = P_{x,j}+2\pi \hbar\, U_B^{*} p_j(b_x) U_B.
$$

The finite spin-controlled shift is retained. It couples the actual codeword label to the measured physical Leech momentum even though the covariant kinetic vacuum has zero kinetic energy. The corresponding spectral projectors, characteristic functions, mixed spin/rotor matrix elements and all reference correlations are retained.

Spectator excitations have their actual positive energies $e_{x,a}$; rotor excitations have their actual $A_x|r|^{2}$. Local removal or exchange of an excitation uses work/ancillary channels and retains their output state and energy. Calling those excitations topologically trivial below never identifies states with different energy, momentum or readout response; the target keeps the full auxiliary Hilbert factors and their Hamiltonians.

The source phase frame is the explicitly supplied marked laboratory frame already needed for the rotor potential probes of Theorem R.4.5b. The internal generalized momenta $P_{j}$ are not conserved charges of this driven source: the admitted character potentials and dressed edge-X terms have nonzero commutators with them. Phase programs, actuator supports, work/torque providers and their records are supplied and charged in Sections 14.5.7n.10–14.5.7n.12.

##### 14.5.7n.7 Complete configuration and regional-charge census

For every finite torus the simultaneous toric stabilizer basis can be labelled by

$$
\begin{aligned}
&(a_v \in \{+1,-1\},\ b_f \in \{+1,-1\},\ \omega_x,\omega_y \in \{+1,-1\}), \\
&\prod_v a_v=\prod_f b_f=1.
\end{aligned}
$$

There are $2mn-2$ independent check signs and two independent commuting logical $Z$ loops, hence exactly $2^{2mn}$ basis vectors. Tensor this with every spectator bit string and every tuple of Leech rotor momenta. This is a complete orthonormal basis of the full target carrier. Applying $T$ gives a complete basis of the actual source. In particular, every source syndrome block, loop sector and rotor mode is included, and the toric parity identities are operator identities of the coupled source.

Choose a contractible probe disk $D$ and a disjoint reference disk $R$, with a sufficiently wide annular collar and with all claimed interior operations supported inside the appropriate contracted disk. Each isolated probe and reference disk must contain at least one eligible star vertex AND one eligible plaquette, with the stated clean, ownership and control collars. The four-class isolated experiment additionally registers that all four compensating charge assignments are nonempty (equivalently, each corresponding constrained charge projection is nonzero). This is checked from the finite stabilizer/region data before the experiment. Small tori or region choices lacking this room remain members of the source family without a four-class isolated experiment. For explicit nonvacuity, a 64-by-64 torus admits separated probe/reference interiors around $(16,16)$ and $(48,48)$, with charge cores of graph radius 2, move support radius 3, control collar through radius 5 and annular loops at radius 10, including their explicitly checked edge-owner supports; global loop representatives on row 0 and column 0 avoid these collars and annular supports. Both disks contain the required vertices and faces, and primal/dual connecting ribbons furnish all four compensating parity assignments in each chosen compatible global-loop block. Define $P_{\mathrm{clean}}$ as the joint $+1$ projector for the toric checks outside the declared probe/reference interiors, including their clean collars. The isolated-defect carrier is $P_{\mathrm{clean}}H_{\mathrm{edges}}\otimes$ the complete spectator/rotor carrier; auxiliary excitations in a collar are retained by this toric check restriction. The disk interiors, permitted ribbon supports and annular collars are registered with enough separation that every claimed interior move is disjoint from the separating annular loops, including its fixed physical drift-control collar. All toric excitations outside $D\cup R$ are absent only in this isolated-defect experiment. Excitations elsewhere remain admitted in the full source and are covered by the general many-disk census below. Define $a,b\in\mathbb{F}_2$ as the parity of violated stars and plaquettes in $D$. The torus parity identities force a compensating parity $(a,b)$ in $R$ when no other disks contain defects. More generally, for any finite collection of disjoint disks $D_{i}$ the sums of their electric and magnetic parities vanish, with any additional explicitly nominated exterior reference region included in those sums.

The original toric annular loops have eigenvalues $(-1)^a$ and $(-1)^b$. Their physical source representatives are products of the actual local dressed edge operators. They are supported on the specified physical collar, and they commute with every admitted interior operation, including arbitrary interior spectator/rotor operations. This last statement follows from physical tensor support and the on-site regional-algebra identity.

All configurations with the same probe parities are related by the original finite interior moves: pairs can be connected and annihilated by ribbons, and differences of homologous interior paths are products of the actual star/plaquette checks. These moves exist inside a contractible disk after the fixed support collar is allowed. Rotor and spectator basis excitations can be moved, prepared, exchanged or recorded by the admitted local finite controls without changing either annular loop. They add response-active local fibers but no additional annular central charge. Conversely, configurations with different a or b cannot be related by an interior operation that preserves the annular/reference data, because that operation commutes with the separating loop.

Thus the complete relative charge list is

$$
1=(0,0),\quad e=(1,0),\quad m=(0,1),\quad \epsilon=(1,1).
$$

This exhausts the declared regional defect class. For clarity, on the clean-collar carrier the charge algebra in this assertion is the commutative algebra generated by the two separating annular parity loops, with the registered reference and loop constraints. It is $\mathbb{C}^4$ when all four probe/reference charge assignments are nonempty. Completeness follows first from the explicit configuration relation: interior Pauli moves connect every same-parity toric configuration, while the separating loops forbid connections between different parities. Every interior quantum control is a linear combination or normal limit of local matrix units and still commutes with those external loops, so it cannot merge different charge fibers. The full local spectator algebra is the type-I factor $B(H_{\mathrm{aux},\mathrm{region}})$, with scalar commutant against all its finite matrix units; consequently no spectator label supplies an additional charge invariant of this declared regional relation. This is a classification of the charge relation. Different local energies, spectra, states and coherences remain distinguishable full physical fibers.

For an arbitrary normal state, the four commuting charge projections $\Pi_{ab}$ provide its complete charge instrument, including coherent off-diagonal $\Pi_{ab}\rho\,\Pi_{a'b'}$ entries in the full joint state before measurement. A sector declaration is a restriction to the corresponding charge fiber; the off-diagonal entries remain in the physical state. The full global operator map and the separating measurements below retain them whenever the preparation/protocol includes the reference data needed to observe them.

Global loop choices are separately retained. A frozen $Q_{\omega}$ experiment registers two actual independent global logical $Z$-loop representatives whose supports are disjoint from the probe/reference interior-move supports AND their physical control collars. More generally one may use the exact commutation test with every admitted interior generator; the stated disjointness is a simple sufficient condition. The finite cellular chain data verify that the chosen loops form a homology basis and that all required interior moves commute with them. Only compatible disk/collar/loop registrations are included in the four-class fixed-$Q_\omega$ assertion. If a chosen interior $X$ ribbon intersects a selected global $Z$-loop, it is a loop-changing operation and belongs to the full-source protocol census with its changed $\omega$ recorded. The complete four-loop source and its actual operator table still cover that operation. For a compatible fixed pair $\omega$, use the original $Q_{\omega}$ block and only within-block protocols when invoking the original frozen-loop theorem. All four $Q_{\omega}$ blocks and all between-block coherences/channels belong to the complete source. A global noncontractible logical operation is a paid long protocol with its actual support.

##### 14.5.7n.8 Fusion, braiding, twists and references

Take actual source ribbons obtained by multiplying the dressed edge $Z$ operators along a primal path and the dressed edge $X$ operators along a dual path. They have the same physical path and bounded collar as their undressed toric counterparts. Products move endpoints and fuse charges by addition in $\mathbb{F}_2^{2}$. The full source includes the compensating endpoints and the reference-region states; creating a lone charge on a closed torus is not admitted.

Since the encoding is multiplicative and local,

$$
\begin{aligned}
\bar{X}(\gamma^{*})\, \bar{Z}(\gamma) \\
&=(-1)^{I_2(\gamma^{*},\gamma)}\, \bar{Z}(\gamma)\, \bar{X}(\gamma^{*}).
\end{aligned}
$$

The complete framed braid word is physically realized by the corresponding local ribbon sequence. A full braid of $(a,b)$ around $(a',b')$ has phase $(-1)^{ab'+ba'}$. Relative to the unexchanged reference process with the same endpoints, $e$ and $m$ have exchange phase $+1$ and $\epsilon$ has exchange phase $-1$. These comparisons preserve the whole auxiliary/reference state up to its explicitly evolved source dynamics and retained control records. Fusion multiplicities are 1 and the four charges are their own inverses.

For multiple disks, the configuration groupoid is the original finite toric disk/reference groupoid tensor its full local state/energy fibers. All admissible parity assignments, all allowed endpoint motions, every global loop block and every finite ribbon word are included. Arbitrary coherent superpositions and classical mixtures are described by the linear extension and their full quantum instruments. This is the exhaustive finite-source census of the family.

##### 14.5.7n.9 Actual separating instruments and the predictive interface

The actual source check-syndrome instrument has Kraus projections

$$
P_{a,b}=\prod_v(I+a_v \bar{A}_v)/2 \prod_f(I+b_f \bar{B}_f)/2,
$$

with impossible parity outcomes assigned zero. Resolve global loop signs when the protocol requires them, without automatically measuring them in protocols intended to retain loop coherence. Tensor the chosen spectator and finite rotor measurements, each with its explicit overflow outcome. The source implementation uses pointer couplings to the physical bounded projectors. Unnormalized postmeasurement states, joint outcome probabilities and conditional normalizations are exactly the conjugates of the target instrument.

For finite-energy tomography choose any finite rotor Fourier set $F$ and all spin/spectator matrix units in that finite block. Real and imaginary parts of finite matrix units, rescaled to norm $\le 1$, give physical binary effects $(I+O)/2$. Include the overflow projection $I-P_F$. The increasing union of these finite-rank effects separates all normal density operators on the complete carrier. Thus the family is response-faithful on the actual full Hilbert space, even though no one finite experiment tomographs an unlimited-energy state. Character multiplication and momentum spectral probes remain the original independently identified Leech observables.

A protocol is an actual finite controlled source experiment: its finite classical descriptor lists preparations, local interaction matrices, phase settings, pulse windows, pointer effects, outcome branches and cost counters. Store the descriptor before the fresh input. Every adaptive branch and failed/overflow outcome remains in the record. Compile the finite program into the qualified native finite control/clock services, with the actual positive tagged-unitary pulse below. The interface consumes the actual pointer outcome. This supplies the physical predictive-response map. The conditional interface/fusion construction of Section 14.5.7 is instantiated by this supplied source and its complete protocol census.

##### 14.5.7n.10 Bounded physical controls and always-on drift

The Hamiltonian and control menus extend the rotor source of Theorem R.4.5b by spectral-projector pointers, encoded toric interactions and full-cell matrix controls. At a finite energy grade these are literal finite matrices on a named finite Fourier/spin span, with the identity outside; their coefficients, supports and program lengths are retained.

For a finite unitary U, introduce a fresh two-level tag and define

$$
\begin{aligned}
W_U&=U \otimes |1\rangle\langle 0|+U^{*} \otimes |0\rangle\langle 1|, \\
W_U^{*}&=W_U,\quad W_U^{2}=I.
\end{aligned}
$$

The positive bounded Hamiltonian $\hbar k(t)(I-W_U)$, with $k\ge 0$ and $\int k=\pi/2$, implements $W_{U}$ at the endpoint, hence $U$ on tag 0 and tag 1 at the output. Its peak norm is $2\hbar\sup k$, full operator-norm action is $\pi\hbar$, and its positive mean action on the stated input tag is $\pi\hbar/2$. The tag is retained and never reset for free. This is the actual supplied bounded-control primitive; its physical matrices, phase program, clock and ready-state resources are counted.

The source drift stays on. On a chosen finite reached subspace, use the interaction-picture drive

$$
K(t)=\exp(-iH_{\mathrm{src}} t/\hbar)\, K_I(t)\, \exp(iH_{\mathrm{src}} t/\hbar).
$$

The total propagator of $H_{\mathrm{src}}+K(t)$ is $\exp(-iH_{\mathrm{src}}t/\hbar)$ times the intended interaction-picture propagator. Known free phases are retained or corrected by an explicit positive finite diagonal phase pulse, with integers chosen so every added coefficient is nonnegative. Preparation therefore preserves arbitrary coherent input and reference states.

This drift compensation remains local. Write $H_{\mathrm{src}}$ as the sum of its mutually commuting local terms. A term disjoint from $K_{I}$ commutes with $K_{I}$ and with all the other drift terms. It consequently still commutes after conjugation by the overlapping terms. Only the terms that originally overlap the support of $K_{I}$ contribute. The support of $K(t)$ lies in their union, a fixed geometrical collar, independent of pulse time and total lattice size on the bounded-range family. The argument uses the commutativity of the drift terms proved in Section 14.5.7n.4.

Rotor kinetic conjugation changes phases but not the finite set of reached Fourier indices. On-site dressing adds only the explicitly bounded finite $p(b)$ shifts. Thus every finite-grade interaction-picture pulse has a finite spectral matrix, computable maximum source energy and bounded norm. All physical collar, phase-resolution, timing and approximation errors are recorded.

The supplied phase frame is a constitutive driven apparatus, as in the original marked rotor source. Its Hamiltonian torque on each internal rotor is the actual commutator $i[H,P_j]/\hbar$, and its calibrated mean work is the actual energy-balance functional integral of $\operatorname{Tr}(\rho\,\partial_t H)$. The program retains that functional and the provider's full output/state and its finite control support; the corresponding mean impulse/work balance is retained. The fixed positive reserve uses the displayed norm bounds and so does not depend on the input state. On a finite reached span, both torque and work have explicit norm upper bounds, so finite positive provider reserves suffice. The source is an externally driven laboratory source.

##### 14.5.7n.11 Finite energy grades and uniform protocol errors

Let $A_{\min}=\min_x A_x$. In the undressed frame take $P_{K}$ to project onto all spin/spectator states and all rotor tuples with $\sum_x|r_x|^{2}\le K$. The projector commutes with $H_{0}$. In the physical frame use $TP_{K}T^*$. For any source state, including an arbitrary untouched reference, with $\operatorname{Tr}(\rho H_{\mathrm{src}})\le E$,

$$
\operatorname{Tr}[\rho(I-T P_K T^{*})] \le E/(A_{\min} K).
$$

This follows because the source is positive and its rotor energy is at least $A_{\min}\sum|r_x|^{2}$. The energy bound is on the complete source.

For a finite protocol, expand the cutoff to contain every Fourier index reachable from this finite input set under its finite list of specified character shifts and finite controls. This is a finite, explicitly enumerable set. Extend each desired finite-subspace isometry to a unitary on the union of its finite input and output spans, and act as the identity on the complement. This gives an actual finite matrix implementation agreeing exactly on the retained input block. A gate that changes rotor momentum uses the expanded output span.

Gentle projection and contractivity give a conservative full protocol output trace-norm error at most

$$
4 \sqrt{E/(A_{\min} K)} + \sum_j \delta_j,
$$

where $\delta_{j}$ bounds the full diamond error of finite gate/pointer j on its reached block. The same bound controls the joint classical-quantum output, all reference correlations and every retained bounded effect. For finite adaptive protocols, use the actual maximum operation depth over the whole finite outcome tree and include every branch. If the expected value of an unbounded energy or momentum observable is requested, supply its separate moment/tail bound or its actual truncation/overflow interval.

Every positive tolerance and finite mean-energy budget therefore has a finite explicit grade: choose K large enough for the first term and assign the remaining tolerance among the finitely many operations. Exact mathematical energy/intertwining statements concern the full self-adjoint source; finite implementation claims are energy constrained with these error bounds.

For controls with coefficient approximations $K_j^{\mathrm{fin}}(t)$, Duhamel gives operator error $\le\hbar^{-1}\int\lVert K_j^{\mathrm{fin}}-K_j\rVert\,dt$, and the corresponding channel diamond error is at most twice this. Pulse-time errors use the actual finite reached total Hamiltonian norm. Preparation errors, free-phase corrections, collar routing, readout overflow and clock errors are added before choosing the grade. Their parameters are fixed before the fresh unknown input.

##### 14.5.7n.12 Complete supplied-resource ledger and finite compiler

For a fixed finite source, finite energy grade, finite protocol tree and horizon $T_{\mathrm{service}}$, enumerate before execution:

- the mn physical cell placements, all rotor inertias and the complete code/momentum table;
- every installed local toric, spectator, kinetic and contact interaction and its finite control terminals;
- the source input preparation, retained errors, ancillary pure states and any phase/reference asymmetry stocks;
- every bounded pulse matrix, coefficient/phase program, tag, pointer, conditional branch and free-phase correction;
- the physical clock calibration and common retained clock history;
- every wire/transport path, routing pulse, waiting interval, source/provider work and torque reserve;
- all output states, failed branches, overflow records, measurement archives and completion/readout resources.

Use the actual fixed positive source tariffs of the supplied control/clock/record services. Write the complete invoice as the sum of their setup, stock, activation, physical service-time, work/peak reserve, measurement, archive and completion charges. The complete finite counts and finite reached matrix norms give an explicit finite upper bound for every category. If a control source reserves rather than expends a resource, keep both the reserved stock and the source-defined mean-work balance with the provider state; do not double-count returned energy as an independent dissipative expenditure.

A simple fully finite conservative envelope for work/peak allocation can be obtained directly from the reached Hamiltonian: if $E_{\max}$ bounds its norm and there are M switched pulses, reserve at least 2M $E_{\max}$ plus the sum of the bounded pulse norms and the independently charged clock/provider stock. Keep the calibrated mean-work functional and provider states inside that envelope. Required source accuracy and service horizon determine the finite precision, record length and controller count before input. The p-table and grades can be very large.

The finite grade is algorithmically bounded. Since the Leech lattice has minimum distance 2, disjoint unit balls centered at lattice points in the radius-$\sqrt{K}$ ball lie in the radius-$(\sqrt{K}+1)$ ball. Thus the number of such points is at most $(1+\sqrt{K})^{24}$. A local reached block on $r$ cells has dimension at most $[2^{24}(1+\sqrt{K_{\mathrm{prime}}})^{24}]^r$, with the larger explicit $K_{\mathrm{prime}}$ that includes its finite momentum-shift list. The full source-energy bound in that grade is

$$
\begin{aligned}
E_{\max} &\le mn(\Delta_e+\Delta_m) + \sum_{x,a}e_{x,a} \\
&\quad+ A_{\max} K' + E_{\mathrm{control,peak}}.
\end{aligned}
$$

A more conservative canonical-momentum reserve can replace $\sqrt{K_{\mathrm{prime}}}$ by $\sqrt{K_{\mathrm{prime}}}+2\sqrt{mn}\max_b|p(b)|$; it includes every known undressing/re-dressing intermediate momentum in the chosen preparation.

All positive source parameters used for a finite implementation come with certified finite interval names at the requested precision. This is a supplied calibration record. Every finite matrix entry, pulse phase and required precision is computed from those records and the predeclared protocol. On a reached block, if $\lVert H_{\mathrm{src}}\rVert\le E_{\max}$, interaction-picture differentiation gives

$$
\lVert dK/dt\rVert \le 2 E_{\max} \lVert K_I\rVert/\hbar + \lVert dK_I/dt\rVert.
$$

Therefore a time mesh h and finite coefficient precision b have a computable uniform Hamiltonian approximation error bounded by h times the displayed Lipschitz constant plus the matrix-dimension times the entry error. Duhamel then bounds the channel error as in Section 14.5.7n.11. Choose h and b before the unknown input. If positivity of an approximate pulse is needed, add its certified spectral error bound times the identity or project its finite matrix spectrum to the nonnegative axis; retain the additional positive reserve and phase.

Physical path lengths and an independently calibrated signal speed fix the routing lower bound on duration; allocate actual pulse windows and completion delays above that bound. Source-clock precision is chosen in advance from the same finite reached norm and accumulated-error bound. External phase/force programs and their work/torque supply are part of this driven source. ∎

<a id="theorem-1457p"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457p-binary-twisted-double-classification-on-the-marked-golay-leech-source"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7p (Binary Twisted-Double Classification on the Marked Golay–Leech Source).** Triangulate each square of the periodic material torus of Theorem 14.5.7n by its northeast diagonal. Fix a branching order, the cell geometry, and the marked on-site unitary $T=\bigotimes_x T_x$ of that theorem. At each vertex assign the first three encoded Golay coordinates to the horizontal, vertical and diagonal edges leaving it. Use a binary normalized-cocycle twisted-double Hamiltonian on these edges, with the full-Hilbert-space extension specified below. Retain all $21$ other spin coordinates per cell and each complete Leech rotor. For every fixed geometry, branching, calibration and source menu, the normalized-cocycle defect family has exactly two response classes. They have the four charges of the toric and double-semion doubles respectively. In both classes the on-site unitary gives exact full-operator, energy, dynamics and instrument intertwiners, and the complete check syndrome has a normalized instrument on all inputs.

##### Binary model and complete syndrome

Let $G=\mathbb F_2$ with addition and let $\omega:G^3\to U(1)$ be normalized. Its only potentially nonunit value is $\omega(1,1,1)$. The cocycle identity at $(1,1,1,1)$ says that this value squared equals one. Both possibilities obey the full identity: for $k=0,1$ put
$$
\omega_k(a,b,c)=(-1)^{kabc},
\tag{14.5.7p.1}
$$
and expand the five terms of the coboundary exponent over $\mathbb F_2$. They sum to zero. A normalized two-cochain has coboundary equal to one at $(1,1,1)$, so the two twists are inequivalent even under normalized cochain changes.

The undressed edge carrier is the tensor product of one qubit for each geometric edge with its chosen orientation, with reversal acting by inversion in $G$. For a triangle $f$ let $P_f$ project onto zero sum of its three edge labels. For a vertex $v$ let $P_{\operatorname{st}v}=\prod_{f\ni v}P_f$. On that flat-star subspace use the cocycle vertex move of Hu, Wan and Wu (2013), Section II and Appendix B. Insert a copied vertex immediately before $v$ in the local branching order with the new vertical edge label $1$. Flip all radial labels at $v$. For an incident ordered triangle $a<b<c$ with input edge labels $u=\ell_{ab}$, $w=\ell_{bc}$, the sign is $(-1)^{k\eta}$ with $\eta=uw$, $(1+u)w$ or $u(1+w)$ in $\mathbb F_2$ when the moved vertex is $a$, $b$ or $c$, respectively. These are the successive-edge cocycle exponents of the three ordered tent tetrahedra. The sign is unchanged by taking the inverse cocycle for negative orientation. Multiply the signs for all incident triangles. Call this finite-table unitary $U_v$ on $P_{\operatorname{st}v}\mathcal H_{\rm edges}$, and define the full-space positive check
$$
P_v=\tfrac12 P_{\operatorname{st}v}(I+U_v)P_{\operatorname{st}v},
\tag{14.5.7p.2}
$$
where the second term in this formula acts by zero on the complementary star sector. Thus a flux in an incident triangle sets the vertex check to zero. This choice is part of the all-energy source.

The local move preserves the sum on every face, because any affected face has exactly two radial labels flipped. Hence each star projection reduces every move on its active domain. The involution and adjacent-vertex commutation on common locally flat stars are the tent-move identities of Hu, Wan and Wu, Appendix B. They use flatness of the triangles in the two stars and the cocycle identity on the interpolating tent. On the common active subspace, each product in (14.5.7p.2) can therefore be reordered. On its complement one factor in either product is zero. Nonadjacent moves commute because a radial flip at either vertex does not change the inputs to the other's phase table. This proves that all the $P_f,P_v$ are commuting orthogonal projections on the full edge Hilbert space.

Let $P_j$ run through every face and vertex check. For each binary string $s$ put
$$
E_s=\prod_{j:s_j=1}P_j\prod_{j:s_j=0}(I-P_j).
\tag{14.5.7p.3}
$$
These mutually orthogonal projections sum to the identity. The complete instrument is $\rho\mapsto E_s\rho E_s$ with the label $s$ retained, including zero-projection labels as zero maps. To implement it, attach a fresh two-level pointer to each check and apply $P_j\otimes X+(I-P_j)\otimes I$ before reading that pointer. All outcomes are retained, and the Kraus product in each branch is $E_s$. For this triangulation there are $mn$ vertex checks and $2mn$ face checks. Thus a complete pass uses $3mn$ pointer bits.

An explicit full syndrome rank census is available even in the nonflat sectors. Fix the face configuration and enumerate the edge words with that face configuration. The active vertices are those with all incident faces flat. Inactive vertices have check value zero. The active move group is generated by the commuting signed involutions, with orbit action on edge words given by flips of the corresponding stars. In an orbit pick one word $a$. A character $\chi$ of the binary active move group occurs with multiplicity one in that orbit precisely when its restriction to the stabilizer of $a$ equals the product of cocycle signs on $a$ for each stabilizing flip. To prove this, for a common eigenvector the coefficient at $a$ determines all others in the orbit by the move eigenvalues and the signed permutation. Consistency around a word-preserving move is exactly the stated restriction. When it holds, that propagation defines a nonzero simultaneous eigenvector, and otherwise the coefficient at $a$ vanishes. The projector rank is the sum of these multiplicities over the finite orbits. This rule counts every configuration and retains all within-syndrome degrees of freedom.

##### Sector classification and the separating response

The all-flat, all-positive check space coincides with the ground space of the unmodified twisted-double model, since the extension agrees on every flat star. On a clean collar use the annular tube operators built from the same locally flat, cocycle-weighted tent matrices. The annular multiplication is the lattice gluing operation of Bullivant and Delcamp (2019), Sections 2.3, 3.3 and 4.2–4.4. Their tube algebra applies here because all moves in the collar use precisely the original flat-star table. The complementary disks and the off-shell vertex penalties are outside the collar. In the present binary case the tube algebra has basis $T_{a,x}$, with fixed boundary flux $a$ and connecting group label $x$. Gluing collars matches their flux and adds their connecting labels, with the phase given by the three cocycle factors:
$$
T_{a,x}T_{c,y}=\mathbf 1_{\{a=c\}}\,(-1)^{kaxy}T_{a,x+y},
\qquad T_{a,x}^*=(-1)^{kax}T_{a,x}.
$$
Thus $T_{a,0}$ is the flux projector, and the two central charge projections on flux $a$ are $(T_{a,0}\pm i^{-ka}T_{a,1})/2$. The operators here are in the retained annular algebra, acting on its clean-collar sector. A clean-collar test and its complementary outcome give their complete readout on the unrestricted source. For the torus twist use the modular operators of Hu, Wan and Wu, Sections IV–VIII. On a flux $a$ the two projective characters are
$$
\chi_{a,b}(x)=i^{kax}(-1)^{bx},\qquad a,b,x\in\{0,1\}.
\tag{14.5.7p.4}
$$
Indeed the multiplier on $x,y$ is $\omega_k(a,x,y)\omega_k(x,y,a)/\omega_k(x,a,y)=(-1)^{kaxy}$. The twisted group algebra on this flux therefore has one generator whose square is $(-1)^{ka}$ and two distinct scalar characters. The two fluxes and these two characters exhaust the four annular charge labels. Their dimensions are one. The torus ground space has dimension four, one per character, by the projective-character basis of the twisted double. Each charge has a compensating inverse charge, so all labels occur in the two-disk readout.

The normalized spin and full monodromy in these labels are
$$
\theta_{a,b}=i^{ka}(-1)^{ab},\qquad
M_{(a,b),(c,d)}=(-1)^{kac+ad+bc}.
\tag{14.5.7p.5}
$$
The spin is the projective character evaluated on its own flux. The monodromy is the product of the two characters on the two opposite fluxes; equivalently the modular matrix is $M/2$ in this real binary case. The four rows are distinct characters of $\mathbb F_2^2$. The Verlinde sum in this finite matrix, or the row multiplication law, gives fusion by addition of the two label bits. For $k=0$ the spins are $1,1,1,-1$; for $k=1$ they are $1,1,i,-i$. The latter are the vacuum, the boson and the two semions. This classification concerns the relative charge and braid responses on the stated collars. It keeps the all-energy check labels and their within-syndrome states on the full carrier.

Include the fixed-orientation torus twist in the readout menu. The ground-space twist operator has the four eigenvalues (14.5.7p.5) and extends by the identity on the complementary finite edge space; the ground test is part of its protocol. A two-level tag can control this extended operator. Measuring tag $X$ and $Y$ gives the real and imaginary parts of its ground-state matrix coefficient. In particular the nonzero imaginary coefficient on a semion twist eigenstate separates the two twists even if charge names are relabeled. The orientation, identity control and phase calibration are held fixed. A transport preserving the complete nominated responses must preserve this spin spectrum. Conversely there are only two normalized cocycles, with no further twist-table choices at fixed source data, so the two responses exhaust the binary normalized-cocycle family.

##### Marked carrier and source accounts

In the undressed coordinates put
$$
H_k=\sum_v \Delta_v(I-P_v)+\sum_f \Delta_f(I-P_f)
     +\sum_x\sum_{a=4}^{24}e_{x,a}\frac{I-Z_{x,a}}2
     +\sum_x H_{R0,x},
\qquad H_{k,{\rm src}}=T H_k T^* .
\tag{14.5.7p.6}
$$
All gap coefficients are positive independent source settings. The reference rotor energies are $A_x|r_x|^2$ as in Section 14.5.7n.3. Each undressed term is positive and commutes with the others. The bounded spin sum on the tensor-sum rotor domain is self-adjoint with compact resolvent. Its ground multiplicity is four and its gap is at least the least of all positive face, vertex, spectator and first rotor gaps. The same statements hold for the dressed source. The macrocells use $3mn$ logical edge spins, $21mn$ spectator spins and $mn$ full rotors. All physical code coordinates and all syndrome words of the marked Golay code are retained. The diagonal edges, their port ownership, all star collars and wire lengths are determined by the source geometry before choosing $k$.

The on-site inverse $T^*$ conjugates the full Hamiltonian and its spectral projections and therefore the unitary time evolution, with the same physical clock. It conjugates all states and bounded operators, and separately carries each set of macrocell support algebras onto the corresponding support algebras. For every operation with Kraus operators $L_i$ its dressed version has Kraus operators $T L_iT^*$, so the outcome probabilities and unnormalized conditional states, including those entangled with a retained reference, agree at every step. Induction extends this identity to all finite adaptive protocols with their complete records. Spectator and rotor projections and finite-rank binary effects separate all normal states by the energy-grade argument of Section 14.5.7n.11. The on-site identity does not identify the two twists with one another. It identifies each marked carrier with its own explicit twisted double and ancillary carriers.

The driven controls and tariffs are those of Sections 14.5.7n.10–14.5.7n.12, using the finite cocycle phase tables and check matrices above. A star gate acts on a finite bounded collar, and a syndrome readout uses one controlled check flip per check and its fresh tag. The modular probe is a specified finite edge-matrix control on its registered torus; its support and every required transport path are charged. At input energy at most $E$ choose rotor cutoff $\sum_x|r_x|^2\le K$ and a finite reached cutoff $K'$ including all specified momentum shifts. The number of local Fourier vectors is bounded by $(1+2\sqrt{K'})^{24}$ per macrocell, so a pulse on $r$ cells has reached carrier dimension at most $[2^{24}(1+\sqrt{K'})^{24}]^r$ before counting its fresh finite tags. The energy bound is
$$
E_{\max}\le \sum_v\Delta_v+\sum_f\Delta_f+\sum_{x,a}e_{x,a}
          +A_{\max}K'+E_{\mathrm{control,peak}} .
\tag{14.5.7p.7}
$$
For $M$ switched pulses a conservative reserve is $2ME_{\max}+\sum_j\|K_j\|$ plus the separately calibrated clock and provider stocks, where $\|K_j\|$ is the peak pulse Hamiltonian norm. Every pulse keeps its actual duration, support, table, coefficient precision, fresh tag, work and torque record. The invoice charges the cell and table setup, every stock and pulse activation, gates, wire travel and waiting time, all pointer/readout and overflow records, the provider work and the peak reserve. Returned reserves are kept as stocks. The ideal operator identities are exact; at finite grade the complete joint output trace error is bounded by $4\sqrt{E/(A_{\min}K)}+\sum_j\delta_j$ as in Section 14.5.7n.11, with $\delta_j$ the actual reached-block channel errors. Every term in the inherited locality proof applies because all terms in (14.5.7p.6) commute and the same on-site momentum shift list controls the rotor tails. ∎

<a id="theorem-1457q"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457q-local-extensions-and-energy-responses-of-the-leech-inversion-net"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7q (Local Extensions and Energy Responses of the Leech Inversion Net).** Let $\mathcal B=\mathcal A_{V_\Lambda^+}$ be the Leech inversion net in Proposition 14.5.7b-L, with its fixed inclusion of the stress tensor, vacuum, orientation and calibrated rotations. Consider irreducible local finite-index conformal extensions of this marked subnet. Include the bounded local instruments and the calibrated vacuum-space energy readout in the response menu. The energy readout is the global spectral readout of $H=\hbar\omega_0(L_0-1)$, with $\omega_0>0$. Equivalence intertwines the local inclusions over $\mathcal B$, the vacuum, the rotations and the declared instruments. There are precisely three equivalence classes, represented by $\mathcal B$, the Leech lattice net $\mathcal A_\Lambda$, and the Moonshine net $\mathcal A^\natural$. Both proper extensions have index two. Their restrictions as vacuum representations of $\mathcal B$ are $1\oplus e$ and $1\oplus m$, respectively. Here $e=V_\Lambda^-$ and $m=V_\Lambda^{T,+}$ are the sectors of Proposition 14.5.7b-L.

**Proof.** The finite-index extension correspondence assigns an irreducible local extension to a connected commutative $Q$-system in the DHR category of the marked subnet. The equivalence in the correspondence is the vacuum covariant equivalence over $\mathcal B$. The DHR category in this case is the pointed toric category, with fusion group $G=\{0,e,m,\epsilon\}\cong \mathbb Z_2^2$ and self-braidings $q(0)=q(e)=q(m)=1$, $q(\epsilon)=-1$. Use its model with trivial associator and write the algebra of a $Q$-system as $A=\bigoplus_{g\in G} A_g$. Connectedness gives $A_0=\mathbb C1$. The Frobenius cap $(a,b)\mapsto \langle1,m(a,b)\rangle$ is nondegenerate by the Frobenius identities and pairs $A_g$ only with $A_{-g}$. For any nonzero homogeneous $a\in A_g$, choose $b\in A_{-g}$ and rescale it so that $ab=1$. Left multiplication acts on a finite-dimensional vector space, so the right inverse is also a left inverse and $ba=1$. Multiplication by $b$ injects $A_g$ into $A_0$. Each occupied degree therefore has dimension one, products of homogeneous units are units, and the support is a subgroup. Commutativity with respect to the braiding applied to $a\otimes a$ gives $a^2=q(g)a^2$. Since $a^2$ is invertible, every occupied degree has self-braiding $1$. This leaves the supports $\{0\}$, $\{0,e\}$, and $\{0,m\}$.

For either order-two support, normalize the unit to length one and take a unit vector $v$ in the nonzero degree. The two unit products fix $v$, and $m(v,v)=\lambda1$. Specialness of the normalized $Q$-system gives $mm^\dagger=dI$. The eigenvalue on $v$ is $2$, while on $1$ it is $1+|\lambda|^2$. Thus $|\lambda|=1$, and a phase change of $v$ makes $\lambda=1$. The support determines the normalized $Q$-system up to unitary equivalence, and the object dimension gives the index. The actual conformal inclusions of Proposition 14.5.7b-L realize the two supports, by the decomposition of the untwisted Leech lattice vacuum and the Moonshine vacuum into the even Leech subnet and their respective additional summands. The two proper extensions are holomorphic. The Leech lattice is self-dual and the Moonshine net has global index one. The two vacuum restrictions to the marked subnet still differ. See Bischoff et al. (2014, Section 5.2.1), Dong and Xu (2004, Proposition 3.15) and Kawahigashi and Longo (2004, Theorem 3.6 and Example 3.8).

The unitary for two representatives of the same marked extension intertwines each bounded local algebra and the calibrated rotations. For a bounded Kraus family $\{K_{a,j}\}$ in a menu instrument, it sends $K_{a,j}$ to $UK_{a,j}U^\dagger$ and the state $\rho$ to $U\rho U^\dagger$. Trace probabilities agree for the entire instrument, including compositions and postselected states. Spectral calculus carries $P_n=1_{\{n\}}(L_0)$ to the corresponding calibrated projection. The two proper extensions share the declared common-subnet transports of Proposition 14.5.7b-L, and their full energy responses already differ.

The Leech lattice has no squared-norm-two vectors. Its $\theta$ series is a weight-twelve modular form with constant coefficient one and vanishing linear coefficient, so it is $E_4^3-720\Delta$. From $E_4=1+240t+2160t^2+\cdots$ and $\Delta=t-24t^2+\cdots$, the quadratic coefficient is $3(2160)+3(240)^2+720(24)=196560$. These are the squared-norm-four vectors. The Leech vacuum weight-one space consists of $24$ single oscillators, all odd under inversion. At weight two there are $24$ single second-mode oscillators, $\binom{25}{2}=300$ symmetric pairs of first-mode oscillators, and $196560$ lattice states. The first term is odd, the second even, and each inversion pair of opposite norm-four vectors contributes one even state. The vacuum character of the Moonshine net gives weight-one dimension $0$ and weight-two dimension $196884$. Hence the vacuum-space spectral ranks are

| Net | $\operatorname{rank} P_0$ | $\operatorname{rank} P_1$ | $\operatorname{rank} P_2$ |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| $\mathcal B$ | 1 | 0 | 98580 |
| $\mathcal A_\Lambda$ | 1 | 24 | 196884 |
| $\mathcal A^\natural$ | 1 | 0 | 196884 |

One finite outcome witness uses the Gibbs preparation of the vacuum representation and the four energy effects $P_0,P_1,P_2,I-P_0-P_1-P_2$. For $\beta>0$ set $t=e^{-\beta\hbar\omega_0}$ and $Z_X=\operatorname{Tr}t^{L_0-1}$ on the net $X$. The Leech lattice character converges for $0<t<1$, the fixed subnet is its invariant subspace, and the Moonshine character is $J$ on the same domain. Thus $t^{L_0-1}/Z_X$ is a normalized preparation in all three cases. For the four-outcome projection instrument,
$$
\frac{\Pr_X(P_n)}{\Pr_X(P_0)}
=\operatorname{rank}(P_n)t^n,\qquad n=1,2 .
$$
At a single common $\beta$ these ratios distinguish the three classes. This witness uses the bounded global spectral effects of the declared calibrated energy readout.

For any of the three nets, a unital local-algebra intertwiner into a finite matrix carrier would restrict to such an intertwiner from the type-III local factor of $\mathcal B$. The two isometries with orthogonal ranges in that factor would map to two matrix isometries, hence to unitaries, although their cross product $v_1^\dagger v_2$ vanishes. This is impossible. An all-energy intertwiner into any finite-dimensional carrier is independently impossible. The common Virasoro vacuum has $L_{-n}\Omega$ at weight $n$ with squared norm $2(n^3-n)>0$ for every integer $n\geq2$. The calibrated Hamiltonian thus has infinitely many distinct eigenvalues in every class. Both obstructions apply with any finite-dimensional matrix spectator. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457r"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457r-finite-group-orbifold-extension-responses"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7r (Finite-Group Orbifold Extension Responses).** Fix a nontrivial completely rational holomorphic conformal net $\mathcal A$, a faithful action of a finite group $G$ by vacuum- and conformal-covariant automorphisms, and its marked fixed-point subnet $\mathcal B=\mathcal A^G$. Fix a realization of the sector category of $\mathcal B$ as the unitary Drinfeld centre $Z(\mathrm{Vec}_G^\omega)$, where $\omega$ is the normalized anomaly cocycle. Give each irreducible local finite-index conformal extension of $\mathcal B$ the same response menu as in Theorem 14.5.7q, with energy $H=\hbar\omega_0(L_0-c/24)$ at central charge $c$ and fixed $\omega_0>0$. The equivalence fixes the marked subnet and intertwines its inclusion, the extension local algebras, the vacuum and the calibrated rotations. The following finite classification gives all response-preserving equivalence classes over this fixed $\mathcal B$.

1. Choose $K\leq G$ and $N\trianglelefteq K$. In $Z(\mathrm{Vec}_K^{\omega|_K})$ take a one-dimensional space with a unit basis vector $e_n$ in each degree $n\in N$. The product and the projective conjugation action have the form
   $$
   e_ne_m=\gamma(n,m)e_{nm},\qquad k(e_n)=\varepsilon_k(n)e_{knk^{-1}},
   \tag{14.5.7r.1}
   $$
   with phases $\gamma,\varepsilon$. In the convention of Davydov and Simmons write $n^k=knk^{-1}$ and use the scalar factors
   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   P(k,l;n)&=\omega(k,l,n)^{-1}\omega(k,n^l,l)\omega(n^{kl},k,l)^{-1},\\
   Q(k;n,m)&=\omega(k,n,m)\omega(n^k,k,m)^{-1}\omega(n^k,m^k,k).
   \end{aligned}
   \tag{14.5.7r.2}
   $$
   The unit and identity action have coefficients one. The remaining equations, for $k,l\in K$ and $n,m,r\in N$, are
   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   \gamma(m,r)\gamma(n,mr)&=\omega(n,m,r)\gamma(n,m)\gamma(nm,r),\\
   \varepsilon_{kl}(n)&=P(k,l;n)\varepsilon_k(n^l)\varepsilon_l(n),\\
   \gamma(n,m)\varepsilon_k(nm)&=Q(k;n,m)\varepsilon_k(n)\varepsilon_k(m)\gamma(n^k,m^k),\\
   \gamma(n,m)&=\varepsilon_n(m)\gamma(m^n,n).
   \end{aligned}
   \tag{14.5.7r.3}
   $$
   They are the associativity, projective action, product equivariance and braided commutativity equations. Transfer the algebra (14.5.7r.1) from the $K$-centre to the $G$-centre by the coset transfer of Davydov and Simmons. Its degree-one part is $\mathbb C(G/K)$ and its corner at the identity coset is (14.5.7r.1). The resulting algebra gives the corresponding extension.

2. Two data sets represent the same class exactly when there is a $G$-equivariant bijection of their coset sets such that the induced corner identification carries the grading, product and projective stabilizer action of one to the other. For this test, take each possible image of the identity coset and compute that target corner in the transferred algebra. Once the coset and corner are fixed, an identification is a phase $e_n\mapsto u_ne'_n$ in each occupied degree. If the corner support agrees, the conditions are
   $$
   u_{nm}\gamma(n,m)=u_nu_m\gamma'(n,m),\qquad
   \varepsilon_k(n)u_{knk^{-1}}=u_n\varepsilon'_k(n).
   \tag{14.5.7r.4}
   $$
   The target phases here are those in the selected corner, in the common centre convention. In particular the equivalence test includes the cocycle factors already present in the transfer.

3. The Jones index is $[G:K]|N|$. The extended DHR category is $Z(\mathrm{Vec}_{K/N}^{\bar\omega})$, with $\bar\omega$ calculated from the data by Davydov and Simmons, equation (20). Restriction of every extended sector to $\mathcal B$ is computed by the projective character branching rule of Evans and Gannon, Theorem 1(b)(iii) and equation (3.3). The extension is holomorphic precisely when $N=K$. In particular such an extension with $N=K$ exists precisely when $\omega|_K$ is a coboundary. For a fixed corner, its possible trivializations form a torsor for $H^2(K,\mathbb T)$ before the coset equivalences.

*Proof.* The finite-group orbifold theorem identifies the sector category with the indicated twisted centre. An irreducible local finite-index extension gives a connected commutative $Q$-system in that category, and equivalent $Q$-systems give vacuum covariant unitary equivalence over the marked subnet. The classification of connected étale algebras in the twisted centre gives the degree-one function algebra on a transitive $G$-set, hence $\mathbb C(G/K)$. At an idempotent corresponding to a coset, the corner is an algebra in the corresponding stabilizer centre with its degree-one part one-dimensional. The nondegenerate multiplication pairing of opposite degrees makes each occupied corner degree one-dimensional. The occupied degrees form a normal subgroup of the stabilizer. Multiplication and the stabilizer action in unit bases give (14.5.7r.1). The phase formulation gives the unitary normalized algebra. Equivalently, over $\mathbb C$ one may divide the nonzero structure constants by their moduli. The logarithms of the moduli satisfy the homogeneous real equations of the unitary phase system. Averaging the real group cochain for the product removes it by positive rephasing since $N$ is finite. The remaining real action coefficients are then additive homomorphisms $N\to\mathbb R$ by product equivariance, so they vanish. The classification and coset transfer yield the converse for every solution of the phase equations. See Davydov and Simmons (2016, Theorems 2.11, 2.19 and 2.20), Evans and Gannon (2018, Proposition 1, Theorem 1 and §3.2) and Bischoff et al. (2014, §5.2.1).

Any isomorphism of the graded algebras permutes the degree-one minimal idempotents $G$-equivariantly. The algebra decomposes over those idempotents and all corners are carried into one another by the group action. Thus an isomorphism is determined by the image of the identity coset and its map on that corner. Conversely an equivariant graded corner isomorphism extends by coset transfer. Its one-dimensional grade maps give (14.5.7r.4). A nonzero complex rescaling satisfying the product condition has modulus a homomorphism $N\to\mathbb R_{>0}$, hence unit modulus. For fixed subgroup data the equations for phases are integer linear equations in phase angles modulo $1$. Their solution set is empty or a translate of the kernel of a torus homomorphism. The connected part of that kernel disappears under the phase changes (14.5.7r.4), by the real averaging and equivariance argument above applied to its tangent space. Smith normal form therefore tests consistency and gives finitely many phase classes, with the finite coset equivalences applied afterwards. The category dimension of the transferred algebra is its underlying vector-space dimension $[G:K]|N|$, giving the index. The local-module classification gives the quoted quotient centre and the branching rule. The trivial quotient has a single sector, while a nontrivial finite group has a Drinfeld centre of global dimension $|K/N|^2>1$. For $N=K$ braided commutativity fixes $\varepsilon$ from $\gamma$, giving the coboundary condition and the stated torsor. See Davydov and Simmons (2016, Proposition 2.15, Lemma 2.16 and Corollary 2.21) and Evans and Gannon (2018, Theorem 1(b)(iii) and Corollary 2).

The unitary for two representatives of one class transports the vacuum, local Kraus instruments, calibrated energy projections and all compositions by conjugation, including instruments with retained reference systems. Conversely an equivalence of the declared marked inclusions and evolutions induces the same dual canonical endomorphism with its multiplication and unit, hence a $Q$-system isomorphism and (14.5.7r.4). Retaining only the quotient DHR category or a matrix of modular data would remove the extension multiplication from this test. If the common subnet has a type-III local factor, there is no exact unital local-algebra intertwiner into a finite matrix carrier, by the two orthogonal isometries argument of Theorem 14.5.7q. The calibrated energy has infinitely many distinct values when $c>0$, since the common descendants $L_{-n}\Omega$ have squared norms $c(n^3-n)/12$ for $n\ge2$. The finite energy obstruction also holds with finite matrix spectators. $\square$

<a id="corollary-1457r1"></a>

<a id="corollary-1457r1-prime-cyclic-anomaly-test"></a>**Corollary 14.5.7r.1 (Prime Cyclic Anomaly Test).** If $G=\mathbb Z_p$ for a prime $p$, there are two marked extension classes for a nontrivial anomaly and three for the trivial anomaly. The two classes common to both cases are the fixed-point net itself and the original holomorphic net. The additional class for the trivial anomaly is the extension by the bosonic flux subgroup. Both proper extensions have index $p$. The two different vacuum restrictions over the fixed-point subnet distinguish them on the marked response menu.

*Proof.* The only choices for $(K,N)$ are $(G,\{1\}),(\{1\},\{1\})$ and $(G,G)$. The first two have the trivial corner and give $\mathcal B$ and $\mathcal A$ by the coset classification. The last exists exactly when the anomaly vanishes. For a cyclic group, every ordinary scalar 2-cocycle is a coboundary. In its twisted group algebra, the generator has a scalar $p$th power, and a phase redefinition makes that power one and gives an untwisted group basis. Thus the last extension, when it exists, is unique over the fixed subnet. In the trivial anomaly gauge its vacuum restriction is the direct sum of the $p$ flux sectors with trivial electric character. The original $\mathcal A$ has the $p$ electric characters at identity flux from the regular representation on the cosets of the identity subgroup. The two lists differ for every $p\ge2$. Both lists are computed from the transfer or the branching rule. A concrete nontrivial case uses the order-three cyclic permutation of three copies of the holomorphic $E_8$ lattice net of central charge eight, whose action has nontrivial anomaly. The order-three cyclic permutation on three copies of the holomorphic Leech lattice net of central charge twenty-four gives the trivial-anomaly case. See Bischoff (2018, Proposition 3.1) and Evans and Gannon (2018, Theorem 2). $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457s"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457s-across-base-classification-of-lattice-inversion-responses"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7s (Across-Base Classification of Lattice Inversion Responses).** For each positive-definite even lattice $L$ of finite positive rank, let $\mathcal A_L$ be its lattice conformal net and let $\mathcal B_L$ be the fixed net for a unitary lift of lattice inversion. Use the oriented local interval algebras, vacuum, stress tensor and calibrated rotations of Theorem 14.5.7q, with a common $\omega_0>0$ and $H=\hbar\omega_0(L_0-\operatorname{rank}(L)/24)$. In comparing two base nets, retain all their bounded local instruments and global calibrated energy projections. The comparison is a vacuum- and stress-preserving unitary sending the algebra at each interval to the algebra at that interval. In this first comparison no parent lattice extension or sector is marked.

1. The inversion nets $\mathcal B_L$ and $\mathcal B_M$ are response-equivalent if and only if $L$ and $M$ are isometric or $L$ and $M$ are the two even unimodular lattices of rank sixteen. Thus $\mathcal B_{E_8\oplus E_8}$ and $\mathcal B_{D_{16}^+}$ have the same declared full response. The corresponding parent nets are inequivalent.
2. On the class in part 1, add the single marked DHR sector $j_L$ of $\mathcal B_L$ that occurs in the inversion-odd subspace of the parent vacuum representation. Require an equivalence to carry $j_L$ to $j_M$. The resulting response-equivalent pairs are exactly the lattice isometries. Equivalently the marked parent inclusions $\mathcal B_L\subset\mathcal A_L$ and $\mathcal B_M\subset\mathcal A_M$ are equivalent exactly when $L$ and $M$ are isometric.
3. The mixed comparison $\mathcal B_L\simeq\mathcal A_M$, on the base-net menu of part 1, holds exactly when there is a doubly even binary code $C$ such that $L$ is isometric to $L_A(C)$ and $M$ is isometric to $L_B(C)$. For an orthogonal basis $(\alpha_i)$ with $(\alpha_i,\alpha_i)=2$, these code lattices are
   $$
   \begin{aligned}
   L_A(C)&=\sum_i\mathbb Z\alpha_i+
         \sum_{c\in C}\mathbb Z\,\tfrac12\sum_i c_i\alpha_i,\\
   L_B(C)&=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb Z(\alpha_i+\alpha_j)+
         \sum_{c\in C}\mathbb Z\,\tfrac12\sum_i c_i\alpha_i .
   \end{aligned}
   \tag{14.5.7s.1}
   $$
   A code is doubly even when every word has weight divisible by four. The zero code of length one gives the nonholomorphic-parent example $\mathcal B_{\sqrt2\mathbb Z}\simeq\mathcal A_{2\sqrt2\mathbb Z}$.

*Proof.* The simple unitary lattice VOA $V_L$ is strongly local and gives the lattice conformal net $\mathcal A_L$ by the lattice locality and identification theorems. Its unitary inversion automorphism commutes with the conformal structure, so the unitary fixed VOA $V_L^+$ gives $\mathcal B_L$ by the fixed-subnet theorem. The reconstruction of the VOA from its conformal net identifies its fields by the local algebras and the vacuum on the finite-energy space. Therefore a unitary intertwining the declared nets and conformal structure induces a VOA isomorphism. In the other direction, transport the invariant positive form along a VOA isomorphism. The essential uniqueness of the normalized unitary form gives a VOA automorphism that makes the isomorphism unitary. Its Hilbert extension intertwines the closed smeared fields and hence the local algebras, the vacuum and the stress tensor. It intertwines $L_0$ and its spectral calculus, and conjugation transports every Kraus operation on the complete menu, with arbitrary retained reference and composition. Thus the response equivalence in part 1 is exactly the corresponding VOA isomorphism. The central charge distinguishes ranks and fixes the energy subtraction. See Carpi et al. (2015, Proposition 5.19, Proposition 7.6 and Theorem 9.2) and Carpi et al. (2023, Theorem 7.19 and its lattice-net identification).

Shimakura's inversion classification now gives exactly the isometric cases and the two even unimodular lattices of rank sixteen. The ordinary lattice VOA classification recovers a lattice up to isometry, proving the inequivalence of the two parents. For the mixed test the same reconstruction reduces the question to the mixed VOA isomorphism criterion, which is precisely the Construction A and B pair (14.5.7s.1). The positive example uses the rank-one lattice of squared generator norm two, which has a nontrivial discriminant group and thus a nonholomorphic parent. See Shimakura (2011, Proposition 3.1 and Theorems 3.9 and 3.10) and Dong and Xu (2004, Proposition 3.15).

For the marked comparison, the fixed-point inclusion for the faithful order-two action has index two, and the parent restriction has sector sum $1\oplus j_L$, with $j_L$ a dimension-one sector of order two. The $Q$-system with this underlying object is unique up to unitary equivalence when it exists. In normalized bases, the unit determines the products with $1$, and the only other component is the scalar map $j_L\otimes j_L\to1$ in a one-dimensional intertwiner space. Nondegeneracy makes it nonzero and specialness fixes its modulus. Rephasing the degree-$j_L$ basis by a square root absorbs any phase difference. Thus a base isomorphism preserving this sector extends to the two parents by the extension correspondence. Parent equivalence forces lattice isometry. Conversely an isometry lifts to a lattice VOA isomorphism and carries the inversion grading after conjugating the possible choices of inversion lift. For clarity, two lifts of the same inversion on $V_L$ differ by a character of the lattice on momentum states, acting trivially on oscillator states. The unitary character torus conjugates inversion by the square of its character. Each character of the free lattice has a square root, so the lifts give conjugate marked inclusions. This proves the converse and shows that the exceptional unmarked equivalence cannot carry the two parent-odd sectors to one another. See Bischoff et al. (2014, §5.2.1) and Shimakura (2011, §1.2).

The exceptional pair also gives a precise limit on what a vacuum energy-only menu decides about a parent. The two rank-sixteen even unimodular $\theta$ series are equal because both are normalized weight-eight modular forms and this space is one-dimensional. The vacuum characters of both parent nets are therefore $\Theta_L/\eta^{16}$ with the same coefficients at every grade. Their weight-one field algebras under the zero-mode bracket are, respectively, the compact forms of $\mathfrak e_8\oplus\mathfrak e_8$ and $\mathfrak{so}_{32}$. The norm-two lattice vectors generate these root algebras and the Cartan currents supply the rank. The first weight-one bracket has two simple ideals and the second one. This is a distinction preserved by the reconstructed local field response, even with equal full vacuum energy multiplicities. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457t"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457t-marked-finite-group-doubles-on-the-golay-leech-carrier"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7t (Marked Finite-Group Doubles on the Golay–Leech Carrier).** Fix the geometry, on-site encoder and calibration of Theorem 14.5.7p. On each of the three outgoing edges at a cell allocate $k$ encoded bits, with $1\le k\le8$, and use the unused $24-3k$ bits as individually retained spectators. In each edge register use the regular basis of a finite group $G$ of order $2^k$. Consider the untwisted finite-group-double Hamiltonians with all flatness and vertex projectors as defined below, including all sectors outside their joint ground space. Retain the edge basis instrument and the full family of left-translation gates on a marked oriented edge, with arbitrary relabelling of the states and translation commands allowed. Then the response-equivalence classes at fixed source calibration are exactly the isomorphism classes of these groups. A terminating census and full syndrome rank formula are given below. The clean-collar sector responses of each source are the finite Drinfeld double of $G$. For the abelian subfamily the clean-collar fusion table by itself distinguishes the group even if the translation mark is forgotten.

*Hamiltonian and all-energy response.* Set $\mathcal H_{\rm edges}=\ell^2(G^{\mathcal E})$, using inverse labels for reversed edge orientations. The vertex action $U_v(a)$ multiplies an outgoing edge on the left by $a$ and an incoming edge on the right by $a^{-1}$. Distinct vertex actions commute since the actions at opposite ends of a shared edge are left and right multiplication. For each face $f$ let $P_f$ be the diagonal projection onto oriented boundary product $1_G$. Each vertex action preserves this predicate, since it either leaves the based holonomy unchanged or conjugates it. Thus the face projectors commute with $P_v=|G|^{-1}\sum_{a\in G}U_v(a)$, which is the orthogonal projection onto the vertex-invariant subspace. All these checks commute on the full carrier. The Hamiltonian is the positive sum of check defects, with the same positive coefficients and independent spectator and rotor energies as in (14.5.7p.6), using the spectator indices outside the edge allocation. It has the self-adjoint tensor-sum domain and compact resolvent of the marked rotor source. Its gap is at least the least positive check, spectator or first rotor energy.

For a face-status word $F$, let $X_F\subseteq G^{\mathcal E}$ be its edge configurations and let $U(\boldsymbol a)=\prod_vU_v(a_v)$. A vertex-status word $S\subseteq\mathcal V$ specifies exactly the vertices with check value one. The multiplicity of the edge syndrome $(F,S)$ is
$$
\sum_{S\subseteq J\subseteq\mathcal V}(-1)^{|J|-|S|}|G|^{-|J|}
\sum_{\boldsymbol a\in G^J}
\big|\{x\in X_F:U(\boldsymbol a)x=x\}\big|.
\tag{14.5.7t.1}
$$
Here $a_v=1_G$ for $v\notin J$. Expanding the factors $I-P_v$ gives inclusion–exclusion, and the trace of the resulting permutation on $X_F$ counts its fixed words. The simultaneous projections, including zero projections, sum to the identity. The controlled check flips of Theorem 14.5.7p implement their full instrument, retaining every label and every within-syndrome state. There is also a full basis instrument for edge registers, including all group elements, implemented by reversible basis copying to a ready pointer. The two instruments are distinct protocols. Ground configurations are flat connections. Quotienting by the vertex action fixes a spanning tree and leaves two commuting torus holonomies up to simultaneous conjugation. The invariant ground space therefore has one basis vector per simultaneous conjugacy orbit of commuting pairs. Its dimension is
$$
\sum_{[g]\in\operatorname{Conj}(G)}\big|\operatorname{Irr}(C_G(g))\big|,
\tag{14.5.7t.2}
$$
