since after fixing the first conjugacy class the second holonomy is classified by conjugation in its centralizer. A finite group's irreducible character count equals its conjugacy-class count.

*Collar charges.* Use a clean annulus, with complementary outcome recorded in each collar instrument. The finite-group ribbon operations are those of the untwisted double (Kitaev 2003). In the annular operator algebra, $T_{g,a}$ records the boundary flux $g$ and carries the transport $a$. Gluing two collars gives $T_{g,a}T_{h,b}=\mathbf1_{g=aha^{-1}}T_{g,ab}$ and adjunction gives $T_{g,a}^*=T_{a^{-1}ga,a^{-1}}$. The flux can change only within a conjugacy class. Choose a representative $g_0$ of a class $C$ and a transporter from $g_0$ to each element of $C$. Every arrow in that block then has two endpoints in $C$ and one stabilizer element in $C_G(g_0)$. The gluing and involution identify the block with $M_{|C|}(\mathbb C[C_G(g_0)])$. Hence the complete simple charges are $(C,\pi)$ with $\pi\in\operatorname{Irr}(C_G(g_0))$, of quantum dimension $|C|\dim\pi$. Fusion is given by tensoring the ribbon representations with the product flux and simultaneous group transport, and exchange by the flux acting on the crossed charge. In particular the spin is the scalar $\chi_\pi(g_0)/\dim\pi$. These matrices use only the finite multiplication table and centralizer representations. For abelian $G$ they give the group of simple charges $G\times\widehat G$, with spin $(g,\chi)\mapsto\chi(g)$ and mutual monodromy $((g,\chi),(h,\psi))\mapsto\chi(h)\psi(g)$. The primary cyclic multiplicities in $G\times\widehat G$ are twice those in $G$, by the primary cyclic decomposition of a finite abelian group. Thus the measured abelian fusion group determines $G$.

The dihedral group of order eight and the quaternion group both give ground dimension 22. Their spin multiplicities in the order $(1,-1,i,-i)$ are respectively $(14,6,1,1)$ and $(12,4,3,3)$. For both groups the identity and nonidentity central conjugacy classes have five irreducible representations each, and the nonidentity central element has eigenvalue $-1$ only in the two-dimensional irreducible representation. For the dihedral group the three noncentral centralizers are $\mathbb Z_4$ and two copies of $\mathbb Z_2^2$, while for the quaternion group all three are $\mathbb Z_4$. Evaluation at the representative gives one of each spin on each $\mathbb Z_4$ block and two of each real spin on each $\mathbb Z_2^2$ block. The same controlled torus twist used in Theorem 14.5.7p therefore separates the two nonabelian sources even without the translation mark.

*Source equivalence and implementation.* Prepare any basis state $|x\rangle$ on the marked edge, apply the left-translation command labelled by $a$, and read the resulting basis value. The retained deterministic result is $ax$. The command composition tables are measurable by two applications and a basis readout. Faithfulness of the left regular action implies that a response equivalence induces a bijection of commands preserving composition, and therefore a group isomorphism. An isomorphism conversely relabels each edge basis, transports the vertex actions, face projectors and ribbon operators, and intertwines every Hamiltonian, readout and time evolution. Transport the entire control menu, including the orientation, local supports, source units, and complements of all registered checks. The isomorphism acts trivially on the spectators and full rotors and conjugates arbitrary retained-input and reference protocols. Enumerating all binary tables on $2^k$ labels, testing the group laws and dividing by label permutations terminates for every $k$. This is a complete census on this finite native encoding grammar. If a full matrix control uses the group entries in a finite table, the table is retained along with its address, pulse and work records.

The dressed implementation conjugates the full action by $T$ as in Theorem 14.5.7p. Each $k$-bit edge uses its whole allocated subregister. A controlled permutation can use a finite self-adjoint logarithm with operator norm at most $\pi\hbar/\tau$ on its positive allotted interval $\tau$. Check flips, reversible readout, torus and collar controls retain the same clocks, support and finite resources as that theorem with the actual finite group tables and pointer sizes. The spin dimension per cell stays $2^{24}$. For rotor modes at the reached cutoff $K'$ the per-cell dimension is bounded by $2^{24}(1+2\sqrt{K'})^{24}$ before any fresh tags. Input truncation and summed pulse error are bounded by $4\sqrt{E/(A_{\min}K)}+\sum_j\delta_j$ on the same finite-grade conditions. Conjugation preserves the registered local support on every macrocell, so it transports the complete instruments with arbitrary retained reference, including their complementary records. There is no projection onto the flat subspace in the Hamiltonian, qualification or comparison. ∎

<a id="theorem-1457u"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457u-marked-finite-group-twists-on-the-golay-leech-carrier"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7u (Marked Finite-Group Twists on the Golay–Leech Carrier).** Use the group-register allocation, fixed geometry, clock and source calibration of Theorem 14.5.7t, with a normalized $U(1)$ three-cocycle $\omega$ of the allocated finite group $G$. Extend the binary flat-star rule of Theorem 14.5.7p to this group by the tent moves below. Retain the marked group multiplication tests and the coherent trivalent-junction recoupling tests. Allow a common relabelling of the binary junction basis phases and their associated local frame on the edge controls. Reserve each control support with its adjacent faces for this frame transport. For each fixed calibration the complete response equivalence of this marked source family is
$$
(G,\omega)\sim(G',\omega')
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\exists\,\varphi:G\xrightarrow{\simeq}G'\;\;[\omega]=[\varphi^*\omega']\text{ in }H^3(G,U(1)).
\tag{14.5.7u.1}
$$
There is a terminating census for each allocation. The comparison includes the full finite-energy carrier and all vertex and face syndromes. The clean-collar charges, fusion and braiding are those of the twisted double of $(G,\omega)$.

*Flat-star tent action and full syndrome.* On an oriented edge record $x\in G$, with the reverse orientation carrying $x^{-1}$. For a face use its oriented flatness projector $P_f$. For a vertex $v$ write $B_v=\prod_{f\ni v}P_f$. On this star sector, for $a\in G$ perform the change of frame at $v$ which left-multiplies its outgoing labels by $a$ and right-multiplies its incoming labels by $a^{-1}$. The phase is the oriented product of $\omega(x_{01},x_{12},x_{23})$ over the local tent tetrahedra, with the vertical edge carrying the change of frame. Use the same convention for all stars. The tent construction supplies a representation $U_v$ of $G$ on $B_v\mathcal H_{\rm edges}$. Indeed gluing two consecutive tents and replacing them by the one with product vertical label has ratio a product of normalized cocycle identities on four-simplices. The flat faces ensure that each intermediate diagonal is the product of its consecutive labels. Two adjacent tents commute on the joint flat-star subspace by filling the two orders of the tent moves with these same identities. Nonadjacent tents leave each other's inputs fixed. These are the local flat-star identities of Hu et al. (2013).

Set $P_v=B_v|G|^{-1}\sum_a U_v(a)B_v$, with the contribution zero outside the flat-star sector. The full-space check is an orthogonal projection. Each gauge move preserves all face-status words, since a based holonomy changes by conjugation or stays fixed. Thus the active-star sectors reduce every relevant move, and the checks commute everywhere by the common-flat-star identities. Use the positive sum of their defects with the native spectator and rotor Hamiltonians. This retains all edge configurations, including the sectors with flux in a star. For fixed face word $F$, let $V_F$ be its active vertices, $X_F$ its edge words and $S\subset V_F$ the vertices required to have check one. The exact joint edge multiplicity is
$$
\sum_{S\subseteq A\subseteq V_F}(-1)^{|A\setminus S|}|G|^{-|A|}
\sum_{\mathbf a\in G^A}\;\sum_{\substack{x\in X_F\\ \mathbf a x=x}}
\langle x|U_A(\mathbf a)|x\rangle,\qquad
U_A(\mathbf a)=\prod_{v\in A}U_v(a_v).
\tag{14.5.7u.2}
$$
Here a positive check at an inactive vertex has multiplicity zero. The formula expands the joint spectral projector over the active vertex complements and takes the trace of its monomial unitary terms. It keeps all nontrivial ranks and includes the zero outcomes in the complete normalized syndrome instrument. A check pass uses one fresh pointer per face and vertex as in Theorem 14.5.7p.

*Collar and junction responses.* A clean collar uses exactly the flat tent weights. Its tube multiplication is the cocycle-weighted gluing of the annular transport groupoid of $G$ acting by conjugation. A conjugacy class represented by $g$ gives a matrix block over the twisted group algebra of $C_G(g)$, with multiplier
$$
\alpha_g(a,b)=\frac{\omega(g,a,b)\omega(a,b,g)}{\omega(a,g,b)}
\quad(a,b\in C_G(g)).
\tag{14.5.7u.3}
$$
This follows by selecting transporters from $g$ to each conjugate and using the three tetrahedra of the composition prism for the stabilizer loops. The simple charges are exactly the irreducible $\alpha_g$-projective representations of these centralizers, with dimensions $|[g]|\dim\pi$. Tensor and exchange are the gluing operations of the same tube algebra, which give the finite Drinfeld centre of the pointed category with associator $\omega$. These operations use the same flat-collar qualification and complementary outcome on the full source. See Bullivant and Delcamp (2019).

The marked junction response uses the boundary group-register basis. The isometric fusion register sends $|a,b\rangle|1_G\rangle$ to $|a,b\rangle|ab\rangle$, retaining both original labels. Comparing the two associations of three composable boundary labels in a coherent register is the tent response of one ordered tetrahedron. Its relative matrix on the retained input labels is diagonal with entry $\omega(a,b,c)$, with orientation fixed. Indeed the two boundary triangulations have successive edges $a,b,c$ and all other edge labels are their partial products. Their oriented interpolation is the one tetrahedron by the same tent rule. All triples can be supplied as flat edge words on a disk, by setting the successive vertex values to $1_G,a,ab,abc$. Identity junctions fix the unit conventions. On such a disk the recoupling protocol uses reversible group multiplication and a single finite diagonal phase table from the source action. An independently clocked coherent comparison of the two histories reads the real and imaginary parts, with the same retained labels and pointers on the two routes. Flat qualification and failure both have output records. A rephasing of the normalized binary junction for $(a,b)$ by a two-cochain changes this relative response by its coboundary. The tetrahedron table is tested coherently; the bulk clean-collar fusion multiplicities alone do not specify the marked junction response.

*Completeness of equivalence.* The deterministic translation tests of Theorem 14.5.7t force an isomorphism $\varphi$ of the marked groups. In the boundary-junction comparison, each labelled channel of fusion is a one-dimensional junction line, once the retained input and product labels are fixed. Unitary transport of the junctions therefore gives a normalized phase $\beta(a,b)$ for each pair, with a single choice shared by both uses of that pair. Transport of the coherent three-input comparison then requires $\varphi^*\omega'/\omega=d\beta$ up to the common inverse convention for $\beta$. This proves necessity.

For sufficiency, first relabel all edges by the isomorphism. A normalized two-cochain $\beta$ transporting the cocycles gives a full-carrier diagonal unitary. On an edge word take the product of $\beta(x_{01},x_{12})$ with orientation signs over precisely its flat faces. In a move on an active star every incident face is flat and remains flat. The product of $d\beta$ over its oriented tent is the ratio of the two face products. The vertical side faces cancel across adjacent incident triangles, leaving the initial and final faces. Thus this unitary conjugates the two active-star tables and commutes with every $P_f$ and $B_v$. Outside an active star both checks are zero. Conjugation transports the full Hamiltonian on every face word. Transport the junction bases by the same cochain. In the marked menu the coherent controls, including their generators and references, use that common transported frame. A conjugated control acts within the original reserved set: the face phase gates commute with one another, and only gates whose faces meet the original control's support enter its conjugation. The diagonal unitary commutes with edge basis projections. Conjugated translation controls have the same labelled permutation and multiplication law, with their coherent phases carried by the transported frame. The identity on unused spin coordinates and full rotors extends the equivalence to arbitrary input and retained reference. This also transports every pulse with the same generator norm, clock and energy account, and transports all outcome complements.

*Finite evaluation.* For each group table use the normalized integral bar boundary matrices in degrees through four. Their Smith forms compute $H_3(G,\mathbb Z)$; evaluation of normalized $U(1)$ cocycles on its cycles gives $H^3(G,U(1))=\operatorname{Hom}(H_3(G,\mathbb Z),U(1))$. This identity follows from the divisibility of $U(1)$, which makes the extension term in the integral universal coefficient sequence zero. The positive-degree homology of a finite group is killed by $|G|$, by transfer to the trivial subgroup, and the finite bar matrices make it finitely generated. The finite invariant factors therefore give a terminating list of characters. Smith form extends each cycle character to a cochain with rational phase angles and gives a normalized coboundary witness when two representatives agree. Divide the resulting finite list by the table automorphisms. With these choices the phase gates are computable from their finite tables. Representatives related by other cochain phases have the same transported response on the stated menu. The joint clock, pointers and paid controls are those of Theorems 14.5.7p and 14.5.7t, with an additional finite product and phase table per junction comparison. Any finite matrix gate has a self-adjoint logarithm of pulse norm at most $\pi\hbar/\tau$ for its allotted positive duration $\tau$. The exact check and recoupling matrices give the same spectator-stable pulse-error and rotor-tail estimates on each admitted finite energy grade. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457v"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457v-full-response-classification-of-finite-lattice-character-orbifolds"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7v (Full-Response Classification of Finite Lattice Character Orbifolds).** Let $L$ be a positive-definite even lattice of positive rank and let $\Gamma$ be a finite subgroup of its unitary character torus $\operatorname{Hom}(L,U(1))$. Let $M$ be its annihilator in $L$. Use the same oriented interval algebras, vacuum, stress tensor, calibrated rotations and full bounded-instrument menu as in Theorem 14.5.7s. Then the fixed net, represented on its own vacuum space, is the lattice net $\mathcal A_M$. Two such fixed-net responses are equivalent precisely when their fixed lattices are isometric. If the parent inclusions are retained, equivalence is exactly isometry of the inclusion pairs $M\subset L$. Every irreducible local finite-index conformal extension of a fixed marked $\mathcal A_M$ is represented by an even overlattice $M\subset M_H\subset M^*$, indexed by an isotropic subgroup $H$ of its discriminant form. The associated charge, rotation and inclusion responses are given below. All these chiral sources exclude exact local-algebra or all-energy realization on a finite matrix carrier in the senses of Proposition 14.5.7b-L.

*Fixed nets and across-base comparison.* The lattice VOA has the conformal decomposition
$$
V_L=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in L}\mathcal F_\alpha,
\qquad \gamma|_{\mathcal F_\alpha}=\gamma(\alpha)I,
\qquad V_L^\Gamma=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in M}\mathcal F_\alpha=V_M,
\tag{14.5.7v.1}
$$
where $\mathcal F_\alpha$ is the Heisenberg Fock space of charge $\alpha$. The automorphisms preserve the vacuum, real form and stress tensor. The invariant field products are the restrictions of those of $V_L$, including its lattice cocycle on $M$. The annihilator $M$ has finite index, and finite abelian character duality gives $[L:M]=|\Gamma|$. The lattice VOAs are strongly local, their conformal nets are the Dong–Xu lattice nets, and the equivariant subnet and reconstruction correspondences identify the fixed net on its invariant vacuum Hilbert space with $\mathcal A_{V_M}$. Thus this is an identity of the actual net, not only of its sector labels. The needed results are Theorems 3.46 and 3.48 of Gui (2020) and the subnet and reconstruction theorems of Carpi et al. (2015).

The oriented vacuum net and stress tensor reconstruct the unitary VOA. Shimakura (2011, Proposition 3.1) gives $V_M\cong V_{M'}$ precisely when $M\cong M'$. A lattice isometry induces a unitary VOA equivalence and therefore preserves every declared interval instrument and the calibrated rotation response. In particular the unmarked fixed net may coincide with an ordinary lattice source independently of which finite parent action produced it. Its equivalence to an inversion source is exactly the lattice-versus-inversion case already classified in Theorem 14.5.7s.

For the marked comparison suppose an equivalence carries $V_M\subset V_L$ to $V_{M'}\subset V_{L'}$. The two Heisenberg degree-one real Cartan subalgebras in the target subalgebra $(V_{M'})_1$ have full rank. Conjugate the image of the first to the standard Cartan by an inner unitary of the compact degree-one Lie algebra. This inner unitary extends to the parent VOA: its generators are unitary zero-mode exponentials of degree-one vectors of $V_{M'}$, and their derivation property preserves both the parent and the subalgebra. The adjusted equivalence therefore preserves the corresponding Cartan grading on both algebras. The joint zero-mode weight sets are exactly the embedded lattices $M'\subset L'$ in the target and $M\subset L$ in the source, by (14.5.7v.1). The first mode product of the Heisenberg fields preserves the positive bilinear form. Hence this equivalence gives one isometry sending $L$ to $L'$ and $M$ to $M'$. Conversely every such isometry lifts to a simultaneous unitary equivalence of the inclusion. If the action is retained up to relabelling its finite group, the action is recovered as the full character annihilator of $M$ and transported by the same isometry. If the individual character names are fixed, the induced dual map must also preserve those names.

*All local extensions of the fixed subnet.* Write
$$
A_M=M^*/M,\qquad Q_M([x])=(x,x)/2\pmod{\mathbb Z}.
\tag{14.5.7v.2}
$$
The irreducible sectors of $\mathcal A_M$ are labelled by $A_M$, with dimension one, fusion by addition, spin $e^{2\pi iQ_M([x])}$ and monodromy $e^{2\pi i(x,y)}$. Their lowest rotation weights are $\min_{u\in x+M}(u,u)/2$. These are the lattice sectors of Dong and Xu (2004, Section 3), equivalently the corresponding Heisenberg charge modules under the braided lattice net correspondence of Gui.

The finite-index extension correspondence used in Theorem 14.5.7r applies to this completely rational net. In its pointed braided category, an irreducible local extension corresponds to a connected commutative separable algebra object. Its nonzero homogeneous degrees form a subgroup $H$ and each component has dimension one. To see this, write the graded algebra object as $A=\bigoplus_{h\in A_M} A_h$ in simple-current coordinates. Connectedness gives $A_0=\mathbb C$. The Frobenius pairing given by the degree-zero component of the product is nondegenerate, so for nonzero $v\in A_h$ there is $w\in A_{-h}$ with $wv=1$. Associativity has only nonzero scalar factors in homogeneous coordinates. It follows that left multiplication by $v$ on any degree is injective, since multiplication by $w$ after it is a nonzero scalar times identity. In particular $vw\ne0$, and multiplication by $w$ on $A_h$ injects it into $A_0$. Thus every occupied degree is one dimensional and products of its nonzero elements are nonzero. Its occupied degrees are closed under products and inverses. Locality with the same nonzero homogeneous vector forces its self-braiding phase to be one. In a pointed unitary lattice category this phase is $e^{2\pi iQ_M(h)}$, so $Q_M$ vanishes on $H$. Conversely every isotropic $H$ gives the even integral lattice
$$
M_H=\{x\in M^*:[x]\in H\}.
\tag{14.5.7v.3}
$$
Its conformal inclusion has vacuum restriction $\bigoplus_{h\in H}h$ and is a local extension. Two multiplication choices on the same support differ by a normalized $U(1)$ two-cocycle. Commutativity fixes its commutator to one, so this cocycle is a coboundary: the central extension by $U(1)$ is abelian, and splitting on cyclic generators is achieved by choosing roots of the relations in $U(1)$. Thus for each $H$ there is precisely one extension equivalence class fixing the base. The correspondence between the lattice VOA inclusions and their conformal-net extensions is also verified by Gui (2025).

The index is $|H|$ by the dimension of the vacuum restriction. The extended sectors have labels $H^\perp/H$, with induced quadratic form from $Q_M$, where $H^\perp$ uses the bilinear form obtained by polarizing $Q_M$. This is $M_H^*/M_H$ from (14.5.7v.3). The complete rotation multiplicities of its vacuum are the coefficients of
$$
\operatorname{Tr}_{V_{M_H}}t^{L_0}=\frac{\sum_{x\in M_H} t^{(x,x)/2}}{\prod_{j\ge1}(1-t^j)^{\operatorname{rank}M}},\qquad 0<t<1,
\tag{14.5.7v.4}
$$
and the same formula with a sector coset in the numerator gives its sector weights. The calibrated shift $-\operatorname{rank}(M)/24$ is retained as in Theorem 14.5.7s. If the base is allowed to move in comparing two marked extensions, the inclusion-pair proof above gives exactly isometry of $M\subset M_H$ with $M'\subset M'_{H'}$. If the base action is fixed pointwise, the direct-sum restriction identifies the same subgroup $H$.

This classification has a finite verifier for each input. Lattice basis matrices give the annihilator of finite rational characters by integer kernel and Smith normal forms. Enumerate the subgroups of the finite group $M^*/M$ and test $Q_M|_H=0$. To compare a pair of lattices or inclusions, enumerate candidate images of basis vectors at their exact specified lengths in the positive-definite target, check inner products and the required lattice and sublattice indices, and stop after this finite set. For example take a rank-one lattice $L=\mathbb Z\alpha$ with $(\alpha,\alpha)=2$ and the order-three character $\gamma(\alpha)=e^{2\pi i/3}$. Its fixed lattice is $M=3L$ with Gram matrix $(18)$, so the fixed net has eighteen sectors and the marked parent extension has index three. The nonholomorphic parent and character action are distinct from the fixed holomorphic-parent and inversion constructions of Theorems 14.5.7r–s.

Each nontrivial interval algebra in this class is a type III factor, so the local algebra contains two isometries with orthogonal ranges. Their relations are impossible under a unital representation on a finite-dimensional carrier. Independently the nonzero Heisenberg oscillators give nonzero states at arbitrarily high rotation weights, which excludes an exact all-energy isometric intertwiner into a finite carrier. The sector correspondence and inclusion classification use the stated chiral nets and their full responses, with no approximation by a finite matrix target. ∎

<a id="theorem-1457w"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457w-an-all-energy-obstruction-on-the-infinite-golay-leech-rotor-carrier"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7w (An All-Energy Obstruction on the Infinite Golay–Leech Rotor Carrier).** Fix finitely many of the actual Golay–Leech macrocells of Theorem 14.5.7n. Retain all their finite spin registers and their complete, infinite-dimensional rotors, any finite-dimensional ancillary stock, and any bounded self-adjoint coupling of these factors. Their native total Hamiltonian, including arbitrary on-site unitary encodings, cannot receive an isometric all-energy intertwiner from the vacuum representation of a positive-rank lattice conformal net, or from its fixed-point net for a finite group of lifted lattice isometries and characters. The calibrated comparison permits any finite affine energy conversion with positive slope. The obstruction holds with infinitely many energy levels on both carriers and also when their clean-collar sector categories agree.

*Native spectrum and every bounded coupling.* Write the undressed aggregate as $\mathbb C^S\otimes\bigotimes_{x=1}^N L^2(\mathbb R^{24}/\Lambda_x)$, with finite $S$ including any supplied finite ancilla. On rotor $x$ the Fourier basis has momenta $\lambda_x\in\Lambda_x^*$ and energies $a_x|\lambda_x|^2$, with $a_x>0$ fixed by the actual rotor and clock. Thus the uncoupled rotor energy is $\sum_x a_x|\lambda_x|^2$. If $g_x$ is the shortest nonzero length in $\Lambda_x^*$, disjoint balls of radius $g_x/2$ centered at lattice points in the ball of radius $R$ give the bound $(1+2R/g_x)^{24}$ on their number. Consequently the free aggregate counting function obeys
$$
N_0(E)=\operatorname{rank}\mathbf1_{(-\infty,E]}(H_0)
\leq S\prod_x(1+2\sqrt{E/a_x}/g_x)^{24}
\leq C(1+E)^{12N},\qquad E\geq0.
$$
This uses all rotor and spin states, including states outside any retained defect sector. The free Hamiltonian has compact resolvent. A bounded self-adjoint addition $W$ preserves compact resolvent and shifts each ordered eigenvalue by at most $\|W\|$ by the min–max principle. In particular $N_{H_0+W}(E)\leq N_0(E+\|W\|)$. A unitary encoding on the entire physical stock preserves the counting function. These arguments include the full nonflat checks, spectators, on-site dressings and complete bounded finite defect Hamiltonians of Theorems 14.5.7n and 14.5.7p and 14.5.7t–u, with the fixed constants and rotor factors retained.

*A superpolynomial vacuum count in every stated chiral source.* Let $r>0$ be the rank of the positive-definite even lattice. Its vacuum Heisenberg Fock space contains independent oscillator creation vectors at each integer grade $n\geq1$. A lifted lattice isometry acts on those modes by its orthogonal action on the real rank-$r$ oscillator coordinates. A lattice character acts trivially on the Heisenberg oscillators. Fix a real orthonormal coordinate basis and write $a^i_{-n}$ for the creation operators. For each $n$, the vector operator
$$
Q_n=\sum_{i=1}^r (a^i_{-n})^2
$$
is invariant under all the stipulated lifts and characters. It has grade $2n$ and stays in the zero lattice momentum sector. For every subset $S_0\subseteq\{1,\ldots,m\}$ the vector $\prod_{n\in S_0}Q_n\Omega$ is nonzero. Distinct subsets lie in different oscillator number patterns by frequency, so their vectors are orthogonal even if their total grades coincide. All of them are in the fixed vacuum space, and their grades are at most $2\sum_{n=1}^m n=m(m+1)$. The same vectors apply without a group. For the common calibrated chiral Hamiltonian $H_{\rm ch}=\hbar\omega_0(L_0-r/24)$ this gives
$$
N_{\rm ch}\bigl(\hbar\omega_0(m(m+1)-r/24)\bigr)\geq 2^m .
$$
The vacuum count applies to lifted isometries accompanied by characters and to conformal extensions retaining this same vacuum subnet and stress tensor. See Dong and Xu (2004).

*The response obstruction.* Suppose an isometry $U$ transported the complete spectral record with conversion $E\mapsto \alpha E+\beta$ for fixed $\alpha>0$ and $\beta\in\mathbb R$. Its restriction to every spectral sublevel space would give
$$
N_{\rm ch}(E)\leq N_{\rm native}(\alpha E+\beta).
$$
Putting $E=\hbar\omega_0(m(m+1)-r/24)$ makes the right side at most a fixed multiple of $(1+m)^{24N}$, while the left is at least $2^m$. For every fixed native source this fails at a finite grade. The rank discrepancy gives a finite response witness: the chiral source has more mutually orthogonal preparations in the indicated energy sublevel than the full native sublevel can hold, and their separating tests can be taken on that finite-dimensional chiral span. The proof applies also to the retained finite-rank chiral sublevel projection as a spectral response, independently of how any sector label is read.

The same finite witness has a quantitative version. Let $M=2^m$, choose the corresponding normalized chiral input vectors, and let $P$ be the native spectral projection at the converted sublevel, of rank $d$. Suppose the proposed implementation produces native output states $\rho_i$ with $\operatorname{tr}[(I-P)\rho_i]\leq\epsilon$ and uses a common decoder POVM $(F_i)$ on the complete native output. Any additional finite receiver outputs on which the decoder acts are included in the native stock and its energy budget. For the equiprobable inputs,
$$
\frac1M\sum_i\operatorname{tr}(F_i\rho_i)
\leq \frac{d}{M}+2\sqrt{\epsilon}.
$$
Indeed $0\leq P\rho_iP\leq P$ gives $\sum_i\operatorname{tr}(F_i P\rho_iP)\leq\operatorname{tr}P$, also when the decoder includes a failure label. A purification of $\rho_i$ changes by Hilbert norm at most $\sqrt\epsilon$ when projected with $P$. The trace norm of the difference of the corresponding rank-one operators is at most $2\sqrt\epsilon$, by adding and subtracting their mixed outer product. Partial trace gives $\|\rho_i-P\rho_iP\|_1\leq2\sqrt\epsilon$. This proves the success bound with all decoded responses on the same native budget. Thus even an approximate simulation that keeps the calibrated sublevel with small leakage must lose the finite separation response once $d/M$ is small. The bound concerns fixed native stock; refinements in which that stock grows with the queried grade retain their separate resource test. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457x"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457x-oscillator-rank-and-native-resource-growth-in-lattice-orbifold-comparisons"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7x (Oscillator Rank and Native Resource Growth in Lattice Orbifold Comparisons).** Use the calibrated chiral lattice vacuum or the fixed vacuum of any finite group of lifted lattice isometries and characters in Theorem 14.5.7w. Let \(r>0\) be the lattice rank, \(N_{\rm ch}(L)\) the number of states of conformal grade at most \(L\), and use natural logarithms. Then
$$
\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{\log N_{\rm ch}(L)}{\sqrt L}
=2\pi\sqrt{r/6}.
\tag{14.5.7x.1}
$$
This holds for the full vacuum and every stated fixed vacuum with its own stress and rotation response. At fixed chiral calibration \(\hbar\omega_0\), the leading growth therefore separates unequal lattice ranks without using the fusion data. It also yields a resource lower bound for native refinements with growing numbers of complete rotor factors and finite auxiliary states.

*Finite-group projection on the full oscillator tower.* Let \(M(0)\) denote the zero-momentum Heisenberg Fock space. Let \(H\) be the finite image of the group on its oscillator coordinates, with the trivial group admitted. For \(t>0\) let \(Z_{\rm osc}(t)=\prod_{n\ge1}(1-e^{-tn})^{-r}\). For \(g\in H\) write \(Z_g(t)\) for the trace of \(g e^{-tL_0}\) on \(M(0)\). At frequency \(n\) the symmetric powers of the oscillator coordinate representation give the factor \(\det(I-e^{-tn}g)^{-1}\), by diagonalizing \(g\) over \(\mathbb C\) and summing the geometric factors for independent occupations. The eigenvalues of real orthogonal \(g\) occur in conjugate pairs, with remaining eigenvalues \(\pm1\). Thus every factor is positive real. For every \(t>0\) the product converges absolutely, and the trace of the invariant projection obeys
$$
\operatorname{Tr}_{M(0)^H}e^{-tL_0}
=|H|^{-1}\sum_{g\in H}Z_g(t)\ge |H|^{-1}Z_{\rm osc}(t).
\tag{14.5.7x.2}
$$
Lattice characters and the character factors of the lifts are trivial in the zero-momentum sector. The full fixed-vacuum trace is bounded above by the full lattice trace \(\Theta_L(t)Z_{\rm osc}(t)\), where the $\theta$ factor is the sum of \(e^{-t\|\lambda\|^2/2}\) over momenta in the lattice, here denoted by \(\lambda\). Lattice point packing bounds this factor by \(C(1+t^{-r/2})\) on \(0<t\le1\), for instance by integrating the counting bound \(O((1+R)^r)\) against the radial Gaussian. The heat trace on the fixed vacuum therefore has
$$
\log Z(t)=a/t+o(t^{-1}),\qquad a=\pi^2r/6 .
\tag{14.5.7x.3}
$$
Indeed \(t\sum_{n\ge1}-\log(1-e^{-tn})\) converges to its integral on \((0,\infty)\) by monotonicity and integrability at both endpoints. Expansion into nonnegative exponentials evaluates the integral as \(\sum_{k\ge1}k^{-2}=\pi^2/6\). These computations use the actual lattice vacuum oscillator representation. See Dong and Xu (2004).

*Passage from the heat trace to calibrated state counts.* The upper bound is \(N_{\rm ch}(L)\le e^{tL}Z(t)\) with \(t=\sqrt{a/L}\). For the lower bound put probability \(e^{-t\ell}/Z(t)\) on each state of grade \(\ell\). Fix \(0<\delta<1\). This probability concentrates on
\([(1-\delta)a/t^2,(1+\delta)a/t^2]\) as \(t\downarrow0\). For the upper tail use the exponential test \(e^{ht\ell}\) with \(0<h<1\). Its bound has log at most
$$
\frac{a}{t}\left(\frac{1}{1-h}-1-h(1+\delta)\right)+o(t^{-1}),
$$
which is negative of order \(1/t\) for sufficiently small fixed \(h>0\). For the lower tail use \(e^{-ht\ell}\), giving the coefficient \(1/(1+h)-1+h(1-\delta)\), also strictly negative for small \(h>0\). At \(t=\sqrt{(1+\delta)a/L}\) the band lies below \(L\); each state in it has unnormalized weight at most \(\exp[-(1-\delta)a/t]\). Hence \(N_{\rm ch}(L)\ge(1-o(1))Z(t)\exp[(1-\delta)a/t]\). Taking logs, then letting \(\delta\downarrow0\), gives the matching limit in (14.5.7x.1). This argument uses only the nonnegative state counting measure and the heat trace bounds.

*Resource inequality for approximate implementations.* At each queried grade \(L\) allow \(n_L\) complete native rotors, finite retained stock of dimension \(S_L\) including all decoder-accessible ancillas, and any bounded self-adjoint interaction \(W_L\). Assume that the kinetic coefficients times squared shortest reciprocal lengths have a uniform positive lower bound \(g_*^2\), so the packing estimate of Theorem 14.5.7w gives
$$
\operatorname{rank}\mathbf1_{(-\infty,E]}(H_{\rm native})
\le S_L\left(1+2\sqrt{\max(0,E+\|W_L\|)}/g_*\right)^{24n_L}.
\tag{14.5.7x.4}
$$
This follows by bounding each nonnegative free rotor energy by the total free sublevel energy. If the shifted sublevel energy is negative the free rank is zero and the inequality still holds. Take a fixed affine calibrated conversion of the chiral grade to native energy \(E_L=\alpha L+\beta\), \(\alpha>0\), and suppose \(\|W_L\|\le C(1+L)\). On equiprobable orthogonal preparations spanning the full chiral sublevel, let the target common decoder attain mean success at least \(p_*>0\) with native sublevel leakage at most \(\epsilon\) on each input. If \(p_*>2\sqrt\epsilon\), the decoding estimate in Theorem 14.5.7w requires native rank at least \((p_*-2\sqrt\epsilon)N_{\rm ch}(L)\). Combining with (14.5.7x.1) and (14.5.7x.4) gives, for a source constant \(C_0\),
$$
\log S_L+12n_L\log L+C_0 n_L
\ \ge\ \left(2\pi\sqrt{r/6}-o(1)\right)\sqrt L .
\tag{14.5.7x.5}
$$
In particular, if \(\log S_L=o(\sqrt L)\), then \(n_L\ge(\pi\sqrt{r/6}/6-o(1))\sqrt L/\log L\). If \(n_L\) is bounded, the finite stock must have \(\log S_L\ge(2\pi\sqrt{r/6}-o(1))\sqrt L\). Thus growing the finite rotor source can avoid the fixed-stock obstruction only with the stated resource growth on this calibrated branch. The bound is for complete separation responses with the specified leakage and energy conversion. Intertwining the chiral local algebras and constructing sufficient physical refinements remain separate tests. \(\square\)

<a id="theorem-1457y"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457y-prime-cycle-permutation-responses-of-holomorphic-lattice-nets"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7y (Prime-Cycle Permutation Responses of Holomorphic Lattice Nets).** Let $L$ range over all positive-rank positive-definite even unimodular lattices, with $r=\operatorname{rank}L$. For an odd prime $p$, take $\mathcal P_{L,p}=\mathcal A_L^{\otimes p}$ with the actual cyclic permutation of its $p$ factors and fixed net $\mathcal B_{L,p}$ in the fixed vacuum representation. Use the oriented interval algebras, vacuum, stress tensor, calibrated rotation clock, and full bounded-instrument comparison of Theorem 14.5.7s. The fixed nets are response-equivalent if and only if both $p$ and the isometry class of $L$ agree. None is response-equivalent to an ordinary lattice net or a finite character fixed net of Theorem 14.5.7v. This equivalence test also classifies the parent-marked inclusions and, with the extension choice below, all their irreducible local finite-index conformal extensions over the marked base.

*Base invariant and across-parent classification.* The vacuum lattice net comes from the unitary lattice vertex algebra $V_L$; tensor products and unitary fixed subalgebras give the corresponding conformal nets. The unitary state-field correspondence for this class reconstructs the vertex algebra from the vacuum conformal net, including the weight-one Lie bracket, invariant current form and zero-mode spectrum. Accordingly any equivalence of the stated oriented conformal responses preserves these invariants. See Carpi et al. (2015).

The fixed weight-one Lie algebra is the diagonal copy of $(V_L)_1$. Indeed the tensor weight-one space is a direct sum of $p$ one-factor weight-one spaces, and the permutation cycles them. The lattice Heisenberg space is a Cartan subalgebra of $(V_L)_1$: its zero modes act diagonally on lattice charges, and its weight-one centralizer consists precisely of its zero-charge oscillator space. Thus its diagonal copy $\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{diag}}$ is a Cartan of the fixed weight-one Lie algebra. Any other compact Cartan is conjugate to it by inner automorphisms generated by weight-one zero modes, which act on the full fixed vertex algebra. The spectrum construction below therefore does not depend on a preferred Cartan or a parent mark.

On $\mathfrak h_{\mathrm{diag}}$, the inherited positive form is $p$ times the lattice form. The joint zero-mode weights on the entire fixed vacuum space are exactly
$$
h_{\mathrm{diag}}\longmapsto\langle h,\alpha\rangle_L,\qquad \alpha\in L .
$$
Every tensor charge is a sum of $p$ lattice charges and gives such a functional. Conversely for $\alpha\ne0$ the sum over cyclic translates of $e^\alpha\otimes\mathbf1^{\otimes(p-1)}$ is a nonzero invariant vector of weight $\alpha$: its summands lie in different tensor charge sectors. The vacuum realizes charge zero. Thus the charge lattice in the dual of the calibrated current form is isometric to $p^{-1/2}L$. Its rank is $r$, and its Gram determinant is $p^{-r}$ because $L$ is unimodular. These two invariants recover $p$. Rescaling the entire charge lattice then recovers $L$, including its glue and more than its root system. A lattice isometry and equality of $p$ conversely implement a unitary on all tensor factors commuting with the same cycle, vacuum and clock. It restricts to the fixed net and intertwines its bounded instruments. It also implements the marked parent inclusion, proving sufficiency there. A generator mark can be fixed by the identical cyclic labelling in the two tensor products.

For an ordinary lattice net of lattice $M$ the same intrinsic Cartan construction gives $M$ with its own calibrated lattice form and determinant a positive integer. Since $p^{-r}<1$, no such comparison is possible. Theorem 14.5.7v identifies every finite character fixed net with a lattice net, so the same invariant excludes that class.

*Defect weights and extension choices.* Put $h_p=r(p-1/p)/24$, the permutation soliton vacuum shift. In each nonzero flux the permutation is a full $p$-cycle, and the soliton rotation generator on the seed Hilbert space is $L_0/p+h_p$. The covering-space rotation formula and character decomposition of the $p$-cycle supply the $p$ irreducible sectors in each flux. In a given flux label the electric characters by the residue $j\in\mathbb Z_p$ of the seed conformal grade, so the sector levels there are $h_p+n/p$ for seed levels $n\equiv j\pmod p$, with their original multiplicities. This convention labels sectors within a flux, and the electric action on that labelling depends on the chosen flux generator. The $p$ sectors of zero flux are the character eigenspaces in $V_L^{\otimes p}$. The fixed-sector representation is realized by the invariant eigenspace. The calibrated energy on each sector is $\hbar\omega_0(L_0-pr/24)$, using the same $\omega_0>0$. See Longo and Xu (2003, Theorem 6.3 and covering rotation formula).

Writing $r=8k$, the anomaly $t\in H^3(\mathbb Z_p,U(1))\simeq\mathbb Z_p$ is zero for $p\ge5$, and $t=2k\bmod3$ for $p=3$ in the standard generator convention. Equivalently $t=p^2h_p\bmod p$. The full sector category is the pointed unitary centre $Z(\mathrm{Vec}_{\mathbb Z_p}^{\omega_t})$; this fixes fusion and twists, including the distinction between $\mathbb Z_p^2$ in the trivial class and $\mathbb Z_{p^2}$ in the nontrivial class. The same conformal-net construction identifies the actual charge embedding coming from the parent action. See Bischoff (2018, Propositions 2.1 and 3.1).

By Theorem 14.5.7r and Corollary 14.5.7r.1, the marked irreducible local extension list consists of the fixed net, the permutation parent, and, exactly for $t=0$, the bosonic flux extension. The last two have index $p$ and are holomorphic. The parent vacuum restricts to the sum of its $p$ zero-flux charge sectors. The additional vacuum is the sum over its bosonic flux subgroup, which meets every flux once. In the residue convention it uses the unique residue $j$ with $h_p+j/p\in\mathbb Z$ in each nonzero flux; uniqueness follows from the spacing $1/p$ of twists there. This gives a calibrated energy and multiplicity prescription for all its added sectors. The common parent isometry and equal $p$ extend to the corresponding extension choice by the algebra-object uniqueness of Theorem 14.5.7r. Conversely, a marked extension equivalence restricts to a base equivalence. The base, parent and flux choices cannot be interchanged even by forgetting the extension mark on a fixed base: the base has nontrivial sector category, while the proper extensions are holomorphic. In the anomaly-free case $h_p>1$ because $p\ge5,r\ge8$ or $p=3,r\ge24$. The flux extension then has the same weight-one space as the base, while the parent has $p$ times its dimension. This distinguishes the two proper extension responses over that base.

The class comprises actual cyclic permutation actions beyond the inversion and character constructions. It gives a complete census of this family and of its extensions with the base marked. Comparisons of unmarked extension nets across different marked bases, other chiral actions and further on-site realizations retain their tests. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457z"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457z-full-cycle-lattice-permutation-classification-and-divisor-census"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7z (Full-Cycle Lattice Permutation Classification and Divisor Census).** Let $L$ be a positive-rank even positive-definite unimodular lattice of rank $r$, let $n\ge2$, and take the single $n$-cycle $g$ on $\mathcal A_L^{\otimes n}$. Use the calibrated oriented vacuum comparison of Theorem 14.5.7y for the fixed net $\mathcal B_{L,n}$. The base responses determine $n$ and $L$ up to isometry. Each irreducible local finite-index conformal extension over the fixed marked base is specified by the divisor and congruence data below. Its index, vacuum restriction, sector structure and calibrated levels follow from those data and the cyclic soliton on the same source.

*Across-base comparison.* The weight-one space of the fixed vacuum is the diagonal copy of the seed weight-one space for a single $n$-cycle, independently of primality. Its Cartan charge support is exactly $L$, realized by cyclic averaging of every nonzero one-factor lattice-charge vector. Its invariant current form is $n$ times the seed form. Thus the intrinsic dual-metric charge lattice of the fixed net is $n^{-1/2}L$, with rank $r$ and determinant $n^{-r}$. The state-field and compact-Cartan arguments of Theorem 14.5.7y apply without change. This recovers the cycle length and lattice isometry class, separates these base responses from every ordinary lattice and finite character fixed net, and also classifies the parent-marked inclusion. A lattice isometry and the same cycle relabeling realize sufficiency and preserve the stated clock.

*Flux and charge convention.* The centre of the cyclic anomaly category is pointed. Write $\mathbf e$ for its primitive charge, with character $g\mapsto e^{2\pi i/n}$, and $\mathbf f$ for the flux-one lift in the following fusion and spin coordinates:
$$
n\mathbf e=0,\qquad n\mathbf f=2t\,\mathbf e,\qquad
q(a\mathbf f+b\mathbf e)=\frac{t a^2}{n^2}+\frac{ab}{n}\pmod 1,\qquad
t=\frac{rn(n^2-1)}{24}\pmod n .
\tag{14.5.7z.1}
$$
Use the representative $0\le t<n$. The type follows from the conformal vacuum shift $r(n-1/n)/24$ of a full permutation cycle, multiplied by $n^2$ modulo $n$. Since $r\in8\mathbb Z$ the displayed value defining $t$ is integral. The cyclic fusion theorem with its charge convention gives the carry $2t$ and the quadratic form in (14.5.7z.1). In particular the relation $n\mathbf f=2t\mathbf e$ agrees with the displayed quadratic form on arbitrary integer representatives. The group $\mathcal G$ has order $n^2$ and its charge subgroup is $\langle\mathbf e\rangle$. See Longo and Xu (2003), van Ekeren et al. (2020, Theorem 5.13) and Bischoff (2018).

*Extension census.* Choose positive integers $d,k$ with $dk\mid n$ and $d\mid t$. Set $D=n/(dk)$ and take one of the residues $j$ modulo $D$ satisfying
$$
d\,j\equiv -t/d\pmod D .
\tag{14.5.7z.2}
$$
The residue exists exactly when $\gcd(d,D)$ divides $t/d$, and the number of residues is then $\gcd(d,D)$. Put $b=-t/d+d j$, considered modulo $n/k$. The extension support in $\mathcal G$ is
$$
J_{d,k,j}=\left\langle (n/k)\mathbf e,\ (n/d)\mathbf f+b\mathbf e\right\rangle .
\tag{14.5.7z.3}
$$
The index is $dk$. The restriction of the extension vacuum to the base is the multiplicity-one sum over $J_{d,k,j}$. The local sector category of the extension has quadratic group $J_{d,k,j}^{\perp}/J_{d,k,j}$ with inherited $q$. It is holomorphic precisely when $dk=n$.

Indeed any subgroup $J$ has a unique projected flux order $d$ and charge intersection order $k$. The first generator in (14.5.7z.3) generates that intersection, and any lift of the distinguished flux $n/d$ has the displayed second form, with $b$ unique modulo $n/k$. For $d=1$ the flux $n/d$ is zero and $n\mathbf f=2t\mathbf e$ gives the same parametrization. The polar pairing between the two generators is $n/(dk)$ modulo one, so isotropy requires $dk\mid n$. The charge generator is isotropic, and for the flux lift its spin is $t/d^2+b/d$ modulo one. Its vanishing requires $d\mid t$ and $b\equiv-t/d\pmod d$. The $d$-fold flux lift lies in the charge intersection exactly when $2t+db\equiv0\pmod{n/k}$. Substituting $b=-t/d+d j$ gives (14.5.7z.2). Conversely these conditions make both generators isotropic and mutually orthogonal, and enforce that the charge intersection is exactly the designated one. This proves completeness and nonredundancy of the subgroup list in the chosen coordinates, including $d=1$, when the unique solution has $b\equiv-2t\pmod{n/k}$. The connected commutative algebra over an isotropic subgroup is unique in the pointed category of Theorem 14.5.7r, giving the index, restriction and local modules above. Changing the auxiliary flux lift changes the names of the same sector subgroups under the corresponding coordinate isometry. Two supports in fixed sector coordinates cannot be identified by a unitary intertwining the identical marked base, since the restricted vacuum sectors differ.

For example, if $t=0$, every divisor $d$ with $k=n/d$ gives exactly one holomorphic extension. The permutation parent is the pure charge entry $d=1,k=n$. In general the number of all extension entries is the sum of $\gcd(d,n/(dk))$ over the pairs $dk\mid n$ for which $d\mid t$ and $\gcd(d,n/(dk))\mid t/d$. Every prime order gives the census in Corollary 14.5.7r.1.

*Calibrated levels for the composite fluxes.* For $0\le a<n$ set $c_a=\gcd(a,n)$, with $c_0=n$, and $m_a=n/c_a$. The permutation $g^a$ has $c_a$ disjoint $m_a$-cycles. Its twisted space is the tensor product of the $c_a$ seed vacuum spaces, with
$$
L_0^{(a)}=\frac1{m_a}\sum_{\nu=1}^{c_a}L_{0,\nu}
+\frac{r c_a}{24}(m_a-m_a^{-1}).
\tag{14.5.7z.4}
$$
The cyclic generator acts by permuting the cycle spaces and applying covering rotations within them. For $a\ne0$, order the $g^a$-cycles by representatives $0,\ldots,c_a-1$ in the cyclic indexing of the $n$ tensor factors. The map $g$ sends each representative to the next one until the last representative crosses to the first cycle at factor $c_a$. In that cycle, the crossing is a shift by $\nu_a$ positions in the $g^a$-order, where $\nu_a$ is the inverse of $a/c_a$ modulo $m_a$. Thus the ground-state-normalized intertwiner $V_a$ cyclically shifts the $c_a$ seed factors and applies $\exp(2\pi i\nu_a L_0/m_a)$ to the factor crossing from last to first. With the positive charge convention of (14.5.7z.1), its $c_a$-th power is $\exp(2\pi i\nu_a(L_0^{(a)}-h_a))$, where $h_a=(r c_a/24)(m_a-m_a^{-1})$. This is the normalized covering action of $g^{c_a}$. Its $n$-th power is one. In particular the grade-block traces before the overall character shift are
$$
\operatorname{Tr}_{\{L_0^{(a)}=s\}}(V_a^\ell)
=e^{2\pi i\nu_a\ell(s-h_a)/c_a}\,
[z^{\,m_a(s-h_a)}]\,
F_L\!\left(z^{\,c_a/\gcd(\ell,c_a)}\right)^{\gcd(\ell,c_a)},
\qquad F_L(z)=\operatorname{Tr}_{V_L} z^{L_0}.
\tag{14.5.7z.5}
$$
Indeed diagonalize the seed grades. A basis tuple contributes to a permutation trace only if its basis labels are constant on every cycle of the $\ell$-fold shift of the $c_a$ factors. If one such cycle has length $c_a/\gcd(\ell,c_a)$, then its common grade crosses the carry a total of $\ell/\gcd(\ell,c_a)$ times. Their summed phases give the exponential in (14.5.7z.5). The cyclic fusion and spin convention normalizes the transport as $U_a(g)=e^{2\pi i u_a/n}V_a$ with a scalar character shift $u_a$ on each flux. One may either use the cyclic construction's normalized intertwiners or identify the actual eigenspaces in (14.5.7z.1) by taking powers of $\mathbf f$ in the sector category. Specifically the ground line is in the charge indexed by $u_a$; its congruence $a u_a/n\equiv h_a-t a^2/n^2\pmod1$ follows from the spin and need not alone fix the charge when $c_a>1$. The fixed primitive charge and chosen flux lift determine the actual charge coordinates by fusion. The multiplicity of level $s$ in the charge-$b$ component is exactly
$$
\frac1n\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}e^{-2\pi i b\ell/n}
\operatorname{Tr}_{\{L_0^{(a)}=s\}}\!\big(U_a(g)^\ell\big).
\tag{14.5.7z.6}
$$
For zero flux $U_0(g)$ is the actual permutation of the seed factors, and the coefficient formula is (14.5.7z.5) with $m_a=1$, $c_a=n$, $h_a=0$ and the exponential set to one. The finite grade spaces in (14.5.7z.6) and the intertwiners are those of the permutation soliton. A change of character normalization reindexes the actual components and the extension supports together. A single residue of the sum of seed grades need not resolve the multiplicities for a flux with several cycles. Each such level is read at energy $\hbar\omega_0(s-nr/24)$, with the same vacuum, stress tensor and fixed clock. The local extension restriction sums these sector multiplicities over its specified support. See Barron et al. (1998, Theorem 6.4) and van Ekeren et al. (2020, Propositions 5.8–5.9).

This class includes all orders of the full cyclic permutation action on holomorphic lattice parents. Theorem 14.5.7aa treats several orbits. Comparisons of unmarked extension nets across different bases, other chiral actions, and further on-site encodings retain their tests. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457aa"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457aa-multi-orbit-lattice-permutations-and-their-marked-local-extensions"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7aa (Multi-Orbit Lattice Permutations and Their Marked Local Extensions).** Let the parent be a finite product of positive-rank even positive-definite unimodular lattice conformal nets, with a permutation $g$ of the factors that permutes copies of the same lattice. Fix the cycle action using the canonical permutation of identified copies. Use the calibrated oriented vacuum response and common stress and clock convention of Theorem 14.5.7z. Split each seed lattice into its orthogonally indecomposable lattice summands, and expand each original cycle into a cycle on the copies of each summand. For each resulting cycle $i$ record its length $n_i$, including one for a fixed factor, and the isometry class of its indecomposable seed lattice $L_i$. The fixed vacuum response determines this multiset of pairs. Equality of multisets is sufficient for both fixed-vacuum response equivalence and an equivalence intertwining the flattened parent and its nominated generator. Any additional marks grouping the summands into the original seed factors are separate input to a comparison of those grouping marks.

Set $N=\operatorname{lcm}_i n_i$ and $r_i=\operatorname{rank}(L_i)$. If $N>1$, every irreducible local finite-index extension over the fixed base with its named sector coordinates is given by the divisor and congruence census (14.5.7z.2)–(14.5.7z.3) for order $N$ and anomaly
$$
t=N^2\sum_i\frac{r_i}{24}\bigl(n_i-n_i^{-1}\bigr)\pmod N,\qquad 0\le t<N.
\tag{14.5.7aa.1}
$$
Its index, restriction, local quadratic sectors and calibrated energy multiplicities are obtained on the same source by the computations below. If $N=1$, the parent is holomorphic and is the only irreducible local extension of the fixed base.

*Intrinsic current and charge lattice.* The parent weight-one space is the direct sum of the factor weight-one spaces, and the fixed weight-one Lie algebra is the direct sum of their diagonals over the permutation orbits. In a lattice factor the Heisenberg weight-one algebra is a compact Cartan after taking the compact real form of the current Lie algebra. To see maximality, its zero modes act diagonally on charges and their weight-one centralizer is precisely the zero-charge oscillator space. This applies to every orbit diagonal and hence to their direct sum in the fixed weight-one algebra. Other compact Cartans are conjugate by inner automorphisms generated by current zero modes on the full fixed vertex algebra. The unitary state-field reconstruction used in Theorem 14.5.7y transports the current Lie bracket, the calibrated current inner product and the joint zero-mode support under any oriented vacuum response equivalence. Thus the spectrum computed using this Cartan gives an intrinsic metric lattice up to isometry.

The inherited current inner product on orbit $i$ is $n_i$ times that of $L_i$, and the Cartan charges on the fixed Hilbert space are the entire product $\prod_i L_i$. Each component of any tensor charge lies in $L_i$ by charge addition. Conversely choose a nonzero one-factor vector of each prescribed nonzero orbit charge, average it over the orbit rotation and take the product of the resulting invariant vectors, using the vacuum for zero charges. Each nonzero average is nonzero because its distinct translates have distinct tensor charge assignments. The product is invariant under $g$. This proves that the charge lattice with the dual current metric is
$$
\mathcal L=\mathop{\perp}_i n_i^{-1/2}L_i .
\tag{14.5.7aa.2}
$$

Any Euclidean lattice has a canonical decomposition into orthogonally indecomposable summands. For a proof, call a nonzero vector indecomposable if it cannot be written as a sum of two nonzero orthogonal lattice vectors. Iterating orthogonal splits generates the lattice from indecomposable vectors because each daughter vector has strictly smaller norm, and only finitely many lattice vectors have norm bounded by the original one. Join two indecomposable vectors whenever their inner product is nonzero. The lattices generated by different connected components are orthogonal, and the lattice is their direct sum. In any orthogonal decomposition an indecomposable vector belongs to a single summand, so every connected component belongs to a single summand. Each component lattice is itself orthogonally indecomposable by the same argument. This proves uniqueness. Scaling a lattice by a positive scalar preserves this decomposition. Each orthogonal lattice summand of an integral unimodular lattice is unimodular, since its positive integer Gram determinant multiplies with the others to one. A summand of (14.5.7aa.2) of rank $r$ and Gram determinant $d$ therefore gives $n=d^{-1/r}$, and scaling it by $\sqrt n$ recovers its seed. This classifies the multiset, including all fixed factors. When some $n_i>1$ the full charge lattice has determinant strictly less than one, so it also separates the response from every ordinary lattice net and every finite lattice character fixed net of Theorem 14.5.7v.

Conversely use the lattice isometries on the paired primitive seed factors and identify the factors in each pair of cycles in cyclic order. Their tensor unitary conjugates the two nominated parent permutations, preserves the vacuum, stress tensor and calibrated clock, and intertwines the parent and fixed local algebras and their instruments. This proves the stated sufficiency without assuming the fixed net itself is the product of separate cycle fixed nets. That product is used only as a marked intermediate subnet.

*Sector normalization on the intermediate subnet.* The independent cycle group is $G=\prod_i\mathbb Z_{n_i}$, and $H=\langle g\rangle$ is its cyclic subgroup with generator the tuple of primitive rotations. The $G$-fixed subnet is the product of single-cycle fixed nets. Use their quadratic groups and chosen flux lifts $\mathbf f_i$ and primitive electric characters $\mathbf e_i$ from (14.5.7z.1), with single-cycle anomalies $0\le t_i<n_i$. Omit the trivial quadratic-group factors with $n_i=1$. Let $J$ be the product electric characters trivial on $H$. The $H$-fixed base is the local extension of the $G$-fixed subnet by $J$: its vacuum consists precisely of the $G$-electric sectors with this trivial restriction. As in Theorem 14.5.7z, the quadratic group of local sectors is $J^\perp/J$. Its elements lift to product sectors whose flux tuple is $(a\bmod n_i)_i$ for one $a\bmod N$, since electric pairing is precisely restriction to the flux. The electric charge of a tuple $(b_i\mathbf e_i)_i$ on $H$ is
$$
b=\sum_i (N/n_i)b_i\pmod N .
\tag{14.5.7aa.3}
$$
The image is the whole character group since $\gcd_i(N/n_i)=1$. Choose an electric $\mathbf e$ in the quotient with $b=1$, and let $\mathbf f_0=\sum_i\mathbf f_i$ in that quotient. Write $S=\sum_i t_i/n_i^2$. The integer $N^2 S$ has residue $t$ from (14.5.7aa.1): replacing $t_i$ by the local $n_i^2$ times vacuum shift changes the sum by a multiple of $N$, since $(N/n_i)^2 n_i$ is divisible by $N$. The local shift times $n_i^2$ is integral because $r_i$ is a multiple of eight and $3$ divides $n_i(n_i^2-1)$. Therefore $B=(t-N^2S)/N$ is an integer. The chosen global flux lift is $\mathbf f=\mathbf f_0+B\mathbf e$. Its spin is $S+B/N=t/N^2$, and $N\mathbf f=2t\mathbf e$ since $N\mathbf f_0$ restricts to electric charge $2N^2S\bmod N$ and $N\mathbf e=0$. Pairing $\mathbf f$ with $\mathbf e$ gives $1/N$. The resulting quadratic group is exactly the one in (14.5.7z.1) with $N,t$. This derives the anomaly from the source and identifies the actual global charge convention.

*Calibrated flux grades.* In flux $a$ write $a_i$ for the residue of $a$ in $[0,n_i)$ and $v_i=(a-a_i)/n_i$. The twisted Hilbert space is the product of the orbit spaces from (14.5.7z.4), with $a_i$ for the local flux. Its conformal grade is their sum, and the local ground shifts and cycle multiplicities follow from those formulas. Use the ground-state-normalized rotation $V_{i,a_i}$ of (14.5.7z.5), and the single-cycle character offset $u_i(a_i)$ in the fixed local fusion coordinates used there. This offset is taken from the actual locally normalized cyclic sector label, as in Theorem 14.5.7z, with $u_i(0)=0$; a spin congruence alone need not select it. In the chosen global flux convention the product ground-state-normalized operator $\bigotimes_i V_{i,a_i}$ is multiplied by the character phase with index
$$
u(a)=\sum_i (N/n_i)\bigl(u_i(a_i)-2t_i v_i\bigr)-aB\pmod N .
\tag{14.5.7aa.4}
$$
Indeed the coordinate $a\mathbf f+b\mathbf e$ has product flux representatives $a_i\mathbf f_i$, and passing from $a\mathbf f_i$ to $a_i\mathbf f_i$ contributes electric charge $2t_i v_i$ on orbit $i$. Passing from $\mathbf f_0$ to $\mathbf f$ contributes $aB$ to the restricted electric charge. Hence the sum of restricted local charge coordinates equals $b+\sum_i(N/n_i)2t_i v_i+aB$. The rotation normalization in (14.5.7aa.4) subtracts exactly those two contributions. The actual charged spaces are consequently the Fourier eigenspaces of this shifted product operator with charge index $b\bmod N$.

For total conformal grade $s$, the trace of the unshifted product rotation to power $\ell$ is
$$
\sum_{\sum_i s_i=s}\ \prod_i
\operatorname{Tr}_{\{L_{0,i}^{(a_i)}=s_i\}}\bigl(V_{i,a_i}^{\,\ell}\bigr).
\tag{14.5.7aa.5}
$$
The sum is finite on each grade, and its factors are (14.5.7z.5); an orbit of length one contributes the ordinary seed grade multiplicities. Multiply (14.5.7aa.5) by $e^{2\pi i\ell u(a)/N}$ and take the charge-$b$ Fourier projection as in (14.5.7z.6) with denominator $N$. This gives the exact charged multiplicities, including on composite fluxes with several cycle orbits. The energy of a grade $s$ is $\hbar\omega_0(s-\sum_i n_ir_i/24)$ with the same $\omega_0>0$ as on the parent. Under a change of local flux coordinates the local offsets, the global lift and the charge labels transport together.

The local extension census now follows from the isotropic-subgroup proof of Theorem 14.5.7z, which depends only on the quadratic group and its electric and flux coordinates. Its support gives the vacuum restriction, its size the index, and its perpendicular quotient the local sectors. Each extension's energy multiplicities sum the actual flux-and-charge multiplicities over that support. The parent equivalence constructed above transports the intermediate subnet and local coordinate choices, so it also transports the marked extension with its corresponding isotropic support. Conversely a marked extension equivalence restricts to a base equivalence and transports that support. With the base and sector coordinates fixed, distinct supports give distinct restricted vacuum sectors. This classifies extensions with the base and its sector coordinates fixed. A comparison that forgets those coordinates or the base inclusion requires an additional equivalence test. The proof covers multi-orbit permutations of holomorphic lattice factors; it does not extend the comparison to arbitrary chiral actions or other on-site encoding grammars. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457ab"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ab-unmarked-local-extension-census-for-prime-lattice-permutations"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ab (Unmarked Local Extension Census for Prime Lattice Permutations).** Let $p$ range over primes and $L$ over positive-rank even positive-definite unimodular lattices. Use the single $p$-cycle permutation of $\mathcal A_L^{\otimes p}$ and the calibrated oriented conformal response of Theorem 14.5.7z. Compare the fixed base, the permutation parent and every other irreducible local finite-index extension over that base as oriented vacuum nets, with the base inclusion and sector labels forgotten. In this family every fixed base is determined by $(p,L)$ up to lattice isometry, and no fixed base is equivalent to a proper extension. Parents are ordinary lattice nets and are equivalent exactly when their full lattices $L^{\perp p}$ are isometric.

A nonparent extension exists exactly in the trivial anomaly class. Write $\mathcal F_{p,L}$ for that flux extension. The flux extensions are pairwise equivalent exactly for identical $p$ and isometric $L$. The sole flux extension in this family equivalent to a parent is $\mathcal F_{2,E_8}$, which is equivalent to $\mathcal A_{E_8}^{\otimes2}$. Every other flux extension is also inequivalent to every ordinary positive-rank lattice net. These are all equivalences of the stated unmarked comparison class.

*Which extensions occur.* The prime case of the isotropic-subgroup census of Theorem 14.5.7z gives the fixed base and the pure electric parent for every $p$. It gives exactly one further extension when the anomaly vanishes, namely the subgroup meeting each flux once in a spin-zero sector. Both proper extensions are holomorphic. The fixed base has $p^2$ sectors, so its oriented conformal response cannot agree with either proper extension. Theorem 14.5.7z classifies the fixed bases. For parents, the intrinsic Cartan charge lattice of the ordinary lattice net recovers its lattice, and a lattice isometry gives the vacuum and clock preserving equivalence. The full orthogonal lattice decomposition of Theorem 14.5.7aa may be used to test that isometry without keeping an original grouping of the factors.

*Flux extensions whose twisted ground grade exceeds one.* Put $r=\operatorname{rank}L$ and $h_p=r(p-p^{-1})/24$. In any nonzero flux, the action is a full cycle, with twisted grades $h_p+m/p$ for nonnegative integral seed grades $m$. If $h_p>1$, the flux extension therefore adds no weight-one currents. Its weight-one Lie algebra is exactly the diagonal seed algebra of the fixed base, with invariant current form $p$ times the seed form. The same current Cartan is intrinsic, as in Theorem 14.5.7aa.

To compute the charge support in the entire extension, take a Cartan current $j$ of seed conformal weight one. The permutation-twisted vertex operator in a sheet of the $p$-fold cover has the form $w'(z)Y(j,w(z))$ for $w(z)$ one branch of $z^{1/p}$. In the permutation coordinate-change formula, the positive Virasoro modes act trivially on this primary state, and the remaining weight-one factor is exactly $w'(z)$. Sum over all $p$ sheets for the diagonal current. In the expansion $Y(j,w)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb Z}j_m w^{-m-1}$, the coefficient of $z^{-1}$ in each sheet is $j_0/p$, so the diagonal zero mode is $j_0$ on the seed module. This is independent of which nonzero flux reorders the sheets. Thus all Cartan charges of every added sector belong to $L$. The untwisted invariant space already realizes every charge of $L$ by the orbit-averaged one-factor vectors. The intrinsic charge lattice of the extension with its dual current metric is consequently
$$
\mathcal L_{\mathrm{flux}}=p^{-1/2}L,\qquad
\operatorname{rank}\mathcal L_{\mathrm{flux}}=r,\qquad
\det\mathcal L_{\mathrm{flux}}=p^{-r}.
\tag{14.5.7ab.1}
$$
The determinant means the Gram determinant. It recovers $p$ and then the rescaled lattice recovers $L$. Its value below one excludes every ordinary lattice net, whose intrinsic integral charge lattice has positive integer Gram determinant. Conversely equality of $p$ and seed isometry gives the parent cycle intertwiner and transports the unique flux support. It therefore extends to a vacuum and clock preserving equivalence of the flux extensions by the local algebra uniqueness used in Theorem 14.5.7z. See Barron et al. (1998, Section 3) and Carpi et al. (2015).

For odd $p$, the condition $h_p>1$ holds whenever a flux extension exists. For $p\ge5$ it follows from $r\ge8$. For $p=3$, zero anomaly requires $r$ to be divisible by $24$, as follows from (14.5.7z.1). For $p=2$ the anomaly is always trivial, since $4h_2=r/4$ is even, and $h_2=r/16$. This leaves only the ranks eight and sixteen.

*Transposition at seed rank sixteen.* Now $h_2=1$. The flux support adds exactly the twisted seed vacuum line to weight one, since every positive seed grade adds at least $1/2$ to the twisted grade. The charge convention selects this line because its twisted grade is integral, which is precisely the spin-zero character in flux one. Its bracket with the diagonal weight-one Lie algebra vanishes: the diagonal zero modes on the twisted module act as the seed zero modes, and the seed vacuum is invariant. Its self-bracket vanishes by antisymmetry. The new line is thus central in the full weight-one Lie algebra. The two rank-sixteen even unimodular lattices are $E_8\perp E_8$ and $D_{16}^{+}$, whose current Lie algebras have derived types $E_8\oplus E_8$ and $D_{16}$ respectively. The new central line preserves this distinction. Both flux extensions have current rank $17$ and central charge $32$, whereas a lattice net of central charge $32$ has current rank $32$. No odd prime in the stated family has $pr=32$ with positive $r$ divisible by eight. Therefore these two extensions are distinct and neither equals another entry or an ordinary lattice net. See Conway and Sloane (1982, Theorem 1 and Table 1).

*Transposition at seed rank eight.* The seed is $E_8$ and $h_2=1/2$. The spin-zero flux sector selects odd seed grades. Its contribution to weight one is precisely the seed grade-one space, an adjoint $E_8$ module under the diagonal current zero modes. The full compact weight-one Lie algebra is therefore $\mathfrak d\oplus\mathfrak m$ as a $\mathfrak d$-module, where $\mathfrak d$ is diagonal compact $E_8$ and $\mathfrak m$ is the odd adjoint copy. The $\mathbb Z_2$ extension grading gives $[\mathfrak m,\mathfrak m]\subset\mathfrak d$. Let $I:\mathfrak d\to\mathfrak m$ be an equivariant isometry for the positive invariant real current form. Such a rescaling of an adjoint isomorphism exists since the invariant forms are proportional. For $x,y,z\in\mathfrak d$, invariance and equivariance give
$$
\langle[Ix,Iy],z\rangle
=\langle Ix,[Iy,z]\rangle
=\langle x,[y,z]\rangle.
\tag{14.5.7ab.2}
$$
Hence $[Ix,Iy]=[x,y]$. The maps $x\mapsto (x\pm Ix)/2$ are Lie homomorphisms onto two commuting compact $E_8$ ideals. Their invariant forms are each half of the diagonal form, since the even and odd subspaces are orthogonal. The diagonal has affine level two, so both ideals have level one.

The two current systems generate the level-one affine $E_8$ tensor subnet of central charge $16$. Its Sugawara central charge is $8$ per factor, equal in sum to that of the extension. The difference stress tensor has zero norm in the unitary vacuum space, so the subnet uses the same conformal Hamiltonian. The level-one $E_8$ affine algebra has only the vacuum integrable highest-weight representation, as every highest-root mark of $E_8$ exceeds one. Its tensor subnet is holomorphic. Equivalently, the unitary extension decomposes into vacuum modules for the two level-one factors, and equality of the conformal Hamiltonians forces their vacuum multiplicity to be one. The two current systems thus exhaust the extension and identify it with $\mathcal A_{E_8}^{\otimes2}$, including its oriented vacuum and clock. This establishes the displayed collision as an actual equivalence of nets. Every other prime-cycle entry has central charge greater than sixteen, except the fixed base and parent from this same pair. The fixed base is distinguished by its sectors. This completes the unmarked census. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457ac"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ac-unmarked-index-p-extensions-of-a-full-p2-cycle"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ac (Unmarked Index-$p$ Extensions of a Full $p^2$-Cycle).** Let $p$ range over primes and $L$ over positive-rank even positive-definite unimodular lattices. On the full $p^2$-cycle source of Theorem 14.5.7z, compare all irreducible local extensions of index $p$ over the fixed base as calibrated oriented vacuum nets, forgetting the base inclusion and sector labels. The unmarked net determines $p$, $L$ up to isometry and whether the extension is pure electric or projects onto nonzero flux. The pure electric choice is unique and occurs on every source.

If the anomaly is nonzero, the pure electric extension is the only one. If the anomaly is zero, the remaining marked extensions have support
$$
J_j=\bigl\langle p\mathbf f+p j\mathbf e\bigr\rangle,\qquad j\in\mathbb F_p,
\tag{14.5.7ac.1}
$$
in the flux and electric coordinates of (14.5.7z.1). Two such extensions on equal-$p$, isometric-seed sources are unmarked equivalent exactly when both residues are zero, or when both are nonzero with square ratio in $\mathbb F_p^\times$. Thus there are three non-electric unmarked responses for odd $p$ and two for $p=2$ on every anomaly-free source. The separating tests are the current lattice, the local sector dimensions and an explicit finite vacuum energy grade.

*Supports and actual normalizer action.* Write $r=\operatorname{rank}L=8q$. The anomaly at order $p^2$ is $q p^2(p^4-1)/3$ modulo $p^2$. It vanishes unless $p=3$ and $3\nmid q$. In that exceptional case the anomaly representative is $t=3$ or $6$, and the divisor congruence (14.5.7z.2) for flux order $d=p$, charge intersection order $k=1$ is $3j\equiv -t/3\pmod3$, which has no solution. At index $p$ the only other divisor choice is $d=1,k=p$, the pure electric subgroup $\langle p\mathbf e\rangle$. In the zero-anomaly case (14.5.7z.2) gives all $p$ choices in (14.5.7ac.1).

Let $g$ denote the original $p^2$-cycle. The tensor-factor relabeling $i\mapsto u i$ for a unit $u$ modulo $p^2$ conjugates $g$ to $g^u$. It preserves the vacuum, stress and clock and induces a metric-group automorphism on the actual fixed sectors. The automorphism scales flux by $u$ and electric labels by $u^{-1}$. In the zero-anomaly coordinates the flux-one lift $\mathbf f$ has spin zero and is unique with that property, because $q(\mathbf f+b\mathbf e)=b/p^2$ modulo one. The image of $\mathbf f$ has the form $u\mathbf f+b\mathbf e$, with spin $ub/p^2$. Its spin vanishes only for $b=0$. Consequently this unitary takes $J_j$ to $J_{u^{-2}j}$. Every nonzero square multiplier occurs, since every nonzero residue modulo $p$ lifts to a unit modulo $p^2$. The equivalence of the local extension follows from uniqueness of the corresponding commutative algebra on that support as in Theorem 14.5.7z. This establishes sufficiency by an actual source unitary and also fixes the transport of all sector coordinates for the following test.

*Recovery of source and type.* The local quadratic group of each extension is $J^\perp/J$ of order $p^2$, so the intrinsic global sector dimension fixes $p$. Its central charge $p^2r$ fixes $r$. The pure electric extension is the fixed net of $\langle g^p\rangle$ on the parent, whose action is $p$ disjoint $p$-cycles. This follows from the restriction of the parent's electric characters to that subgroup. Its intrinsic current has rank $pr$ and Theorem 14.5.7aa recovers $L$ from the primitive cycle multiset.

In each nonzero flux of $J_j$ the original permutation has $p$ cycles of length $p$. Its common twisted ground grade is
$$
h=\frac{r(p^2-1)}{24}.
\tag{14.5.7ac.2}
$$
It is an integer in the zero-anomaly case, including $p=3$ when $3\mid q$. Except for $(p,r)=(2,8)$ it is greater than one, so no added state contributes a weight-one current. The current of a non-electric extension is then the diagonal seed current with its $p^2$-fold invariant form. Its entire charge support in the seed coordinates is $L$: in a twisted sector the diagonal zero mode is the sum of the $p$ seed zero modes, one for each cycle, by the sheet calculation in Theorem 14.5.7ab. The untwisted invariant space already witnesses every charge in $L$. Thus the dual-metric current lattice is $p^{-1}L$ and recovers the seed. In the exceptional pair $(2,8)$ the lattice is the unique $E_8$ seed of that rank. There can be at most one extra weight-one line, the twisted seed-product vacuum at $h=1$ when its charge is selected by $J_j$. It commutes with the diagonal current because the seed-product vacuum is trivial under the sum of zero modes, and its self-bracket vanishes. Hence the current rank of a non-electric extension is always $r$ except for that possible rank-nine response. In all cases this differs from the electric rank $pr$.

*Finite grade test on the residues.* Use $F_L(z)=\operatorname{Tr}_{V_L}z^{L_0}$. For every integer $s\ge0$ put
$$
A_s=[z^{ps}]F_L(z)^p,\qquad a_s=[z^s]F_L(z).
\tag{14.5.7ac.3}
$$
The coefficient $a_s$ is positive: the vacuum supplies $s=0$, and powers of any nonzero Heisenberg creation operator of grade one give states for every positive grade. Let $a=pv$ with $v\in\mathbb F_p^\times$. The actual ground line in that flux has electric coordinate $u(a)$ in (14.5.7z.6). Because its spin is $h\bmod1=0$ and the anomaly is zero, (14.5.7z.1) gives $v u(a)\equiv0\pmod p$. Thus $u(a)$ is a multiple of $p$. The normalizer sends the unique ground line of flux $p$ to the unique ground line of flux $pv$, and scales its electric coordinate by $v^{-1}$ modulo $p$ after division by $p$. Consequently there is one $c\in\mathbb F_p$ with $u(pv)=p c/v$ modulo $p^2$, namely the ground charge at $v=1$ divided by $p$. It is the actual ground charge from the normalized cyclic soliton, with the same flux lift throughout.

At conformal grade $h+s$ in flux $pv$, specialize (14.5.7z.5) to $c_a=m_a=p$ and $\nu_a=v^{-1}\pmod p$. The ground-normalized trace of $V_a^\ell$ is $A_s$ when $p\mid\ell$. Otherwise the $\ell$-fold shift is one $p$-cycle on the seed factors, so its trace is $a_s e^{2\pi i v^{-1}\ell s/p}$. This follows directly by using grade-homogeneous seed basis tensors: when the shift is a full cycle the only surviving tensors have all $p$ seed labels equal, and hence have common seed grade $s$. The accumulated covering rotation contributes the displayed phase. The support $J_j$ selects electric charge $p v j$ in flux $pv$. The ground offset multiplies the trace by $e^{2\pi i\ell c v^{-1}/p}$, with $1/v$ interpreted in $\mathbb F_p$, and the projection to $p v j$ contributes $e^{-2\pi i\ell vj/p}$. The Fourier sum (14.5.7z.6) therefore reduces to a sum over the $p$ residues of $\ell$ modulo $p$. Its zero-residue term is $A_s/p$. The sum of the other roots of unity is $p-1$ when $jv^2=c+s$ and $-1$ otherwise. The multiplicity in the selected sector is
$$
\frac{A_s-a_s}{p}+a_s\,1_{\{jv^2=c+s\}}.
\tag{14.5.7ac.4}
$$
Only integer grades can enter the local extension, because the selected sector is spin zero. Formula (14.5.7ac.4) gives every selected multiplicity at the integer grades starting at $h$. In particular at $s=0$ the ground is included exactly when $jv^2=c$.

The vacuum response of the extension is that of the fixed base plus the contributions from the $p-1$ nonzero fluxes, since $J_j$ has trivial pure electric intersection. At conformal grade $h+s$ its added multiplicity is exactly
$$
\frac{(p-1)(A_s-a_s)}{p}
+a_s\,\#\{v\in\mathbb F_p^\times:jv^2=c+s\}.
\tag{14.5.7ac.5}
$$
The clock reads this grade at energy $\hbar\omega_0(h+s-p^2r/24)$ in the common convention. As $s$ runs from $0$ to $p-1$, $c+s$ runs through $\mathbb F_p$. The test at $c+s=0$ distinguishes $j=0$ from every nonzero residue. For odd $p$, the test at $c+s=1$ distinguishes the square and nonsquare nonzero classes.
