For $p=2$ there is one nonzero class, and the first test already separates both entries. Positivity of $a_s$ makes each stated difference strict. The central charge, intrinsic charge support and sector dimensions used above ensure that only entries with equal $p$ and isometric $L$ need this last comparison. Their fixed-base characters agree, so the difference in (14.5.7ac.5) is a difference of their actual vacuum energy multiplicities. Together with the normalizer equivalence this completes the census. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457ad"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ad-unmarked-index-p-extensions-of-full-prime-power-cycles"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ad (Unmarked Index-$p$ Extensions of Full Prime-Power Cycles).** Let $N=p^m$ with prime $p$ and $m\ge2$. Use the full $N$-cycle permutation of the holomorphic lattice parent of Theorem 14.5.7z with positive-rank even positive-definite unimodular seed $L$. Compare its irreducible local extensions of index $p$ as calibrated oriented vacuum nets, forgetting the base inclusion and sector labels. The unmarked response determines $p$, $m$, the seed up to lattice isometry, and whether the extension is pure electric or has nonzero flux. There is one pure electric response on each source. The remaining responses are as follows.

1. At zero anomaly there are $p$ marked flux supports. Their unmarked classes are the zero slope and the nonzero square classes, giving two flux responses for $p=2$ and three for odd $p$.
2. Nonzero anomaly occurs only for $p=3$. At $m=2$ there is no flux extension. At every $m\ge3$ all three marked flux supports occur and are unmarked equivalent by actual tensor-factor relabelings.

Theorem 14.5.7ab supplies the exponent-one case. Its index-$p$ entries are holomorphic, whereas the entries of this theorem have nontrivial local sector category.

*Supports.* Put $M=N/p$, let $r=\operatorname{rank}L=8q$, and use $\mathbf e,\mathbf f,t$ in (14.5.7z.1) at order $N$. The anomaly is $qN(N^2-1)/3$ modulo $N$. If it is nonzero, $p=3$ and $t=MT$ with $T=1$ or $2$. The pure electric support is $\langle M\mathbf e\rangle$. Every other subgroup of order $p$ must project onto the flux subgroup of order $p$, and is generated by its unique lift $M\mathbf f+b\mathbf e$ of the flux $M$. Vanishing spin and exact order $p$ give respectively
$$
t+pb\equiv0\pmod{p^2},
\qquad
2t+pb\equiv0\pmod N .
\tag{14.5.7ad.1}
$$
At $t=0$ the solutions are $b=Mj$ for $j\in\mathbb F_p$. At nonzero $t$ and $m=2$ the two congruences contradict each other since $t$ is a nonzero multiple of $p$ modulo $p^2$. At nonzero $t$ and $m\ge3$ they give exactly
$$
b=b_0+Mj,\qquad b_0=-2t/p,\qquad j\in\mathbb F_p .
\tag{14.5.7ad.2}
$$
Indeed the order condition gives those solutions modulo $N$, and $t$ is then divisible by $p^2$, so the spin condition holds. The cyclic support classification of Theorem 14.5.7z realizes every solution as a unique marked local extension.

*Anomalous normalizer transport.* The actual tensor-factor relabeling $i\mapsto ui$ conjugates the cycle generator to its $u$-th power for a unit $u$ modulo $N$. It preserves vacuum, energy and all local net responses. It acts on sectors by scaling flux by $u$ and electric charge by $u^{-1}$. Write its image of $\mathbf f$ as $u\mathbf f+B\mathbf e$. Spin preservation determines $B$ uniquely modulo $N$ by
$$
t(u^2-1)+uBN\equiv0\pmod{N^2}.
\tag{14.5.7ad.3}
$$
At $t=0$ this gives $B=0$, so the relabeling transports slopes by $j\mapsto u^{-2}j$ as in Theorem 14.5.7ac. At nonzero $t=MT$ choose $u=1+pv$ with integer $v$. Let $u^{-1}$ be evaluated modulo $pN$ where it is used before division by $p$. Then (14.5.7ad.3) is equivalent to
$$
B=T(u^{-1}-u)/p\pmod N .
$$
The division is integral because $u\equiv1\pmod p$. In particular $B\equiv-2Tv\pmod p$. The image of the support generator in (14.5.7ad.2) is
$$
uM\mathbf f+\bigl(MB+(b_0+Mj)u^{-1}\bigr)\mathbf e .
$$
Using $uM=M+vN$, its flux is already $M$ and the cyclic carry adds $2tv$ to its electric coordinate. Thus its normalized electric coefficient is $b_0+Mj'$ with
$$
j'\equiv B+j u^{-1}+\frac{b_0(u^{-1}-1)}{M}+2Tv
\equiv j+2Tv\pmod p .
\tag{14.5.7ad.4}
$$
Here $b_0/M=-2T/p$ and $(u^{-1}-1)/p\equiv-v\pmod p$. Since $p=3$ and $T$ is nonzero, the actual relabelings act transitively on the three anomalous slopes. The support intertwiners lift to the local extensions by the uniqueness of the corresponding algebra object.

*Recovering the source and the electric choice.* Every extension has intrinsic global sector dimension $(N/p)^2=M^2$, by the order of $J^\perp/J$ in Theorem 14.5.7z. Unique prime factorization recovers $p$ and $m$ since $M\ge2$. The central charge $Nr$ fixes $r$. The pure electric extension is the parent's fixed net under $\langle g^p\rangle$, which acts by $p$ cycles of length $M$. Its current rank is $pr$ and the intrinsic primitive cycle inventory of Theorem 14.5.7aa recovers $L$.

Every nonzero flux in an extension in (14.5.7ad.1) is $a=Mv$ with $v\in\mathbb F_p^\times$. Its permutation has $M$ cycles of length $p$, with common ground grade
$$
h=\frac{rM}{24}(p-p^{-1})
=\frac{r\,p^{m-2}(p^2-1)}{24}.
\tag{14.5.7ad.5}
$$
Except for $(p,m,r)=(2,2,8)$ this exceeds one, so the flux extension adds no weight-one states. It then has the diagonal seed current with invariant form $N$ times the seed form. Its full charge support is $L$, since on each twisted cycle the diagonal zero mode is its seed zero mode, so the full diagonal zero mode is the sum over cycles by the covering calculation in Theorem 14.5.7ab. Every charge is already witnessed by the fixed base. Thus the intrinsic dual-metric charge lattice is $N^{-1/2}L$ and recovers $L$. In the exceptional case Theorem 14.5.7ac gives the unique rank-eight seed and a current of rank at most nine. The flux current rank is $r$ in all other cases and differs in every case from the pure electric rank $pr$. Consequently only supports of the same type on the same $p,m,L$ need further comparison.

*Finite grade separation at zero anomaly.* The ground grade (14.5.7ad.5) is an integer in this case. If $p\ne3$, divisibility follows from $r=8q$ and $3\mid(p^2-1)$. If $p=3$, zero anomaly forces $3\mid q$. For flux $Mv$ write its ground electric label as $u(Mv)$ in the rotation convention of Theorem 14.5.7z. Relabelings by units $u\equiv1\pmod p$ preserve this flux and its unique ground line, and at zero anomaly scale its electric label by $u^{-1}$. Taking $u=1+p$ forces that label to be divisible by $M$. The units acting transitively on $v$ then give $u(Mv)=Mc/v$ modulo $N$ for one $c\in\mathbb F_p$, determined by the actual ground line at $v=1$.

Write $F_L(z)=\operatorname{Tr}_{V_L} z^{L_0}$ and $a_s=[z^s]F_L(z)>0$ for every integer $s\ge0$. The positive grade claim follows from the powers of a grade-one Heisenberg creation operator. Test the finite conformal grades
$$
h+\frac Mp\,s,\qquad 0\le s<p .
\tag{14.5.7ad.6}
$$
In flux $Mv$, the source rotation in (14.5.7z.5) has $c_a=M$, cycle length $p$ and covering carry $v^{-1}$ modulo $p$. The numerator in the seed grade coefficient is $p((M/p)s)=Ms$. If $p$ does not divide the rotation power $\ell$, the shift on the $M$ cycles is itself one full cycle. Only constant seed basis tuples contribute to its trace, with common grade $s$. The unshifted trace is therefore $a_s e^{2\pi i v^{-1}\ell s/p}$. The ground offset contributes $e^{2\pi i\ell c v^{-1}/p}$, and the Fourier projection onto the actual support charge $Mvj$ contributes $e^{-2\pi i\ell vj/p}$. For powers divisible by $p$, these three phases are one. Their full contribution to the selected multiplicity depends on $p,m,L,s$ and not on $j$ or $v$, by (14.5.7z.5). Denote this contribution including the Fourier factor $1/N$ by $D_s$. The other powers have each nonzero residue modulo $p$ exactly $M$ times. Their summed contribution is
$$
\frac{a_s}{p}\bigl(p\,1_{\{jv^2=c+s\}}-1\bigr).
\tag{14.5.7ad.7}
$$
This directly gives the contribution at all the tested grades even when the lowest allowed weight of a selected sector is above its flux ground grade. In particular the ground line belongs to the selected sector exactly when $jv^2=c$. The spin-zero condition ensures that the selected extension only has integral grades. The tested grades are integral and have the common clock conversion $\hbar\omega_0(h+(M/p)s-Nr/24)$.

After summing over nonzero $v$ and adding the common fixed-base grade multiplicity, the only $j$-dependent part is
$$
a_s\,\#\{v\in\mathbb F_p^\times:jv^2=c+s\}.
\tag{14.5.7ad.8}
$$
The test with $c+s=0$ separates the zero slope from all nonzero slopes. For odd $p$, the test with $c+s=1$ separates the nonzero square and nonsquare slopes. For $p=2$ the first test gives the two classes. Every difference is strict since $a_s>0$. The normalizer relabelings already identify every pair within a nonzero square class, preserving all physical response data. Together with (14.5.7ad.4) and the source recovery, this proves the entire stated prime-power index class. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457ae"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ae-unmarked-prime-index-extensions-of-arbitrary-full-cycles"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ae (Unmarked Prime-Index Extensions of Arbitrary Full Cycles).** Let $N\ge2$, let $p$ be a prime divisor of $N$, put $M=N/p$, and use the full $N$-cycle permutation of the holomorphic lattice parent with seed $L$ of Theorem 14.5.7z. Compare the irreducible local extensions of index $p$ over the fixed base as calibrated oriented vacuum nets, forgetting the index, base inclusion and sector labels in the response. Theorem 14.5.7ab on prime cycles decides the holomorphic entries $M=1$. For $M>1$ the exact comparison is as follows.

1. The pure electric entry always occurs. Its response is that of the fixed net of $\langle g^p\rangle$ on the parent. Compare two pure electric entries by the primitive-cycle inventory of Theorem 14.5.7aa, namely $p$ copies of each pair $(M,L_i)$ for the orthogonally indecomposable summands $L_i$ of $L$. Equality of these inventories, with multiplicity, is necessary and sufficient even for different original $N,p,L$.
2. A non-electric entry is never equivalent to a pure electric entry with $M>1$. The non-electric response determines $(N,p,L)$ up to lattice isometry. If $p\nmid M$, at most one non-electric entry occurs and it occurs exactly when $p\mid t$. If $p\mid M$, non-electric entries occur exactly when $p^2\mid t$, and there are then $p$ marked flux supports. When $p=3$ and $t\ne0$, those supports give a single unmarked response. In every other case their unmarked equivalence is the zero and nonzero square-class test about the center $t/N\bmod p$ in the slope convention below. This gives two flux responses for $p=2$ and three for odd $p$.

Here $t$ is the integer anomaly representative in (14.5.7z.1). The center is used only when its denominator is invertible modulo $p$, or it is zero. This theorem compares all prime-index entries across all full-cycle orders and seeds, including both anomalous and anomaly-free cycles.

*Support calculation.* Write $r=\operatorname{rank}L=8q$, $\delta=t/N$, and use the primitive electric charge $\mathbf e$ and the specified flux-one lift $\mathbf f$ of Theorem 14.5.7z. The anomaly formula gives $\delta\in\{0,1/3,2/3\}$, with nonzero $\delta$ possible only when $3\mid N$. The pure electric support is $\langle M\mathbf e\rangle$. Any other support of order $p$ projects isomorphically onto the flux subgroup of order $p$ and has a unique generator $M\mathbf f+b\mathbf e$ over the flux $M$. Its spin and order conditions are
$$
t+pb\equiv0\pmod{p^2},\qquad 2t+pb\equiv0\pmod N.
\tag{14.5.7ae.1}
$$
The first condition implies $p\mid t$. If that divisibility holds, write all order solutions as $b=b_0+Mj$, where $b_0=-2t/p$ and $j\in\mathbb F_p$. The remaining spin condition is $Mj\equiv t/p\pmod p$. It has one solution for $p\nmid M$, and all $p$ or no solutions for $p\mid M$ according as $p^2$ divides $t$ or does not. Every isotropic support is realized by Theorem 14.5.7z.

*Source and type recovery.* The global dimension of the local sector category is $M^2$, so $M$ is read from the response. Holomorphic entries have $M=1$ and therefore cannot coincide with entries here having $M>1$. The response also gives its central charge $C=Nr$. An electric entry has current rank $pr=C/M$ and the actual parent action on the intermediate fixed net has $p$ disjoint $M$-cycles. This proves its exact comparison by Theorem 14.5.7aa. In any nonzero flux of a non-electric entry, the permutation has $M$ disjoint $p$-cycles and ground grade
$$
h=\frac{rM}{24}(p-p^{-1}).
\tag{14.5.7ae.2}
$$
For $M>1$ this exceeds one except at $(p,M,r)=(2,2,8)$. Away from that exception the only weight-one currents are the diagonal seed currents with invariant form $N$ times the seed form. By the covering calculation of Theorem 14.5.7ab, the full diagonal zero mode is the sum of the seed zero modes on the $M$ twisted cycles. The untwisted fixed net already supplies all charges in $L$, so the intrinsic dual-metric charge lattice of the full response is $N^{-1/2}L$. Its rank $r$ gives $N=C/r$ and $p=N/M$, and its scaled lattice gives $L$. Its current rank is strictly below the electric rank $C/M$. In the exceptional pair Theorem 14.5.7ac gives seed $E_8$, central charge $32$, $M=2$, and rank at most nine, below the electric rank $16$. No other non-electric entry at $C=32,M=2$ exists: $p$ must divide $16$, and the seed rank is a positive multiple of eight. Thus recovery and separation hold everywhere.

*Transport of the slopes.* For a unit $u$ modulo $N$, the tensor relabeling $i\mapsto ui$ conjugates the actual cycle to its $u$-th power. It acts on electric charges by $u^{-1}$ and takes $\mathbf f$ to $u\mathbf f+B\mathbf e$ with
$$
t(u^2-1)+uBN\equiv0\pmod{N^2}.
\tag{14.5.7ae.3}
$$
This follows from the spin of the flux-one lift and determines $B$ modulo $N$. In the $p$-support case write $b=b_0+Mj$. Decompose $u=w+pk$ with $1\le w<p$. After reducing the image flux $uM$ to $wM$, the cyclic carry contributes $2tk$ to its electric label. Subtracting the baseline label $wb_0$ leaves
$$
MB+b_0(u^{-1}-u)+Mj u^{-1}.
\tag{14.5.7ae.4}
$$
Indeed $2tk=(w-u)b_0$. Dividing (14.5.7ae.4) by $M$ and normalizing the flux multiplier $w$ modulo $p$ gives the new slope.

Suppose first that $p\ne3$ or $t=0$. Evaluate inverses modulo $3N$ when the denominator three is present. Equation (14.5.7ae.3) then reads $B=\delta(u^{-1}-u)$ modulo $N$, and $b_0/M=-2\delta$. Reduction of (14.5.7ae.4) gives
$$
j'=\delta+u^{-2}(j-\delta)\pmod p.
\tag{14.5.7ae.5}
$$
Reduction modulo $p$ makes the denominator three legitimate in this branch. Every nonzero residue modulo $p$ lifts to a unit modulo $N$, by the Chinese remainder theorem. Thus all centered slopes in a given square class are equivalent by actual parent relabelings and uniqueness of the extension algebra over a transported support.

Suppose instead that $p=3$, $t\ne0$, and the supports exist. Write $t=MT$ for $T\in\{1,2\}$. Choose units $u$ satisfying $u\equiv1+pv\pmod{p^2}$ for each $v\in\mathbb F_p$ and $u\equiv1$ on the other primary factors. Then $B=T(u^{-1}-u)/p$ modulo $N$, using the inverse modulo $pN$. The same carry, with $b_0/M=-2T/p$, gives $j'=j+2Tv$ modulo $p$. The shifts are transitive. These are actual equivalences of the three unmarked extensions, independently of the arbitrary choice of coordinates on the fixed sectors.

*Strict finite grade comparisons in the centered case.* It remains to show that slopes in different centered square classes give inequivalent responses when $p\mid M$ and either $p\ne3$ or $t=0$. Write the centered slope as $j$ in the rest of the proof. Choose a representative $b_*$ of the support corresponding to the center $\delta\bmod p$ in (14.5.7ae.5). This support is fixed by the full cycle normalizer. The support of centered slope $j$ uses electric label $v(b_*+Mj)$ over flux $Mv$, where $v\in\mathbb F_p^\times$. Denote by $G_v$ the electric coordinate of the unique ground line in flux $Mv$ in (14.5.7z.6), and set $C_v=G_v-vb_*$ modulo $N$. Under a normalizer unit $u$ and resulting flux multiplier $uv\bmod p$, this difference transforms by $u^{-1}$, since the ground line and the fixed support charge both undergo the same affine electric transport. In particular $(u-1)C_v=0$ modulo $N$ for every unit $u\equiv1\pmod p$.

Let $e=2$ if $p$ is odd and $M$ even, and $e=1$ otherwise, and put $D=M/e$. The greatest common divisor of $N$ and all $u-1$ for units $u\equiv1\pmod p$ is $pe$. At the primary factor of $p$ the minimum valuation is one, witnessed by $1+p$ with other factors chosen independently. At an odd primary factor prime to $p$ it is zero, by using the residue $2$. At the primary factor of two prime to $p$ the minimum is one, since all units are odd and the residue $3$ witnesses it when needed. These choices use the Chinese remainder theorem. Thus $C_v=Dc_v$ for a residue $c_v$ modulo $ep$. The transitive normalizer transport gives
$$
c_v\equiv c/v\pmod p,\qquad c_v\equiv\varepsilon\pmod e,
\tag{14.5.7ae.6}
$$
where $c=c_1\bmod p$ and $\varepsilon=c_1\bmod e$. If $e=1$ the second condition is empty. This uses the actual ground charges, not just the spin of a degenerate flux label.

Choose a nonnegative integer $s$ with $s\equiv\varepsilon\pmod e$ and test the conformal grade $h+(D/p)s$. This is an integer. Indeed $D/p$ is integral because $p\mid M$ and $e$ is prime to $p$. Also $h=qM(p^2-1)/(3p)$ is integral: for $p\ne3$ use $3\mid(p^2-1)$, and for $p=3,t=0$ use $3\mid q$. In (14.5.7z.5) at flux $Mv$, the cycle number is $M$, length $p$, and covering carry $\nu$ is $v^{-1}$ modulo $p$. If $e=2$, choose an odd representative of $\nu$. For a rotation power $\ell$ put $d_\ell=\gcd(\ell,M)$. The real trace coefficient before the covering phase is
$$
A_{d_\ell,s}=[z^{d_\ell s/e}]F_L(z)^{d_\ell},
\qquad F_L(z)=\operatorname{Tr}_{V_L}z^{L_0},
\tag{14.5.7ae.7}
$$
with coefficient zero if the exponent is not integral. This follows by inserting the chosen total seed grade $Ds$ into the cycle trace of (14.5.7z.5). Changing the integer representative of $\nu$ has no effect on any nonzero trace: if $e=2$, a trace with odd $\ell$ and odd $s$ has odd $d_\ell$ and vanishes by (14.5.7ae.7). The normalized sector projection onto the support charge combines the covering phase and the ground difference $C_v$ into
$$
\exp\!\left(\frac{2\pi i\ell}{p}
               \frac{c_v+\nu s-e vj}{e}\right).
\tag{14.5.7ae.8}
$$
The inner quotient is integral because $s\equiv\varepsilon\pmod e$, $c_v\equiv\varepsilon\pmod e$, and $\nu$ is odd when $e=2$. It is zero modulo $p$ exactly when $e jv^2\equiv c+s\pmod p$.

For $\ell$ divisible by $p$ the phase is one. For each possible $d_\ell$ with $p\nmid\ell$, its frequency among $0\le\ell<N$ is the same in each nonzero residue of $\ell\bmod p$. Indeed $d_\ell$ then depends only on the component of $\ell$ in the part of $M$ prime to $p$, and the $p$-primary component has equal counts in each nonzero residue. Consequently the sum of the weights $A_{d_\ell,s}$ in each nonzero residue class is the same positive number $W_s$. It is strictly positive because $d_\ell=e$ occurs in those classes and $[z^s]F_L(z)^e>0$, witnessed by grade-one Heisenberg creations. Summing (14.5.7ae.8) over the nonzero residues gives $p-1$ when its inner quotient is zero modulo $p$ and $-1$ otherwise. After summing over fluxes and adding the common untwisted character, the only slope-dependent term in the vacuum grade multiplicity is therefore
$$
\frac{pW_s}{N}\,
\#\{v\in\mathbb F_p^\times:e jv^2=c+s\}.
\tag{14.5.7ae.9}
$$
Choose $s\equiv\varepsilon\pmod e$ with $c+s\equiv0\pmod p$ to distinguish the zero slope. For odd $p$, choose one with $c+s\equiv e\pmod p$ to distinguish nonzero square and nonsquare slopes. The Chinese remainder theorem supplies nonnegative representatives, and the positive coefficient in (14.5.7ae.9) makes each difference strict. These are actual calibrated vacuum energy multiplicities at the common clock. Together with (14.5.7ae.5) and the anomalous transport, the tests give all unmarked equivalences in the theorem. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457af"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457af-complete-unmarked-coherent-collar-census-for-finite-twisted-doubles"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7af (Complete Unmarked Coherent Collar Census for Finite Twisted Doubles).** Use the admitted twisted-double sources of Theorem 14.5.7u with their fixed oriented clean-collar gluing. Compare the finite logical response on these collars, retaining the vacuum, sector channels, coherent splitting and fusion spaces, associators, exchange, and the unitary duality and trace. Retain every finite composition of these operations with coherent comparison and complete outcome instruments on the qualified logical inputs. Allow relabeling of simple sectors and changes of orthonormal bases in the fusion and splitting spaces. The marked edge multiplication and junction frame of Theorem 14.5.7u are absent from this comparison. The tests of microscopic inputs outside the clean collar and the full-lattice syndrome ranks are separate readouts.

Two sources $(G,\omega)$ and $(G',\omega')$ have equivalent responses in this complete unmarked collar comparison exactly when the pointed categories $\operatorname{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}$ and $\operatorname{Vec}_{G'}^{\omega'}$ are categorically Morita equivalent. There is a terminating exact test with a finite unitary witness for every positive result. The census includes an equivalence between the untwisted order-eight dihedral source and a nontrivially twisted $\mathbb Z_2^3$ source, while the two full torus sources are separated by an actual joint face-flatness readout even with the original junction marks forgotten.

*Logical response and structural criterion.* In Theorem 14.5.7u the retained collar algebra is obtained from flat-tent gluing and the actual oriented ribbon operations. Decompose its finite unitary tube algebra into simple blocks, resolve the tensor-product intertwiners, and use the Hilbert-space inner product on the multiplicity spaces. The resulting simple sectors, fusion and splitting, composition, gluing associator, exchange and unitary duality give the unitary Drinfeld center $\mathcal Z(\operatorname{Vec}_{G}^{\omega})$. The use of the complete logical response means that the tensor intertwiners can be compared coherently, including in a superposition of fusion channels. A fusion table, a list of dimensions, or spin and modular matrices alone would be a weaker comparison.

To see necessity of the stated equivalence, an intertwiner of the complete response transports the minimal sector projectors, maps the resolved fusion spaces unitarily, and intertwines each generator of coherent collar gluing. These data give a braided unitary tensor equivalence of the centers, by extending the transported maps under tensor product, direct sum and adjoint. Conversely such a functor supplies the sector and multiplicity-space maps. Its coherence and braid equations give an intertwiner of the generators, hence of all composed words and outcome probabilities, also with a fixed spectator. The logical instruments can keep their full sector outcome and complementary logical outcomes: orthonormal intertwiners resolve the identity in each fusion space, and transporting all of them transports the complete projection and conditional maps. In the physical source the finite fusion-space basis comparisons act on the qualified clean-collar registers using the finite controls of Theorem 14.5.7u. They can use fresh pointer registers and bounded Hermitian logarithms on paid positive intervals. The logical comparison carries its declared source energy and clock calibration, and leaves the microscopic rejection branch, native register transport across the two entire lattices, and comparison of their microscopic Hamiltonians to the full-source tests.

The center theorem for group-theoretical fusion categories identifies braided equivalence of these centers with categorical Morita equivalence of the pointed inputs. Equivalently, there is an indecomposable module over $\operatorname{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}$ with pointed dual tensor equivalent to $\operatorname{Vec}_{G'}^{\omega'}$. The module classification enumerates subgroups $H\le G$ with trivial restricted three-class and module cochains $\mu$ satisfying $d\mu=\omega|_H$, up to the chosen inverse convention. For each such subgroup the cochain classes are a torsor for $H^2(H,U(1))$. Only normal abelian $H$ can have a pointed dual; the relevant transported cochains must satisfy the invariance test for pointedness. The pointed dual has a finite group of simple objects and a scalar associator, so its identification with $(G',\omega')$ reduces to a group isomorphism and cohomology equality. This structural classification applies to the unitary comparison: a braided tensor equivalence between the unitary centers can be chosen unitary, and the canonical positive trace and twist are preserved. See Naidu (2006, Theorems 3.4, 4.9 and 5.8), Naidu and Nikshych (2007, Corollary 4.20) and Reutter (2019).

*Exact finite test.* The Smith construction of Theorem 14.5.7u permits normalized roots-of-unity representatives of every cocycle class. A change of representative has the same logical response by the unitary cochain transport there. With these representatives, the finite tube tables and their gluing coefficients are algebraic. Simple tube modules, isotypic projectors and fusion-space bases can be obtained by finite linear algebra over complex algebraic numbers: decompose the semisimple algebra using its center, select primitive matrix blocks and orthonormal bases, and solve the linear intertwiner equations in each block. Spectral projections and positive square roots of algebraic Hermitian matrices stay algebraic. Evaluating gluing and braid maps in these bases gives algebraic unit, associativity and exchange matrices. Equivalently the same calculation uses the projective centralizer representations and tube gluing of Theorem 14.5.7u. The actual native control coefficients need only be read after the cochain equivalence to the algebraic representative.

List the finitely many simple-label permutations $\pi$ preserving the tensor unit and fusion multiplicities. For each, introduce a matrix $U_{ab}^c$ between the source and target multiplicity spaces for the ordered fusion channel $(a,b;c)$. Choose the left and right unit conventions in advance, or include the unit intertwiner as a scalar variable. Require each $U_{ab}^c$ to be unitary. For every triple of input labels and each output label require that the source associator followed by the corresponding direct sums of tensor products of $U$ equals the target associator preceded by the corresponding direct sums. For each exchange require that the source braid followed by the reversed-channel $U$ equals the original-channel $U$ followed by the target braid. Include the unit equations. One may also impose the evaluation, coevaluation and twist equations in the chosen duality conventions, with phase variables for identifications of dual simple labels if needed. The canonical unitary trace is fixed by these data. These are finitely many polynomial equations in the real and imaginary entries of the unknown matrices, with real algebraic coefficients. Their validity is exactly the coherence of a braided unitary equivalence in skeletal multiplicity-space coordinates, since all objects are finite sums of the simples and all words use the declared tensor generators. This argument uses coherent intertwiners, including all multiplicity spaces, even when the category is nonpointed.

Real closed field elimination decides each system. It also gives a real algebraic sample point of every nonempty solution set, which is a unitary witness for the logical response equivalence. Conversely, if every system is empty, no retained-response intertwiner exists, since any such intertwiner would supply one of the displayed permutations and matrix solutions. Applying the pairwise test to the finite group and cohomology list of Theorem 14.5.7u yields the complete finite collar census at every admitted allocation. The proof of the test works for all finite group tables; the physical Golay–Leech realization remains on the declared bit allocations.

*An actual collision and an off-shell separator.* Let $G$ be the dihedral group of order eight with generators $a,b,z$, with $a^2=b^2=z^2=1$, $z$ central and $ba=zab$. For the central subgroup $H=\langle z\rangle$ and quotient $K=\mathbb Z_2^2$, the section $s(x,y)=a^x b^y$ satisfies
$$
s(x,y)s(x',y')=z^{yx'}s(x+x',y+y').
\tag{14.5.7af.1}
$$
Take trivial input cocycle and trivial module cochain on $H$. The dual pointed multiplication is the direct product $\widehat H\times K$, since the action is central and the dual extension cochain is trivial. The associator is evaluation of the extension factor (14.5.7af.1) by the first dual character, from the pointed dual formula. In coordinates $(\chi,x,y)\in\mathbb Z_2^3$ it is
$$
\omega'\bigl((\chi,x,y),(\chi',x',y'),(\chi'',x'',y'')\bigr)
=(-1)^{\chi y' x''}.
\tag{14.5.7af.2}
$$
The triadditive exponent directly gives a normalized three-cocycle. It is nontrivial: at the flux $(1,0,0)$ the stabilizer multiplier of (14.5.7u.3) is $(-1)^{y'x''}$, whose commutator on the $x$ and $y$ coordinate vectors is nontrivial. A coboundary multiplier on an abelian group has trivial commutator. Thus the actual dihedral and twisted elementary-abelian sources have equivalent complete unmarked collar responses by the module dual just constructed. Both occur with three edge bits in the same source geometry and with the same spectator and rotor allocations. See Naidu (2006, equation (24) and Theorem 4.9).

Now retain the physical face-flatness instrument on the whole finite torus of Theorem 14.5.7u and read its joint all-flat outcome, before imposing the vertex-invariant checks. Off-shell retention here means that this instrument acts on every microscopic edge input, with both the all-flat and complementary outcomes, including inputs that fail the vertex checks. Its all-flat projection has edge rank
$$
|G|^{|\mathcal V|-1}\,|\{(u,v)\in G^2:uv=vu\}|.
\tag{14.5.7af.3}
$$
To count flat words, fix a spanning tree and a root. A unique choice of vertex frames with root frame one sends the tree edges to the identity. This gives $|G|^{|\mathcal V|-1}$ independent choices of original tree labels for each normalized flat word. The normalized flat words are homomorphisms from the torus fundamental group into $G$: the two ordered based holonomies commute, and the remaining edges are determined by these holonomies and the face relations. No simultaneous conjugacy quotient occurs, since the root frame has been fixed. Thus (14.5.7af.3) counts edge configurations rather than gauge-invariant states.

The dihedral group has five conjugacy classes, so its number of commuting ordered pairs is $8\cdot5=40$ by the centralizer count. For $\mathbb Z_2^3$ every pair commutes, giving $64$. The cocycle changes the vertex phases and leaves each face-flatness predicate unchanged. Both sources have identical spectator and rotor states and edge dimension at this allocation. In the maximally mixed edge input, with the common spectator and rotor preparations, the all-flat outcome probabilities are the respective ranks divided by $8^{|\mathcal E|}$. They differ, and can be measured on the same declared clock by the actual complete face instrument. Hence the full torus response retaining this test distinguishes the two sources even with the original edge and junction labels forgotten. The exact collar census does not identify their complete microscopic responses. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457ag"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ag-finite-off-shell-response-census-for-unmarked-twisted-doubles"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ag (Finite Off-Shell Response Census for Unmarked Twisted Doubles).** Use the oriented torus geometry, branching, edge allocation, calibration, and marked native spectator and rotor dictionary of Theorem 14.5.7u. Use actual algebraic phase tables for the normalized cocycles and algebraic finite matrices for the specified collar generators. Compare the entire edge carrier, including inputs that fail any combination of checks, with arbitrary retained references. Keep the named full matrix edge factors of each macrocell, the complete joint face and vertex check instrument, the calibrated free Hamiltonian, and a specified finite list of coherent collar generators with their actual finite edge matrices and complete outcome records. A choice of this list may include the complete finite presentation of Theorem 14.5.7af when its generators are supplied with their physical edge embeddings and complementary branches. Keep each such generator's specified geometric support. Allow a common change of the finite logical collar sector labels and fusion-space bases throughout the list. The translation commands and the original microscopic junction marks are omitted. Compare the operator responses generated by the declared menu and their finite adaptive compositions. The native spectator and rotor factors are independently retained and are identified by their common dictionary.

There is a terminating exact equivalence test for this full off-shell menu, with a finite intertwiner in every affirmative case. The test works either with the unlabelled sharp edge instruments retained or with them omitted. In the former case any response equivalence induces an isomorphism of the group tables, even though multiplication commands are absent. The face constraints and the support, modulus, and phase-cycle data of the actual vertex projectors give explicit tests. The marked cocycle class is not assumed to be an intrinsic invariant of this menu. For the two sources of Theorem 14.5.7af the joint face-flatness probabilities already distinguish their off-shell responses.

*Operators and exact physical equivalence.* In the undressed edge presentation, let $K_x$ be the tensor product of the three outgoing edge factors of macrocell $x$, and write $K=\bigotimes_xK_x$. A comparison keeps every named full matrix algebra $\mathcal B(K_x)$ and uses a complex-linear normal isomorphism of the complete response algebras, intertwining the declared states, instruments and control words. On $K$ such an isomorphism is conjugation by a unitary $U$. Preservation of each named full matrix factor implies
$$
U=\bigotimes_x U_x
\tag{14.5.7ag.1}
$$
up to a scalar: the action on each matrix factor is inner, and after dividing by the product of those local unitaries the remaining operator commutes with every factor, hence with all of $\mathcal B(K)$. Conversely (14.5.7ag.1) preserves every declared macrocell support. Keeping the common spectator and rotor dictionary extends $U$ by the identity on these factors. The known on-site encoder of Theorem 14.5.7u transports this statement to the dressed physical algebra.

Let $P_f,P_v$ denote the face and vertex projectors. The check instrument has a branch $E_s\rho E_s$ for each status word, including the zero branches, with $E_s$ the joint spectral projection. Its coarse-grained outcome effect for a given positive check is the corresponding $P_f$ or $P_v$. Intertwining the complete instrument is therefore equivalent to intertwining each of those projectors with the same named outcome. In one direction take the coarse-grained effects. In the other, the joint projectors are products of these commuting projectors and their complements, and conjugation intertwines their Kraus maps on all inputs and all references. The fixed-calibration edge Hamiltonian is their weighted defect sum, so its evolution is then intertwined for every time. The native Hamiltonian acts on the independently retained factors and is unchanged. The finite edge embedding of each collar generator is part of the specified menu. In the comparison all its actual physical outcome maps are intertwined, including the complement of the collar qualification when that instrument tests it. The logical fusion changes are applied consistently to its finite logical ports and to every repeated use. This declares which outcome maps belong to the test even away from clean-collar inputs.

*The group table from an unlabelled edge basis.* Suppose the sharp edge instruments are retained. Their rank-one effects on each edge, tensored with the identity on the other factors, give a product basis without a choice of names for its atoms. An equivalence induces a permutation of each edge's atoms. On an ordered triangle $a<b<c$, the all-flat response to product atoms is the graph of the equation $x_{ab}x_{bc}=x_{ac}$, with inverse labels used where physical edge orientation disagrees. Thus a match of that single face supplies bijections $\alpha,\beta,\gamma:G\to G'$ with
$$
\alpha(x)\beta(y)=\gamma(xy)\qquad(x,y\in G),
\tag{14.5.7ag.2}
$$
after accounting for the registered orientations. Put $a_0=\alpha(1)$ and $b_0=\beta(1)$. Taking one input at the identity gives
$$
f(x)=a_0^{-1}\alpha(x)=\beta(x)b_0^{-1}
=a_0^{-1}\gamma(x)b_0^{-1}.
$$
Equation (14.5.7ag.2) then implies $f(x)f(y)=f(xy)$. Hence $f$ is a group isomorphism. This inference only uses the physical flatness check and the sharp atom preparations and readouts. It leaves all edge permutations that also pass the rest of the response tests to the finite census.

*Exact phase invariants of the bulk projectors.* Enumerate all permitted edge atom permutations in the sharp case and require their product permutation $\sigma$ to match every face predicate. Align the labels of the target edge matrices by $\sigma$. In these aligned coordinates an intertwiner preserving the atom effects is diagonal, with entry $z_X\in U(1)$ on each complete edge word $X$. For each named vertex $v$ and each entry $P_v(X,Y)$, first require its zero pattern and magnitude to match those of the aligned target $P_v'(X,Y)$. At every nonzero entry define
$$
r_{v,X,Y}=\frac{P_v'(X,Y)}{P_v(X,Y)}.
\tag{14.5.7ag.3}
$$
The necessary and sufficient unconstrained word-phase equation is $r_{v,X,Y}=z_X/z_Y$. Build the finite directed multigraph on edge words with one arrow $Y\to X$ for each entry, labelled by $r_{v,X,Y}$, and use the reciprocal label when traversing an arrow backwards. All closed walk products must be one. In particular every nonzero diagonal entry must agree, two different vertex entries with the same endpoints must give the same ratio, and every polygon through entries from possibly different vertices must give equal products in source and target after dividing their magnitudes. These products are invariants of the bulk response under all allowed word rephasings. Conversely assign a phase at one word in each component, then transport it along a spanning tree by the arrow ratios. The closed walk tests make every remaining arrow equation hold. This gives an exact cycle test for a global diagonal phase, including all disconnected and degenerate syndrome sectors.

To preserve the named macrocell factors as well, write $z_X=\prod_x z_{x,X_x}$ where $X_x$ is the triple of outgoing labels at $x$. The vertex ratios then impose
$$
r_{v,X,Y}=\prod_x z_{x,X_x}\,z_{x,Y_x}^{-1}.
\tag{14.5.7ag.4}
$$
The exponent rows have a $+1$ at each local word $X_x$ and a $-1$ at each $Y_x$, with cancellation when the two agree. For every integral relation among these rows the corresponding product of the ratios must equal one. This includes the closed walk products, but also imposes locality relations. For example, two entries in which the ordered local endpoint pairs agree at every changed macrocell must give equal ratios even if their unchanged spectator words differ. The full integer relation test is sufficient. Apply Smith normal form to the integer exponent matrix. Row operations multiply the target circle elements in the same integer combinations, column operations reparameterize the circle variables, and the nonzero diagonal entries impose only taking roots of circle elements. The zero diagonal rows must have target one, exactly the stated integral relation tests. This supplies a local unitary witness whenever the bulk phase conditions pass. For algebraic input ratios its phases can be chosen algebraic. Thus the actual projected vertex responses supply both ordinary phase-cycle invariants and additional locality constraints after all original junction marks are dropped.

*Complete exact test with collar operations.* For the sharp presentation one may first use (14.5.7ag.2)–(14.5.7ag.4) to reject impossible edge permutations. For each remaining one, use as variables the unit circle entries in (14.5.7ag.4) and the common logical collar basis transformations. In the presentation omitting the sharp probes, use general complex unitary matrices $U_x$ on the factors of (14.5.7ag.1), with no atom permutation step. Require $UP_fU^*=P_f'$ and $UP_vU^*=P_v'$ for all checks. Enumerate the finitely many allowed sector relabelings of the collar and impose the unitary constraints on each of its specified fusion multiplicity transformations. Impose the associator, braiding and other basis consistency equations as in Theorem 14.5.7af. For each retained actual physical operation compare the finite outcome maps after the same basis changes, including its qualifications and complements as declared. If a generator is a coherent unitary control, compare its stated controlled matrix as well, so that a relative phase observable by the declared coherent word is included. If it is an instrument, equality of the finite Choi matrices of its branches, with the retained registers, gives equality on arbitrary references. Preparations and declared logical embeddings use the same conjugations and branch equations. The list is finite and every supplied finite gate or outcome matrix entering the test is algebraic. The matching physical clock and Hamiltonian weights are fixed inputs to the comparison. These conditions are polynomial equations in real and imaginary entries of finitely many unknowns. The actual clock units and matching supports are fixed by the declared comparison.

Real closed field elimination decides the finite systems and gives an algebraic sample solution in each nonempty case. Conversely every equivalence of the stated response gives a solution, because its factor unitary, any required atom permutations and common collar gauge intertwine all generators. An affirmative solution transports the complete check instruments, free evolution and the declared physical control list. Composition and conditioning on retained outcomes preserve the intertwining for every finite adaptive word, including all inputs with failed checks. This proves sufficiency on the whole finite edge carrier without projecting onto the ground state or clean collar. The unchanged native factors carry their full energies and outcomes, and tensoring their identities preserves arbitrary retained references and the named support factors. For each finite list of source tables the test computes all equivalence classes. In particular it applies to a finite list of normalized algebraic representatives from Theorem 14.5.7u. For the stricter fixed macrocell support comparison a cohomology change is not a quotient assumed by the algorithm, since a cochain unitary involving adjacent faces can have expanded support. The actual representatives are the inputs to the test.

*Independent all-face invariant.* The all-flat effect $\prod_fP_f$ belongs to the check instrument and its rank is invariant even in the basis-free version. By the rooted spanning tree count in Theorem 14.5.7af this rank on the edge carrier is
$$
|G|^{|\mathcal V|-1}\,|\{(a,b)\in G^2:ab=ba\}|.
\tag{14.5.7ag.5}
$$
For the two order-eight sources there, the commuting-pair factors are $40$ for the dihedral group and $64$ for the twisted elementary-abelian group. The same maximally mixed edge preparation and independent native factors give different all-flat probabilities. This separates their actual complete off-shell responses before any collar data, basis marks or junction marks are used. It also shows why the collar Morita test cannot be substituted for the full declared generator comparison. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457ah"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ah-electric-intermediates-and-mixed-cyclic-extension-recovery"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ah (Electric Intermediates and Mixed Cyclic Extension Recovery).** Use a lattice-permutation parent $\mathcal P$ and its actual cyclic action $\langle g\rangle$ of order $N$ from Theorem 14.5.7aa, with calibrated clock and oriented vacuum response. Every irreducible local finite-index extension of $\mathcal P^{\langle g\rangle}$ factors through its unique maximal pure-electric intermediate. Pure-electric extensions have an exact unmarked comparison across all cycle orders, indices and lattice-permutation parents of that theorem. A mixed extension whose contributing nonzero fluxes have twisted ground grade greater than one determines the flattened lattice-cycle inventory of its electric intermediate and the order of its remaining flux subgroup. This gives a necessary comparison test even when the original parent and original index are omitted.

*The physical intermediate.* In the sector coordinates of (14.5.7z.1), let $J$ be the isotropic support of the extension, and put $K=J\cap\langle\mathbf e\rangle=\langle(N/k)\mathbf e\rangle$, of order $k$. Write $M=N/k$. The actual intermediate net with support $K$ is
$$
\mathcal P_K=\mathcal P^{\langle g^k\rangle}.
\tag{14.5.7ah.1}
$$
Indeed the parent decomposes over the base into the electric characters of $g$. A charge $b\mathbf e$ has eigenvalue $\exp(2\pi i b/N)$ and is invariant under $g^k$ exactly when $b\equiv0\pmod M$. Taking these eigenspaces gives the fixed subalgebra with its inherited products, vacuum and rotation, and extension uniqueness over the marked support gives (14.5.7ah.1). Inclusion of pure-electric intermediate nets corresponds to inclusion of these subgroups, since their vacuum restrictions contain precisely their marked sectors. This proves maximality and uniqueness of $\mathcal P_K$ as an intermediate inside the extension supported on $J$.

For each indecomposable seed cycle $(n_i,L_i)$ of the original flattened parent, the action $g^k$ breaks it into $\gcd(n_i,k)$ cycles of length $n_i/\gcd(n_i,k)$ on the same seed $L_i$. Form the multiset $\mathcal I_K$ of these pairs with multiplicities. Two pure-electric extensions, including the parent case $M=1$, have equivalent unmarked calibrated responses exactly when their resulting multisets agree. This is the intrinsic charge-lattice and parent comparison of Theorem 14.5.7aa applied to the actual intermediate actions, and allows different original indices and original parents.

*Remaining flux support.* The local quadratic sector group of $\mathcal P_K$ is $K^\perp/K$. Its electric and flux-one coordinates can be chosen as
$$
\mathbf e'=\mathbf e+K,\qquad
\mathbf f'=k\mathbf f-\ell\,\mathbf e+K,\qquad
t'=t\bmod M,\quad 0\le t'<M,\quad \ell=(t-t')/M .
\tag{14.5.7ah.2}
$$
In fact orthogonality to $(N/k)\mathbf e$ requires the old flux label to be divisible by $k$, by the polar pairing of (14.5.7z.1). The two displayed classes generate $K^\perp/K$, each $\mathbf e'$ having order $M$, with
$$
M\mathbf f'=2t'\mathbf e',\qquad
q(a\mathbf f'+b\mathbf e')=\frac{t'a^2}{M^2}+\frac{ab}{M}\pmod 1 .
\tag{14.5.7ah.3}
$$
The relation follows from $N\mathbf f=2t\mathbf e$, and the spin formula follows by substituting $(ak,b-a\ell)$ into (14.5.7z.1). The new anomaly is also the restricted permutation anomaly. If $h_k$ is the twisted ground grade for $g^k$, the original cyclic spin congruence gives $h_k\equiv t/M^2\pmod{1/M}$, so $M^2h_k\bmod M=t'$. Flux over the subgroup generator $g^k$ is read in the original group as the corresponding power of $g$. Equation (14.5.7ah.2) fixes the flux lift by transporting the original coordinates to the local sectors with their fusion and spins.

The original support has order $dk$, where $d$ is its projected flux order. In the notation (14.5.7z.3), the remaining support over $\mathcal P_K$ is
$$
J/K=\left\langle (M/d)\mathbf f'
+\bigl(b+(M/d)\ell\bigr)\mathbf e'\right\rangle .
\tag{14.5.7ah.4}
$$
It has order $d$ and intersects the new electric subgroup only in zero. Conversely every isotropic support over $\mathcal P_K$ with zero electric intersection lifts by inverse image in $K^\perp$ to an isotropic support over the original base with electric intersection $K$. The condition on the intersection follows directly from the quotient, and the spin descends on $K^\perp$ because $K$ is isotropic. To see that the factorization preserves the actual extensions, first extend by the local algebra supported on $K$. In the pointed local sector category its local modules are indexed by $K^\perp/K$, with the module of a class supported on the corresponding $K$-coset in the base. Extending next by $J/K$ therefore restricts over the original base to the multiplicity-one sum over exactly $J$. The unique connected commutative extension algebra over that support gives the same marked physical net. This uses the local-module and uniqueness results of Theorems 14.5.7r and 14.5.7z. See Davydov and Simmons (2017) and van Ekeren et al. (2020).

*Recovering the intermediate inventory without marks.* Write $(m_i,L_i')$ for the cycles in $\mathcal I_K$, and $r_i'=\operatorname{rank}L_i'$. In the following comparison suppose either $d=1$, or for each nonzero flux $a$ in the projected support of $J/K$ the independently calculated permutation ground grade satisfies
$$
h(a)=\sum_i\frac{r_i'm_i}{24}
\left(1-\frac{\gcd(m_i,a)^2}{m_i^2}\right)>1 .
\tag{14.5.7ah.5}
$$
Here $a$ uses the generator $g^k$. Its power $a$ splits cycle $i$ into $\gcd(m_i,a)$ equal cycles, giving the displayed grade by the covering rotation formula of Theorem 14.5.7aa. Since $J/K$ meets the electric subgroup only in zero, every added summand has nonzero flux and cannot add weight-one currents under (14.5.7ah.5). The existing weight-one Cartan and its invariant current form are therefore those of $\mathcal P_K$. For each cycle the Cartan zero mode on a twisted summand is the sum of the seed zero modes over its shorter cycles. The covering map for a weight-one current preserves its zero mode, and the original diagonal current sums the contributions of all shorter cycles, as in the covering calculation of Theorem 14.5.7ab. Thus every twisted charge is in the same product $\prod_i L_i'$ as the intermediate charge support. The intermediate vacuum already realizes all of that product by the orbit average of Theorem 14.5.7aa. Consequently the intrinsic dual-current metric lattice of the entire unmarked extension is exactly
$$
\bigoplus_i m_i^{-1/2}L_i'.
\tag{14.5.7ah.6}
$$
Its intrinsic orthogonal decomposition recovers $\mathcal I_K$ as in Theorem 14.5.7aa, and $M$ is the least common multiple of the recovered $m_i$. The remaining local sectors have global categorical dimension $(M/d)^2$, from the isotropic quotient by $J/K$, so the unmarked response also recovers $d$. In particular, two branches satisfying (14.5.7ah.5), or the pure-electric convention $d=1$, cannot agree if their intermediate inventories or their $d$ differ.

For a single original full cycle of central charge $C>32$, condition (14.5.7ah.5) holds for every contributing nonzero flux on the electric intermediate. Its flattened cycles have the same length $M$, and any nonidentity power has an effective cycle length $m\ge2$, giving $h=C(1-m^{-2})/24\ge C/32>1$. For a general multi-orbit intermediate the sum (14.5.7ah.5) is the required test. The recovery result does not decide equivalence of different residual supports on a common recovered inventory at the same $d$, nor the branches where a contributing twisted ground grade is at most one. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457ai"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ai-complete-unmarked-lattice-permutation-extension-census-through-central-charge-thirty-two"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ai (Complete Unmarked Lattice-Permutation Extension Census Through Central Charge Thirty-Two).** Use all cyclic lattice-permutation parents of Theorem 14.5.7aa whose total central charge $C$ is at most $32$, and all their irreducible local finite-index extensions. Compare the resulting oriented vacuum nets with their calibrated rotations, forgetting the original parent, base inclusion, cycle generator and sector marks. The following census is exhaustive up to that response equivalence.

1. Reduce each entry to its maximal electric intermediate and residual flux support by Theorem 14.5.7ah. If the residual flux is trivial, compare the resulting fixed nets by their flattened cycle inventories. In particular the ordinary lattice entries are compared by lattice isometry.
2. A residual flux extension of a single transposition of two $E_8$ factors, with any fixed spectator factors, is equivalent to its lattice parent with the same spectators.
3. At $C=32$, the residual nontrivial flux extension of an order-two action on two moving $E_8$ pairs is a holomorphic net with weight-one Lie algebra of semisimple type $E_8\oplus E_8$ and a one-dimensional center. The corresponding extension of a transposition of two rank-sixteen indecomposable even unimodular factors is holomorphic with semisimple type $D_{16}$ and a one-dimensional center. These are two distinct unmarked responses.
4. At $C=32$, the full four-cycle of $E_8$ factors has two inequivalent unmarked flux-only index-two extensions, of current ranks eight and nine, and one holomorphic flux-only index-four extension of current rank eight. The index-two entries are the two responses classified in Theorem 14.5.7ac. These three entries have no other unmarked equivalences in the census.

Apart from the equivalences in items 1 and 2 and equality of the entries already specified in items 3 and 4, there are no identifications. The two holomorphic responses of item 3 and the holomorphic response of item 4 are inequivalent to every ordinary lattice net.

*Exhaustion of the reduced inventories.* After factoring out the maximal electric intermediate, the remaining support meets the electric subgroup only at zero, so it is flux-injective. All flattened seed ranks are positive multiples of eight. The only indecomposable seed of rank eight is $E_8$, and the only indecomposable positive-definite even unimodular lattice of rank sixteen is $D_{16}^+$, the other rank-sixteen lattice being $E_8^{\perp2}$. Hence, under the stated central charge bound, every intermediate of nontrivial permutation order has one of the following flattened moving inventories, with additional fixed factors where the total rank allows:
$$
\begin{array}{c|l}
\text{order}&\text{moving cycles}\\ \hline
2&(2,E_8),\quad (2,E_8),(2,E_8),\quad (2,D_{16}^+)\\
3&(3,E_8)\\
4&(4,E_8).
\end{array}
$$
Fixed factors contribute zero to the anomaly and have their original conformal vectors and clocks. The order-three action has anomaly $t=2\pmod3$ by (14.5.7aa.1), so has no nontrivial flux-injective isotropic support. The order-two cases have zero anomaly. The only nontrivial flux-injective support for each is the bosonic flux subgroup of order two. For one moving $E_8$ pair, the fixed spectator net is holomorphic, and both the fixed base and its flux extension factor as their moving counterparts tensored with that same spectator. One can see the extension factorization by taking the bosonic flux extension of the moving factor and tensoring its algebra with the spectator identity. Its vacuum restriction over the full fixed base is exactly the required support, so the uniqueness in Theorem 14.5.7z identifies the extensions. The transposition equivalence in Theorem 14.5.7ab proves item 2. See Chenevier and Lannes (2015, Chapter II, §3.4).

*The two order-two flux responses of rank seventeen.* For two moving $E_8$ pairs or one moving $D_{16}^+$ pair the whole central charge is $32$, and the twisted ground grade is exactly $1$: a two-cycle of seed rank $r$ contributes $r(2-1/2)/24=r/16$. The twisted tensor ground line is one dimensional and is in the unique spin-zero sector of nonzero flux. Indeed the two charge sectors in that flux have spins separated by one half, and the vacuum ground line has integer grade. Thus the holomorphic extension adds exactly that line at weight one. All positive seed excitations raise the grade, by the covering formula. The line is annihilated by all zero modes of the original weight-one algebra: on each twisted cycle the diagonal current has the seed zero mode, and every seed vacuum is invariant under it. The line's Lie bracket with itself is zero. Consequently it is central in the new weight-one algebra, which is the direct sum of that line and the old diagonal Lie algebra. The two old diagonal Lie algebras have semisimple types $E_8\oplus E_8$ and $D_{16}$, with rank sixteen. This proves the two distinct rank-seventeen entries of item 3.

*The order-four ground label.* The full four-cycle has anomaly zero by (14.5.7z.1). Its flux-injective supports of order two are $\langle2\mathbf f\rangle$ and $\langle2\mathbf f+2\mathbf e\rangle$. The sole flux-injective support of order four is $\langle\mathbf f\rangle$. Its odd fluxes have ground grade $5/4$, and its flux-two ground grade is $1$. Write $u_a$ for the electric coordinate of the ground line in flux $a$ in the canonical zero-anomaly convention of (14.5.7z.1). Spin gives $u_1=1\bmod4$ and $u_2=0$ or $2\bmod4$. The sign distinguishing the two flux-two possibilities is fixed by the calibrated twisted traces as follows.

Let $f(\tau)=\operatorname{Tr}_{V_{E_8}}\exp(2\pi i\tau(L_0-1/3))$, and let $T(a,b;\tau)$ denote the trace in flux $a$ with cyclic insertion $g^b$ in these normalized sector coordinates, using the total conformal vacuum shift $32/24$. The order-four covering action in (14.5.7z.5)–(14.5.7z.6) gives
$$
T(1,2;\tau)=-e^{i\pi/3}f((\tau+2)/4),\qquad
T(2,-1;\tau)=e^{-2\pi i u_2/4}e^{-i\pi/3}f(\tau-1/2).
\tag{14.5.7ai.1}
$$
For the first formula the seed grade $m$ has covering phase $e^{2\pi i(2m/4)}$, ground phase $e^{2\pi i(2u_1/4)}=-1$, and total shifted grade $(m-1/3)/4$. For the second, the transposition of the two seed cycles leaves only equal basis labels in the trace. Their combined shifted grade is $m-1/3$, with covering phase $e^{-i\pi m}$ and ground phase $e^{-2\pi i u_2/4}$. These two observations give the displayed factors.

The zero-anomaly sector Fourier matrix in these coordinates gives $T(1,2;-1/\tau)=T(2,-1;\tau)$. Sum the cyclic orbifold sector $S$-matrix with the electric insertion phases to obtain this identity. The seed character is $S$-invariant and transforms under $\tau\mapsto\tau+1$ by $e^{-2\pi i/3}$. If $x=\tau-1/2$, then $(2-1/\tau)/4=x/(2x+1)=ST^{-2}S^{-1}x$, so $f((2-1/\tau)/4)=e^{4\pi i/3}f(x)$. Substituting in (14.5.7ai.1) gives $e^{-2\pi i u_2/4}=-1$. Thus $u_2=2\bmod4$. See van Ekeren et al. (2020, Theorem 5.13).

The holomorphic support $\langle\mathbf f\rangle$ excludes the unique grade-one flux-two line, so it has exactly the diagonal $E_8$ current algebra, of rank eight. The two index-two supports give ranks eight and nine respectively. The added line at rank nine commutes with the diagonal currents by the seed-vacuum zero-mode argument above. The two index-two extensions have sector global dimension four.

*Exhaustive separation.* At $C=32$ the fixed nets of order two have current rank at least sixteen: the moving pairs contribute one Cartan per pair and the fixed factors contribute their full ranks. Hence they cannot agree with the two order-four index-two flux responses, even though all these nonholomorphic responses have sector global dimension four. The order-three fixed nets have sector global dimension nine and the order-four fixed base has sector global dimension sixteen. Their unmarked base comparison is item 1. A holomorphic net has sector global dimension one, so cannot agree with any of these nonholomorphic entries. An ordinary lattice net at $C=32$ has current rank thirty-two, separating it from all the extra holomorphic entries, of ranks seventeen or eight. The semisimple types separate the two rank-seventeen responses and the rank separates the order-four holomorphic response from both. At lower central charges there are no additional moving inventories or extension entries beyond those already compared. Central charge itself is determined by the calibrated conformal response. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457aj"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457aj-unmarked-residual-flux-comparison-for-cyclic-lattice-permutations"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7aj (Unmarked Residual Flux Comparison for Cyclic Lattice Permutations).** Factor the irreducible local extensions in Theorem 14.5.7aa through their actual maximal electric intermediates as in Theorem 14.5.7ah. For extensions with the same intermediate lattice-cycle inventory, the unmarked calibrated responses are equivalent exactly when the residual flux orders agree and the residual type parameters belong to the same unit-square orbit defined below. This criterion applies to arbitrary composite orders and cyclic anomaly types. It is intrinsic on the full family satisfying the ground-grade hypothesis of Theorem 14.5.7ah, where the response determines the intermediate inventory and residual flux order.

*Residual coordinates.* Fix an intermediate of order $M$ and type $t$, with $0\le t<M$, in the charge and flux coordinates $(\mathbf e,\mathbf f)$ of (14.5.7ah.3). Let the flux-injective isotropic support $J$ over it have order $d\mid M$ and put $A=M/d$. Its uniquely normalized generator has flux $A$ and is $A\mathbf f+b\mathbf e$. The order condition $2t+db\equiv0\bmod M$ and the spin condition $t+db\equiv0\bmod d^2$ imply $d\mid t$. Put $v=t/d$. The order condition gives $b=-2v+Aj$ modulo $M$, where $j$ is a residue modulo $d$. Isotropy now reads $Aj\equiv v\bmod d$. Consequently
$$
T=\frac{v-Aj}{d}\in\mathbb Z,\qquad dT\equiv v\pmod A.
\tag{14.5.7aj.1}
$$
Changing the lift of $j$ modulo $d$ changes $T$ by a multiple of $A$. Conversely an allowed residue $T\bmod A$ determines $j=(v-dT)/A\bmod d$, hence determines the whole normalized support. Thus (14.5.7aj.1) is a bijection between the residual supports and the indicated congruence classes of $T$.

*Intrinsic quadratic response.* The pairing of $a\mathbf f+c\mathbf e$ with the support generator is $(aj+c)/d\bmod1$. The orthogonal subgroup is therefore generated by $\mathbf f-j\mathbf e$ and $d\mathbf e$. Write $\mathbf x,\mathbf z$ for their classes in $J^\perp/J$. The electric class $\mathbf z$ has order $A$ because $J$ intersects the electric subgroup trivially. Since $A\mathbf f=-b\mathbf e$ in this quotient, $A\mathbf x=-(b+Aj)\mathbf e=2(v-Aj)\mathbf e=2T\mathbf z$. Substitution in the parent quadratic form gives
$$
A\mathbf z=0,\qquad A\mathbf x=2T\mathbf z,\qquad
q(a\mathbf x+c\mathbf z)=\frac{T a^2}{A^2}+\frac{ac}{A}\pmod1 .
\tag{14.5.7aj.2}
$$
The displayed relations present the group of order $A^2$. The polar pairing convention is $B(x,y)=q(x+y)-q(x)-q(y)$, so $B(\mathbf x,\mathbf z)=1/A$. In particular (14.5.7aj.2) uses the twist $\exp(2\pi i q)$ itself and applies at even order as written.

*Intrinsic necessity without an electric mark.* A response equivalence induces an isometry of the DHR sector group with its spin. Its cardinality first fixes $A$. If $\mathbf x$ maps to $a\mathbf x'+c\mathbf z'$ for the second presentation, comparing the two spins modulo one and multiplying by $A^2$ gives $T\equiv a^2T'\bmod A$. The inverse isometry gives $T'\equiv a'^2T\bmod A$. For every $p^e\Vert A$ the two congruences force the same truncated valuation $\min(e,v_p(T))=\min(e,v_p(T'))$, with $v_p(0)=+\infty$. Wherever this valuation is less than $e$, the first congruence forces $a$ to be a unit modulo $p^e$. Wherever it equals $e$, both type parameters vanish modulo $p^e$ and any unit works. The Chinese remainder theorem produces a unit $u\bmod A$ such that
$$
T\equiv u^2T'\pmod A .
\tag{14.5.7aj.3}
$$
The argument uses the quadratic twist, with no division by two. In particular if $2^e\Vert A$, the maximum power of two in the order of a twist is $2^{2e-\min(e,v_2(T))}$. Every twist in (14.5.7aj.2) has at most that power in its denominator. The twist of $\mathbf x$ attains it when $v_2(T)<e$, and the twist of $\mathbf x+\mathbf z$ attains it when $v_2(T)>e$, including $T=0$. When $v_2(T)=e$, the twist of $\mathbf x$ attains it. These orders are unchanged by projecting to the $2$-primary subgroup because the odd-primary part has odd twist order. For $A=1$ there is a single residue and the test is vacuous.

*Physical realization of sufficiency.* Use the flattened inventory comparison of Theorem 14.5.7aa to identify the two intermediate lattice-permutation parents and their cyclic actions. Choose a lift of $u$ in $(\mathbb Z/M)^\times$, which exists because $A\mid M$. On each indecomposable seed cycle of length $m\mid M$, label its factors by $\mathbb Z/m$ and relabel them by $i\mapsto ui$. This actual tensor permutation preserves the vacuum and calibrated rotation, normalizes the intermediate cyclic action and sends a flux label $a$ to $ua$. It preserves the electric subgroup and carries $J$ to some $J''$ of the same order and with zero electric intersection. In the target quotient the image of $\mathbf x$ is $u\mathbf x''+c\mathbf z''$ for some $c$, since its old flux before passing to the quotient is $u$ and the electric subgroup of the orthogonal group is generated by $d\mathbf e$. Spin preservation therefore gives $T\equiv u^2 T''\bmod A$. Equation (14.5.7aj.3) and the bijection (14.5.7aj.1) imply $J''=J'$. Transport of the unique local extension over this isotropic support gives an isomorphism of the actual extensions preserving the oriented vacuum response and calibrated clock. The factorization in Theorem 14.5.7ah identifies these with the original extended carriers, including when their original parents or original indices differ. The intrinsic sector formulas use the cyclic orbifold quadratic-form construction. See van Ekeren et al. (2020).

*Coverage and a composite example.* For a full cycle of length sixteen with seed $E_8$ and residual order $d=4$, one has $t=0$ and $A=4$. All four residues of $T$ occur, and every unit square modulo four is one, so the four residual supports give inequivalent unmarked responses. For any pair satisfying the ground-grade hypothesis of Theorem 14.5.7ah, the three complete comparison data are the recovered intermediate inventory, $d$, and the orbit of $T$ under multiplication by unit squares modulo $A$. Without that hypothesis the flux criterion still decides every comparison with matching intermediate inventories. Different intermediate inventories in higher-central-charge branches with low-grade flux require a further intrinsic comparison. Theorem 14.5.7ai covers those branches through central charge thirty-two.

<a id="theorem-1457ak"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ak-low-grade-involutions-and-unmarked-cyclic-extension-classes"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ak (Low-Grade Involutions and Unmarked Cyclic Extension Classes).** Use the lattice-permutation extensions of Theorem 14.5.7aa and reduce each through its maximal electric intermediate by Theorem 14.5.7ah. All extensions from single original full cycles have a complete unmarked comparison at every central charge. For arbitrary flattened inventories, every possible new weight-one current lies in the unique involution flux, and the moving inventory for that flux belongs to the four cases below. The resulting comparison is complete on the union of the extensions with no new currents and those with new currents from an intermediate order divisible by two exactly once. The current-adding order-four case has the stated intrinsic separator from this union.

*All single original full cycles.* If the original full cycle has total central charge $C>32$, (14.5.7ah.5) holds for every participating nonzero flux on the electric intermediate, whose cycles all have the same length. Theorems 14.5.7ah and 14.5.7aj compare any two such responses by their recovered inventories, residual flux orders and unit-square orbits of the residual types. Theorem 14.5.7ai compares every pair with $C\le32$. The calibrated conformal response preserves $C$, so this decides the unmarked equivalence of every pair of entries in the class of single original full cycles, allowing different original orders, lattices and indices.

*Exhaustion of fluxes at low grade.* Write $M$ for the intermediate action order. For any power of its generator, split the moved factors into the actual cycles of that power. A moved cycle of length $m\ge2$ on a seed of rank $r$ contributes $rm(1-m^{-2})/24\ge rm/32$ to its ground grade. Thus ground grade at most one requires moved central charge at most thirty-two. The moving inventory of the power can only be one or two $E_8$ transpositions, one $D_{16}^{+}$ transposition, one $E_8$ triple, or one $E_8$ four-cycle. This uses the same indecomposable rank-eight and rank-sixteen lattice census as Theorem 14.5.7ai. The four-cycle has ground grade $5/4$. The triple has ground grade $8/9$, and every excitation is an integer multiple of $1/3$ above it, including integer excitations from the fixed factors. No grade in that flux is integral, so an isotropic support contains none of its sectors. All other possibilities are involutions. The cyclic action on the flattened parent is faithful, so the relevant flux, when present, is the unique involution $a=M/2$.

Put $s=v_2(M)$. The intermediate cycles moved by this involution have $v_2(m)=s$, and each of them splits into $m/2$ transpositions on its own seed. The possible intermediate moving inventories and ground grades are therefore
$$
\begin{array}{c|l|c}
s & \text{intermediate cycles moved by the involution} & h(M/2)\\ \hline
1 & (2,E_8) & 1/2\\
1 & (2,E_8),(2,E_8) & 1\\
1 & (2,D_{16}^{+}) & 1\\
2 & (4,E_8) & 1
\end{array}
\tag{14.5.7ak.1}
$$
The other intermediate cycles are fixed by the involution. In particular no supported flux adds currents at odd intermediate order or when $s\ge3$. If the supported nonzero flux sectors contribute no weight-one state, the proof of (14.5.7ah.6) still gives the entire current charge lattice. The existing currents are unchanged and all added charge labels belong to the same product of seed charge lattices by the zero-mode covering calculation in Theorem 14.5.7ah. Thus Theorem 14.5.7aj compares the whole class with no new currents even when an unsupported sector in a contributing flux has a low ground grade.

*Exact factorization at $s=1$.* Write $M=2B$ with $B$ odd. The three possible moving inventories in the first three rows of (14.5.7ak.1) have action of order two. The remaining intermediate factors have odd cycle lengths with least common multiple $B$. The Chinese remainder decomposition of the actual cyclic action on the parent makes the intermediate fixed net a tensor product of the active order-two fixed net and the passive odd-order fixed net. Its sector quadratic group is the corresponding product, with orders four and $B^2$. If the flux-injective isotropic support contains involution flux, then its primary factors give $J=J_2\times J_B$. The factor $J_2$ is the order-two spin-zero flux support of the active net, and $J_B$ is flux-injective over the passive net. Extending the two nets by those supports and tensoring gives the same multiplicity-one sector support $J$ over the same fixed net, so extension uniqueness identifies the actual extended carrier and its calibrated vacuum response. The active factor is holomorphic. The passive odd-order factor adds no weight-one currents by the flux exhaustion above.

For a single $E_8$ transposition, the active extension is the ordinary lattice parent on the two $E_8$ factors by Theorem 14.5.7ab. Replace that factor by two fixed lattice factors on the same passive source. The resulting presentation adds no currents to its intermediate. This reduces every comparison involving this branch to the comparison of the resulting responses in their presentations without new currents, except when the other response has rank congruent to one modulo eight as below.

*Intrinsic comparison of the rank-seventeen active factors.* For the second and third rows of (14.5.7ak.1), Theorem 14.5.7ai gives the respective active holomorphic factors of charge thirty-two. Their weight-one Lie algebras have semisimple parts $E_{8,2}\oplus E_{8,2}$ and $D_{16,2}$ and have a one-dimensional center. The subscript is the affine level relative to long roots of squared norm two. The old diagonal currents have twice the seed form and the new line is central, giving the stated levels. Every passive simple ideal has odd level because its current form is its odd cycle length times the level-one seed form. The even-level simple ideals of the entire response thus identify the active semisimple type intrinsically. Also the total Cartan rank is one modulo eight, whereas an extension without new currents has rank zero modulo eight, including the presentations obtained by replacing the single transposition.

For two rank-seventeen tensor branches of the same active type, use the intrinsic Cartan charge lattice with the current form and project it orthogonally away from the Cartan of those even-level simple ideals. The tensor product makes the charge support the orthogonal product of the active and passive charge supports. For the active factor the projected lattice is a rank-one lattice. In detail, the charge group of a unitary strongly regular vertex algebra is a full lattice with rational form in its Cartan, and the semisimple Cartan here is spanned by its root charge vectors in that lattice. Its orthogonal projection is therefore rational and has a discrete rank-one image in the orthogonal line. The passive charge lattice is exactly the orthogonal sum of the scaled indecomposable seed lattices in (14.5.7ah.6). These summands all have ranks divisible by eight. The unique orthogonal decomposition of the projected lattice recovers the passive summands, with the rank-one summand from the active center. Rescaling the passive summands to determinant one recovers the seed lattice and the odd cycle length of each as in Theorem 14.5.7aa. See Mason (2011, Sections 11–14).

The active factor is holomorphic, so the full response has exactly the passive sector quadratic group. After recovering the passive inventory, its cardinality gives the residual passive order and its quadratic spins distinguish the unit-square orbits by Theorem 14.5.7aj. Conversely equality of these data identifies the passive extensions by that theorem and the active factors are the same actual extensions from Theorem 14.5.7ai. Their tensor product gives sufficiency. This decides the cross-inventory comparisons for both rank-seventeen branches with arbitrary odd-order passive sources.

*Current-adding four-cycle case.* In the fourth row of (14.5.7ak.1), the involution ground line is unique. If it is retained by the support, it is a central current because all its seed zero modes are vacuum zero modes. It increases total Cartan rank by one and leaves the diagonal $E_8$ at level four. Every passive cycle length has two-adic valuation at most one, so this is the unique simple current ideal with level divisible by four. The rank and level tests distinguish the current-adding fourth row from every response without new currents and from both rank-seventeen branches. When the ground line is omitted, the extension belongs to the class without new currents. The level and rank tests leave the cross-inventory comparison of two current-adding fourth-row branches with coupled even-order passive actions.

<a id="theorem-1457al"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457al-complete-unmarked-cyclic-lattice-permutation-extension-census"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7al (Complete Unmarked Cyclic Lattice-Permutation Extension Census).** Consider the local finite-index extensions of all flattened cyclic lattice-permutation actions of Theorem 14.5.7aa, with the original parent, action and extension support omitted from the calibrated response. Theorems 14.5.7aj and 14.5.7ak, together with the reconstruction below, give a complete unmarked comparison at every central charge, for arbitrary cycle multiplicities and arbitrary allowed cyclic anomaly. For the current-adding fourth row of (14.5.7ak.1), the response recovers the whole maximal electric intermediate inventory. Thus the residual flux order and unit-square orbit decide all comparisons in that row.

*Passive subnet and the new current.* Write the intermediate parent as $\mathcal P_{\rm a}\otimes\mathcal P_{\rm p}$. Its action is generated by $g=(g_{\rm a},g_{\rm p})$, where $g_{\rm a}$ is the four-cycle on four $E_8$ factors, $v_2(M)=2$ for $M=\operatorname{ord}(g)$, and all cycle lengths in $\mathcal P_{\rm p}$ have two-adic valuation at most one. In particular $g_{\rm p}^{M/2}=1$. The embedded subnet $\mathbf 1\otimes\mathcal P_{\rm p}^{\langle g_{\rm p}\rangle}$ belongs to the intermediate fixed net, even when the group action does not split on the two factors. The unique new current $w$ lies on the involution ground line. It is the tensor of the active permutation-twisted ground with the passive untwisted vacuum, since the involution acts trivially on the passive factors. This tensor description takes place before the sector eigenspace projection. Passive fixed fields act there as the identity on the active twisted module and as their original vacuum fields on the passive factor. The sector eigenspace restriction preserves this action. See Barron et al. (1998).

If $v$ is any state of the passive fixed subnet, its nonnegative modes annihilate $w$. Indeed the passive field on its vacuum has only nonnegative powers of the field coordinate by the creation axiom. The formal identity $Y(w,z)v=e^{zL_{-1}}Y(v,-z)w$ then gives $w_0v=0$. In addition $w$ is central in the full weight-one algebra. Its bracket with each old current vanishes by the invariant ground-state argument, and the exhaustion in Theorem 14.5.7ak gives no other new current. The original current form pairs $w$ orthogonally to all old currents. The residual support gives a cyclic grading of the extension, and pairing with an old current in grade zero vanishes because $w$ has the nonzero involution grade. The pairing also follows by applying a character of the cyclic support that maps its generator to a primitive root of unity. This automorphism fixes the old currents and sends $w$ to $-w$ while preserving the invariant form.

*Intrinsic projected charge lattice.* The affine $E_8$ current ideal of level four is unique by Theorem 14.5.7ak. Choose a compact Cartan of the weight-one algebra, containing the central line $\mathbb Rw$, a Cartan of that ideal and a passive Cartan. In the full dual Cartan charge lattice, project orthogonally off the Cartan of the $E_8$ ideal using the invariant current form. After metric identification, let $\mathscr L$ denote the projected lattice in $\mathbb Rw\oplus\mathfrak h_{\rm p}^{*}$. The full charge support is a full rational lattice by the strongly regular charge-lattice theorem, and the subspace projected out is rational because it is spanned by the $E_8$ root charges. Thus $\mathscr L$ is a full lattice in this orthogonal sum. See Mason (2011, Sections 11–14).

Write $\mathscr P=\bigoplus_i m_i^{-1/2}L_i$ for the passive charge lattice, with $(m_i,L_i)$ running over the passive intermediate inventory. Every charge in the full response has passive component in $\mathscr P$. For every permutation flux, the passive diagonal zero mode is the sum of the seed zero modes over the shorter cycles of that power, and the metric on the passive current is still its original one by orthogonality to $w$.
