This is the covering argument of (14.5.7ah.6) on the passive factors and applies before any sector eigenspace projection. Conversely every $p\in\mathscr P$ is realized in the passive fixed subnet by the orbit average of Theorem 14.5.7aa, and each such state has zero $w$ charge by the preceding mode calculation. Consequently
$$
\{0\}\perp\mathscr P\ \subseteq\ \mathscr L\ \subseteq\ \mathbb Rw\perp\mathscr P,\qquad
\mathscr L=(\mathscr L\cap\mathbb Rw)\perp\mathscr P .
\tag{14.5.7al.1}
$$
The equality follows by subtracting $(0,p)$ from each projected lattice vector with passive component $p$. The remaining intersection with $\mathbb Rw$ is a discrete rank-one lattice since $\mathscr L$ has full rank. Unique orthogonal decomposition therefore singles out the passive scaled seed lattices, all of whose ranks are multiples of eight, and removes the rank-one factor. Determinants recover the lengths $m_i$ and rescaling recovers the seeds. The active inventory is the single four-cycle of $E_8$, already identified intrinsically by the level-four ideal and the rank criterion. This proves recovery of the full intermediate inventory from the unmarked response.

*Comparison.* This row is separated from the other rows and from the class without new currents by Theorem 14.5.7ak. Within it, the recovered inventory fixes $M$ and the intermediate type. The DHR cardinality $A^2$ gives $d=M/A$, and the spin comparison (14.5.7aj.3) gives the last invariant. Equality is realized by the actual parent cycle relabeling and extension uniqueness of Theorem 14.5.7aj. Together with Theorem 14.5.7ak this covers all the stated lattice-permutation extensions, including different original parents and different original extension indices. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457am"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457am-marked-local-extensions-of-lattice-inversion-nets"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7am (Marked Local Extensions of Lattice Inversion Nets).** Fix a positive-definite even lattice $L$ of rank $r>0$ and the marked inversion fixed net $\mathcal B_L\subset\mathcal A_L$ of Theorem 14.5.7s. Compare irreducible local finite-index extensions of $\mathcal B_L$ over that marked base. Let $H$ run through isotropic subgroups of the lattice discriminant form $L^*/L$, and let $M\subset L^*$ be the even overlattice with $M/L=H$. The following finite census includes all ranks and discriminants.

1. The extensions supported entirely on untwisted sectors are the ordinary lattice extensions $\mathcal A_M$ and the fixed nets $\mathcal A_M^{\langle\vartheta\rangle}$, where $\vartheta$ runs through lifts of inversion on $M$ restricting to the fixed inversion on $L$. For a fixed $M$, the ordinary lattice entry is unique. The marked fixed-net entries are indexed by the lifts modulo conjugation by $\widehat H=\operatorname{Hom}(H,U(1))$. After choosing a reference lift, their parameters are $\widehat H/(\widehat H)^2$. Their indices over $\mathcal B_L$ are $2|H|$ and $|H|$, respectively.
2. Every extension with twisted support has a unique untwisted intermediate from the fixed-net entries in part 1. Fix a representative lift for each such marked entry. The extensions over that intermediate are its index-two extensions by a twisted simple current $X$ of spin one and with $X\otimes X\simeq 1$. The marked comparison over the original $\mathcal B_L$ identifies $X$ only under the action of $\widehat H[2]$ on the intermediate. These entries have index $2|H|$. This part is empty unless $8\mid r$. At ranks with $8\nmid r$, the unmarked output comparison is also complete. Ordinary lattice and inversion fixed-net outputs are compared by the lattice, inversion and mixed code tests of Theorem 14.5.7s.

*Untwisted intermediate.* Denote the odd sector of the marked parent by $j$, so its algebra is $E=1\oplus j$. The orbifold category has a $\mathbb Z_2$ grading by untwisted and twisted types. Double braiding with $j$ is the identity on the untwisted types and the scalar $-1$ on the twisted types. These are the trivial and nontrivial fluxes evaluated by the electric sign character in the equivariantization of the graded category. The untwisted full subcategory is the equivariantization of the lattice pointed category under charge inversion. These statements follow from the orbifold equivariantization and use only its neutral part and grading. The sector labels and fusion are those of the lattice inversion fixed algebra. See Galindo et al. (2024, §7.1), Abe and Dong (2002a, Theorem 7.7) and Abe et al. (2003, Theorem 5.1).

Let $A$ be the connected local extension algebra, initially supported on untwisted types. If $j$ occurs in $A$, the parent algebra $E$ embeds. The Frobenius pairing on the $j$ multiplicity space is nondegenerate and multiplication of its elements lands in the one-dimensional degree-$1$ space. A nonzero degree-$j$ element with nonzero square is invertible, so multiplication by it injects that space into the degree-$1$ space. The unique normalized algebra on $1\oplus j$ gives the parent inclusion. The extension of that parent is exactly the even overlattice $M$ by the pointed lattice extension theorem of Theorem 14.5.7v. Conversely restriction of each $\mathcal A_M$ to the marked $\mathcal B_L$ gives this entry. The copy of $E$ is unique as a subalgebra and its two identifications over $\mathcal B_L$ differ by inversion. Inversion preserves each lattice overlattice, so the marked comparison does not identify distinct $H$.

If $j$ is absent, form the braided tensor product of algebras $A\otimes E$. It is a commutative separable algebra because $A$ centralizes $E$. It is connected since
$$
\dim\operatorname{Hom}(1,A\otimes E)
=\dim\operatorname{Hom}(1,A)+\dim\operatorname{Hom}(j,A)=1 .
$$
It is therefore the local algebra of an actual extension containing the marked $\mathcal A_L$. The sign on the second tensor factor fixes exactly $A\otimes1$. The pointed lattice census identifies the larger extension with $\mathcal A_M$ over $\mathcal A_L$, and its sign becomes an involution $\vartheta$ restricting to the prescribed inversion on $L$. On the entire Heisenberg space, already present in the full-rank $L$, it acts as minus the identity. Thus it is a lift of lattice inversion on $M$. Conversely each such lift gives a fixed-net extension of $\mathcal B_L$ whose restriction has no $j$. It has only untwisted types, since it is a subnet of $\mathcal A_M$.

For each $M$ a reference lift exists. Start with any order-two lift of inversion on $M$, then multiply by a character of $M$ extending the character needed to match the prescribed lift on $L$. Every character of the finite-index sublattice extends to $M$, and inversion conjugates a character to its inverse, so the adjusted lift still has order two. The other allowed lifts differ by characters trivial on $L$, precisely $\widehat H$. The automorphisms of $\mathcal A_M$ fixing $\mathcal A_L$ pointwise are exactly those characters. This also follows from the multiplicity-one sector decomposition over $\mathcal A_L$, since each scalar on a charge sector must multiply under the products. Conjugation of the lift by a character changes its parameter by the square of that character. Conversely every equivalence of the marked fixed-net extensions induces one of their tensor-product algebras $A\otimes E$ by tensoring with the identity of $E$. This induced map is over $\mathcal A_L$ and intertwines the sign involutions. It therefore forces equality of $H$ and precisely the square equivalence of the lift parameters. The same argument identifies the pointwise automorphisms of the marked intermediate as the restrictions of $\widehat H[2]$. Such a restriction has trivial kernel, since fixing both $A\otimes1$ and $1\otimes E$ fixes the algebra generated by them. The indices follow from the index-two fixed-point inclusions and $[\mathcal A_M:\mathcal A_L]=|H|$.

*Twisted extension step.* Suppose twisted sectors occur in $A$. The grading gives an automorphism of the full extension acting by $+1$ on the untwisted summands and by $-1$ on the twisted summands. This follows on the algebra object because the fusion grade is multiplicative. Its actual fixed subnet is precisely the untwisted intermediate and has index two in the extension. That intermediate cannot contain $j$. In fact if $j$ occurs, multiplication by an invertible homogeneous element in that sector is an isomorphism on each $j$-translated homogeneous summand. In algebra-object form, composing multiplication by $j$ twice gives the nonzero unit product $j\otimes j\to1$, so the multiplication map with any occupied summand is nonzero. Local commutativity applied twice would require that map to equal its composition with the double braiding, which is $-1$ for a twisted summand. This excludes coexistence. The untwisted intermediate is therefore one of the fixed nets from part 1.

For the inclusion over this intermediate, the index-two fixed-point theorem gives exactly one nonvacuum sector $X$, of statistical dimension one and of order two. Locality gives spin one. Under restriction to $\mathcal B_L$, it has twisted type. Equivalently this is a twisted sector of the intermediate inversion net itself. Indeed the neutral types for either inversion net centralize the inversion-odd electric sector. Upon passing from $\mathcal B_L$ to its untwisted intermediate, the electric sector of $\mathcal A_M^{\langle\vartheta\rangle}\subset\mathcal A_M$ is the induced local module $j\otimes A_{\rm un}$, where $A_{\rm un}$ is the intermediate algebra. Monodromy with that induced module is inherited from monodromy with $j$. This can also be seen before condensation, since the tensor algebra $A_{\rm un}\otimes E$ decomposes as the intermediate vacuum and precisely that induced module. Hence intermediate sectors with twisted restrictions are exactly its twisted sectors. For each candidate $X$, the dimension-one symmetric subcategory generated by the spin-one order-two object is $\operatorname{Rep}(\mathbb Z_2)$, and its regular algebra gives the unique connected local extension $1\oplus X$. The intermediate is intrinsic over the original base, so the only further identifications come from its computed automorphism group $\widehat H[2]$. This proves part 2. The finite labels and the check $X\otimes X\simeq1$ use the module and fusion tables above. For the index-two fixed-point and local-extension correspondences, use the same unitary $Q$-system convention as Theorems 14.5.7q and 14.5.7s. See Bischoff et al. (2014).

The inversion-twisted Heisenberg oscillators are half-integer moded and give a vacuum shift $r/16$, with no momentum contribution because inversion has no fixed Heisenberg direction. The two parity types occupy grades in $r/16+\frac12\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$. An integer grade is possible only if $8\mid r$, which proves the exclusion. The same rank applies to every overlattice. For unmarked untwisted outputs, all lifts on a fixed $M$ are conjugate by the character torus of the free lattice $M$, where every character has a square root. Theorem 14.5.7s then compares the resulting ordinary and inversion nets even across different original lattices and extension indices. The remaining unmarked comparisons are those involving twisted outputs in ranks divisible by eight. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457an"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457an-twisted-lattice-inversion-extension-test-and-unmarked-holomorphic-census"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7an (Twisted Lattice-Inversion Extension Test and Unmarked Holomorphic Census).** Use a marked untwisted intermediate $\mathcal A_M^{\langle\vartheta\rangle}$ from Theorem 14.5.7am, with $M/L=H$, rank $r$, and discriminant group $\Gamma=M^*/M$. A twisted index-two local extension exists exactly when
$$
8\mid r,\qquad 2\Gamma=0,\qquad
(\lambda,\lambda)\in\mathbb Z\quad\text{for every }\lambda\in M^* .
\tag{14.5.7an.1}
$$
In the affirmative case, set $R=M\cap 2M^*$ and $D=L+2M$. There are exactly
$$
\frac{|\Gamma|}{[R+D:D]}
\tag{14.5.7an.2}
$$
such extensions of this intermediate up to equivalence over the original marked $\mathcal B_L$. Once the base inclusion is omitted, the extensions from a fixed isometry class of $M$ all have the same calibrated vacuum response, independent of the twist character and lift. Among the resulting holomorphic outputs, all responses of central charge at most twenty-four are classified below.

*Twisted labels and fusion.* For a chosen standard inversion lift, write $\widehat M$ for the lattice central extension by the sign $\kappa$ and $K=\{a^{-1}\vartheta(a):a\in\widehat M\}$. The quotient $\widehat M/K$ has projection to $M/2M$. Its commutator on lattice images $\alpha,\beta$ is $(-1)^{(\alpha,\beta)}$. Thus its center projects onto $R/2M$, with kernel generated by $\kappa$. The twisted module labels are the central characters $\chi$ with $\chi(\kappa)=-1$, together with a parity. The characters form a torsor for $\operatorname{Hom}(R/2M,U(1))$. Existence follows by extending the sign character to the finite abelian center. The inversion-twisted modules with these labels exhaust the twisted sectors, and the two parities have weights congruent to $r/16$ and $r/16+1/2$ modulo one. When $8\mid r$, each character therefore supplies exactly one spin-one parity. For other ranks neither parity is spin one. See Abe and Dong (2002a).

By the contragredient formula of Abe et al. (2003), a twisted character is self-dual exactly when $\alpha^2\in4\mathbb Z$ for every $\alpha\in R$. Indeed taking the dual multiplies $\chi$ on a central element over $\alpha$ by $(-1)^{\alpha^2/2}$, independently of its parity. In the self-dual case, their twisted-square fusion rule gives precisely one untwisted summand for each charge orbit under $\lambda\mapsto-\lambda$ satisfying $\chi^{(\lambda)}=\chi$. Here $\chi^{(\lambda)}(a)=(-1)^{(\bar a,\lambda)}\chi(a)$ on a lift of $\bar a\in R$. If the charge orbit is a singleton, exactly one of its two parity labels occurs. The condition is therefore
$$
(\alpha,\lambda)\equiv0\pmod2\quad\text{for every }\alpha\in R.
\tag{14.5.7an.3}
$$
Dividing $\alpha$ by two identifies $R/2M$ with $\Gamma[2]$. Thus (14.5.7an.3) says that the charge class annihilates $\Gamma[2]$ in the perfect discriminant pairing. The annihilator is exactly $2\Gamma$, since $2\Gamma$ is contained in it and both have order $|\Gamma|/|\Gamma[2]|$. The charge-zero summand is the vacuum by self-duality, and there is just that summand exactly when $2\Gamma=0$. In that event $R=2M^*$, so the duality criterion is $(\lambda,\lambda)\in\mathbb Z$ for all $\lambda\in M^*$. This proves (14.5.7an.1), including the necessity of order two as distinct from spin one. See Abe et al. (2003, Proposition 3.7 and §5.4).

*Character action and exact count.* A sign character $\eta$ of $M$ acts on the twisted label by multiplying $\chi$ by the restriction of $\eta$ to $R$. This follows from the lift on the lattice central extension, which multiplies an element over $\alpha$ by $\eta(\alpha)$ and fixes $\kappa$. The conformal grades fix the spin-one parity, so this action preserves the selected parity. The over-base automorphism group is the group of sign characters trivial on $L$, namely the dual of the elementary abelian group $M/D$. Its restriction image on $R$ has order $[R+D:D]$, because characters of a subspace of $M/D$ extend to $M/D$. Its orbits on the central-character torsor all have that size. In the affirmative case $R/2M\simeq\Gamma$, which proves (14.5.7an.2). The argument is unchanged for any chosen lift extending the fixed inversion on $L$, since conjugacy by the character torus of $M$ identifies it with a standard lift, and that torus commutes with every sign character. See Shimakura (2006, Proposition 1.9 and the character lift preceding it).

If the base mark is omitted, all sign characters of $M$ are available. Their restrictions onto the dual of $R/2M$ are surjective, because $R/2M$ is a subspace of $M/2M$. Thus they transitively carry the twisted characters and their unique spin-one parities. Extension uniqueness gives actual unitary isomorphisms of the resulting local nets. Lattice isometries and inversion-lift conjugacies carry this conclusion between isometric $M$.

*Holomorphic outputs through central charge twenty-four.* The global sector dimension of the inversion fixed net is $4|\Gamma|$, from the two homogeneous parts of the orbifold category and its $\mathbb Z_2$ equivariantization. The twisted local extension has index two, so its global sector dimension is $|\Gamma|$. Thus the output is holomorphic exactly when $M$ is unimodular. In particular, a holomorphic twisted output cannot be equivalent to any inversion fixed-net output, whose global sector dimension is at least four. Its possible ordinary lattice comparators are exactly the unimodular lattice nets of the same rank. The following list compares all such holomorphic twisted and ordinary lattice outputs through charge twenty-four.

1. At charge eight there is a single response, with the twisted $E_8$ output equivalent to the ordinary $E_8$ net.
2. At charge sixteen there are two responses. The twist from $E_8\perp E_8$ gives the ordinary $D_{16}^+$ net and the twist from $D_{16}^+$ gives the ordinary $E_8\perp E_8$ net.
3. At charge twenty-four the twenty-four twisted inputs give twenty-three distinct responses. The two inputs $E_8^3$ and $D_{16}E_8$ both give the ordinary $D_8^3$ net. There are exactly nine pairs of twisted input and equivalent ordinary Niemeier output, involving eight distinct ordinary outputs. The table names each source and comparator by its lattice root system. $\Lambda$ denotes the rootless Leech lattice. Together the ordinary and twisted constructions give thirty-nine responses.

| Twisted source | Equivalent ordinary lattice |
|---|---|
| $E_8^3$ | $D_8^3$ |
| $D_{16}E_8$ | $D_8^3$ |
| $D_8^3$ | $D_4^6$ |
| $D_4^6$ | $A_1^{24}$ |
| $A_1^{24}$ | $\Lambda$ |
| $D_{24}$ | $D_{12}^2$ |
| $D_{12}^2$ | $D_6^4$ |
| $D_6^4$ | $A_3^8$ |
| $D_{10}E_7^2$ | $D_5^2 A_7^2$ |

*Proof of the holomorphic comparison.* Use the actual straight and reflection-twisted lattice constructions of Dolan et al. (1994). For a unimodular $M$ the only central character and its unique integral parity give their twisted vacuum construction, by the uniqueness of the index-two extension in Theorem 14.5.7am. Their current algebra calculations in §6 give the rank-eight and rank-sixteen responses as stated. Their Table 2 gives the full weight-one Lie algebra comparison at charge twenty-four. The fifteen twisted inputs absent from the table above have distinct weight-one Lie algebras of rank less than twenty-four, so they are distinguished from each other and from the ordinary lattice nets. Their §7 binary-code triality provides conformal state-field isomorphisms for the nine mixed pairs, including the two inputs with the same $D_8^3$ output in their Figure 4. Their state-field isomorphisms carry the vacuum and conformal vector. Essential uniqueness of the normalized invariant unitary structure makes these isomorphisms unitary after transport, as in Theorem 14.5.7s. Their Hilbert extensions therefore identify the closed local fields, local algebras, bounded-instrument response and $L_0$ spectral calculus at the same calibrated clock. This applies directly to the admitted local conformal extensions because the extension construction identifies their vacuum state-field products with those of the unique simple-current extensions. See Dolan et al. (1994, §6 and §7) and Carpi et al. (2015).

<a id="theorem-1457ao"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ao-finite-operator-product-comparison-of-lattice-inversion-outputs"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ao (Finite Operator-Product Comparison of Lattice-Inversion Outputs).** Compare the oriented vacuum nets with the calibrated rotations and complete local instrument menu of Theorem 14.5.7s. Given any two local extension outputs in Theorem 14.5.7am, there is a terminating exact test for response equivalence after the base inclusions are omitted. It applies to ordinary lattice, inversion fixed-net and twisted outputs at arbitrary allowed ranks and discriminants. The test constructs finite mode data from the lattice and twist labels, and decides a system of polynomial equations over the real algebraic numbers. An affirmative result gives an algebraic map on finitely many grades that uniquely extends to the full vacuum response equivalence.

*Finite mode test.* Let $V$ and $W$ be simple strongly local unitary vertex operator algebras of CFT type. The grade-zero space is the vacuum line and all higher grades are finite dimensional. Choose $B\ge2$ such that the positive grades through $B$ strongly generate both algebras. Strong generation means spanning by words of the form $x^1_{-k_1}\cdots x^\ell_{-k_\ell}\mathbf1$ with $k_i\ge1$ and the $x^i$ chosen from homogeneous bases of the generator grades. Use a homogeneous basis $S$ of the positive grades through $B$ in $V$. For every $x,y\in S$ and $n\ge0$ with $h=\mathrm{wt}(x)+\mathrm{wt}(y)-n-1\ge0$, express the singular product $x_n y$ in $V$ by a linear combination $P_{x,y,n}$ of negative-mode words of grade $h$. The vacuum counts as the empty word. There are finitely many such words in a fixed grade, since each letter has positive weight and its mode increases grade by at least that weight. Compute the expressions by linear algebra. At negative $h$ both algebras have zero product by grading.

Seek a grade-preserving unitary linear map $F$ from the grades through $B$ of $V$ onto the corresponding grades of $W$. Require it to preserve the vacuum and conformal vector, commute with the two antilinear PCT operators, and intertwine $L_1$ on those grades. Require in addition
$$
(Fx)_n(Fy)=P_{x,y,n}(FS)
\tag{14.5.7ao.1}
$$
for every listed singular product, where the words on the right are evaluated with the same coefficients and negative mode indices in $W$. Necessity follows from the vacuum and conformal field equivalence. For sufficiency, grade preservation, unitarity and PCT compatibility will extend from the finite generator space to the Hilbert completions by the following word argument.

*Reduction of words and Gram matrices.* For a word in generator modes, call the sum of the positive conformal weights of its letters its weight sum, independent of the mode indices. For a negative-mode word of conformal grade $h$ this sum is at most $h$. Evaluate an arbitrary homogeneous word on the vacuum. Commute its rightmost mode with nonnegative index to the right past the modes that follow it, using
$$
[x_m,y_n]=\sum_{i\ge0}\binom mi(x_i y)_{m+n-i}.
\tag{14.5.7ao.2}
$$
The sum is finite. On the commute-through term the chosen mode either reaches the vacuum and annihilates it, or is absent if the word already uses only negative modes. Each commutator term replaces two letters by a mode of their singular product, which has grade strictly below the sum of their conformal weights. By the prescribed expression for that product and (14.5.7ao.1), the replacement has the same negative-word expression on both sides. The iterate identity expands each mode of a negative word into products of its constituent generator modes. On a fixed homogeneous tail this expansion has finitely many nonzero terms. At each recursive application, the two sums in the iterate identity have a mode on the right whose index tends to $+\infty$, and its action on the tail then has negative conformal grade. The upper bound on those indices is obtained from the input and field grades, so the truncations work on both sides. The generator weights in the expanded terms sum to at most the grade of the singular product. Every commutator term thus strictly decreases the weight sum. Induction on that sum, with finitely many commutations at each step, gives the same reduction to negative-mode words in both algebras.

In particular the two vacuum matrix coefficients agree for every generator-mode word of conformal grade zero. Its all-negative terms can only be multiples of the empty word. For any two generated words, their inner product is such a vacuum matrix coefficient after taking adjoints on the left word. For a generator $x$ of weight $h$, its adjoint modes are
$$
(x_m)^\dagger=(-1)^h\sum_{j\ge0}\frac{1}{j!}
(L_1^j\Theta x)_{2h-m-j-2},
\tag{14.5.7ao.3}
$$
where $\Theta$ is the antilinear PCT operator. Every field here is in the chosen grades or is the vacuum, and the sum is finite. The required $L_1$ and PCT identities ensure that these adjoint coefficients transform identically under $F$. Words of different output grades have zero inner product by orthogonality. The full Gram matrices therefore agree. Positivity identifies all linear relations, so the assignment of each vacuum word to its corresponding word is a well-defined isometry on the finite-energy spaces. It is onto because $FS$ spans the generating grades of $W$. It agrees with $F$ on the original space by the creation axiom. It intertwines generator modes by applying the mode to the word before transport. Applying the iterate identity to generated states extends the intertwining to all vertex operators. The map fixes the conformal vector and extends to a unitary preserving the full graded rotation. Strong locality and vacuum reconstruction then identify the closed smeared fields and all local algebras. Conjugation carries the complete bounded-instrument menu and spectral response, including instruments with a retained reference. See Carpi et al. (2015).

*Computing a strong generating cutoff.* Suppose finite ordinary vertex generating sets and effective graded mode tables are given. An ordinary generating set permits arbitrary mode indices. Choose $B$ at least the maximal grades of those sets and of the conformal vectors. In each algebra enumerate the negative-mode words in its grades through $B$ for every output grade at most $2B-1$, and test whether they span each singular product of its generators in the whole positive space through $B$. The reduction just proved shows that success implies the negative words span the ordinary generated algebra. To see that ordinary generation spans via arbitrary generator modes on the vacuum, the iterate identity shows that the span of these words is closed under modes of its own elements. If a cutoff fails, increase it by one. When both algebras are $C_2$-cofinite, a finite strong generating set exists, so the procedure terminates. One direct proof takes homogeneous representatives for a finite basis of $V/C_2(V)$ with $C_2(V)=\operatorname{span}\{a_{-2}b\}$. The positive-weight terms $a_{-2}b$ have both input weights below their output weight, unless $a$ is the vacuum and the term vanishes. Induction on weight and the iterate identity, or the $C_2$ spanning theorem, give strong generation by the representatives (Buhl 2007).

*Actual ordinary generators on the lattice source.* For the lattice algebra $V_M$, take the Heisenberg states of a lattice basis and the two momentum states for each basis vector and its negative. The leading coefficient of the exponential field product yields the sum-momentum vector with nonzero coefficient, so all lattice momenta follow, and the Heisenberg modes produce their oscillator spaces.

For $V_M^+$ choose an algebraic orthonormal Heisenberg basis $h_i$. The fixed zero-momentum Heisenberg algebra is generated by the rank-one fixed-algebra generators $\omega_i,J_i$ of weights two and four and by $h_i(-1)h_j(-1)\mathbf1$ for $i<j$. In fact the even-sign tensor factors each have the stated rank-one generators. The odd-sign rank-one module is irreducible over its fixed algebra, so modes of the individual fixed factors generate the two-odd component from the indicated tensor of their nonzero lowest vectors. Negative products of components on disjoint pairs give every even parity pattern. For a lattice basis $(\beta_i)$, add $E_\alpha=e^\alpha+\vartheta(e^\alpha)$ for the nonzero $\alpha\in M$ satisfying $|(\alpha,\beta_i)|\le(\beta_i,\beta_i)$ for every $i$. The box is compact and has an effective finite lattice enumeration. A nonzero momentum pair $\{\alpha,-\alpha\}$ in $V_M^+$ is the irreducible Fock module of momentum $\alpha$ over the fixed zero-momentum Heisenberg algebra, so $E_\alpha$ generates that entire pair. If $\alpha$ is outside the box, choose a signed basis vector $\beta$ with $(\alpha,\beta)>\|\beta\|^2$. Both $\|\alpha-\beta\|^2$ and $\|\alpha-2\beta\|^2$ are less than $\|\alpha\|^2$. In $Y(E_\beta,z)E_{\alpha-\beta}$ the sum-momentum channel first contributes a nonzero multiple of $E_\alpha$ at power $z^{(\beta,\alpha-\beta)}$. At that power the only other contribution is in the already generated lower-norm orbit of $\alpha-2\beta$. This follows directly from the leading exponential field coefficient and inversion invariance. Subtract it and induct on the squared norm. For a twisted output add any nonzero homogeneous state of its irreducible twisted summand to these generators. Its orbit under the fixed algebra modes spans that whole summand (Dong and Nagatomo 1998, 1999b).

The ordinary lattice and inversion fixed algebras are $C_2$-cofinite. The twisted output is the sum of the fixed algebra and one irreducible ordinary module over it. That module is $C_2$-cofinite as a module over the fixed algebra. The two module $C_2$ subspaces lie in the larger algebra's $C_2$ subspace, so the output is $C_2$-cofinite too (Abe et al. 2002b).

*Exact algebraic data and decision.* For the ordinary and fixed lattice algebras, each requested grade is a finite enumeration of momentum vectors below an explicit norm bound and oscillator partitions. The invariant basis for the fixed algebra is obtained by the order-two projector. The straight lattice modes, invariant Hermitian Gram form, conjugation and Virasoro modes are computed by the lattice oscillator formulas and sign cocycle. Their finite coefficients are algebraic. The untwisted unmarked outputs may all use a standard inversion lift by Theorem 14.5.7am.

For a twisted output, Theorem 14.5.7an supplies the local extension condition. Its character and lift transitivity permits the use of any standard inversion lift and any one central character for that $M$. Use the half-integer oscillator basis and a representation of the finite lattice central extension in the chosen central character. The matrices can be constructed over the algebraic numbers by splitting the finite group algebra and orthonormalizing the selected summand using a group-averaged positive form. The straight and twisted oscillator formulas of Dolan et al. (1994) give the fixed algebra action on that summand. All formal coefficients are computed at finite degree by binomial expansions and normal ordering, with lattice pairings, the finite group matrices and factors $4^{-\|\lambda\|^2/2}$ from the zero-mode correction. The twisted conjugation uses a symmetric unitary matrix intertwining the complex-conjugate finite group representation with the original one. Such a matrix exists by the real twisted-module construction for $\sqrt2 M^*$ even, which is the norm condition in (14.5.7an.1). The finite matrix equations for the lattice basis elements imply the equations for the entire representation, and these equations together with symmetry and unitarity have algebraic coefficients. Real algebraic quantifier elimination therefore chooses an algebraic solution. The operators of equations (3.8), (4.3) and (4.17) of Dolan et al. (1994) give the remaining field blocks from the known fixed action by translation and the adjoint using that conjugation. Thus each requested twisted–twisted mode coefficient is a finite algebraic calculation too. Their construction in §5 gives the local extension on the integral parity of the twisted sector, including when the ground grade itself is half integral. These fields give the same actual extension by uniqueness over the twist label and the admitted conformal extension correspondence (Dolan et al. 1994, §§3–5).

All conditions on $F$ now consist of finitely many real polynomial equations in the real and imaginary parts of its matrix entries. These include its Hermitian Gram equality in the chosen bases. The equality in (14.5.7ao.1) uses fields and states at grades at most $2B-1$, since all prefixes of a negative word, evaluated from the vacuum toward the left, have grades bounded by the word's output grade. Real closed field quantifier elimination decides feasibility and yields an algebraic sample matrix when feasible. By the word argument, infeasibility separates the two full responses and a sample matrix gives the full intertwiner. This classifies all pairs of unmarked outputs in Theorem 14.5.7am, including mixed ordinary, fixed and twisted comparisons, without a finite-dimensional replacement of the local net (Mahboubi and Cohen 2012).

<a id="theorem-1457ap"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ap-unmarked-noncyclic-lattice-permutation-fixed-nets"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ap (Unmarked Noncyclic Lattice Permutation Fixed Nets).** Let $\mathcal A$ be a tensor product of holomorphic even unimodular lattice nets, expressed in its indecomposable orthogonal lattice factors. Let $G$ be any finite subgroup of the permutations of these factors that preserves their lattice isometry types. Use the plain tensor permutation action in fixed identifications between copies of each type. Compare the unmarked fixed nets with the oriented vacuum, calibrated rotations and complete local instruments of Theorem 14.5.7s. Suppose every nonidentity permutation in each acting group has twisted ground grade greater than one. Two such fixed-net responses are equivalent exactly when a bijection of indecomposable factors preserving the lattice isometry types conjugates one permutation group to the other. The same test classifies all purely electric outputs of Theorem 14.5.7r from these sources, including outputs from different original parents and different original inclusion indices.

*The ground-grade test.* For $g$ in the group, let $\mathcal C(g)$ be its disjoint cycles on the actual factors. For each cycle $C$ of length $m_C$, let $r_C$ be its seed lattice rank. The test is
$$
h(g)=\sum_{C\in\mathcal C(g)}
\frac{r_C}{24}\left(m_C-\frac1{m_C}\right)>1
\qquad(g\ne1).
\tag{14.5.7ap.1}
$$
The vacuum shift of a cyclic cover is $r_C(m_C-m_C^{-1})/24$. The arbitrary permutation twisted module is the tensor product of those cycle modules, so their shifts add. This determines the least grade before projection onto an irreducible projective centralizer character. Every projected sector therefore has grade at least $h(g)$. The estimate applies to each flux conjugacy class in Theorem 14.5.7r (Barron et al. 1998; Evans and Gannon 2018).

*The unique full-rank extension.* Let $c$ be the central charge of $\mathcal A$. Every irreducible local finite-index extension of $\mathcal A^G$ has the data $(K,N,\gamma,\varepsilon)$ of Theorem 14.5.7r. In its transferred algebra the identity group degree is the electric algebra of functions on $G/K$. Its representation and multiplication are those of the vacuum extension from $\mathcal A^G$ to $\mathcal A^K$, by the finite-group fixed-net correspondence. The homogeneous degree of a field here is its flux degree in the group, distinct from its conformal grade. The identity flux defines a subalgebra and hence the actual intermediate fixed subnet $\mathcal A^K$ in the extension (Bischoff 2016; Davydov and Simmons 2016).

All other summands of the vacuum extension restriction are in nonidentity flux and have conformal ground grade greater than one by (14.5.7ap.1). Thus its weight-one Lie algebra, with bracket from zero modes, is precisely that of $\mathcal A^K$. On each $K$-orbit of equal lattice factors, the fixed weight-one algebra is the diagonal copy of the seed weight-one algebra. Its Cartan rank is the seed lattice rank. The rank of the extension is therefore
$$
\operatorname{rank}(V_{\rm ext})_1
=\sum_{\mathcal O\in K\backslash\{\text{factors}\}}r_{\mathcal O}.
\tag{14.5.7ap.2}
$$
For lattice nets the Cartan rank of the weight-one algebra is the lattice rank, including its abelian directions. The sum in (14.5.7ap.2) equals $c$ exactly when each $K$-orbit is a singleton. The permutation action is faithful, so this forces $K=1$. Then $N=1$ and the extension is the lattice parent itself with the unique electric multiplication over the fixed net. Hence the unmarked fixed net intrinsically selects the parent as the unique irreducible local finite-index extension whose current rank equals its central charge. More generally the weight-one algebra with its invariant form on each $K$-orbit is the seed algebra with that form multiplied by the orbit size. The algebra with these forms and the sector global dimension $|K/N|^2$ give exact separation tests for any two extension outputs in the stated high-grade class. Every output with $K\ne1$ is separated from all ordinary lattice outputs by the current rank.

*Recovering the permutation action.* An isomorphism of the fixed nets preserves the conformal structure and identifies their irreducible local finite-index extensions, including the weight-one brackets and ranks. It therefore lifts to an isomorphism of their distinguished parent inclusions. At each parent the group of vacuum-covariant automorphisms fixing the base subnet pointwise is the original finite group by fixed-point Galois correspondence. The lift consequently conjugates the actual actions.

The parent response determines its lattice up to isometry by the lattice state-field comparison of Theorem 14.5.7s. Every parent automorphism acts on the set of indecomposable lattice tensor factors by a type-preserving permutation. In detail, the lattice VOA automorphism theorem generates the full automorphism group by weight-one inner automorphisms and lifts of lattice isometries. Each weight-one state is a sum of states from the individual tensor factors, whose zero modes on distinct factors commute. Its exponential preserves every factor. A lattice isometry permutes the unique orthogonally indecomposable summands. A lift takes the Heisenberg space of each summand and the corresponding momentum fields to those of the permuted summand, so it takes the whole factor to that factor. Thus restriction to this set is a homomorphism and sends each original permutation to its exact permutation action. Applied to the lift of the unmarked response isomorphism, it gives the required type-preserving conjugacy. Conversely, given such a conjugacy, choose a common representative of each lattice type, and use the actual tensor-factor relabeling. It intertwines the two group actions, vacuum and rotations and restricts to an isomorphism of the fixed nets with the full declared response. The equivalence test is finite on lattice isometry data and finite permutation groups (Dong and Nagatomo 1999a; Carpi et al. 2015).

*Purely electric outputs and noncyclic examples.* The purely electric entries are exactly $N=1$ in Theorem 14.5.7r. Their transferred algebras give the actual fixed nets $\mathcal A^K$. Condition (14.5.7ap.1) is inherited by every subgroup, so comparing these outputs amounts to comparing the actual acting subgroups on their typed indecomposable factors. In particular take copies of an indecomposable seed of rank $r$ indexed by any finite group $G$ and use its left regular action. A nonidentity element of order $m$ has cycles all of length $m$, so with $c=r|G|$ its twisted grade is $c(1-m^{-2})/24\ge c/32$. Every such action with $c>32$ satisfies (14.5.7ap.1), including the left regular nonabelian groups. For example the regular actions of the dihedral group of order eight and the quaternion group of order eight on eight $E_8$ factors have inequivalent unmarked fixed-net responses by the conjugacy test. The criterion also separates a fixed-net response of this class from any purely electric output with a nonconjugate subgroup. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457aq"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457aq-actual-electric-intermediates-and-quotient-actions-of-finite-orbifold-extensions"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7aq (Actual Electric Intermediates and Quotient Actions of Finite Orbifold Extensions).** Use the finite-group holomorphic orbifolds and local extensions of Theorem 14.5.7r. Every extension with data $(K,N,\gamma,\varepsilon)$ factors through the actual intermediate fixed net $\mathcal A^K$. The extension over that intermediate is the local extension of the degree-one-dimensional algebra on $N$ with the given product and action. For plain lattice permutations, every output with cyclic $K$ therefore belongs to the complete unmarked classification of Theorem 14.5.7al, even when its original acting group is nonabelian. For the higher-ground-grade class of Theorem 14.5.7ap, an intrinsic current rank separates the purely electric outputs from the other outputs. Under the quotient faithfulness condition below the same invariant recovers a unique holomorphic extension and its actual quotient action.

*Actual intermediate and extension multiplication.* Write $\mathscr E$ for the transferred extension algebra in $Z(\operatorname{Vec}_G^\omega)$. Its identity-degree subalgebra is $P=\operatorname{Fun}(G/K)$, with pointwise multiplication and the natural action of $G$. It is precisely the electric algebra of the physical inclusion $\mathcal A^G\subset\mathcal A^K$. Under the local-extension correspondence, the algebra inclusion $P\subset\mathscr E$ gives the intermediate inclusion in the actual extension net. The category of local $P$-modules identifies with $Z(\operatorname{Vec}_K^{\omega|_K})$ by taking the fiber at the identity coset. Explicitly, if $p=1_K$ denotes its indicator, locality forces a homogeneous vector in that fiber to have degree stabilizing $p$. Indeed for a vector of degree $g$, the double braiding with the electric algebra sends the idempotent $p$ to $g\cdot p$. Distinct coset idempotents are orthogonal, so a nonzero vector in this fiber has degree in $K$. The fiber inherits the twisted $K$-action and monoidal product, and coset transfer gives the inverse. The algebra of the second extension is $\mathscr E p$, with multiplication inherited on this fiber. Its degree-$n$ unit line, product and stabilizer action are exactly (14.5.7r.1) for $n\in N$. The relative tensor product over $P$ identifies its product with the restriction of the transferred product, so the factorization identifies the full connected local extension algebra. The indices in the tower are $[G:K]$ and $|N|$ (Davydov and Simmons 2016, Theorem 2.11 and Corollary 2.13; Evans and Gannon 2018, §§4.1–4.2).

*Cyclic stabilizer.* Suppose $\mathcal A$ is a holomorphic lattice-permutation parent of Theorem 14.5.7ap, with no ground-grade assumption in this paragraph, and that $K$ is cyclic. Each projective centralizer representation in its pointed center is one-dimensional. The corner has exactly one such sector over each flux in $N$, since it has one vector in each supported degree. It has only the vacuum sector over identity flux, so its pointed support has trivial electric intersection. Braided commutativity makes the support isotropic. It is thus the actual flux-injective extension of $\mathcal A^K$ in Theorems 14.5.7ah–al. The physical generator of $K$ acts by its disjoint cycles on the actual tensor factors. Those cycles provide the intermediate inventory and the inherited quadratic sector coordinates. Theorem 14.5.7al compares every pair of these outputs with the original parents, inclusions and ambient groups omitted. This includes comparisons with all outputs of cyclic original actions. Conversely every local extension of $\mathcal A^K$ of this flux-injective form composes with $\mathcal A^G\subset\mathcal A^K$ and occurs in Theorem 14.5.7r over $\mathcal A^G$, with the same final net. The equivalence lifts to the full calibrated response by the local-extension correspondence.

*Intrinsic maximal current rank of an output.* For the rest of the theorem assume the ground-grade condition (14.5.7ap.1). Denote the final output by $\mathcal C$, its corner over $\mathcal A^K$ by $B$, and the tensor-factor index set by $I$. Regard any irreducible local finite-index extension $\mathcal D$ of $\mathcal C$ as an extension of $\mathcal A^K$ via the tower. It is irreducible because the tensor unit in the category of local $B$-modules is $B$. If $D$ is the local extension algebra of $\mathcal D$ before forgetting its $B$-action, then $\operatorname{Hom}_{B}(B,D)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(1,D)$ by evaluation on the unit of $B$. Thus the composite vacuum multiplicity is one. In the finite-group census for the $K$ action its data have stabilizer $H\le K$ and support $F\trianglelefteq H$. The inclusion of $B$ forces $N\subseteq F$. To see this, let $b_n$ span the nonzero degree-$n$ line of $B$. Its product with $b_{n^{-1}}$ is a nonzero multiple of the unit. In the algebra for $\mathcal D$, every minimal electric idempotent is central in the ordinary multiplication across degree one, as follows by braided commutativity applied with the electric element on the left. The product of the two projected vectors in each corner is the same nonzero multiple of that idempotent. Hence each corner has nonzero degree-$n$ space. At the identity coset its support is $F$, proving the inclusion. Since every nonidentity flux is above grade one, the current algebra of $\mathcal D$ is precisely that of $\mathcal A^H$. Its Cartan rank is therefore bounded above by the sum of seed ranks over the $N$-orbits, because each $H$-orbit is a union of $N$-orbits.

The upper bound is attained. Put $Q=K/N$ and let $P_N=\operatorname{Fun}(K/N)$ be the electric algebra in $Z(\operatorname{Vec}_K^{\omega|_K})$. The algebra $B$ centralizes $P_N$ since all its degrees are in $N$ and $N$ acts trivially on $K/N$. Their tensor product is a connected commutative separable algebra. Its vacuum multiplicity is one because $P_N$ is entirely electric and $B$ has only a vacuum line in identity degree. The extension it gives, denoted $\widehat{\mathcal C}$, contains $B$ and has stabilizer and support both equal to $N$. Thus it is holomorphic and is the extension of $\mathcal A^N$ with the corner data restricted to $N$. Consequently the following quantity is intrinsic to the unmarked output:
$$
\max_{\mathcal C\subset\mathcal D}
\operatorname{rank}(V_{\mathcal D})_1
=\sum_{\mathcal O\in N\backslash I}r_{\mathcal O}.
\tag{14.5.7aq.1}
$$
The maximum is over irreducible local finite-index extensions, including $\mathcal C$ itself. It equals the central charge exactly when $N=1$, because every nontrivial subgroup of the faithful permutation group moves a factor of positive rank. Thus an output with purely electric support cannot be equivalent to an output with nontrivial flux support in this class.

*Recovery of the quotient action.* Suppose the induced action of $Q$ on $N\backslash I$ is faithful. Equality in (14.5.7aq.1) implies that $H$ preserves each $N$-orbit, since otherwise positive ranks are lost in the merger. Faithfulness then gives $H=N$ and $F=N$. For a chosen electric idempotent over the identity coset, the given embedding of $B$ into the algebra of $\mathcal D$ restricts on its corner to an isomorphism of graded algebras with the restricted $N$-action. It is nonzero on each support line by the product-with-inverse argument. Two such embeddings give an isomorphism on this corner by identifying their copies of $B$. The identity on the $K/N$ coset labels and this corner isomorphism extend by coset transfer, and equivariance then identifies the embeddings on every corner. These are isomorphisms of the connected étale algebras. Equivalently one uses their normalized unitary $Q$-systems. The intertwiner is over $\mathcal C$, so $\widehat{\mathcal C}$ is the unique extension attaining (14.5.7aq.1) up to equivalence over $\mathcal C$.

Inside the local $B$-modules, $B\otimes P_N$ becomes the regular electric algebra of $Q$. In the quotient description of the local modules, the identity-flux component is the degree-one fiber, where $B_1=\mathbb C$ and the $K$-action on $P_N$ is already the quotient action. Its multiplication is pointwise. More directly, the right translations of $K/N$ act on $P_N$ by algebra automorphisms commuting with the left $K$ action. They act trivially on $B$ and give actual vacuum-covariant automorphisms of $\widehat{\mathcal C}$ fixing $\mathcal C$, by the extension correspondence. Their fixed algebra is $B\otimes1$. The quotient action is faithful and exhausts the automorphisms over $\mathcal C$ by fixed-net Galois. Hence the unmarked response of $\mathcal C$ determines the pair consisting of its distinguished holomorphic extension and this quotient subgroup of vacuum-covariant automorphisms. Conversely a vacuum-covariant unitary conjugating these quotient actions restricts to the unmarked output equivalence. This is an exact reduction of the comparison to actual quotient-action conjugacy. The terminal comparison requires an actual vacuum-covariant conjugating unitary.

For example let $K$ act regularly on copies of a holomorphic indecomposable lattice seed, with total central charge greater than thirty-two. Every $N$-orbit is a left coset orbit of the normal subgroup $N$ and $K/N$ acts faithfully on the orbit set. All these sources satisfy the two recovery hypotheses. The criterion includes nonabelian quotients whenever the admitted corner phases exist. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457ar"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457ar-unmarked-noncyclic-quotient-actions-with-an-active-involution"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7ar (Unmarked Noncyclic Quotient Actions with an Active Involution).** Take as active parent either four $E_8$ factors with the simultaneous swap on two pairs, or two $D_{16}^{+}$ factors with their swap. Write $\mathcal A_{\rm a}$ for this parent, $s$ for the involution, $\mathcal U$ for the holomorphic flux extension of $\mathcal A_{\rm a}^{\langle s\rangle}$ from Theorem 14.5.7ai, and $\delta$ for its dual sign. Take a passive holomorphic lattice parent $\mathcal A_{\rm p}$ and any finite group $Q$ of plain type-preserving permutations of its indecomposable factors satisfying (14.5.7ap.1) on the passive parent. Let $\chi:Q\to\mathbb Z_2$ be a character. The extensions over
$$
\mathcal A_{\rm a}^{\langle s\rangle}\otimes \mathcal A_{\rm p}^{Q}
\tag{14.5.7ar.1}
$$
with electric stabilizer $K=\langle s\rangle\times Q$ and full active flux support $N=\langle s\rangle$ in Theorem 14.5.7r are precisely the actual nets
$$
\mathcal C_\chi=(\mathcal U\otimes\mathcal A_{\rm p})^{
\{\,\delta^{\chi(q)}\otimes q:q\in Q\,\}} .
\tag{14.5.7ar.2}
$$
The unmarked oriented vacuum and calibrated responses of two such nets agree exactly when the active types agree and there is a type-preserving bijection of passive indecomposable factors conjugating the passive permutation groups and carrying $\chi$ to the other character. This includes nonabelian $Q$ and nontrivial sign characters.

*Actual extension and quotient action.* The active involutions have zero anomaly because their twisted ground grade is one and their order is two. Use the product category of the two actual fixed nets in (14.5.7ar.1), with a cocycle pulled back from the passive action. For the flux support $N$ the corner of Theorem 14.5.7r has one nonvacuum line, in degree $s$. Rephase it so that its square is one. The product equivariance equation then forces each action coefficient on this line to be a sign. In this product cocycle convention the factors in the action equation for this degree are all one, so these signs give a genuine character of $K$. Braided commutativity gives sign $+1$ for $s$, so the character is exactly $\chi$ on $Q$. Rephasing the line cannot change its character. Thus the nonvacuum sector over (14.5.7ar.1) is the active bosonic flux tensored with the passive electric character $(-1)^\chi$.

The vacuum representation of $\mathcal U$ restricts to the active base as the even vacuum sector and the odd flux sector for $\delta$. The $\chi$-isotypic subspace of the passive parent is the passive electric sector for $(-1)^\chi$, with multiplicity one because the group representation is one-dimensional. Taking the invariants in (14.5.7ar.2) therefore yields the vacuum sector of (14.5.7ar.1) and precisely the nonvacuum summand just calculated. Both are the support of an order-two bosonic simple-current algebra. Its connected local multiplication is unique up to equivalence. The fixed-point net thus implements the actual classified extension with its vacuum, rotations and fields. This also applies over a larger original group when (14.5.7ar.1) is the physical intermediate of Theorem 14.5.7aq.

*Recovering the completion and its tensor factors.* Every nonidentity permutation of $K$ acting on the original lattice parent has ground grade greater than one except the active $s$ with trivial passive component, which has grade exactly one. This exceptional flux contributes the one central current of $\mathcal U$. To recover the completion, apply Theorem 14.5.7r to the actual action (14.5.7ar.2) on its holomorphic parent $\mathcal P=\mathcal U\otimes\mathcal A_{\rm p}$. For an element with passive component $q$, the tensor product of the $\delta^{\chi(q)}$-soliton of $\mathcal U$ and the $q$-soliton of $\mathcal A_{\rm p}$ has the required product twist across the same cut. It is irreducible because the representations of the two tensor factors are irreducible, so it is the unique twisted soliton of the holomorphic product for that twist. Its two stress-tensor rotations add. Every nonidentity element has passive twisted ground grade greater than one, and the active soliton has nonnegative conformal weight. The combined ground grade thus exceeds one, including after the centralizer sector projection. Consequently the weight-one algebra of every local extension of $\mathcal C_\chi$ is the weight-one algebra of its electric intermediate in $\mathcal P$. The fixed algebra of a subgroup has rank at most the full active rank in $\mathcal U$ and strictly smaller passive rank whenever the subgroup is nontrivial. The strict loss follows from faithful passive permutations as in (14.5.7ap.2). Therefore the full rank of $\mathcal P$ is the unique maximal rank attained by an extension over $\mathcal C_\chi$, and $\mathcal P$ is the unique extension attaining it. Here the full rank is $17+c_{\rm p}$, where $c_{\rm p}$ is the passive lattice rank and central charge. In the census of Theorem 14.5.7r, attaining it forces the electric stabilizer to be trivial and hence selects the actual parent algebra. Any equivalence of two outputs lifts to a unitary isomorphism of their unique maximal completions and conjugates their actual quotient groups by Galois (Müger 2004; Evans and Gannon 2018; Bischoff 2016).

In $\mathcal P$ the level-two simple ideals of the weight-one Lie algebra are precisely the active semisimple algebra, of type $E_{8,2}\oplus E_{8,2}$ or $D_{16,2}$. The passive lattice factors have all simple ideals at level one. This identifies the active type intrinsically. Choose a compact Cartan and project its full charge lattice orthogonally off the Cartan of the level-two ideals. By the tensor product, the projection is an orthogonal product of the projected active charge lattice on its central line and the passive lattice itself. The active projection is a discrete rank-one lattice. The active Cartan charge support is a full lattice with rational metric, and the active semisimple Cartan is spanned by its root charges, so its orthogonal projection is rational. As in Theorem 14.5.7ak, the orthogonal decomposition of this projected lattice singles out the rank-one summand, since the passive indecomposable even unimodular lattices have ranks divisible by eight. It therefore identifies the passive Cartan subspace of the full Cartan and its metric (Mason 2011, §§11–14).

The passive Heisenberg stress tensor from that Cartan is the conformal vector of the whole passive lattice factor. For its rotation generator $L_0^{\rm p}$ the finite-energy kernel of $L_0-L_0^{\rm p}$ is exactly the passive lattice algebra on the active vacuum, because the active weight-zero space is the vacuum line. Thus this construction recovers the actual passive factor for a chosen Cartan, and the other factor is its commutant. An arbitrary isomorphism of completions maps the chosen Cartan to a compact Cartan. Align it with the target Cartan by an inner automorphism from the target compact weight-one group. That group acts factorwise on the product, since the two weight-one summands have commuting zero modes. Consequently this alignment preserves the target's actual passive factor and its commutant. The original isomorphism therefore also maps the actual factors to their counterparts (Carpi et al. 2015).

*Finite comparison.* Let an isomorphism of completions conjugate their quotient actions. Its restrictions to the recovered factors are isomorphisms. On the passive factor, the action of a lattice automorphism on the set of indecomposable lattice factors is a type-preserving permutation, as proved in Theorem 14.5.7ap. Applying this homomorphism to the passive isomorphism yields the asserted permutation conjugacy. Since the passive group acts faithfully, each passive group element determines its unique lift in the quotient graph in (14.5.7ar.2). The active restriction of that element is the identity exactly when its character is zero. This property is invariant under conjugacy. Thus the passive permutation conjugacy also carries the character. Conversely a type-preserving passive conjugacy respecting the character is realized by relabeling lattice factors. For the same active type use the unitary identification of the actual holomorphic flux extension with its dual sign. The product intertwines both quotient actions and hence the fixed nets, including the complete declared response. This gives a finite equivalence test on the two active types, the passive lattice isometry classes and the finite permutation and sign data.

*A nonabelian source.* The left regular action of the dihedral group of order eight on eight passive $E_8$ factors satisfies (14.5.7ap.1), with passive central charge sixty-four. Take either active source. The zero character and any nonzero character give distinct unmarked outputs on the same product parent. They are separated by the actual quotient action on the recovered active factor. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457as"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457as-calibrated-generalized-dihedral-lattice-orbifolds"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7as (Calibrated Generalized Dihedral Lattice Orbifolds).** Let $L$ be any positive-definite even lattice of finite positive rank, let $\Gamma$ be a finite subgroup of its unitary lattice character torus, and let $s$ be any unitary order-two lift of lattice inversion. Use the oriented vacuum net, stress tensor, calibrated rotations and local-instrument comparison of Theorem 14.5.7s. Put $M=\{x\in L:\gamma(x)=1\text{ for every }\gamma\in\Gamma\}$ and $D=\langle\Gamma,s\rangle$. Then the following comparisons and actual restrictions hold.

1. The group is the generalized dihedral group $\Gamma\rtimes_{-1}\mathbb Z_2$. Its fixed net on its vacuum representation is the actual inversion net $\mathcal B_M$ on the character-annihilator lattice. Its complete unmarked comparison, including the mixed comparison with ordinary lattice nets, is exactly Theorem 14.5.7s applied to $M$. In particular the nonisometric rank-sixteen pair in that theorem remains the only exceptional inversion-to-inversion comparison. The local finite-index extensions of this base use the admitted marked inversion classification of Theorem 14.5.7am, with the unmarked comparison of Theorem 14.5.7ao.
2. On the parent-marked comparison, equivalence is equivalent to conjugacy of the finite acting subgroups by a unitary lattice-VOA isomorphism. There is a terminating exact algebraic test for this comparison, including rooted parents and elementary two-groups. If the intermediate character fixed net is marked, the comparison reduces to one isometry of pairs $M\subset L$. The same reduction already holds without the intermediate mark when $\Gamma$ has an element of order greater than two, or when the parent lattice has no roots of squared norm two.
3. The inclusion has index $2[L:M]$ on each interval. Its full intermediate-net lattice is the subgroup lattice of $D$ with reversed order. For every named subgroup its actual intermediate net and its finite-energy invariant-space projector are supplied below. The same finite grade test distinguishes every deck label as an automorphism of the parent. When the parent is forgotten, no deck action on the fixed vacuum net is inferred from these marked tests.

*Fixed net, lift choices and deck group.* In the momentum decomposition of Theorem 14.5.7v, every character acts by its value on the momentum label. The inversion lift sends that momentum to its negative, so $s\gamma s^{-1}=\gamma^{-1}$. It acts on the nonzero Heisenberg space by minus identity, whereas characters act there by identity. Thus the two cosets are disjoint, and the action is faithful. The character fixed VOA is $V_M$ with its inherited field products, and $s$ restricts to an order-two inversion lift on it. Taking invariants under this last lift yields $V_M^+$ and its actual conformal net by the same equivariant reconstruction as in Theorems 14.5.7s and 14.5.7v. Any two inversion lifts on $V_L$ differ by a lattice character. The square-root character exists on a free lattice, and conjugation by it changes one lift to the other while commuting with $\Gamma$. Thus the fixed inclusion and the stated unmarked comparison do not depend on this choice. Theorems 14.5.7am and 14.5.7ao apply to this identified inversion base; the parent $V_L$ carries its additional deck mark.

An inclusion equivalence induces a VOA isomorphism of the parent and fixed subalgebra by Theorem 14.5.7s. To recover the deck group from these marks, use the interval fixed-point inclusion. A nonidentity global vacuum-preserving automorphism acts outerly on each interval factor. Indeed, if it acts by a unitary $u$ in one interval algebra, it fixes the vacuum state there. The unitary is then in the centralizer of that faithful vacuum state. Bisognano–Wichmann identifies its modular flow with the interval dilations. A dilation-invariant vector is invariant under the two unipotent subgroups, by strong continuity after conjugating a fixed unipotent element by a contracting dilation. These subgroups generate the Möbius group. Thus $u\Omega$ is a scalar multiple of the vacuum, and the separating vacuum gives scalar $u$. Identity on one interval gives identity globally because the vacuum implementer fixes the dense set of vectors obtained there from the vacuum. The interval fixed-point inclusion of the outer action has index $|D|$. For its inclusion homomorphism $\iota$, each vacuum-preserving automorphism $\alpha$ fixing the subnet pointwise satisfies $\alpha\iota=\iota$. Frobenius reciprocity embeds its irreducible dimension-one sector in $\iota\bar\iota$. Distinct vacuum automorphisms have distinct such sectors, since their relative automorphism would otherwise be inner on that interval. The dimension of $\iota\bar\iota$ is the index, so $D$ already exhausts all possible deck labels (Bischoff et al. 2014).

The finite-group Galois theorem for simple vertex operator algebras gives every intermediate VOA as $V_L^H$ for a unique subgroup $H\le D$. The corresponding unitary conformal subnets are recovered by the vacuum-net correspondence (Hanaki et al. 1999). If $H\le\Gamma$, its intermediate is the ordinary lattice net on its annihilator $M_H\subset L$. Otherwise $H=\langle\Gamma_H,\gamma_0s\rangle$ with $\Gamma_H=H\cap\Gamma$ and any chosen representative $\gamma_0s$ of the other coset. Its intermediate is the inversion fixed net on the annihilator of $\Gamma_H$, using the restriction of the displayed lift. The index of the intermediate inclusion into the parent is $|H|$. At each finite conformal grade the fixed-space projector is exactly $|H|^{-1}\sum_{h\in H}h$, acting on the actual finite momentum and oscillator basis. This realizes the subgroup restrictions as physical invariant fields and calibrated energy spaces. A parent-marked equivalence must conjugate the pointwise deck groups, and the converse follows by taking invariants.

*Direct lattice criteria.* When $V_M$ is marked, a conjugacy of the parent and this intermediate is exactly the simultaneous lattice isometry of Theorem 14.5.7v. The isometry conjugates the character subgroup; its lift can be corrected by a character square root to match the two inversion lifts. Conversely the simultaneous lattice isometry preserves the whole fixed inclusion. If $\Gamma$ has an element $a$ of order greater than two, then $\Gamma$ is generated by the elements of order greater than two in $D$. Every outside element has order two. For any $x\in\Gamma$ of order at most two, either $ax$ has order greater than two, so $x=a^{-1}(ax)$ belongs to the generated group, or $(ax)^2=a^2=1$, which contradicts the choice of $a$. This makes $\Gamma$ intrinsic under the marked comparison. If the parent has no roots, its entire weight-one space is its Heisenberg space, and $\Gamma$ is the kernel of the action of $D$ on that space. A marked equivalence recovers it in this case as well. These arguments establish the asserted direct lattice-pair criteria.

*Finite exact test for all parent-marked cases.* Ordinary lattice-VOA classification first requires isometry of the parent lattices. Enumerate their finitely many isometries by choosing images of a lattice basis with its prescribed Gram matrix and verifying integral invertibility. This is finite because the lattices are positive definite and each basis vector has fixed norm. Lift one isometry to identify both parents with one $V_L$. It suffices to decide conjugacy of the two resulting finite deck groups inside the unitary automorphism group of $V_L$.

Choose $g$ at least two and at least the conformal weights of all momentum vectors $e^{\pm b_j}$ for an integral lattice basis $(b_j)$. Put $W=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{g}(V_L)_n$. The Heisenberg currents, those momentum vectors, and the conformal vector lie in $W$. The lattice mode formula gives a nonzero leading term proportional to $e^{\alpha+\beta}$ in the product of $e^\alpha,e^\beta$, and the current modes create every oscillator monomial. This proves that $W$ generates $V_L$, so its finite representation of the automorphism group is faithful. All matrices of finite lattice lifts, root zero modes and finite characters on $W$ have algebraic entries in the momentum-oscillator basis. These entries follow by the finite lattice cocycle signs, mode coefficients, roots of unity and normalized oscillator factors. All finite data can be given in exact algebraic form.

The unitary automorphism group is generated by the compact weight-one inner group and the finite lattice lifts. To see the unitary statement, act on the real compact reductive weight-one Lie algebra. An inner unitary sends the image of the Heisenberg Cartan to the standard Cartan. The adjusted automorphism then preserves the joint weight lattice and its Euclidean form. Removing a lift of this lattice isometry leaves an automorphism acting trivially on the Cartan, hence a unitary lattice character by the momentum decomposition and nonzero leading products. These characters already belong to the compact inner group, since exponentiating the Cartan zero modes gives the full character torus. This is the compact-form version of the lattice-VOA automorphism decomposition (Dong and Nagatomo 1999a, §2).

Here is a finite algebraic description of the inner group's entire matrix image on $W$. Its Lie algebra is spanned by the Cartan and the compact root directions from the squared-norm-two roots. For every root, include its compact $SU(2)$ zero-mode action, using a finite irreducible decomposition of that action on $W$. In an algebraic weight basis this is the ordinary polynomial symmetric-power representation of the two-dimensional $SU(2)$ matrix, with algebraic changes of basis. Such bases can be constructed by finding highest-weight vectors in the kernels of the algebraic raising matrices and normalizing successive lowering vectors. The Cartan torus acts as the phases $\prod_j z_j^{n_j}$ on lattice momentum $\sum_j n_j b_j$, with $|z_j|=1$ and algebraic Laurent matrix entries. In real coordinates these phases, the equations of $SU(2)$, and their matrix images are semialgebraic. All these transformations are actual unitary VOA automorphisms generated by the indicated zero modes.

Let $K_0$ be the set of products using each of these root images and the torus in one fixed order, with identity available in every factor. Let $K=K_0K_0^{-1}$. This is a symmetric compact semialgebraic set containing identity. The differential of the product at the identity spans the full compact inner Lie algebra, so $K$ contains a neighborhood of identity in the connected compact inner group. The subgroup it generates is open and hence all of that connected group. By compactness there is some finite $m$ for which $K^m$ already equals the group: the interiors of the nested powers cover the group, since multiplication by the identity neighborhood gives an interior neighborhood of each previously reached point. The least stabilizing power can be found without knowing a bound, by incrementing $m$ and checking $K^{m+1}\subseteq K^m$ using real algebraic quantifier elimination. At a successful step the nested products stabilize and equal the whole generated group. This supplies a terminating finite parametrization by products of bounded length.

Finally enumerate the finitely many lattice lifts and all bijections between the two finite deck lists. For each choice check whether some matrix in the parametrized compact inner group, multiplied by that lift, intertwines the two deck lists on $W$ under the given bijection. These are real algebraic equations with exact algebraic coefficients. Quantifier elimination either supplies a witness or rejects the choice. Faithfulness of $W$ proves that any solution conjugates the entire original action, and exhaustiveness of the compact-group calculation proves the converse. A successful candidate gives a product of actual unitary transformations and hence a calibrated vacuum-net response equivalence. The same finite $W$ representation distinguishes every deck element from every other element on the parent; it can be tested by a finite family of preparations and effects on that grade cut. If only local bounded instruments are used, two distinct vacuum implementers already induce different automorphisms on any interval by cyclicity. The fixed vacuum response and the parent-retained response thus have their respective tested domains. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457at"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457at-finite-automorphism-orbifolds-and-their-unmarked-responses"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7at (Finite Automorphism Orbifolds and Their Unmarked Responses).** Take as parent any finite tensor product of ordinary even positive-definite lattice conformal nets or the local extension nets of Theorem 14.5.7ao, with their vacuum field models. Let any finite group act faithfully by unitary conformal automorphisms. Compare oriented vacuum nets with stress tensor, calibrated rotations and the complete local instruments of Theorem 14.5.7s. There is a terminating exact classification of the parent-marked fixed inclusions and of the unmarked fixed nets, across all these parents and groups. For a fixed parent and finite abstract group, the construction gives a finite list of exact representatives of all actions up to parent conjugacy. It decides every comparison of their actual intermediate fixed nets, including mixed comparisons with the original parents. In particular it exhausts the unmarked fixed nets and all purely electric outputs of arbitrary finite lattice permutations at every ground grade, and applies to finite orbifolds of the actual twisted lattice-inversion extension nets.

*Finite field data on the parents.* Theorem 14.5.7ao constructs exact graded field tables and strong generating grades for ordinary lattice, inversion fixed and twisted inversion extension vacuum models. Each table includes the positive Hermitian form, the antilinear PCT operator, the vacuum and conformal vector, and the modes between the requested finite grades. Tensor products have these data by finite convolution at each grade. Their strong generators are the constituent generators tensored with vacuum vectors in the other factors. Write $V$ for a parent vacuum algebra, and let $W=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{b}V_j$ contain such generators and the conformal vector. Apply (14.5.7ao.1) with $V$ as both input and output. Its equations cut out the image of the full unitary conformal automorphism group on $W$, by the mode and Gram reconstruction in Theorem 14.5.7ao. The image is closed in the product of the grade unitary groups and is a compact matrix Lie group $A$ with real algebraic defining data. Each matrix in $A$ acts on any requested grade by transporting negative-mode words from $W$. Evaluating such words and solving the resulting finite linear systems computes those action matrices. The vacuum net and its unitary field model have the same automorphisms by conformal vacuum reconstruction (Carpi et al. 2015).

*Exact enumeration of the finite actions.* Fix a finite group table $G$. Put one variable $M_g\in A$ at each group element, impose $M_1=I$ and $M_gM_h=M_{gh}$, and for faithful actions require $M_g\ne I$ for $g\ne1$. Conjugacy of two such tuples in $A$ is a quantified finite real algebraic condition. There are only finitely many conjugacy classes of homomorphisms from $G$ into $A$. To see local constancy of the class at $\rho:G\to A$, equip the identity component of $A$ with a bi-invariant Riemannian metric. For a nearby homomorphism $\sigma$, the maps $x\mapsto\sigma(g)x\rho(g)^{-1}$ are isometries of that component and form a finite action. The orbit of the identity is arbitrarily close to the identity when $\sigma$ is sufficiently close to $\rho$. The squared distances to the orbit have a unique minimizing center there: in a sufficiently small geodesically convex ball around the identity the squared distances have positive Hessian, and any global minimum lies in that ball when the orbit is sufficiently close to the identity. The latter follows by comparing with the value at the identity and using the triangle inequality for points outside the ball. This center is fixed under the finite action, so it conjugates $\rho$ to $\sigma$. This argument includes disconnected $A$, since nearby $\sigma(g)\rho(g)^{-1}$ belong to the identity component. The space of all homomorphisms is closed in the compact product $A^{|G|}$, so its cover by open conjugacy classes is finite. Real algebraic quantifier elimination now chooses one algebraic tuple at a time outside the classes already selected and decides when the complement is empty. The same comparison can quotient by the finite relabelings of $G$, if the action labels are omitted. Thus every actual finite action has a representative on algebraic field data. An action can be supplied by its algebraic matrices on $W$ or by its representative in this finite census.

*A certified generating cutoff for the fixed algebra.* For an algebraic representative set $B=V^G$ and compute each of its graded spaces by the averaging projector. The singular-mode test of Theorem 14.5.7ao needs a certified generating cutoff for $B$. The finite polynomial module in the proof of the finite-orbifold derivative bound gives such a cutoff. The following specifies its computation on this source. Take $U=\bigoplus_{j=1}^{b}V_j$ and $r=|G|$. For every $g\ne1$ choose a homogeneous $a_g\in U$ with $u_g=(1-g)a_g\ne0$. Such a vector exists because $W$ generates the parent and $g$ fixes the vacuum. Choose a homogeneous $w_g$ with $(u_g)_{\mathrm{wt}(u_g)+\mathrm{wt}(w_g)-1}w_g=\mathbf1$. These choices are found by exact linear algebra and grade enumeration. A vacuum partner exists by the nondegeneracy of the vacuum form in the simple CFT-type algebra, or by the vacuum-coefficient lemma in the cited proof. Replace each auxiliary state by its finite $G$-span when forming the input spaces.

For each $1\le k\le r$ and each choice of $k$ weights in $U$, take the corresponding $k$ homogeneous spaces of $U$. Append $2r-1$ copies of the ordered pair of auxiliary spaces for each $g\ne1$. Let $\widetilde E$ be this ordered tensor product and $h_i$ its slot weights. In the auxiliary slots let $\xi$ be the tensor of the selected pairs $(u_g,w_g)$. Let $R=\overline{\mathbb Q}[p_1,\ldots,p_m]$, where $m$ is the total number of slots. The notation $P_I$ denotes the group average acting diagonally on the slots of $I$, and as the identity on the other slots. Form the homogeneous relation submodule
$$
J=\sum_{\varnothing\ne I\subseteq\{1,\ldots,m\}}
 R\left(\sum_{i\in I}p_i\right)^{1+\sum_{i\in I,\ j\notin I}(h_i+h_j)} P_I\widetilde E
 \qquad\subseteq R\otimes\widetilde E.
\tag{14.5.7at.1}
$$
For each $i$ find a positive power $p_i^{\nu_i}$ taking every basis tensor in the original slots tensored with $\xi$ into $J$. One can test each power by linear algebra in its homogeneous degree, multiplying the finitely many homogeneous generators of $J$ by all monomials of the needed complementary degree. The support argument in the finite-orbifold derivative bound proves that these searches terminate. It localizes at a nonzero coordinate vector $\lambda$. Any subset with $\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_i\ne0$ kills the image of its invariant projector in the localization of $(R\otimes\widetilde E)/J$. If $\lambda$ has at least $2r-1$ nonzero positions, $r$ of them lie in one open half-plane, so all their nonempty subset sums are nonzero. The polarization of the finite-group orbit polynomial says that these subset invariant images span the tensor product of those $r$ slots. If $\lambda$ has fewer nonzero positions, choose one such position and a zero position carrying a copy of each $u_g$. Summing $hv\otimes\bigotimes_{g\ne1}(ha_g-ga_g)$ over $h\in G$ leaves only the term $h=1$. Expanding the same sum gives invariant-projector terms on subsets containing the chosen nonzero position and otherwise only the selected zero positions. The prescribed tensors therefore vanish in every localization away from zero. The Nullstellensatz gives the required powers, and membership over $\mathbb C$ is equivalent to membership over $\overline{\mathbb Q}$ by the exact homogeneous linear equations (Riesen 2026, §4).

All monomials of degree at least $q_0=1+\sum_i(\nu_i-1)$ now kill the prescribed tensors. To apply this certificate, quotient the parent by the span of $b_{-2}v$ for $b\in B$ and $v\in V$. In that quotient the modes $b_{-n}$ with $n\ge2$ vanish by translation. In a vacuum product regularized by $\prod_{i<j}(z_i-z_j)^{h_i+h_j}$, taking invariants in the slots of $I$ and translating them together at fixed relative displacements gives fields of $B$ applied to the complementary product. Its surviving powers of the common coordinate are bounded above by the degree of the cross factors, namely $\sum_{i\in I,\ j\notin I}(h_i+h_j)$. Derivatives in that common direction thus give (14.5.7at.1) on Taylor coefficients. The regularized product on the prescribed tensors has Taylor degree below $q_0$ in this quotient. The weight of a coefficient is its Taylor degree plus $\sum_i h_i$ minus the degree of the regularizing polynomial, so $\sum_i h_i+q_0-1$ is an upper bound. Colliding the auxiliary pairs one by one takes their vacuum coefficient and leaves a nonzero scalar polynomial times the regularized original product.
