At a fixed weight multiplication by this polynomial is injective. The weight bound consequently holds for the original vacuum product. Averaging a sum of terms $b_{-2}v$ replaces each $v$ by its invariant projection, so the invariant part of the relative relations is exactly $C_2(B)$. The bound therefore applies to the images in $B/C_2(B)$ of invariant words on the original slots (Riesen 2026, §§3–4).

Take the maximum of these bounds over the finite slot choices and of $2$. This gives a strong generating cutoff for $B$. Indeed the associated graded for the filtration by sum of generating weights is a commutative differential algebra generated by the derivatives of $U$. Taking invariants is exact, and the Noether degree bound gives invariant generators with at most $r$ factors, regardless of derivative order. Their invariant negative-mode lifts are the words just bounded. The images of those lifts generate $B/C_2(B)$ by induction on filtration degree. The positive-grade lifts then strongly generate $B$ by the $C_2$ lifting criterion. This argument supplies an exact stopping test through the powers in (14.5.7at.1) at every twisted ground grade (Riesen 2026, §§2.2 and 5).

*The response equivalences and intermediate nets.* The projected fixed field model is strongly local and is the fixed subnet on its vacuum Hilbert space by the fixed-point and subalgebra correspondence. Its finite multiplication tables are those of the parent restricted to the computed invariant spaces. For two such models, use the greater of the certified cutoffs and apply (14.5.7ao.1). The exact polynomial test returns either a full vacuum-covariant unitary or infeasibility. Vacuum reconstruction makes the test necessary, and the word and Gram argument makes it sufficient for the complete local responses and the calibrated spectral measures. For the parent-marked comparison instead use the finite parent field equations with the additional requirement that the candidate map conjugate the acting subgroups. This requirement is necessary by fixed-net Galois correspondence, which recovers the acting group as the full group over the fixed subnet. It is sufficient since conjugation transports the actual fixed projections, inclusions and instruments. There is one intermediate net $\mathcal A_V^H$ for each $H\le G$, with index $[G:H]$ above $\mathcal A_V^G$. Its actual grade projection is $|H|^{-1}\sum_{h\in H}h$. Applying the cutoff to each $H$ classifies every unmarked intermediate and its comparisons across different parents. If several intermediate inclusions are retained as marks, the same parent equation requires the conjugating map to carry their subgroups simultaneously. This also handles every purely electric output of Theorem 14.5.7r over the lattice permutation parents, since the output is the actual fixed net of its electric stabilizer (Hanaki et al. 1999; Carpi et al. 2015).

*A non-electric example below the high-grade condition.* Use the active Leech inversion fixed net $\mathcal B_\Lambda$ and its holomorphic Moonshine flux extension $\mathcal A^\natural$ from Theorem 14.5.7q. Write $\delta$ for its dual sign, whose odd space is the active bosonic flux. On three $E_8$ factors take the plain passive action of $S_3$. For either character $\chi:S_3\to\mathbb Z_2$, the graph action $q\mapsto\delta^{\chi(q)}\otimes q$ acts on $\mathcal A^\natural$ tensored with the passive parent. Its fixed net is an actual local index-two extension over $\mathcal B_\Lambda\otimes(\mathcal A_{E_8}^{\otimes3})^{S_3}$. The invariant vacuum restriction is the vacuum together with the active bosonic flux tensored with the passive electric character $(-1)^\chi$. Both summands follow by taking invariants separately in the two sign spaces of $\delta$, and the flux summand has dimension one as a sector. Its support corresponds to the full noncyclic stabilizer $\mathbb Z_2\times S_3$ and nontrivial normal flux $\mathbb Z_2\times\{1\}$ in Theorem 14.5.7r. The passive transposition has twisted ground grade $1/2$, and the three-cycle has grade $8/9$. The trivial and sign couplings give distinct unmarked responses: by Theorem 14.5.7q the first odd space in $\mathcal A^\natural$ has grade two and dimension $196884-98580=98304$. It contributes in full at grade two for the trivial coupling. It contributes zero at that grade for the sign coupling since the passive vacuum has trivial character. The even contributions agree exactly. The finite mode test applies also to arbitrary finite passive groups on the stated parents and all such graph actions, including mixed comparisons with ordinary lattice nets and the other fixed nets in this theorem. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457au"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457au-intrinsic-edge-records-and-on-site-twisted-double-comparison"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7au (Intrinsic Edge Records and On-Site Twisted-Double Comparison).** Use the periodic northeast-triangulated Golay–Leech sources of Theorems 14.5.7p and 14.5.7u, with the named complete matrix factors of the macrocells, the calibrated free Hamiltonian, and the complete named face and vertex instruments of Theorem 14.5.7ag. Retain the native rotor and spectator dictionary. The sharp edge instruments and microscopic junction and translation commands may be omitted. The named face effects alone recover all three sharp edge algebras in every cell up to independent relabelings of their atoms. Consequently even the comparison omitting the sharp probes forces the two finite groups to be isomorphic. All bulk equivalences are given by the finite connection and phase tests below. For arbitrary normalized cocycle representatives the bulk test uses only finitely many exact product equalities of the source phases. For a registered algebraic coherent collar menu, the same result reduces the whole-carrier comparison to finite edge permutations, cell phase variables and the logical collar variables of Theorem 14.5.7ag.

*Intrinsic reconstruction of the edge atoms.* Write $e_x,n_x,d_x$ for the east, north and northeast diagonal labels of the edges leaving $x$, in those physical orientations. Let $E,N$ denote the unit east and north steps. The two triangular effects in the square based at $x$ are the sharp predicates
$$
e_xn_{x+E}=d_x,
\qquad
n_xe_{x+N}=d_x.
\tag{14.5.7au.1}
$$
Reversing an orientation changes the corresponding group label to its inverse. Changing the starting point of the flat boundary word conjugates that word. Thus (14.5.7au.1) describes the same effects for any of the registered branchings. For an operator on two cells, take its linear slice space on one cell by applying all linear functionals on the other cell. This space is intrinsic to the operator and the two named factors. The slice of the first effect on $x+E$ is the span of the atom projections of the north register at $x+E$. Indeed the effect has one term for each north label $b$, with projection onto the nonempty disjoint sets $e_x^{-1}d_x=b$ at $x$. Those projections are linearly independent, so all north atom projections belong to the slice. The same argument gives the east algebra at $x+N$ from the second effect. Every cell occurs in both roles.

On $x$, the first effect also supplies the diagonal algebra of $e_x^{-1}d_x$. Its join with the recovered east algebra is exactly the algebra of all functions of $(e_x,d_x)$. The second effect supplies the diagonal algebra of $n_x^{-1}d_x$, whose join with the recovered north algebra is exactly the algebra of all functions of $(n_x,d_x)$. Their intersection is the full diagonal algebra of $d_x$ alone, since $e_x$ and $n_x$ vary independently for each fixed $d_x$ on the complete cell carrier. This recovers all three atom instruments from the two-cell effects, with their original supports. In particular the unlabelled sharp edge instruments and their complements are carried by any equivalence of the whole-carrier response even when those sharp instruments are omitted from the menu. The three edge diagonals generate the complete cell diagonal. Thus a unitary equivalence preserving the named cell factors and face effects is a product of cell monomial matrices, by Theorem 14.5.7ag. Its permutation in each cell is a product of separate edge permutations because it preserves the three recovered edge algebras.

*All face-preserving edge permutations.* Let $\sigma_{xy}:G\to G'$ be the permutation of the atom labels on an edge $xy$, and set $\sigma_{yx}(a)=\sigma_{xy}(a^{-1})^{-1}$. For the target group put $a_{xy}=\sigma_{xy}(1)$. On each oriented triangle, flatness on all product atoms is equivalent to
$$
\sigma_{xy}(u)\sigma_{yz}(v)=\sigma_{xz}(uv).
\tag{14.5.7au.2}
$$
This equation holds for all orientations of the triangle if it holds for one, by cyclic permutation, inversion and substitution. In particular $a_{xy}a_{yz}=a_{xz}$. Normalize by writing $\sigma_{xy}(u)=\varphi_x(u)a_{xy}$. The normalization at the initial vertex is independent of the edge. For two edges leaving the same vertex in a triangle, this follows from (14.5.7au.2) with $v=1$. The link of every vertex is connected, so the conclusion propagates to every incident edge. Using $u=1$ shows $\varphi_y=\operatorname{Ad}(a_{xy}^{-1})\varphi_x$. Substitution back into (14.5.7au.2) proves $\varphi_x(uv)=\varphi_x(u)\varphi_x(v)$, so each $\varphi_x$ is a group isomorphism.

Choose a rooted spanning tree and a vertex frame $b_x\in G'$ taking all tree transports to the identity, by $a_{xy}\mapsto b_x^{-1}a_{xy}b_y$. Fix the root frame at the identity. In that frame all isomorphisms agree with one $\varphi:G\to G'$. The edge transports become elements $z_{xy}$ in the center $Z(G')$, since their conjugations fix the image of the surjective $\varphi$. The complete list of edge permutations is therefore
$$
\sigma_{xy}(u)=b_x\varphi(u)z_{xy}b_y^{-1},
\qquad z_{yx}=z_{xy}^{-1},\qquad z_{xy}z_{yz}=z_{xz}.
\tag{14.5.7au.3}
$$
The flat central connection has tree values one. Its two torus periods can be chosen independently in $Z(G')$ and determine it uniquely in the tree frame. Indeed flatness gives a representation of the triangulated torus fundamental group into the center, and fixed tree transport recovers each remaining edge from its based loop. Conversely every such flat central connection, choice of the $|\mathcal V|-1$ vertex frames and group isomorphism gives (14.5.7au.2) and its reverse implication. The fixed tree and root make these data unique. The number of candidate permutations is thus exactly $|\operatorname{Iso}(G,G')|\,|G'|^{|\mathcal V|-1}|Z(G')|^2$. These candidates are derived from the actual face effects rather than an extra mark on the group multiplication.

*Complete bulk comparison.* For each permutation (14.5.7au.3), align the target vertex matrices by that permutation and compare zero patterns and moduli with the source. Every remaining candidate unitary differs from the permutation by a phase at each cell atom, and the product of these cell phases is its diagonal on the whole edge word. Consequently the aligned vertex ratios give exactly the exponent rows (14.5.7ag.4). A basis of the integral row relations can be computed by Smith normal form. The products of the ratios along that basis must all be one. If they are, the diagonal form of the integer matrix gives the local phases by taking roots in the circle. This is both necessary and sufficient, and applies to all normalized $U(1)$ phases for which the stated exact product equalities are compared. In particular it is a terminating algebraic calculation for the exact algebraic records of Theorem 14.5.7ag. For arbitrary phases presented only by finite numerical intervals the criterion is an exact identity and an interval alone need not decide equality. A failed check pattern, failed phase equation or absence of a group isomorphism separates the actual bulk responses. A solution intertwines every spectral projector of the full check instrument, the free Hamiltonian, and their adaptive compositions with arbitrary references on the complete carrier. No flatness condition is imposed on the input.

*Cohomology changes with strict on-site support.* For two normalized cocycles related by $d\beta$, the bulk comparison always has a strict on-site phase solution on this triangulation. For each triangle define a full-domain diagonal factor as follows. Use its two edge labels leaving the common vertex $x$ of (14.5.7au.1), fill the third edge from the flat equation, then evaluate the oriented branching face factor $\beta$ of Theorem 14.5.7u on that reconstructed flat face. Both original labels are in the cell $x$, so this factor is strictly on-site. Multiply these factors over the faces. On a move in an active star, every incident triangle is actually flat before and after the move, so the ratio of these factors is the ordinary coboundary ratio of the two tent actions. On a triangle outside the star no edge is changed, and its reconstructed factor cancels regardless of its actual status. An inactive vertex has zero check in both representatives. This supplies an on-site product intertwiner on every face word, including words failing checks away from the moved star. It leaves the original rotor, spectator and calibrated source accounting intact. In particular the bulk test is a finite classification over the group tables and normalized cohomology representatives of Theorem 14.5.7u, with any further identifications decided by (14.5.7au.3) and the vertex phase tests. This on-site bulk calculation imposes no conclusion about independently fixed physical collar embeddings.

*Coherent collar controls and a basis-free separation.* For a registered algebraic collar menu, first keep the bulk survivors just described, and then impose every controlled-matrix and outcome Choi equation of Theorem 14.5.7ag on their remaining cell phases and common logical basis changes. The feasible matrices describe all whole-carrier equivalences. This follows because every such equivalence already has the intrinsic monomial form and must preserve the actual retained control words and branches. Conversely every solution transports those generators, including complementary outcome maps and all adaptive compositions. Quantifier elimination decides this finite system. For example, take the untwisted dihedral and quaternion groups of order eight on the same allocation. Both have 40 commuting pairs and the same all-face rank (14.5.7ag.5), but the omitted sharp edge comparison cannot identify them because the face effects themselves force a group isomorphism. They are separated before a collar operation, translation command or sharp edge probe is invoked. $\square$

<a id="theorem-1457av"></a>

<a id="theorem-1457av-finite-extension-towers-and-holomorphic-quotient-comparisons"></a>**Theorem 14.5.7av (Finite Extension Towers and Holomorphic Quotient Comparisons).** Fix a marked finite orbifold $\mathcal B=\mathcal A^G$ of Theorem 14.5.7r with the supplied twisted-centre identification. For every two irreducible local finite-index extensions of $\mathcal B$, all conformal embeddings of one into the other over $\mathcal B$ are given by a finite coset and phase test. The test classifies every actual intermediate net with its inclusion, the automorphism groups over every intermediate, and all finite towers with a fixed bottom $\mathcal B$. For any one such output $\mathcal C$, it also gives the complete finite list, up to equivalence over $\mathcal C$, of all its holomorphic finite-index Galois completions. Every $\mathcal C$ has at least one.

For any two outputs, possibly from different finite actions and parents, their unmarked calibrated vacuum responses agree if and only if at least one pair of these listed Galois completions has conjugate quotient actions on vacuum-equivalent holomorphic nets. This is an exact reduction using all completions, including completions other than the one obtained from the electric quotient in Theorem 14.5.7aq. If the candidate holomorphic completions on the two lists have the finite exact field models of Theorem 14.5.7at, the resulting unmarked test terminates.

*Embeddings of the transferred algebras.* Write $\mathscr E_i$ for the transferred connected commutative separable algebra of extension $i$, with data $(K_i,N_i,\gamma_i,\varepsilon_i)$ as in Theorem 14.5.7r. Use the index $i=1$ for the proposed smaller extension. Its degree-identity part is $\mathbb C(G/K_1)$, and the corresponding part of the target is $\mathbb C(G/K_2)$. A unital equivariant homomorphism restricts to pullback along a $G$-equivariant map
$$
\pi:G/K_2\longrightarrow G/K_1 .
\tag{14.5.7av.1}
$$
This map is determined by the image $tK_1$ of $K_2$, and exists for that image exactly when $K_2\subseteq tK_1t^{-1}$. Any such map is onto. Let $K_1^t=tK_1t^{-1}$, $N_1^t=tN_1t^{-1}$, and obtain $\gamma_1^t,\varepsilon_1^t$ by using the actual source corner at $tK_1$ in the transferred algebra. This convention retains all conjugation phases from the common ambient cocycle. Do not replace them by untwisted transport.

Project the proposed homomorphism to the target corner at $K_2$. Only the source corner at $tK_1$ contributes, by (14.5.7av.1). A homogeneous generator in source degree $n$ has product with its inverse degree generator equal to a nonzero multiple of the corner unit. Its image in the target corner must therefore be nonzero. Consequently $N_1^t\subseteq N_2$ is necessary. If this holds, each homogeneous map is a scalar $u_n$, and the exact remaining equations are
$$
\begin{aligned}
u_1&=1,\\
u_{nm}\gamma_1^t(n,m)&=u_nu_m\gamma_2(n,m)
&& (n,m\in N_1^t),\\
\varepsilon^t_{1,k}(n)u_{knk^{-1}}
&=u_n\varepsilon_{2,k}(n)
&& (k\in K_2,\ n\in N_1^t).
\end{aligned}
\tag{14.5.7av.2}
$$
The subgroup $K_2$ acts on $N_1^t$ because it is contained in $K_1^t$. Taking moduli in the product equation gives a homomorphism $N_1^t\to\mathbb R_{>0}$, which is trivial by finiteness. Thus each $u_n$ is a unit phase. Given a solution, transport this corner map to every target coset using equivariance. It is well defined precisely by the stabilizer equation. It respects multiplication because each electric idempotent is central in the ordinary graded product, and cross-corner products vanish. The unit and full action are preserved. Every source coset receives at least one target corner, on which all of its occupied degree maps are nonzero. Hence the resulting algebra map is injective. Conversely an embedding is determined by these corner maps and (14.5.7av.1).

For fixed coset, division of any two solutions of (14.5.7av.2) gives a character of $N_1^t$ invariant under $K_2$ conjugation. Every such character gives another solution by multiplication. Thus the solution set is finite whenever nonempty, with its size given by the order of
$$
\operatorname{Hom}\!\left(N_1^t/[K_2,N_1^t],U(1)\right).
\tag{14.5.7av.3}
$$
Here the denominator is generated inside $N_1^t$ by the indicated commutators. The product and action equations are linear in the phase angles modulo integers. Smith normal form gives all solutions, together with consistency certificates. For algebraic input phases, the finite solutions can be obtained with algebraic phase operations. Transporting the corner maps and composing them as linear maps gives the exact composition law, so finite chains and coincidences of their embeddings are also decided.

*Physical inclusions and relative automorphisms.* Use the connected commutative $Q$-system correspondence of Theorem 14.5.7r, including its compatibility with subalgebras and inclusions as used in Theorem 14.5.7aq. The finite algebra calculation above uses the unit normalization before taking the standard $Q$-system normalization. In that normalization a morphism of extension nets is the inclusion of the indicated sub-$Q$-system with its index normalization. The normalization is fixed by the units and vacuum traces and introduces no extra parameter in (14.5.7av.2). In the coset basis the vacuum functional is zero off identity degree, and gives equal mass to every electric atom by transitivity. An algebra map found above preserves that functional, since an equivariant onto map of transitive finite sets has equal fiber sizes. The unit-modulus corner maps preserve the corresponding positive paired form. Thus each such algebra embedding realizes an actual conformal embedding over the marked subnet, with the given action on its charged spaces. Conversely any actual embedding over the base induces that algebra map on the dual canonical endomorphisms and their units and products. In particular the comparison transports the full bounded-instrument menus and the rotation spectral projections on the corresponding inclusions.

For a fixed large extension $\mathcal D$, enumerate each possible smaller algebra and all its embeddings by (14.5.7av.1)–(14.5.7av.2). Two of these give the same intermediate net as a subobject exactly when their embedded images agree, equivalently when they differ by a source isomorphism over $\mathcal B$. This follows by identifying both embeddings with the same inclusion and using the correspondence. If the maps of a tower are themselves retained, keep them before that quotient. For an inclusion $i:\mathcal C\to\mathcal D$ the relative automorphism group is the finite stabilizer
$$
\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal D/\mathcal C)
=\{g\in\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal D/\mathcal B):g\circ i=i\}.
\tag{14.5.7av.4}
$$
The right side is computed by (14.5.7av.1)–(14.5.7av.2) with target and source both $\mathcal D$ and by the finite composition law. These are the actual vacuum- and stress-preserving automorphisms of the inclusion, rather than arbitrary permutations of sector names.

*Completeness of the holomorphic list.* Every irreducible local finite-index extension $\mathcal D$ of $\mathcal C$ is also an irreducible local finite-index extension over $\mathcal B$ by composition. For the irreducibility, let $A_\mathcal C$ be the connected extension algebra over $\mathcal B$. If $A_\mathcal D$ is the algebra over $\mathcal C$ viewed as a local $A_\mathcal C$-module, evaluation at the unit gives
$$
\operatorname{Hom}_{A_\mathcal C}(A_\mathcal C,A_\mathcal D)
\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal B}(\mathbf1,A_\mathcal D).
$$
The first space has dimension one by irreducibility over $\mathcal C$. The second tests irreducibility over $\mathcal B$. This is the same composition argument as in Theorem 14.5.7aq. Thus every such $\mathcal D$, including an overnet supplied only as an extension of the unmarked $\mathcal C$, occurs in the bottom-marked census of Theorem 14.5.7r, together with an actual embedding of $\mathcal C$ into it. Test holomorphicity by $N_{\mathcal D}=K_{\mathcal D}$. For each candidate inclusion compute the relative index as $[\mathcal D:\mathcal B]/[\mathcal C:\mathcal B]$ and the group (14.5.7av.4). It is Galois exactly when
$$
|\operatorname{Aut}(\mathcal D/\mathcal C)|=[\mathcal D:\mathcal C].
\tag{14.5.7av.5}
$$
Indeed let $H$ be this group. It fixes $\mathcal C$ pointwise. A nonidentity vacuum net automorphism is outer on the interval factors of this rational fixed-net setting, and the fixed-net index is $|H|$. Thus $\mathcal C\subseteq\mathcal D^H$ and multiplicativity of indices gives equality of nets exactly under (14.5.7av.5). Conversely any finite fixed-point inclusion has its given group as the full group over the fixed subnet, by fixed-net Galois correspondence. The enumeration may quotient equivalent pairs $(\mathcal D,i)$ by their relative unitary isomorphisms over $\mathcal C$, again by the finite map test. This is a finite exact test for every entry. A nonempty list is guaranteed by the electric quotient completion of Theorem 14.5.7aq: the algebra $A_\mathcal C$ over $\mathcal A^{K_\mathcal C}$ is tensored with the electric algebra $\operatorname{Fun}(K_\mathcal C/N_\mathcal C)$, which centralizes it. The resulting holomorphic extension has actual quotient action by right translations. This existence uses only the algebra and action part of that theorem and imposes no high-grade hypothesis.

*Unmarked comparison.* If $\mathcal C\cong\mathcal C'$ by a calibrated vacuum response equivalence, take any Galois completion $i:\mathcal C\to\mathcal D$. Composing $i$ with the inverse equivalence gives an actual extension of $\mathcal C'$ into $\mathcal D$, with the same image and quotient group. It need not preserve either original parent mark. The completeness just proved puts it in the list formed over the actual bottom subnet of $\mathcal C'$. Conversely, a vacuum- and stress-preserving unitary between holomorphic candidates that conjugates their finite quotient groups restricts to a unitary between their fixed subnets, and hence intertwines the full declared responses of $\mathcal C,\mathcal C'$. Thus the list test is necessary and sufficient. When both holomorphic models have certified finite strong generating grades and algebraic field tables as in Theorem 14.5.7at, its finite field and Gram equations with the finite group conjugacy condition decide each candidate pair. That finite-model requirement is on the actual candidate completions. A braided equivalence of their DHR data alone supplies neither their vacuum field table nor an unmarked equivalence. $\square$

## 14.5.8 Paradox-Avoidance Residue and the Statistics of Self-Referential Observation

#### Historical Background

Anomalous correlation between mental content and physical events has long been discussed at the boundary of psychology, philosophy, and physics. Carl Jung's formulation of *synchronicity* (Jung 1952), developed in correspondence with Wolfgang Pauli and later published with Pauli in *The Interpretation of Nature and the Psyche* (Jung and Pauli 1955), introduced "meaningful coincidence" as an acausal connecting principle — events linked by significance rather than by ordinary energetic transfer. That program raised a structural question that remains intelligible inside the present framework: whether observer-indexed constraints can alter local outcome statistics without opening a controllable signalling channel.

The historical parapsychology literature includes Rhine's card-guessing reports, the Stanford Research Institute sensory-shielding studies, the PEAR laboratory program, Ganzfeld meta-analyses, precognition experiments and replication critiques (Rhine 1934; Targ and Puthoff 1974; Jahn and Dunne 1987, 2005; Bem and Honorton 1994; Bem 2011; Storm, Tressoldi, and Di Risio 2010; Ritchie, Wiseman, and French 2012; Bem et al. 2016). These sources report proposed effects and replication tests with differing conclusions. They supply historical context, not empirical premises for the PU results. This section states which observer-indexed statistical residue is structurally admissible inside PU.

#### Definitions and Tier Structure

<a id="definition-1458-paradox-avoidance-residue"></a>**Definition 14.5.8 (Paradox-Avoidance Residue).** A *paradox-avoidance residue* is a hypothetical observer-indexed correction to local outcome statistics that would arise from enforcing self-consistency of a self-referential predictor without transmitting a controllable signal through the CC channel.

<a id="definition-1458a-passiveactive-separation"></a>**Definition 14.5.8a (Passive/Active Separation).** Call such a correction *passive* when it does not rely on CC-mediated modulation, and *active* when it is carried by the CC mechanism of Appendix L.

The framework admits a layered treatment of these two cases.

1. *Active tier.* On the Appendix L model branch, a supplied mapping $\mathcal M$ and CC response law are constrained by Theorems 39 and 51. Those theorems bound a response if it exists. The bound is theorem-level on its hypotheses, while existence of an active physical effect remains model- and certificate-level.

2. *Passive tier.* The hard-core construction below can determine the support of a specified perspectival Gibbs measure. A passive outcome residue additionally requires an actualization map, a quantitative outcome-shift law, reflexive-depth dependence, separation from active CC, and a no-signaling marginal-invariance certificate.

#### The Environmental SPAP Hypothesis

The passive tier depends on the following modeling hypothesis.

<a id="hypothesis-1458-environmental-spap-hypothesis"></a>**Hypothesis 14.5.8 (Environmental SPAP Hypothesis).** The kernel $G_{\text{persp}}$ places zero weight on configurations that would instantiate SPAP violation for any Property-R predictor embedded in the configuration. The hypothesis additionally requires that the support of the kernel coincide with the SPAP-admissible submanifold of $\Sigma$; this full-support condition does not follow from zero weight on forbidden configurations alone.

**Status.** The Environmental SPAP Hypothesis is a load-bearing modeling hypothesis. Theorems 10–11 establish the internal diagonalization obstruction for self-referential predictors; Theorem M.10.3 establishes a computational-cost lower-bound divergence along asymptotic task families carrying the pattern-specific reduction certificate of Corollary B.2.1 and using the same computational cost units, as $\delta_S(E)\to0^+$, equivalently as $\mu_S(E)\to\infty$. The hypothesis adds the claim that these constraints shape the kernel's *support*, a claim about the relationship between internal predictor consistency and the global dynamics of the network whose independence from both results is proved by Proposition 14.5.8b.

<a id="proposition-1458b-cost-gradient-bias-does-not-imply-support-exclusion"></a>**Proposition 14.5.8b (Cost-Gradient Bias Does Not Imply Support Exclusion).** The Environmental SPAP Hypothesis is not derivable from Theorems 10–11, Theorem M.10.3, and the PCE cost-gradient machinery alone. On the registered cost-gradient and pattern-specific reduction branch, those results imply an avoidance bias away from SPAP-divergent configurations; they do not imply that $G_{\text{persp}}$ assigns zero support to every configuration outside the SPAP-admissible support.

*Proof.* Let $\Sigma$ be a Polish configuration space with its Borel sets, including a finite space with the discrete topology, and let $\Sigma_{\mathrm{adm}}\subseteq\Sigma$ be the Borel SPAP-admissible subset. Every support below is taken in this declared topology. Theorems 10–11 identify the diagonal obstruction for embedded Property-R predictors. On its pattern-specific reduction branch, Theorem M.10.3 supplies a lower-bound computational-cost divergence as the SPAP gap closes. Under the nonempty-realizer and uniform reduction hypotheses of Lemma P.16.1, the extended lower-bound potential diverges along a sequence for which the infimum of proximity over all admitted realizers tends to infinity. Its interpretation as a dynamical cost potential additionally requires the separately registered cost-gradient dynamics.

A cost preference does not determine the null sets of a probability kernel. A finite penalty cannot yield support exclusion. Indeed, if a Gibbs kernel has density
$$
dG_\beta(x)=Z_\beta^{-1}e^{-\beta V(x)}d\lambda(x)
$$
and a measurable forbidden set $B\subseteq\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{adm}}$ satisfies $\lambda(B)>0$ with $V(x)<\infty$ on a subset of $B$ of positive $\lambda$-measure, then
$$
G_\beta(B)
=
Z_\beta^{-1}\int_B e^{-\beta V(x)}d\lambda(x) >0
$$
for every finite $\beta$. Therefore a support conclusion requires an implication of the form
$$
B\subseteq\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{adm}}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
G_{\text{persp}}(B)=0,
$$
or an equivalent hard-core rule identifying infinite SPAP cost with zero kernel weight. No such implication is contained in Theorems 10–11, Theorem M.10.3, or the cost-gradient definition of PCE.

Equivalently, let $G_{\mathrm{adm}}$ be a kernel supported on $\Sigma_{\mathrm{adm}}$ and let $H_B$ be any probability kernel supported on a measurable set $B\subseteq\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{adm}}$ that has not already been assigned zero weight by a separate support axiom. For $0<p<1$,
$$
G_p=(1-p)G_{\mathrm{adm}}+pH_B
$$
has the same diagonal-obstruction statements and the same cost-gradient ordering on the admissible sector as $G_{\mathrm{adm}}$, but
$$
\operatorname{supp}G_p\cap(\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{adm}})\ne\varnothing.
$$
Ruling out the $pH_B$ term is precisely the hard-support claim. If one declares that no such $B$ can carry kernel weight because every point in it has infinite SPAP cost, that declaration is an additional support theorem or primitive support restriction, not a consequence of a gradient bias alone. Therefore the hard-support exclusion is independent of the cited theorem stack. ∎

<a id="proposition-1458c-conditional-recovery-limit-boundary-charging"></a>**Proposition 14.5.8c (Conditional Recovery-Limit Boundary Charging).** Let $W$ be a finite operational window containing an Effective Operational Property-R predictor $S$ satisfying the independent-register amplification conditions of Theorem M.10.4 and a separate interpolation-realization theorem of the kind identified as additional by Corollary M.10.4.1. Assume that this realization supplies a parameter curve continuous through $\lambda=1$ inside one registered local-embedding neighborhood of Proposition M.10.9. For its perspective configurations $x_\lambda$, assume that the envelope below satisfies $U_W(x_\lambda)<\infty$ for $\lambda<1$ and $U_W(x_1)=+\infty$ in the sense of Definition P.16.3, and that each Dirac kernel $\delta_{x_\lambda}$ with $\lambda<1$ has a finite-resource realization with a certified finite budget. Let
$$
F_W:=\{x\in\Sigma:U_W(x)=+\infty\}
$$
be the window-local infinite-cost set for the envelope
$$
U_W(x):=\sup_{S'\in\mathfrak P_W}U_{S',\theta_{0,S'}}(\theta_{S',W}'(x))
$$
of the cost potentials of Definition P.16.3. On this branch, there is no theorem-level implication of the form
$$
\bigl[\,G_{n,W}\Rightarrow G_W^{(\infty)},\quad G_{n,W}(F_W)=0\ \forall n\,\bigr]
\;\Longrightarrow\;
G_W^{(\infty)}(F_W)=0
$$
for finite-resource kernels $G_{n,W}$ supported on finite-cost configurations. The obstruction is internal to PU on the stated branch: the assumed interpolation-realization theorem supplies a finite-cost approach to the SPAP boundary that witnesses boundary charging under weak convergence.

*Proof.* It suffices to consider a window $W$ whose predictor set contains a single system $S$ satisfying the independent-register amplification conditions of Theorem M.10.4 and the separate interpolation-realization theorem assumed in the proposition. Let $E^*$ be a boundary pattern with $\mu_S(E^*)=\infty$ from Theorem M.10.4. The separate interpolation-realization theorem supplies a one-parameter admissible family with
$$
\mu_S(\lambda)<\infty\quad(\lambda<1),
\qquad
\mu_S(\lambda)\to\infty\quad\text{as }\lambda\to1^-,
$$
and boundary value $\mu_S(1)=\infty$. The interpolation-realization certificate supplies the finite values $U_W(x_\lambda)$ for $\lambda<1$, the endpoint value $U_W(x_1)=+\infty$, and finite-resource realizations of the subboundary Dirac kernels with certified finite budgets. These are values of the extended certified lower-bound envelope of Definition P.16.3; its infinite boundary value does not assert pointwise infinite processing cost. The registered local embedding of Proposition M.10.9 is defined throughout the supplied parameter curve and its endpoint. Endpoint continuity therefore gives configurations $x_\lambda\in\Sigma$ with
$$
x_\lambda\notin F_W\quad(\lambda<1),
\qquad
x_1\in F_W,
\qquad
x_\lambda\to x_1.
$$
Choose $\lambda_n\uparrow1$ and choose finite budgets $R_n$ exceeding both $U_W(x_{\lambda_n})$ and the certified realization budget for $\delta_{x_{\lambda_n}}$. The Dirac kernels
$$
G_{n,W}:=\delta_{x_{\lambda_n}}
$$
are finite-resource kernels and satisfy $G_{n,W}(F_W)=0$ for every $n$. Nevertheless,
$$
G_{n,W}=\delta_{x_{\lambda_n}}\Rightarrow \delta_{x_1}=:G_W^{(\infty)}
$$
weakly on the compact perspective manifold $\Sigma$, and
$$
G_W^{(\infty)}(F_W)=\delta_{x_1}(F_W)=1.
$$
Thus weak recovery limits can charge the infinite-cost SPAP boundary on the stated branch. A derivation of the Environmental SPAP Hypothesis by finite-resource approximation would need a stronger convergence mode, such as total-variation convergence with explicit boundary avoidance, or an additional support theorem. Without such an interpolation-realization theorem, this proposition supplies no boundary-charging witness. ∎

<a id="proposition-1458d-window-family-gluing-no-go"></a>**Proposition 14.5.8d (Window-Family Gluing No-Go).** Let $\mathcal W$ denote the family of finite operational windows with the inclusion partial order $W\subseteq W'$. POP, PCE, PPI, and the present M-appendix kernel machinery do not force the projective consistency condition
$$
(\rho_{W'W})_{\#}G_{W'}=G_W,
\qquad W\subseteq W',
$$
for window-local kernels obtained by PCE-minimization. Consequently, no Kolmogorov-style extension assembling the family $\{G_W\}_{W\in\mathcal W}$ into a global support-excluding kernel on $\Sigma$ is currently available from the existing theorem stack.

*Proof.* The obstruction is visible already in a finite two-window PCE subproblem. Let $W\subseteq W'$ and let the restriction map send two terminal configurations $A,B\in\Sigma_{W'}$ to $a,b\in\Sigma_W$ by
$$
\rho_{W'W}(A)=a,
\qquad
\rho_{W'W}(B)=b.
$$
Let the $W$-objective and $W'$-objective be
$$
V_W(a)=0,
\qquad
V_W(b)=1,
$$
and
$$
V_{W'}(A)=1,
\qquad
V_{W'}(B)=0.
$$
This is an admissible finite PCE pattern: enlarging the window changes the predictor set, context data, feasibility constraints, and resource ledger, so the additional terms in the $W'$ objective can reverse the ordering of terminal configurations without contradicting Definition 15 or Definition P.6.2. The corresponding zero-temperature PCE kernels are
$$
G_W=\delta_a,
\qquad
G_{W'}=\delta_B.
$$
Pushing forward gives
$$
(\rho_{W'W})_{\#}G_{W'}=\delta_b\ne\delta_a=G_W.
$$
Thus PCE-minimization need not commute with window restriction.

The manuscript currently has no theorem excluding this finite pattern. M.3.3 fixes the structural decomposition, normalization, and ideal-limit clauses of $G_{\mathrm{persp}}$, together with the explicit drift-diffusion realization of M.5a-M.5b; it does not establish a projective family of finite-window minimizer kernels. Therefore the standard Kolmogorov extension theorem cannot be invoked to derive the Environmental SPAP support claim. A global support-exclusion theorem requires an additional commutation theorem for PCE-minimization under window restriction or an alternative global construction that does not pass through window-local minimizers. ∎

<a id="definition-1458e-hard-core-perspective-gibbs-datum"></a>**Definition 14.5.8e (Hard-Core Perspective Gibbs Datum).** Let $\mathcal W$ be the directed family of finite operational windows. For $W\subseteq W'$, write $\rho_{W'W}:\Sigma_{W'}\to\Sigma_W$. A hard-core perspective Gibbs datum is
$$
\left(
(\Sigma_W,\mathcal B_W,V_W,\beta_W,\lambda_W,\mathcal E_W)_{W\in\mathcal W},
(\rho_{W'W})_{W\subseteq W'}
\right)
\tag{14.5.8e.1}
$$
with the following properties.

1. Each $(\Sigma_W,\mathcal B_W)$ is a Polish response quotient, each $\lambda_W$ is a Borel probability measure, and every $\rho_{W'W}$ is a continuous surjection with
$$
\rho_{WW}=\operatorname{id}_{\Sigma_W},
\qquad
\rho_{W''W}=\rho_{W'W}\circ\rho_{W''W'}.
$$
The directed set has an increasing countable cofinal chain. Let
$$
\Sigma=\varprojlim_W\Sigma_W
$$
with projections $\pi_W$. Retained open cylinders form a shrinkable basis: if $x\in U$ and $U$ is open, there is a retained open cylinder $C$ with $x\in C$ and $\overline C\subseteq U$. If $C=\pi_{W_0}^{-1}(B_C)$, define its realization for $W\supseteq W_0$ by
$$
C_W:=\rho_{WW_0}^{-1}(B_C).
$$
The same convention defines $A_W$ for every retained Borel cylinder $A$. Let $\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}\subseteq\Sigma$ be nonempty and closed, with nonempty Borel images
$$
\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}:=\pi_W(\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}).
$$

2. $\beta_W>0$ and $\beta_W\to\infty$ along refinement.

3. Each $V_W:\Sigma_W\to\mathbb R\cup\{+\infty\}$ is Borel measurable,
$$
m_W:=\inf_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}}V_W\in\mathbb R,
\qquad
0<Z_W:=\int e^{-\beta_WV_W}\,d\lambda_W<\infty,
$$
and
$$
dG_W=Z_W^{-1}e^{-\beta_WV_W}\,d\lambda_W.
\tag{14.5.8e.2}
$$

4. For every retained open cylinder $C$ with $\overline C\subseteq\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$,
$$
R_W(C):=\inf_{x\in C_W}(V_W(x)-m_W)\longrightarrow+\infty,
\qquad
\inf\varnothing:=+\infty.
$$

5. The pulled-back normalized potentials $(V_W-m_W)\circ\pi_W$ $\Gamma$-converge in the inverse-limit topology to a proper lower semicontinuous $V_\infty$ that is finite on $\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$.

6. Each $\mathcal E_W$ is a declared closed Dirichlet form on $L^2(\Sigma_W,G_W)$. With
$$
\operatorname{Cap}_W(B):=
\inf\left\{\mathcal E_W(f,f)+\lVert f\rVert_{L^2(G_W)}^2:
f\in\mathcal D(\mathcal E_W),\ f\ge1\ G_W\text{-a.e. on an open neighborhood of }B\right\},
$$
the boundary condition is
$$
\inf_{U\supseteq\partial\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}}
\limsup_W\operatorname{Cap}_W(U_W)=0,
$$
where $U$ ranges over retained open-cylinder neighborhoods.

7. The Gibbs laws are projectively consistent:
$$
(\rho_{W'W})_\#G_{W'}=G_W.
\tag{14.5.8e.3}
$$
Projective consistency is an explicit hypothesis of this theorem and must be supplied directly for the chosen family $A_W=\beta_WV_W$.

8. For every retained Borel cylinder $A$, there are $W_A$ and $C_A<\infty$ such that
$$
Z_W^{-1}e^{-\beta_Wm_W}
\lambda_W(A_W\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W})\le C_A
\qquad(W\supseteq W_A).
\tag{14.5.8e.3a}
$$

9. For every nonempty retained open cylinder $U$ meeting $\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$,
$$
\liminf_WG_W(U_W)>0.
\tag{14.5.8e.4}
$$
This noncollapse condition prevents a nonconstant finite $V_\infty$ from restricting the limiting support to only its minimizers.

<a id="theorem-1458f-hard-core-environmental-spap-support"></a>**Theorem 14.5.8f (Hard-Core Environmental SPAP Support).** Assume that the perspective kernel family satisfies items (1)-(4) and (7)-(9) of Definition 14.5.8e. Then the finite-window Gibbs kernels determine a unique global Borel probability measure $G_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$ whose projection to every retained window is $G_W$, and
$$
\operatorname{supp}G_{\mathrm{SPAP}}=\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}.
\tag{14.5.8f.1}
$$
Items (5) and (6) of Definition 14.5.8e are additional variational and capacity records but are not hypotheses of this support theorem.

*Proof.* Choose the increasing countable cofinal chain $(W_n)_{n\ge1}$ from Definition 14.5.8e(1). Item (7) gives
$$
(\rho_{W_mW_n})_\#G_{W_m}=G_{W_n}
\qquad(m\ge n).
\tag{14.5.8f.2}
$$
Each $\Sigma_{W_n}$ is Polish and hence standard Borel. Push $G_{W_n}$ through $(\rho_{W_nW_1},\ldots,\rho_{W_nW_{n-1}},\operatorname{id})$ to a law on $\prod_{k\le n}\Sigma_{W_k}$. Equation (14.5.8f.2) makes these finite-dimensional laws consistent, so Gray (n.d., Theorem 2.7.1) gives a unique probability law $\mu$ on $\prod_{n\ge1}\Sigma_{W_n}$. For each $n$, the adjacent-bond event $x_n=\rho_{W_{n+1}W_n}(x_{n+1})$ has $\mu$-probability one. The intersection $I$ of these countably many events is closed because the bonding maps are continuous, and $\mu(I)=1$. Restricting $\mu$ to $I$ gives a unique Borel law on the inverse limit: its finite-coordinate cylinders determine the law. By the cofinal-chain identification in Definition 14.5.8e(1), transport this law to $\Sigma$ as $G_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$; for every window $W$,
$$
(\pi_W)_\#G_{\mathrm{SPAP}}=G_W.
$$
Consequently the projected laws on every retained cylinder agree exactly.

It remains to identify the support. Let $C_{\mathrm{out}}=\pi_{W_0}^{-1}(B_{\mathrm{out}})$ be a retained cylinder whose closure lies in $\Sigma\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$. For $W\supseteq W_0$, write $C_{\mathrm{out},W}:=\rho_{WW_0}^{-1}(B_{\mathrm{out}})$. By hard-core divergence in Definition 14.5.8e(4), for every $R>0$ there exists $W_R$ such that, for all $W\supseteq W_R$ and all $x\in C_{\mathrm{out},W}$,
$$
V_W(x)
\ge
\inf_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}}V_W+R.
$$
Writing
$$
dG_W(x)=Z_W^{-1}e^{-\beta_WV_W(x)}d\lambda_W(x),
$$
put $m_W:=\inf_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}}V_W$. Because $C_{\mathrm{out}}$ is disjoint from $\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$, one has
$$
C_{\mathrm{out},W}\cap\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}=\varnothing
$$
for every $W\supseteq W_0$. Indeed, if $x$ belonged to this intersection, then $x=\pi_W(y)$ for some $y\in\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$ and $\rho_{WW_0}(x)\in B_{\mathrm{out}}$; hence $\pi_{W_0}(y)\in B_{\mathrm{out}}$, so $y\in C_{\mathrm{out}}\cap\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$, a contradiction. Definition 14.5.8e(8), applied to $C_{\mathrm{out}}$, gives $W_C$ and $C_{\mathrm{out}}^*<\infty$ such that
$$
Z_W^{-1}e^{-\beta_Wm_W}
\lambda_W(C_{\mathrm{out},W}\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W})
\le C_{\mathrm{out}}^*
\qquad(W\supseteq W_C).
$$
For $W$ large enough that the hard-core gap is at least $R$,
$$
\begin{aligned}
G_W(C_{\mathrm{out},W})
&=Z_W^{-1}\int_{C_{\mathrm{out},W}\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}}
e^{-\beta_WV_W(x)}\,d\lambda_W(x)\\
&\le
Z_W^{-1}e^{-\beta_W(m_W+R)}
\lambda_W(C_{\mathrm{out},W}\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W})\\
&\le C_{\mathrm{out}}^*e^{-\beta_WR}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{14.5.8f.3}
$$
Projective consistency gives, for every $W\supseteq W_0$,
$$
G_W(C_{\mathrm{out},W})
=G_{W_0}(B_{\mathrm{out}})
=G_{\mathrm{SPAP}}(C_{\mathrm{out}}).
$$
The left side is therefore a constant in $W$. Since $\beta_W\to\infty$, (14.5.8f.3) forces that constant to be zero. Hence
$$
\operatorname{supp}G_{\mathrm{SPAP}}\subseteq\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}.
$$

Conversely, let $C_{\mathrm{adm}}=\pi_{W_0}^{-1}(B_{\mathrm{adm}})$ be a nonempty retained open cylinder meeting $\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$, and put $C_{\mathrm{adm},W}:=\rho_{WW_0}^{-1}(B_{\mathrm{adm}})$. Item (9) gives
$$
\liminf_{W}G_W(C_{\mathrm{adm},W})>0.
$$
Exact projective consistency gives, for every $W\supseteq W_0$,
$$
G_W(C_{\mathrm{adm},W})
=G_{W_0}(B_{\mathrm{adm}})
=G_{\mathrm{SPAP}}(C_{\mathrm{adm}}).
$$
The common value is positive by the displayed liminf. Thus every retained neighborhood meeting $\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$ has positive $G_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$-measure, and
$$
\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}\subseteq\operatorname{supp}G_{\mathrm{SPAP}}.
$$
Combining the inclusions proves (14.5.8f.1). ∎

<a id="corollary-1458g-passive-residue-magnitude-and-protocol-invariance"></a>**Corollary 14.5.8g (Passive-Residue Magnitude and Protocol Invariance).** On a hard-core perspective Gibbs branch, for every retained cylinder event $A$, Definition 14.5.8e(8) supplies $W_A$ and $C_A<\infty$ such that
$$
G_W(A_W\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W})
\le
C_A\exp[-\beta_W R_W(A)]
\qquad(W\supseteq W_A),
\tag{14.5.8g.1}
$$
where
$$
R_W(A)=\inf_{x\in A_W\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}}
\big(V_W(x)-\inf_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}}V_W\big).
\tag{14.5.8g.2}
$$
If $R(A)=\liminf_W R_W(A)>0$, then for every $0<r<R(A)$ the outside-support probability is eventually bounded by $C_Ae^{-\beta_Wr}$. The limiting cylinder law is invariant under cofinal finite-window exhaustions that preserve the same projectively consistent specification.

*Proof.* Put $m_W=\inf_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}}V_W$. On $A_W\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W}$, one has $V_W\ge m_W+R_W(A)$. Hence
$$
\begin{aligned}
G_W(A_W\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W})
&\le Z_W^{-1}e^{-\beta_W(m_W+R_W(A))}
\lambda_W(A_W\setminus\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP},W})\\
&\le C_Ae^{-\beta_WR_W(A)}
\end{aligned}
$$
by Definition 14.5.8e(8). If $r<R(A)$, then $R_W(A)\ge r$ eventually, proving the asserted decay. Projective consistency makes every retained cylinder probability independent of the chosen cofinal exhaustion. ∎

#### The Residue Conjecture

<a id="conjecture-1458-residue-conjecture"></a>**Conjecture 14.5.8 (Residue Conjecture).** *Assume either the Environmental SPAP Hypothesis or the hard-core support theorem 14.5.8f.* Then for a system $S$ with Effective Operational Property R, the distribution of actualized outcomes in $S$'s local environment is the restriction of the unconstrained kernel to the SPAP-admissible submanifold of $\Sigma$. The shape of the excluded region is not directly observable to $S$, but its complement — what remains after exclusion — carries statistical structure correlated with the reflexive depth $\sigma_S(E)$ of patterns $S$ is currently integrating. This structure is the passive-tier paradox-avoidance residue.

As an orientation: the mechanism posited here is negative in the same sense that the second law is negative. Entropy increase is not enforced by an agent acting on microstates but by the measure-theoretic dominance of high-entropy macrostates, and observers inside such systems experience the asymmetry as a lawlike tendency. A passive residue, if one existed, would stand in the analogous relation to SPAP: no positive faculty arranges correlated events; the configurations in which $S$'s self-model would be driven into diagonalization are simply absent from the support of $G_{\text{persp}}$, and what $S$ encounters is whatever the admissible support permits.

#### Constraints on Any Derivation

Two constraints are binding on any future attempt to derive the Residue Conjecture or to strengthen it.

1. **No-signalling constraint.** Support restriction alone does not imply no-signaling. A passive-residue completion must prove that every allowed remote intervention leaves each pre-lightcone local marginal invariant, or supply an equivalent process-tensor causality certificate. Any response-active late-context marginal shift belongs instead to the external branch-(iii) anomaly class. Global-in-time notation and absence of a propagating field do not by themselves establish either marginal invariance or chronology protection.

2. **CC separation constraint.** Appendix L supplies conditional bounds for a nominated active CC response law, and the Section 13 CC protocols test whether a nonzero physical modulation occurs. A passive completion must additionally prove that its outcome correction is not already contained in the active map, response kernel, artifact budget, or conditioning ledger, thereby preventing double counting.

The Pauli–Jung intuition that observer and observed could not be fully separated is recovered, in the present framework, as the perspectival structure of $G_{\text{persp}}$ itself: the kernel's admissible support depends on the self-model state of the system whose perspective indexes it. This identification is independent of whether any specific empirical residue exists, and it is the point at which the framework makes precise contact with a philosophical intuition that had no formal home in the physics of Jung and Pauli's period.

#### Relation to the Historical Corpus

If the Residue Conjecture and its missing outcome, magnitude, covariance, reflexive-depth, and no-signaling certificates were accepted, the resulting signature could be compared with historical observer-effect claims through a declared protocol and artifact ledger. The evidence record identifies which historical claims informed the signature and which measurements test it independently.

#### Open Problems

The hard-core datum gives measure construction and support exclusion. Theorem 14.5.8n supplies actualization and depth dependence on a finite source with zero correction. A nonzero exclusion-induced shift needs its own source and outcome law.

1. *Support-exclusion result.* Definition 14.5.8e supplies projective consistency, boundary control, and noncollapse; Theorem 14.5.8f then identifies the support of the constructed measure with $\Sigma_{\mathrm{SPAP}}$.

2. *Outcome and magnitude obligation.* Corollary 14.5.8g bounds finite-window probability outside the accepted support. It does not convert the rate gap $R(A)$ into a local outcome shift, covariance, sign, or reflexive-depth correlation. Those require a separately normalized actualization map and quantitative response certificate.

3. *Invariance obligation.* Corollary 14.5.8g gives exhaustion invariance for cylinder laws preserving the same projectively consistent specification. Observable protocol invariance and no-signaling additionally require proof that the outcome map and every allowed intervention commute with the projective limits and preserve the relevant local marginals.

<a id="theorem-1458h-exact-passiveactive-mixture-nonidentifiability"></a>**Theorem 14.5.8h (Exact Passive/Active Mixture Nonidentifiability).** Let $Y$ be a finite outcome set with $|Y|=m\ge2$, let $P$ lie in the interior of its probability simplex, and fix $0<\lambda<1$. Suppose the retained protocol observes only the mixture
$$
P=\lambda P_{\mathrm{act}}+(1-\lambda)P_{\mathrm{pass}}.
\tag{14.5.8h.1}
$$
Then the pair $(P_{\mathrm{act}},P_{\mathrm{pass}})$ is not identifiable from $P$. More precisely, for every nonzero vector $h\in\mathbb R^Y$ satisfying $\sum_yh_y=0$ and small enough that the following coordinates remain nonnegative,
$$
P_{\mathrm{act}}^{(h)}=P+(1-\lambda)h,
\qquad
P_{\mathrm{pass}}^{(h)}=P-\lambda h
\tag{14.5.8h.2}
$$
are probability laws and give exactly the same mixture $P$. The local fiber of the observation map therefore has dimension at least $m-1$.

*Proof.* The zero-sum condition makes both vectors in (14.5.8h.2) have total mass one. Since $P$ has strictly positive coordinates, every sufficiently small $h$ preserves nonnegativity. Direct substitution gives
$$
\lambda[P+(1-\lambda)h]+(1-\lambda)[P-\lambda h]=P.
$$
The zero-sum subspace has dimension $m-1$, and a neighborhood of its origin supplies distinct decompositions with identical retained responses. Hence no functional of the mixture law alone can recover either component. ∎

Identification requires additional information, such as a known active kernel, a proved exclusion restriction with an invariant active kernel and distinct known weights, or a separating parametric constraint. Theorem 14.5.8n supplies a finite design with the active law known and invariant, the passive exclusion restriction, the reflexive-depth law and the no-signaling identities.

<a id="theorem-1458i-intervention-design-classification-for-passiveactive-separation"></a>**Theorem 14.5.8i (Intervention-Design Classification for Passive/Active Separation).** Let $Y$ be a finite outcome set with $|Y|=m\ge2$, and let $\mathcal C$ be a finite nonempty set of retained protocol settings. Suppose that each $c\in\mathcal C$ observes
$$
P^{(c)}=\lambda_cP_{\mathrm{act}}^{(c)}+(1-\lambda_c)P_{\mathrm{pass}},
\qquad
0<\lambda_c<1,
\tag{14.5.8i.1}
$$
with every weight $\lambda_c$ known and one passive law $P_{\mathrm{pass}}$ common to all settings, which is the exclusion restriction. Then:

1. **Known active law.** If $P_{\mathrm{act}}^{(c_0)}$ is known for one $c_0\in\mathcal C$, then
$$
P_{\mathrm{pass}}=\frac{P^{(c_0)}-\lambda_{c_0}P_{\mathrm{act}}^{(c_0)}}{1-\lambda_{c_0}},
\qquad
P_{\mathrm{act}}^{(c)}=\frac{P^{(c)}-(1-\lambda_c)P_{\mathrm{pass}}}{\lambda_c}
\quad(c\in\mathcal C).
$$

2. **Invariant active law with weight variation.** If $P_{\mathrm{act}}^{(c)}=P_{\mathrm{act}}$ for every $c$ and $\lambda_{c_1}\ne\lambda_{c_2}$, then
$$
P_{\mathrm{pass}}=\frac{\lambda_{c_1}P^{(c_2)}-\lambda_{c_2}P^{(c_1)}}{\lambda_{c_1}-\lambda_{c_2}},
\qquad
P_{\mathrm{act}}=\frac{(1-\lambda_{c_2})P^{(c_1)}-(1-\lambda_{c_1})P^{(c_2)}}{\lambda_{c_1}-\lambda_{c_2}}.
\tag{14.5.8i.2}
$$
If estimates satisfy $\lVert\widehat P^{(c_j)}-P^{(c_j)}\rVert_1\le\varepsilon_j$ for $j=1,2$, the values obtained by inserting them into (14.5.8i.2) obey
$$
\lVert\widehat P_{\mathrm{pass}}-P_{\mathrm{pass}}\rVert_1
\le\frac{\lambda_{c_1}\varepsilon_2+\lambda_{c_2}\varepsilon_1}{|\lambda_{c_1}-\lambda_{c_2}|},
\qquad
\lVert\widehat P_{\mathrm{act}}-P_{\mathrm{act}}\rVert_1
\le\frac{(1-\lambda_{c_2})\varepsilon_1+(1-\lambda_{c_1})\varepsilon_2}{|\lambda_{c_1}-\lambda_{c_2}|}.
\tag{14.5.8i.3}
$$

3. **Constant weight.** If $P_{\mathrm{act}}^{(c)}=P_{\mathrm{act}}$ for every $c$, all weights are equal, and the common law $P^{(c)}$ lies in the interior of the simplex, then Theorem 14.5.8h applies to that common law, and the pair $(P_{\mathrm{act}},P_{\mathrm{pass}})$ has a local fiber of dimension at least $m-1$.

4. **Unconstrained active laws.** If $P_{\mathrm{pass}}$ and every $P_{\mathrm{act}}^{(c)}$ lie in the interior of the simplex, then for every nonzero $h\in\mathbb R^Y$ with $\sum_yh_y=0$ and small enough norm, the laws
$$
P_{\mathrm{pass}}-h,
\qquad
P_{\mathrm{act}}^{(c)}+\frac{1-\lambda_c}{\lambda_c}\,h
\quad(c\in\mathcal C)
\tag{14.5.8i.4}
$$
are probability laws that reproduce every $P^{(c)}$ in (14.5.8i.1). Without a known active law or a cross-setting constraint on the active laws, the exclusion restriction and the known weights therefore leave a local fiber of dimension at least $m-1$ for $P_{\mathrm{pass}}$.

*Proof.* Item 1 solves (14.5.8i.1) at $c_0$ for $P_{\mathrm{pass}}$, which is possible because $\lambda_{c_0}<1$, and then solves each setting for its active law, which is possible because $\lambda_c>0$. For item 2, substitution of (14.5.8i.1) gives
$$
\lambda_{c_1}P^{(c_2)}-\lambda_{c_2}P^{(c_1)}=(\lambda_{c_1}-\lambda_{c_2})P_{\mathrm{pass}},
\qquad
(1-\lambda_{c_2})P^{(c_1)}-(1-\lambda_{c_1})P^{(c_2)}=(\lambda_{c_1}-\lambda_{c_2})P_{\mathrm{act}},
$$
and division by $\lambda_{c_1}-\lambda_{c_2}\ne0$ proves (14.5.8i.2). The same linear combinations applied to the estimation errors, together with the triangle inequality, prove (14.5.8i.3). In item 3 every setting observes the same law $\lambda P_{\mathrm{act}}+(1-\lambda)P_{\mathrm{pass}}$, so Theorem 14.5.8h gives the stated fiber. For item 4,
$$
\lambda_c\left(P_{\mathrm{act}}^{(c)}+\frac{1-\lambda_c}{\lambda_c}h\right)+(1-\lambda_c)(P_{\mathrm{pass}}-h)=P^{(c)},
$$
the zero-sum condition preserves total mass one, and interiority preserves nonnegativity for every sufficiently small $h$ because $\mathcal C$ is finite. Distinct $h$ give distinct passive laws, and the zero-sum subspace has dimension $m-1$. ∎

Theorem 14.5.8i classifies the intervention designs that separate the passive component under the exclusion restriction: a design with a known active law at one setting, or with an invariant active law and two distinct known weights, identifies it, with the error propagation (14.5.8i.3). An exclusion restriction on the passive law alone leaves the fiber (14.5.8i.4).

<a id="theorem-1458j-finite-protocol-invariance-under-conditional-perspective-replacement"></a>

**Theorem 14.5.8j (Finite-Protocol Invariance under Conditional Perspective Replacement).** Consider a registered finite source protocol with a fixed initial quantum preparation, a maximum of $n<\infty$ verification stages, a finite outcome set at every stage, and the outcome-first law (M.2)–(M.3). At each stage, the preparation, quantum evolution, instrument, continuation or stopping decision, and retained outcome selection depend on the previous outcome history and on fixed source inputs, independently of the sampled perspective history. Replace every conditional perspective kernel by another normalized kernel, leaving those quantum maps and decisions unchanged. Then every retained outcome-history law, prefix law and outcome-selected law is unchanged. The replacement may depend on the full earlier perspective and outcome history.

*Proof.* A stopped branch can be padded to $n$ stages with a one-outcome identity instrument, so it suffices to compare fixed-length histories. For an outcome history $h=(k_1,\ldots,k_t)$, let $\mathcal J_h$ be the composition of its registered preparation, evolution and outcome-branch maps. The history's unnormalized quantum state is $\mathcal J_h(\rho_0)$, independently of the sampled perspective history, because each selected map depends only on the fixed inputs and earlier outcome labels. At the empty history both protocols have the same state and mass one.

Assume equality of the outcome-history probability through stage $t<n$. At a positive-probability history $h$, both protocols have the same normalized quantum state $\rho_h$ and next instrument $\{\mathcal I_{h,k}\}_k$. For each next outcome $k$ with $\operatorname{tr}\mathcal I_{h,k}(\rho_h)>0$, Equation (M.2) and normalization (M.3), conditional on any earlier perspective history, give
$$
\int \operatorname{tr}\mathcal I_{h,k}(\rho_h)\,
\delta_{\rho_{h,k}}(d\rho')\,
\widetilde G_h(ds'\mid k)
=\operatorname{tr}\mathcal I_{h,k}(\rho_h).
\tag{14.5.8j.1}
$$
This is the single-stage identity (M.7). Its right side is independent of the earlier perspective history. Averaging over that history and multiplying by the common probability of $h$ therefore gives the same probability for every extension $(h,k)$. Zero-weight extensions and histories contribute zero and require no normalized poststate. Induction establishes equality through stage $n$, including every prefix. Summation proves equality of every outcome coarse-graining. Conditioning on any retained outcome-selection event of positive probability preserves equality because its numerator and denominator are the same in both protocols. ∎

Theorem M.10.4.3 supplies a qualified Property-R instance of this instrument branch. In its nominated witness, the actual own-model update is $d\to0$ with $d=4153/3200$, the selected Fisher metric is $1$, the external update component is zero, and therefore $\sigma_S(E)=1$. Its later own-model diagnostic has law $N(0,1)$, so the finite readout $\mathbf1_{\{W\ge0\}}$ has probability $1/2$. Every normalized conditional perspective replacement preserves that probability and the other retained finite-outcome coarse-grainings under the theorem's policy condition. The imported source retains its complete bounded interpreter, paid preparation/control/readout services and original MPU qualification.

A response-active completion can leave this invariant class by changing an outcome instrument or preparation, or by allowing a later control or selection to read the changed perspective history. The new map must then carry its source, reflexive-depth, active/passive separation and marginal-invariance certificates. Physical realization of a hard-core kernel and identification of its reference measure with an unconstrained source law are separate admission fields of Conjecture 14.5.8.

<a id="proposition-1458k-hard-core-support-does-not-fix-reference-conditional-weights"></a>

**Proposition 14.5.8k (Hard-Core Support Does Not Fix Reference-Conditional Weights).** All nine conditions of Definition 14.5.8e admit a finite datum for which the global Gibbs law differs from the normalized restriction of its reference measure to its support.

*Proof.* Let $W_1\subset W_2\subset\cdots$ be the window family. At every window take the same discrete space
$$
X=\{a_0,a_1,f_0,f_1\},\qquad A=\{a_0,a_1\},
$$
with identity bonding maps, all subsets retained as cylinders, and designated admissible set $A$. Give $x$ the deterministic retained response law $\delta_x$ on outcome alphabet $X$. Equality of these laws has singleton classes, so $X$ is the declared response quotient. Set
$$
\lambda_n(x)=\frac14,\qquad \beta_n=n,
\qquad
V_n(a_0)=0,\quad V_n(a_1)=\frac{\ln3}{n},\quad
V_n(f_0)=V_n(f_1)=+\infty.
\tag{14.5.8k.1}
$$
Then $m_n=0$, $Z_n=(1+1/3)/4=1/3$, and the Gibbs law is constant in $n$:
$$
G_n=G=(3/4,1/4,0,0).
\tag{14.5.8k.2}
$$

For condition 1, finite discrete spaces are Polish, the identity maps are continuous surjections satisfying every coherence identity, the displayed chain is countable and cofinal, and every subset is clopen, giving the shrinkable cylinder basis. The inverse limit is $X$; $A$ is nonempty and closed with image $A$ at every window. Condition 2 is $n\to\infty$. The Borel potentials and computed positive finite normalizers prove condition 3. Every cylinder disjoint from $A$ has infinite potential gap at every stage, proving condition 4, with the same conclusion for the empty cylinder under the declared infimum convention.

For condition 5, $V_n-m_n$ $\Gamma$-converges to $V_\infty=0$ on $A$ and $+\infty$ off $A$. Every convergent sequence in this finite discrete space is eventually constant, giving the liminf inequality; constant sequences are recovery sequences. This limit is proper, lower semicontinuous and finite on $A$. For condition 6, choose the zero Dirichlet form with domain all of $L^2(X,G)$. It is closed because its form norm is the complete $L^2$ norm, and it is Markovian. The discrete boundary $\partial A$ is empty, whose capacity is zero, witnessed by the zero function and the empty retained neighborhood. This proves the required boundary-capacity infimum.

The constant law gives condition 7. For every retained Borel cylinder $C$,
$$
Z_n^{-1}e^{-\beta_nm_n}\lambda_n(C\setminus A)
=3\lambda(C\setminus A)\le\frac32,
$$
so condition 8 holds uniformly with $C_C=3/2$. Every nonempty cylinder meeting $A$ has $G_n$-mass at least $1/4$, giving condition 9. All nine conditions are therefore satisfied. Nevertheless,
$$
\lambda(\cdot\mid A)=(1/2,1/2,0,0),
\qquad
\|G-\lambda(\cdot\mid A)\|_{\mathrm{TV}}=\frac14.
\tag{14.5.8k.3}
$$
This proves the proposition. ∎

The unconstrained-law identification is essential. In the same example, replace the two infinite potentials by zero while retaining the finite potential on $a_1$. The resulting unconstrained Gibbs law is
$$
Q=(3/10,1/10,3/10,3/10),
\qquad Q(\cdot\mid A)=G.
\tag{14.5.8k.4}
$$
Thus the reference measure $\lambda$ and the barrier-removed Gibbs law $Q$ give different answers to the restriction claim. For a specified $Q$ with $Q(A)>0$ and $G\ll Q$, equality $G=Q(\cdot\mid A)$ is exactly the condition $dG/dQ=\mathbf1_A/Q(A)$ almost everywhere. For a specified normalized actualization kernel $K$, the corresponding outcome assertion is
$$
\int K(B\mid x)\,G(dx)
=\frac{\int_A K(B\mid x)\,Q(dx)}{Q(A)}
\quad\text{for every retained outcome event }B.
\tag{14.5.8k.5}
$$
These equalities state the additional source-to-outcome requirement of Conjecture 14.5.8; support equality supplies neither the identification of $Q$ nor that of $K$.

<a id="theorem-1458l-causal-hard-core-actualization-criterion"></a>**Theorem 14.5.8l (Causal Hard-Core Actualization Criterion).** Fix a finite horizon $n$, finite setting sets $C_t$ and finite history alphabets $X_t$, $1\le t\le n$, which include every retained perspective, source, outcome and controller record. Let
$$
Q_c(x_1,\ldots,x_n)=\prod_{t=1}^nq_t(x_t\mid x_{<t},c_{\le t})
\tag{14.5.8l.1}
$$
be a causal source with normalized kernels $q_t$ and open-loop setting words $c=(c_1,\ldots,c_n)$. Let $A_c$ be a set of complete histories with $Z_c=Q_c(A_c)>0$ for every $c$, put $G_c(x)=\mathbf 1_{A_c}(x)Q_c(x)/Z_c$, and for a prefix $h=x_{\le t}$ put $R_t(h;c)=Q_c(A_c\mid h)/Z_c$ when $Q_c(h)>0$ and $R_t(h;c)=0$ otherwise. Then:

1. The following are equivalent: (a) every prefix law $G_c(x_{\le t})$ depends only on $c_{\le t}$; (b) $R_t(h;c)=R_t(h;c')$ for every $Q$-positive prefix $h$ of length $t$ and all $c,c'$ with $c_{\le t}=c'_{\le t}$; (c) there are normalized causal kernels $g_t(x_t\mid x_{<t},c_{\le t})$ whose product is $G_c$ for every $c$. When they hold, on every $G$-positive parent history
$$
g_t(x\mid h,c_{\le t})=q_t(x\mid h,c_{\le t})\,\frac{R_t(hx;c_{\le t})}{R_{t-1}(h;c_{<t})},
\tag{14.5.8l.2}
$$
and any normalized kernel may be used at $G$-null parents.
2. Let a retained independent selector choose any finite mixture of open-loop setting words, revealing $c_t$ only at stage $t$. A single causal $g$-family reproduces, under every such mixed or adaptive policy $\pi$, the law $Q^\pi(\cdot\mid A)$ of $Q$ run under $\pi$ and conditioned on acceptance, and preserves the selector law, if and only if the criterion of item 1 holds and $Z_c$ has one common value $Z>0$.
3. For each parent history and setting, a unitary sending a fresh register $|0\rangle$ to $|v_{h,c}\rangle=\sum_x\sqrt{g_t(x\mid h,c)}\,|x\rangle$, controlled by the retained history and setting registers, followed by a computational-basis readout, is a finite normalized instrument whose record law is $g_t$.

*Proof.* Summing $G_c$ over completions of a prefix gives $G_c(h)=Q_c(h)R_t(h;c)$. Since $Q$ is causal, $Q_c(h)$ depends only on $c_{\le t}$, so (a) and (b) are equivalent at $Q$-positive prefixes, and $Q$-null prefixes carry zero $G$-mass. Under (b) the tower identity $\sum_xq_t(x\mid h,c_{\le t})R_t(hx;c_{\le t})=R_{t-1}(h;c_{<t})$ shows that (14.5.8l.2) is nonnegative and normalized; its product telescopes to $Q_c(x)R_n(x;c)/R_0=\mathbf 1_{A_c}(x)Q_c(x)/Z_c=G_c(x)$, and a $G$-positive leaf has no $G$-null parent, so (b) implies (c). A product of normalized causal kernels has setting-independent earlier marginals by successive summation over later records, so (c) implies (a).

For item 2, deterministic policies give the necessity of the criterion of item 1. If the selector chooses $c$ or $c'$ with probabilities $s$ and $1-s$, conditioning on acceptance gives the selector weights $(sZ_c,(1-s)Z_{c'})/[sZ_c+(1-s)Z_{c'}]$, and preserving $(s,1-s)$ forces $Z_c=Z_{c'}$. Conversely, under a stochastic adaptive policy with node probabilities $\alpha_t(c_t\mid x_{<t},c_{<t})$ the joint execution law is the product of the $\alpha$ and $g$ factors; on every positive leaf the $g$ factors telescope as above, and with a common $Z$ this law is $\prod_t\alpha_t(c_t\mid x_{<t},c_{<t})\,Q_c(x)\mathbf 1_A(x,c)/Z$. It sums to one, so $Q^\pi(A)=Z$ and the law equals $Q^\pi(\cdot\mid A)$; the independent selector's law is unaffected. Private controller memory is included in the retained setting history.

For item 3, if $|v\rangle\ne|0\rangle$ the reflection $U=I-2|w\rangle\langle w|$ with $w=(|0\rangle-|v\rangle)/\lVert|0\rangle-|v\rangle\rVert$ satisfies $U|0\rangle=|v\rangle$, because the amplitudes are real and both vectors are unit vectors; it is generated in a positive duration $\tau$ by $H=(\pi\hbar/\tau)|w\rangle\langle w|$. If $|v\rangle=|0\rangle$ use $I$. The Born rule for the readout gives $g_t$. ∎

Item 1 is a temporal no-signaling criterion: a later setting cannot change an earlier prefix law of the conditioned family. Under adaptive intervention the global conditional reading requires the stronger condition of item 2. For independent fair bits $Y,B$ with $c$ chosen after $Y$, $A_0=\{B=0\}$ and $A_1$ the full set, every open-loop $G_c$ has $Y$ fair and passes item 1, whereas under the policy $c=Y$ the source conditioned on acceptance has $\Pr(Y=1\mid A)=2/3$; item 2 fails because $Z_0=1/2\ne Z_1=1$. Item 1 is also stronger than constant acceptance: for a fair $Y$, a later free bit $c$ and $A_c=\{Y=c\}$, $Z_c=1/2$ for both settings while $G_c(Y=c)=1$.

<a id="theorem-1458m-normalized-filtering-and-reference-preservation"></a>**Theorem 14.5.8m (Normalized Filtering and Reference Preservation).** Let $T:B(\mathcal H)\to B(\mathcal K)$ be a nonzero completely positive trace-nonincreasing map on finite-dimensional carriers, with $E=T^*(I)$, $q(\rho)=\operatorname{Tr}(E\rho)$, $D_+=\{\rho:q(\rho)>0\}$ and $F(\rho)=T(\rho)/q(\rho)$ on $D_+$. Then:

1. $F$ is affine on $D_+$ if and only if either $E=qI$ for a constant $q>0$, or there is one state $\sigma$ with $T(X)=\operatorname{Tr}(EX)\,\sigma$ for every $X$.
2. There is a completely positive trace-preserving map $L$ with
$$
(L\otimes\mathrm{id}_R)(\omega)=\frac{(T\otimes\mathrm{id}_R)(\omega)}{\operatorname{Tr}[(T\otimes\mathrm{id}_R)(\omega)]}
\tag{14.5.8m.1}
$$
for every finite reference $R$ and every joint state $\omega$ of positive success if and only if $E=qI$ with $q>0$; then $L=T/q$. Necessity already holds when (14.5.8m.1) is required only for a two-valued classical reference.
3. For a nonzero proper projection $P_A$ and $T(X)=P_AXP_A$, and likewise for a proper classical hard-core set $A$ with $E=\mathbf 1_A$, normalized hard-core projection has no full-input reference-preserving deterministic realization.
4. If a code subspace $C$ satisfies $P_CP_AP_C=qP_C$ with $0<q\le1$, then $V=P_AP_C/\sqrt q$ is an isometry on $C$, and for any state $\tau$ the map $L(X)=VP_CXP_CV^*+\operatorname{Tr}((I-P_C)X)\,\tau$ is completely positive and trace preserving and realizes the normalized restriction $\rho\mapsto P_A\rho P_A/\operatorname{Tr}(P_A\rho)$ on states supported on $C$, with every reference preserved. For $\mathcal H=\operatorname{span}(a_0,a_1,f_0,f_1)$, $A=\operatorname{span}(a_0,a_1)$ and $C=\operatorname{span}\{(a_j+f_j)/\sqrt2\}$, one has $P_CP_AP_C=P_C/2$ and $V(a_j+f_j)/\sqrt2=a_j$.

*Proof.* For $\rho,\sigma\in D_+$ with $a=q(\rho)$, $b=q(\sigma)$ and $0<t<1$, linearity of $T$ gives $F(t\rho+(1-t)\sigma)=[taF(\rho)+(1-t)bF(\sigma)]/[ta+(1-t)b]$, and equality with $tF(\rho)+(1-t)F(\sigma)$ is equivalent to $(a-b)(F(\rho)-F(\sigma))=0$. If $F$ is not constant, choose $\rho_0,\rho_1$ with different outputs; then $q(\rho_0)=q(\rho_1)$, and every other state differs in output from $\rho_0$ or from $\rho_1$, so $q$ is constant on $D_+$. Mixing any state with a faithful state shows that $D_+$ is dense, so $q$ is constant on all states and $E=qI$. If $F$ is constant with value $\sigma$, then $T(\rho)=q(\rho)\sigma$ on $D_+$, and continuity and linearity extend this to all operators. The converses are immediate. For item 2, take $\omega=t\rho_0\otimes|0\rangle\langle0|+(1-t)\rho_1\otimes|1\rangle\langle1|$ with $q(\rho_0),q(\rho_1)>0$. A trace-preserving map on the first factor leaves the reference marginal $(t,1-t)$ unchanged, while the right side of (14.5.8m.1) has reference marginal $(tq(\rho_0),(1-t)q(\rho_1))/[tq(\rho_0)+(1-t)q(\rho_1)]$; equality forces $q(\rho_0)=q(\rho_1)$, and the density argument of item 1 gives $E=qI$, with $L=T/q$ from the trivial reference. Conversely $T/q$ is trace preserving and $(T\otimes\mathrm{id})$ has constant normalization $q$. Item 3 is item 2 with $E=P_A$ or $E=\mathbf 1_A$, which is not a multiple of the identity for a proper nonzero $A$. For item 4, $V^*V=P_CP_AP_C/q=P_C$; the map $L$ is a sum of completely positive maps with $\operatorname{Tr}L(X)=\operatorname{Tr}(P_CX)+\operatorname{Tr}((I-P_C)X)=\operatorname{Tr}X$; for $\omega$ supported on $C\otimes R$, $(V\otimes I)\omega(V\otimes I)^*=(P_A\otimes I)\omega(P_A\otimes I)/q$ and $\operatorname{Tr}[(P_A\otimes I)\omega]=\operatorname{Tr}[(P_CP_AP_C\otimes I)\omega]=q$. In the example $P_A(a_j+f_j)/\sqrt2=a_j/\sqrt2$, which gives both identities. ∎

A flagged instrument that retains its success and failure branches leaves every reference marginal unchanged; Theorem 14.5.8m concerns promoting the normalized success branch of one fixed trace-decreasing map to a deterministic operation on the full input domain. It binds any identification of the passive actualization with such a full-domain normalized filter. Conjecture 14.5.8 asks instead for a specified prepared source whose outcome law satisfies (14.5.8k.5); Theorem 14.5.8l gives the causal test for such a conditioned law, and item 4 shows that a source on an equal-acceptance code realizes a proper hard-core restriction deterministically. Theorem 14.5.8n identifies the admissible support, the source law and the normalized instrument on a fully admitted finite source.

<a id="theorem-1458n"></a>

<a id="theorem-1458n-finite-passive-actualization-with-zero-correction-and-nonconstant-reflexive-depth"></a>**Theorem 14.5.8n (Finite Passive Actualization with Zero Correction and Nonconstant Reflexive Depth).** There is a finite initialized P17 source with Effective Operational Property R and $C_{\rm agg}>C_{\rm op}$, an independently specified unconstrained law $Q$, and a non-postselected physical actualization instrument satisfying (14.5.8k.5) on its exact Environmental-SPAP support. On the same source, reflexive depth and its outcome correlation are nonconstant, the two active/passive designs have known weights $1/4$ and $3/4$, and the active and passive corrections both vanish. Each pre-lightcone marginal is invariant under every admitted complete remote intervention.

*Proof.* **Source and capability.** Use the qualified physical P17 cell, original task, ideal finite gates and original prefix presentation of Theorem C.6c.P17a, parts H.2–H.8. At least two ready cells are required, with independent original calibration stocks. Both cores execute their original forecasting and verification tasks with risk $3/16$ against the fair risk $1/4$, and the aggregate copies a retained calibration output from one cell to a fresh payload of another and consumes it as an input port of its bounded evaluator. This actual intercell gate is part of its retained complete response. It does not modify either core's qualifying task. Prepare enough additional unchanged ready cells that every basis, controller, record and auxiliary bit of the following finite word has a named supplied factor. Charge their initialization, core ROM, controller and clocks by the complete word rule of H.8. The ideal finite matrix and record services act for the registered positive gate intervals, and each communicated payload has its declared route and completion time. Their actual local pulses are the involution Hamiltonians of H.4 or the normalized measurement services of that source.

For definiteness at each finite resource grade fix bounds for the finite interpreter: program-record width $B_{\rm prg}$, state and alphabet counts, input width and simulated step bound $T$. The complete initialized program and input space consists of all basis words of those widths. The total finite next-step function reads the table, current configuration and validity flags. Invalid instructions, overflow, halted states and a final timeout have defined retained status branches. Use the exact Boolean compilation of H.5. For each Boolean input row write its minterm with CNOT and Toffoli into ready work, write the declared outputs, and retain the program, inputs, output rows and controller. Each compiled gate has its full-column unitary action, and all record branches have a complete normalized sum. Repetition implements every bounded trace. A fixed finite catalog of finite encoding, transition-check, simulation, memory, Boolean and diagonal-wrapper recipes chooses a grade large enough for their literal words before source initialization. H.5–H.6 construct the required arithmetic encodings and finite self-code wrapper, including the actual forecast-then-complement response and an explicit timeout. This gives the direct finite capability of Definition A.0.1 at zero error on the declared catalog. The family supplies larger finite grades for larger bounds. Every admitted input within a grade, including a failed or invalid one, remains in that grade's source image.

The host's fixed prefix machine yields $C_{\rm op}=1$ by its exact original qualification and short-code census. The aggregate's ready descriptor is different from the one-cell output of the only defined physical input of length at most three. Its actual finite port, word, ownership and initialized-state specification are a valid physical descriptor for the same typed decoder. The literal branch of H.3 supplies its finite all-stage program. Consequently $4\le C_{\rm agg}<\infty$ and $C_{\rm agg}>C_{\rm op}$, with both complexities in the same fixed presentation. The finite control schedules, preparations, input descriptions, individual gates and all archives are additional owned physical resources; their cost is distinct from program length.

**Own-model operation and physical response.** On separate owned payloads prepare independent fair internal and external bits $Z_s,Z_e$. The first bit is an actual internal state of the predicting aggregate; the second belongs to its external source. For $i=s,e$ the first observation is $O_i=Z_i\oplus N_i$, and the later tested bit is $Y_i=Z_i\oplus N'_i$.
