Each noise bit is the AND of two independently prepared fair bits, with independent first and later stocks. Each model independently receives its observation when its fair mask $m_i=1$. The mask and unused observations remain in the source records, but the forecast operation reads only the delivered observation and its mask. Prepare future noise on separate fresh source factors and consume it only after the two new forecasts freeze. This is the ordinary finite source law, with no restriction based on a later outcome.

The physical model stores the prior $1/2$ for each latch and the finite transition table $z\mapsto\Pr(Y_i=1\mid Z_i=z)=1/4+z/2$. Its update stores the posterior $1/2$, $1/4$, or $3/4$ according as the observation was undelivered, delivered zero or delivered one. It uses $q_i=\Pr(Y_i=1\mid m_i,\text{delivered }O_i)$, respectively $1/2,3/8,5/8$, as its new prediction. These identities follow by Bayes with the fair prior and the displayed independent noises. The internal model record includes its literal role and forecast fields, the transition law and the actual subsequent verification and accuracy records. The conditional physical state of the modeled latch and emitted bit is the basis density with weights
$$
\Pr(Z_i=z,Y_i=y\mid m_i,\text{delivered }O_i)
= \Pr(Z_i=z\mid m_i,\text{delivered }O_i)\Pr(Y_i=y\mid Z_i=z).
$$
Its three possible rational tables are emitted from the same mask/observation inputs. The aggregate holds the actual source, task and evaluator descriptions and the states, forecasts and status records specified in H.7. The fixed native perspective $s_0$ of H.4 is retained in its supplied coordinate basis, and the internal model stores this perspective identifier and the above density tables in that basis. This is a finite internal model of an actual own-state factor and its observable dynamics, with no requirement that the model know the inaccessible realized latch or contain an expansion of its own complete live state.

To realize the forecast, use fresh three-bit binary numerator targets. For either $i$, write $011$ on the row $(m_i,O_i)=(1,0)$, $101$ on $(1,1)$, and $100$ on the two rows with $m_i=0$. These are normalized deterministic Boolean writes using the four-row minterm circuit of H.5, with the actual input and scratch retained. For the own-bit decision $d$ apply Toffoli from the leading and last bits of that committed numerator to a fresh zero. Thus $d=m_sO_s$, including tie decision zero for the prior. Compute the later hit $H=1\oplus d\oplus Y_s$ on a fresh output using X and two CNOTs. All forecasts and the decision are formed before the relevant future noise is used. The whole scheduled word has fixed positive duration and fixed ownership for every observation. In particular the forecast causally uses the actual learned internal model.

The identifiable prediction stratum for these two Bernoulli laws has Fisher form $\operatorname{diag}(4,4)$ at its prior point $(1/2,1/2)$. The own and external coordinates supply the orthogonal split of Definition M.10.2. The increments have absolute size $1/8$ in each delivered coordinate and are zero in the others. With zero depth at zero increment, the own reflexive fraction is $0$ for masks $00,01$, $1$ for $10$, and $1/\sqrt2$ for $11$. A delivered observation makes the committed MAP decision correct with probability $5/8$; an undelivered one gives $1/2$. Hence
$$
\mathbb E[H\mid \sigma=0]=1/2,\qquad
\mathbb E[H\mid \sigma=1]=
\mathbb E[H\mid \sigma=1/\sqrt2]=5/8,\qquad
\operatorname{Cov}_Q(\sigma,H)=\frac{1+1/\sqrt2}{64}.
\tag{14.5.8n.1}
$$
The same report is the unique Brier minimizer conditional on its actual delivered information, with squared-loss gain $1/64$ per delivered observation. This uses the Fisher metric for the actual registered model update. A pattern-specific integration reduction or an identification of this metric with a flag-manifold metric is a separate certificate.

**Admissibility and actualization.** For a fixed finite grade and public setting word, define $\Sigma$ as the operational response quotient of its entire independently initialized source. It includes every input, control, fair-stock and measured branch of the finite word, including all failures and all retained unused records. It is a finite discrete response space. In particular the input and seed records are kept, so different input/seed choices that differ in any such retained record are distinct. The unconstrained law $Q$ is determined by their independent stock laws and the physical gates, before imposing the Environmental-SPAP predicate. It has full support on this response space. The policy version uses the same independent local source stocks and normalized causal operations, with future stocks untouched by the past. The setting and stock law is included in the complete record.

Here the Environmental-SPAP test is the original actual-capability and completed-integration predicate. Every actually admitted subsystem or aggregate predictor, including any executed virtual predictor, acts on these physical inputs and histories through the installed operations and their restrictions and compositions. A bare syntactic forecast, invalid request, erroneous report or timeout is recorded as such. It is not a prediction capability. For an installed prediction/response test on a diagonal-closed comparison class, exact intervention-stable forecasting would have to remain correct when its real response is the complement of its own committed binary prediction, an impossibility. For an exact rational-probability report $p$, the threshold response is zero for $p>1/2$ and one otherwise, so the claimed exact law fails on that same available response. These are Theorems 10–11 on their matched actual domains. At a fixed grade the assertion is only about diagonal-closed classes and actual available wrappers. On the refining family the installed finite recipes realize every nominated bounded wrapper at a sufficiently large grade, and the all-grade guarantee would also have to hold there. Exact forecasts for an unresponsive finite target remain ordinary admissible histories. For the separate certified-integration obstruction, a finite completed service purporting to cover a whole zero-gap family under the reduction of Theorem M.10.6 would give a finite uniform cost upper bound from the finite word and stocks, contradicting that theorem. Finite prefixes and uncertified integration claims remain retained; a represented boundary label alone does not instantiate the prohibited completion.

Thus no actually initialized history here supplies an original forbidden capability or completed service. The independently defined full image gives the exact admissible set $A=\Sigma$ for this branch, with $Q(A)=1$. The topology is the retained finite discrete one; a mere formal demand outside the physically initialized source is not a new point of this response quotient. This proof supplies the Environmental-SPAP hypothesis for the declared finite source and keeps its support on the entire admitted image.

Use the actual finite source instrument as $K$, with its complete output record and no postselection. On the response quotient, its kernel for the completed record is the deterministic readout of that record. Equivalently, from the initialized stock description it is the total chronological implementation just given, followed by the record readout. Every local readout has the full projective sum, and every feed-forward operation is a total controlled word. The barrier-removed and actual laws agree, $G=Q$, so (14.5.8k.5) holds for every retained event. In (14.5.8l.1) these chronological operations give the normalized causal kernels. Every positive prefix has acceptance ratio one, and the common acceptance probability is one for every open-loop, mixed or adaptive policy. Theorem 14.5.8l therefore applies on the same source. In particular (14.5.8n.1) describes an actual relation between model update and outcome, whose passive correction is zero.

**Typed active law and identifying design.** Prepare an independent aggregate context bit on an owned live port and copy it by a causal CNOT to the target control $N$. Take the installed target control family to be $U_{\rm CC}(N)=I$ on the full target and spectator carrier for both control values, implemented in a fixed positive reserved interval with a zero interaction Hamiltonian. The context preparation, copied control, clock, archive and its route are paid. Ready stocks are consumed and the copy and all outcomes are held. There is no extra reset or reused erased bit. This is the zero-change instrument alternative of Section M.6.8, with exact signed interval $[0,0]$ independently of outcome data. The copy and the target slot have distinct supports and costs. There is no presumed nonzero Appendix L effect.

Two fresh fair selector bits physically choose the active branch. At setting zero use their AND, and at setting one use their OR, computed by the finite native Boolean gates; pad to the same declared duration and keep both coins and the selected branch. The active frequencies are exactly $1/4$ and $3/4$. The independent stock and identical target control leave the designated experimental outcome $Y=(m_s,m_e,q_s,q_e,H)$ independent of the selector and setting. Both branch laws on $Y$ are the same known physical law computed above. This is the passive exclusion restriction and the invariant known active law in (14.5.8i.1), with two distinct weights and determinant $-1/2$. Theorem 14.5.8i identifies the passive law even when the component laws agree. The full selector and controller records retain their actual setting dependence, including the complete conditional coin distribution, and obey the all-event actualization equality. There is no identification claim using those extra records as the setting-invariant response alphabet.

**Reference and causality check.** Every complete local map in a pre-lightcone test is CPTP on its full source algebra, with all of its declared readout branches summed for a remote operation. For any finite-dimensional joint density $\rho_{AB}$, Kraus completeness gives $\operatorname{Tr}_B[(\operatorname{id}_A\otimes\Gamma_B)(\rho_{AB})]=\operatorname{Tr}_B\rho_{AB}$ for each complete remote CPTP intervention $\Gamma_B$. Thus every local outcome probability after its own causal local word agrees under that remote intervention. This includes arbitrary fixed initially correlated references and finite adaptive remote instruments with their full branch sum. Conversely local complete operations preserve remote marginals. A later communicated record requires an actual causal route in the source; selected remote postselection is a different conditional experiment. The complete finite instrument, its gate, resource and clock list, the full response quotient and the identifying comparisons prove the claimed same-source realization. ∎

#### Experimental Signature

If both tiers exist, separating them by aggregate-complexity scaling requires a source-separation certificate. The active tier must have a registered response law for its dependence on $C_{\mathrm{agg}}$, subject on the bounded-bias branch to its independently declared ceiling and Theorem 39's endpoint-complete consequence. A passive-tier law must separately establish its dependence on reflexive depth and whether it is invariant under the tested changes of $C_{\mathrm{agg}}$. Under such a certificate, an experiment varying aggregate complexity while holding the registered reflexive variables constant can test the two specified response laws. Without it, Theorem 14.5.8h leaves the component decomposition unidentified.

#### Status Summary

Theorem 14.5.8n realizes the active/passive comparison on one finite source, with nonconstant reflexive depth and exactly zero corrections. Nonzero active or passive shifts require their own source certificates.

**Technical ledger.**

The active bounds apply to the nominated Appendix L law. The hard-core branch gives the support theorem 14.5.8f and the outside-support bound of Corollary 14.5.8g. Theorem 14.5.8n gives a normalized actualization, a quantitative depth correlation, identification at two physical mixture weights and no-signaling on its admitted finite branch. The source has zero active and passive corrections. The sealed causal core licenses no acausal control channel; a positive late-context marginal channel instead lies outside that core by Theorem 39c, as the explicit external branch-(iii) alternative that a certified detection would select.

## 14.6 Scope of the Core Derivations

The manuscript is strongest when its outputs are sorted by logical level. Some results are fixed inside the discrete predictive scaffold. Some are conditional consequences of explicit bridge assumptions already stated in the paper. Others are quantitative model layers built on top of the fixed backbone.

### 14.6.1 What is fixed by the core chain

Within the formal model, the discrete backbone is closed on the minimal Appendix Z / attractor branch. Proposition T.59 gives the forked dependency ledger
$$
\begin{gathered}
K_0=3\Longrightarrow N_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\min}=8\Longrightarrow d_0\ge8\Longrightarrow d_0=8,\\
\text{registered binary verification quotient}\Longrightarrow\varepsilon_0=\ln2,\\
(d_0=8,\varepsilon_0,\text{ active-record/no-surplus gates})\Longrightarrow a=2\Longrightarrow b=6\Longrightarrow M=24,\\
(M=24,\text{ MacWilliams gate})\Longrightarrow k=12,\\
(M=24,\text{ Bures tangent-cell contract})\Longrightarrow D=4,
\end{gathered}
$$
and Corollary T.59a records the resulting backbone data
$$
(K_0,d_0,\varepsilon_0,a,b,M,k,D)=(3,8,\ln2,2,6,24,12,4).
$$
On this branch, $K_0=3$ together with the named local finite-response contracts (the entropy-capacity/PPI gate, the Peirce-Grassmann tangent count, the predictive-recovery MacWilliams gate, and the Bures tangent-cell mode-channel contract) closes the discrete backbone exactly: no further discrete branch choice enters within the backbone itself. Appendix R decomposes the proof graph into source roles $(\mathcal C_3,\mathcal C_{\mathrm{cap}},\mathcal C_{\mathrm{tan}},\mathcal C_{\mathrm{kis}})$ and downstream coherence invariants $(J_{\mathrm{top}},J_{\mathrm{Cl}},J_{\mathrm{ar}})$, and proves that the current source roles do not collapse into one supplied parent invariant. The registered binary quotient contributes only the structural log-cardinality $\varepsilon_0=\ln2$; a physical Landauer bound applies to a separately implemented reset and is not a consequence of SPAP alone.

Within the Appendix Z Bures tangent-cell mode-channel contract, the backbone includes Euclidean response-carrier dimension $D=4$ (Theorem Z.11), and the predictive-recovery MacWilliams self-dual-rate gate fixes the interface code dimension $k=12$ (Definition Z.13b.0; Theorem Z.13b.0a; Theorem Z.13b). The abstract gauge algebra appears on the finite-response block-frame positive-marginal capacity branch of Appendix G.8 (Theorem G.8.4b; Corollary G.8.4c). Appendix R gives the smallest admissible family-charge count in its declared SM15/SM16 candidate classes when a nonzero CKM-type rephasing invariant is required (Theorem R.3.4; Lemma R.3.4a), and Proposition R.3.5.1a selects realized $N_g=3$ only under its displayed additive-monotone objective. The $D_4$ triality/$E_8$/Leech record is a compatibility result (Proposition R.4.2). Theorem Z.26 specifies a Thomson core candidate and an exact identity with the complete physical remainder; a certified Thomson interval requires the accepted normalization record of Definition Z.27.11a and Theorem Z.27.11c. Every sector therefore retains the explicit finite-response contracts named there, and the family result is a conditional least-family selector.

Sections 11 and 12 should be read through the operational-continuum distinction. Theorem 43 is the manuscript's formal regularity-necessity theorem. ### 14.6.3 Continuum-closure status boundary

Appendix C.7 makes the present status boundary explicit. Appendix C proves that large-scale geometric irregularity is penalized by the viability requirements (LV), (GC), and (RE), and Theorem C.6 packages this as conditional coarse-grained doubling and local $(1,2)$-Poincaré control under its stated hypotheses. Lemma C.6d and Theorem C.6e add $D_4$ first-shell tensor isotropy; geometric noncollapse remains an independent continuum-certificate entry on the $M=24$, $D=4$ branch; Theorem D.6e supplies the finite-defect microscopic selection and Mosco/quadratic limit-energy identification; Theorem C.6c supplies the noncollapsed $\mathrm{RCD}^*(K,4)$ bridge when the curvature-transfer defect vanishes along the selected sequence; and Theorem 44a supplies the regular-branch Euclidean-rigidity conclusion when the rigidity defect vanishes along the selected sequence. The AQFT convergence layer is Theorem F.0, with the local Hamiltonian retained in the continuum generator. The local-horizon thermodynamic bridge used in Section 12 is formalized separately in Theorem 48a. For nearby mathematical literature on graph Poincaré inequalities and split-type properties in lattice systems, see (Levi et al. 2023; Matsui 2013; Naaijkens and Ogata 2022).

### 14.6.4 Thomson limit versus matching-scale gauge data

Theorem Z.26 and Appendix T solve different problems. Theorem Z.26 is a Thomson-limit statement in terms of the Appendix Z bulk, interface, curvature, holonomy, and $SU(2)$ geodesic-chord data. The lifted spectral threshold tuple $(\Delta_1,\Delta_2,\Delta_3)$ of Appendix T belongs to PU-to-SM gauge matching at the scale $\mu_G$ and to the later RG flow to $M_Z$; it does not enter the Thomson-limit formula itself.

### 14.6.7 Final scope statement

Read in this way, the manuscript has a clear and economical architecture: a theorem-level discrete backbone $K_0=3,N_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\min}=8,d_0=8,\varepsilon_0=\ln2,a=2,b=6,M=24,k=12,D=4$ on the stated branch; a conditional continuum/AQFT/gravity bridge whose remaining load is explicitly visible; a gravity step whose local-horizon thermodynamic input is sharpened by Theorem 48a; a metric-fluctuation closure given by Definition 12.1d, Theorem 12.1c, Definition 12.1d.4, Theorem 12.1e, Corollary 12.1e.1, Corollary 12.1e.2, Definition 12.1f, and Theorem 12.1f.1 on the nondegenerate finite-response gravitational branch, deriving the linearized emergent metric and its covariance without an independent microscopic graviton Hilbert sector; a retained-horizon no-deletion closure given by Definition E.9.5d, Theorem E.9.5e, Definition E.9.5f, Theorem E.9.5f.1, and Corollary E.9.5e.1, closing the structural-conservation layer while keeping deterministic exterior recovery under its section/sufficiency certificate, Page purity under its moment-design certificate, and von Neumann Page-entropy estimates under the trace-coupled certificate $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{PageTV}}$; a theorem-level or branch-theorem gauge algebra selection inside Appendix G; a tightly controlled but branch-normalized fine-structure calculation in Appendix Z; a cosmological-constant sector whose current five-mode numerical value is a reference convention rather than an unconditional theorem; electroweak/flavor quantitative layers whose certificate gaps are visible instead of hidden; and a final spectral calibration layer in Appendix V.3.11 that removes continuous moduli exactly on branches carrying an accepted $\mathfrak S_*$ together with the required unit bridges, RG/threshold route, and registry record $\zeta$. The Global Strict-Certificate Ledger of Convention P.14.1k is the manuscript-level status table recording, sector by sector, which strict PPI/PCE certificates are declared closed, which final spectral calibration records $\zeta$ are declared accepted, and which rows remain certificate-pending. A machine audit additionally requires a scope-complete populated instance, exact input hash, versioned executable semantic verifiers or proof kernel, their termination and soundness certificates, and a recorded successful (C1)--(C18) execution; these are not supplied. Conditional on genuinely accepted physical entries and commuting overlap maps, Theorem P.14.1k.1 selects a compatible sector tuple; the following scope criterion separately requires a nonempty global-realizer fiber for existence and a singleton fiber for uniqueness up to global response equivalence. Corollary P.14.1k.2 localizes remaining sectorwise obligations, and Theorem V.3.11f eliminates continuous moduli only after final calibration. A branch condition is a live degree of freedom only until the relevant finite-response certificate either excludes it, quotients it as response-null surplus, supplies a strict PPI/PCE gap in the sense of Definition D.8.9a, or supplies an accepted finite spectral calibration datum in the sense of Definition V.3.11a together with the required $\zeta$ record. This identifies exactly where the derivation is already strongest and where further closure still depends on stated assumptions, conventions, downstream finite records, or final spectral symbols, unit bridges, and RG/threshold routes.

### 14.6.8 Input-output economy and comparative adequacy

A framework is not more economical merely because its master equation is shorter. Economy is measured by the ratio of derived physical structure to external input structure. A variational principle defined on a preselected arena may be compact in notation while carrying substantial input through the arena itself: dimension, signature, admissible variables, constraint class, and observable map. PU therefore distinguishes notational compactness from foundational economy.

PU claims economy when structures normally supplied as arena data are instead derived from the predictive finite-response chain, including
$$
d_0=8,\qquad a=2,\qquad b=6,\qquad M=24,\qquad D=4,
$$
and the capacity-saturating gauge split
$$
6=3+2+1.
$$
A comparison framework that begins with an operator space, signature class, Hilbert space, constraint set, or background admissible arena may remain mathematically valuable, but those arena choices count as inputs in the input-output economy ledger.

The same economy criterion is formalized methodologically in Appendix P.14.9 as a shared-domain comparison rule: if two frameworks cover the same empirical domain, the one requiring fewer explicit inputs, free parameters, arena assumptions, bridge assumptions, and validation-selected normalizations has the stronger compression claim on that domain. Additional domains can justify additional structure only when the added predictive yield is carried with its own status ledger.

This is also the correct reading of PU's self-positioning. Its meta-level claim is that unified frameworks should be compared by predictive yield per structural description cost, and PU submits to that criterion. The coherence between PU's subject matter and that criterion is a methodological posture; the Thomson normalization row, the local generator row, the flavor and baryogenesis rows, and every other open sector listed in Section 14.6.6 are promoted only by their own certificates.
